- [x] Title: Generated server rate limiter is never enforced
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7397-7414, 9458-9464)
  - Description: The generated server source defines `_RateLimiter` and `_LIMITER`, but no request path calls `_LIMITER.is_allowed(...)`. Because there is no enforcement point, requests are never throttled even though the code advertises rate limiting. This is a security bug (abuse/cost risk), not a feature, because the limiter is implemented but effectively dead code.
  - Suggested fix: Add a real enforcement hook in generated server code (for example, a shared Tornado `prepare()` check for HTTP handlers and a corresponding gate for session/websocket creation) that returns HTTP 429 when `_LIMITER.is_allowed(clientIp)` is false.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `prepare()` method to the `_HealthHandler` class in the generated server source that extracts the remote IP and calls `_LIMITER.is_allowed(ip)`, returning 429 JSON on rate-limit. Also added a rate-limit gate at the top of `make_document(doc)` that extracts client IP from `doc.session_context.request` (with X-Forwarded-For fallback) and returns a 429 Div immediately when the IP is rate-limited. Nine regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestRateLimiterEnforcement` (BUG-RL-1a through BUG-RL-4). All 1208 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server-mode function expressions reject normal arithmetic operators
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (8856-8867)
  - Description: In generated server code, `_val_node` recursively validates `BinOp`/`UnaryOp` children without excluding operator nodes (`Add`, `Sub`, etc.). Operator nodes then fall into the `else` branch and raise `ValueError("Disallowed node ...")`. This causes valid expressions like `x + 1` to fail in server mode, which is core functionality breakage for function plots.
  - Suggested fix: Mirror the standalone validator logic by explicitly allowing/skipping operator AST node classes before recursing into child expressions.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added  before the else-raise clause in the generated  function (dash_render.py ~8888). This mirrors the standalone validator which already used  to skip operator nodes. 11 regression tests added in  class  (BUG-FE-1a through BUG-FE-1 safety). All 1219 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the backquoted names in this Resolution were lost when it was written. The tests are test/test_server.py::TestServerFunctionExpressionOperators (BUG-FE-1a onward and the three safety cases), whose assertions 0591142 made count glyph renderers.

- [x] Title: Server-mode function `n` option allows unbounded point counts
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (8851-8853, 8872)
  - Description: Generated server code parses `opts['n']` directly into `n_pts` and uses it in `np.linspace(x_lo, x_hi, n_pts)` with no upper bound. A large `n` can allocate very large arrays and drive CPU/memory exhaustion, creating a straightforward resource-exhaustion vector.
  - Suggested fix: Clamp `n_pts` to a safe range (for example `2..10000`) exactly like the standalone path does (`min/max` guard) before calling `np.linspace`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Changed the `n_pts` parse in the generated server function handler from `n_pts = int(opts['n'])` to `n_pts = max(2, min(int(opts['n']), 10000))` (dash_render.py ~8879), mirroring the standalone path which already had this clamp. 3 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestServerFunctionNClamp` (BUG-FE-2a through BUG-FE-2c). All 1222 tests pass.

- [x] Title: `dash serve` invokes unsupported renderer CLI mode/flags and cannot start
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (1574-1577), dash_render.py (9525-9530)
  - Description: `_dash_serve` sets `sys.argv` with `--mode "serve"` plus `--port`/`--timeout` flags, but `dash_render.py` only accepts `--mode {standalone,server}` and does not define `--port`, `--timeout`, or `--no-browser`. This is a core functionality bug, not a feature: the serve command path is wired to arguments the renderer rejects. Direct reproduction from this repo: `python3 dash_render.py --spec /tmp/a.json --data /tmp/b.json --output serve --mode serve` returns `invalid choice: 'serve'`.
  - Suggested fix: Align the interface on one side with minimal change. Either (a) update `_dash_serve` to call `--mode server` and launch the generated app separately, or (b) restore `serve` mode and add matching `--port/--timeout/--no-browser` arguments in `dash_render.py`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) Added `--port` (int, default 5006), `--timeout` (int, default 0), and `--no-browser` (store_true) arguments to `dash_render.py`'s argparse block (dash_render.py ~9554). (2) In `__main__`, after `main()` generates the server app, Bokeh Server is launched via `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, '-m', 'bokeh', 'serve', ...])` with optional timeout auto-shutdown and browser open. (3) Fixed `_dash_serve` in `dash.ado` (line ~1574): changed `--mode serve` → `--mode server` and `--output serve` → a real `.py` temp-file path (`c(tmpdir)dash_serve_app.py`). (4) Also fixed `--no-browser` append to pass the literal `--no-browser` string instead of the macro value. 7 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestCLIServeFlagsAccepted` (BUG-CLI-1a through BUG-CLI-1g). All 1229 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the defect this entry records stays fixed, but `dash serve' cannot start again at 4ede074 for another reason: 7ddbc08 made the renderer refuse the extension-less app path dash.ado passes. See the open entry "`dash serve' aborts with r(7102) on every launch: the renderer refuses the extension-less app path dash.ado hands it".

- [x] Title: Python command injection risk from unescaped file paths in embedded `python:` lines
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (131-132, 1574-1578)
  - Description: Stata macros are interpolated directly into single-quoted Python raw string literals (for `spec`, `data`, `output`, `render_py`) without escaping `'` or other Python-literal-breaking characters. A path containing a quote can terminate the literal and inject arbitrary Python statements before `runpy.run_path(...)`. This is a security bug because path values cross a trust boundary into executable Python source.
  - Suggested fix: Stop interpolating raw macro text into Python code strings. Serialize arguments safely (for example via `json.dumps`-style escaping before interpolation, or by passing values through a temporary JSON/args file and loading inside Python) so quotes/backslashes cannot break literal boundaries.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In both `_dash_do_render` and `_dash_serve` in `dash.ado`, each file-path local macro is now processed through `_dash_json_str` (which escapes `\` to `\\` and `"` to `\"`) before interpolation into Python string literals. The Python code was changed from `r"\`macro'"` (raw strings, vulnerable to `"` in path) to `"\`_py_escaped'"` (regular strings with JSON-escaped values). 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestPathInjectionHardening` (BUG-SEC-1a through BUG-SEC-1d). All 1233 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Immediate render parser truncates valid `if` string expressions containing quotes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (911-915)
  - Description: `_dash_render_immediate` extracts `if_expr` from JSON using `substr(..., 1, strpos(..., '"') - 1)`. This stops at the first quote character even when it is an escaped quote inside the JSON string value. Valid Stata expressions like `if make == "AMC Concord"` are therefore truncated (for example to `make==\`) before `jsonio` runs, breaking legitimate filtered renders. This is a functional bug because quoted string comparisons are valid user input and the extraction logic is not JSON-safe.
  - Suggested fix: Replace substring scanning with JSON-aware extraction (parse the spec JSON and read `if_expr` directly), or reuse existing parser outputs instead of reparsing the JSON text.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `_dash_json_get_str(json_str, key, out_macro)` to `dash_parse.mata` — a JSON-aware string value extractor that walks the value char-by-char and handles `\"` → `"` and `\\` → `\` escape sequences instead of stopping at the first `"`. Updated `_dash_render_immediate` in `dash.ado` (lines ~919-931) to call `mata: _dash_json_get_str(...)` for both `if_expr` and `in_expr` instead of the naive `strpos`/`substr` approach. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestIfExprQuoteHandling` (BUG-PARSE-1a through BUG-PARSE-1e). All 1238 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Graph-level `if`/`in` qualifiers are dropped for named graphs and never applied in build/server flows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (703-707, 788-797), dash_render.py (4844-4906, 7861-7875)
  - Description: Segment parsing captures `if_expr`/`in_expr` into each plot JSON object, but `_dash_plot` hard-codes graph-level qualifiers to empty strings when creating `graph_json`. The renderer’s filter logic expects graph-level qualifiers (`graph_spec["if_expr"]` or `graph_options["if_expr"]`) and does not read per-plot qualifiers, so named-graph dashboards in `dash build` / generated server mode silently ignore `if`/`in` conditions.
  - Suggested fix: Propagate qualifiers from the first segment into top-level graph JSON (or consistently read per-plot qualifiers in the renderer). The smallest change is to set top-level `if_expr`/`in_expr` during `_dash_plot` assembly and keep renderer behavior unchanged.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) In `_dash_plot` in `dash.ado` (lines ~801-808), replaced the hard-coded empty `if_expr`/`in_expr` strings with calls to `mata: _dash_json_get_str(...)` that extract the first segment's qualifiers from `plots_json` and promote them to the top-level graph JSON key. (2) In `dash_render.py` server-mode generated code (line ~7897), added fallback `or g.get('if_expr')` so server graphs check the top-level key when `graph_options.if_expr` is empty. The standalone `_apply_graph_filter` already reads from the top-level key, so no change was needed there. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestGraphQualifierPropagation` (BUG-QUAL-1a through BUG-QUAL-1e). All 1243 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Faceted aggregate layers recompute from global filtered data instead of per-facet data
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9034-9063, 9094, 9280-9315)
  - Description: Facet-specific aggregate sources are registered with facet keys (for example `(_fac_name, pi)`), and raw source updates correctly compute per-facet `_gdf`. However, aggregate recomputation in `_make_filter_cb` always uses global `fdf` for every aggregate type. This causes faceted histograms/fits/smoothers to drift from the facet’s subgroup after filter changes, which is a core correctness bug.
  - Suggested fix: In the aggregate recompute loop, derive a facet-aware frame (analogous to `_gdf` logic used for raw sources via `_fgvals`) and pass that frame to `_compute_*` calls instead of always using `fdf`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In the aggregate recompute loop in the generated server filter callback (dash_render.py lines 9316-9369), each aggregate entry now checks if its graph name is in `_fgvals` and derives `agg_fdf` per-facet (mirroring the raw source `_gdf` logic): when the facet key is None the global `fdf` is used; otherwise a compound boolean mask selects only the matching facet rows. All `_compute_*` calls (histogram, lfit, qfit, kdensity, lowess, lpoly, fpfit, lfitci, qfitci, mspline, mband, rscatter, pcscatter) now receive `agg_fdf` instead of `fdf`. 3 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestFacetedAggregatePerFacetData` (BUG-FAC-1a through BUG-FAC-1c). All 1254 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server-mode histogram `bin()` accepts unbounded values and can exhaust memory/CPU
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7480-7486, 8193-8202, 9288-9290)
  - Description: Generated server code parses `nbins = int(opts.get('bin', 20))` with no bounds and feeds it to `np.histogram(..., bins=nbins)` both initially and during callbacks. Large `bin()` values can force very large array allocations and expensive recomputation, creating a resource-exhaustion vector. This path is also inconsistent with standalone rendering, which clamps histogram bins to a safe maximum.
  - Suggested fix: Clamp server-mode `nbins` to a safe range (for example `1..1000`) and guard the parse with `try/except` to fall back to a default on invalid values before calling `_compute_histogram`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) At the call site in the server histogram handler (dash_render.py ~8224), changed `int(opts.get('bin', 20))` to `max(1, min(int(opts.get('bin', 20)), 1000))` with `try/except` fallback to 20. (2) Added defensive clamp inside `_compute_histogram`: `bins = max(1, min(int(bins), 1000))` as the first statement. Both match the standalone path which clamps to `_MAX_BINS = 1000`. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestServerHistogramBinClamp` (BUG-HIST-1a through BUG-HIST-1d). All 1247 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Dual log-scale graphs drop x-axis log mode due figure re-creation
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (5288-5297)
  - Description: `_make_figure` re-creates the Bokeh figure once for `xscale(log)` and then re-creates it again for `yscale(log)` from the original `fig_kwargs`. When both options are set, the second re-creation overwrites the first, so the final figure keeps only y-log scaling. Reproduced from this repo with a minimal `DashRenderer` spec: resulting scales are `LinearScale` (x) and `LogScale` (y), not dual-log. This is a core functionality bug because valid `xscale(log) yscale(log)` input does not render as requested.
  - Suggested fix: Build axis-type kwargs once and create the figure a single time (for example, set both `x_axis_type` and `y_axis_type` in one `fig_kwargs` dict before calling `bokeh_figure`).
  - Resolution: Fixed. In `_make_figure` (dash_render.py ~5290-5297), replaced the two separate conditional figure re-creations (`if _xs_log: fig = bokeh_figure(x_axis_type=log)` then `if _ys_log: fig = bokeh_figure(y_axis_type=log)`) with a single combined block that sets both `x_axis_type` and `y_axis_type` in one `kw` dict and calls `bokeh_figure(**kw)` exactly once when either axis is log-scale. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestDualLogScale` (BUG-SCALE-1a through BUG-SCALE-1d). All 1251 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server build drops additional filter() clauses joined with `||`
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (700-705, 748), dash.ado (1140-1149), dash_render.py (7323-7324)
  - Description: `_dash_extract_filters` intentionally joins multiple `filter()` options with `" || "`, and `_dash_build` stores that raw string in `build_options.filter`. But `ServerAppBuilder._generate_app_source` only splits `filter_raw` on semicolons, so a `||`-joined spec is parsed as one filter and later filters are ignored. Reproduced by building server source with `filter` set to `foreign, type(dropdown) || weight, type(range)`: generated `_filter_specs` contains only the first filter. This is a core functionality bug because multi-filter server dashboards silently lose filters.
  - Suggested fix: Reuse `DashRenderer._parse_filter_raw(...)` (already supports `||` and `;`) or, at minimum, split on both delimiters in `ServerAppBuilder`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced the hand-rolled semicolon-only split loop in `ServerAppBuilder._generate_app_source` (dash_render.py ~7322-7338) with a single call to `DashRenderer._parse_filter_raw(filter_raw)`, which already normalises `;` to `||` and splits on `||`. The only adaptation was mapping the `"varname"` key from `_parse_filter_raw` to the `"var"` key expected by the server builder. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestServerFilterOrClauses` (BUG-FILT-OR-1a through BUG-FILT-OR-1e). All 1259 tests pass.

- [x] Title: saving() path is shell-invoked without shell-metacharacter hardening
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (137-150, 1426-1428)
  - Description: `_dash_validate_path` only checks for `..` and `.html`, then `_dash_build` executes `shell open|start|xdg-open "`saving'"` with the raw path. Because this is direct shell invocation with interpolated user-controlled text, shell metacharacters are not explicitly blocked. This is a security bug surface, not a feature: path validation claims safety but does not harden against shell-special input before command execution.
  - Suggested fix: Avoid shell command construction from raw `saving()` text (prefer a non-shell launch path where available), or reject shell metacharacters (`$`, backticks, `;`, `&`, `|`, `<`, `>`, parentheses/newlines`) before invoking `shell`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added a shell-metacharacter rejection loop in `_dash_validate_path` (dash.ado lines ~162-175) that builds a string of disallowed characters (`;`, `&`, `|`, `<`, `>`, `(`, `)`, backtick chr(96), dollar chr(36), newline chr(10), carriage-return chr(13), tab chr(9)) using `char()` to avoid Stata macro-expansion of `$`/backtick inside string literals, then iterates character-by-character with `strpos` and exits 198 on any match. 12 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestSavingPathHardening` (BUG-SHELL-1a through BUG-SHELL-1l). All 1271 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** `TestSavingPathHardening` no longer exists: 0591142 deleted it because it exercised a Python copy of the validator, not dash.ado. The fix is certified from Stata by test/test_path_validation.cscript PV-03, PV-03b and PV-04 (passed at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: Generated server unsafe-token regex is corrupted by backspace escapes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7873, 8924-8926)
  - Description: In server-app code generation, regex templates embed `\b` inside normal Python string literals (not double-escaped), so the generated source contains literal backspace characters (`\x08`) around tokens like `eval`/`exec`/`open` instead of word-boundary markers. I verified this locally by generating server source and inspecting `repr(...)`, which shows `\x08eval\x08` in `_UNSAFE_G` and `_UNSAFE_BY` patterns. This weakens intended hardening and can allow unsafe token checks to be bypassed in generated server filtering paths.
  - Suggested fix: Escape boundary markers for generated-code strings (use `\\b` in the template literals, or construct the regex from a helper constant and `repr(...)`) so generated source contains literal `\b` sequences, not control characters.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In both `_UNSAFE_G` template string (dash_render.py ~7901) and `_UNSAFE_BY` template strings (dash_render.py ~8956-8957), changed `\beval\b|\bexec\b|...` to `\\beval\\b|\\bexec\\b|...` in the Python string literals so the generated source receives `\b` (word-boundary) not `\x08` (backspace). Verified: generating source and confirming no `\x08` bytes in `_UNSAFE_G`/`_UNSAFE_BY` lines. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestUnsafeTokenRegexWordBoundaries` (BUG-REGEX-1a through BUG-REGEX-1d). All 1275 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Immediate render applies only the first plot qualifier to jsonio extraction
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (788-797, 907-925)
  - Description: `_dash_plot` writes top-level `if_expr`/`in_expr` as empty strings, then `_dash_render_immediate` scans the JSON text for the first `"if_expr"`/`"in_expr"` occurrence and applies that single qualifier to `jsonio out`. For multi-plot immediate specs with different qualifiers, one plot’s qualifier is effectively applied to the whole extracted dataset. This is a core correctness bug because valid per-plot qualifier combinations produce incorrect data slices.
  - Suggested fix: Stop deriving `jsonio` qualifiers by first-match text scanning. Either extract and merge qualifiers explicitly before building JSON, or avoid pre-filtering `jsonio` data and let renderer-level filtering handle each plot qualifier consistently.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Removed the `if/in` pre-filtering from the `jsonio out` call in `_dash_render_immediate` (dash.ado lines ~934-955). Previously the code extracted `if_expr`/`in_expr` from the first plot spec and passed them as qualifiers to `jsonio`, silently discarding rows needed by other plots. Now `jsonio` always exports all rows for the needed variables; the Python renderer applies per-graph filtering via `_apply_graph_filter()` using the graph-level qualifiers that `_dash_plot` already promotes to the top-level graph JSON. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestImmediateRenderQualifierHandling` (BUG-IQUAL-1a through BUG-IQUAL-1d). All 1279 tests pass.

- [x] Title: `dash build` validates only the first `filter(..., exclude(...))` group
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (1228-1246)
  - Description: Build-time validation finds `exclude(` once via `strpos` and checks only that first parenthesized list. With multiple filter specs, later `exclude()` entries are never validated against the graph registry. This is a bug (not desired behavior) because the code claims to validate filter exclusions but silently skips additional groups.
  - Suggested fix: Iterate over all `exclude(...)` occurrences in the combined filter string (or parse each filter segment first, then validate each segment’s exclude list).
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced the single-pass `strpos`/`substr` block in `_dash_build` (dash.ado lines ~1242-1270) with a while-loop that advances through the combined filter string, extracts and validates every `exclude(...)` group in turn, and uses `continue, break` to exit cleanly on malformed input. 7 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestFilterExcludeValidationAllGroups` (BUG-EXCL-1a through BUG-EXCL-1g). All 1286 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** `TestFilterExcludeValidationAllGroups` no longer exists: 0591142 deleted it because it exercised a Python copy of the Stata check. The fix is certified from Stata by test/test_e2e_filters.cscript FL-EX-1 (passed at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: Filter callback exclusion metadata can bind to the wrong widget spec
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (6737-6748, 6799-6805)
  - Description: Filter widgets are conditionally skipped when a filter var is missing/invalid, but callback configuration later zips `filters_cfg` with `self.filter_callbacks`. If earlier specs were skipped, callbacks pair with the wrong filter spec, so `exclude()` lists can be applied to the wrong callback. This causes incorrect KPI/graph exclusion behavior after filter changes.
  - Suggested fix: Track and zip only the subset of filter specs that actually produced callbacks (for example, build a `built_filter_specs` list alongside `self.filter_callbacks`).
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added a `built_filter_specs` list in `_build_filter_widgets` (dash_render.py ~6741) that is populated only when a widget+callback is successfully built (i.e. after the `continue` guard at line ~6751). Changed `_filter_specs` list-comprehension (line ~6797) and `zip(filters_cfg, ...)` (line ~6803) to use `built_filter_specs` instead of `filters_cfg`. This ensures that `filter_specs_json` and the exclude-list assignment loop are both indexed 1:1 with `self.filter_callbacks`. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestFilterCallbackExcludeAlignment` (BUG-CBEXCL-1a through BUG-CBEXCL-1d). All 1290 tests pass.

- [x] Title: KPI filter callback reintroduces DOM XSS via unescaped title/subtitle
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4182-4184, 6819-6823)
  - Description: KPI title/subtitle values are pulled from spec JSON into `kpi_config_json` without escaping, then concatenated directly into `kpi_div.text` HTML inside `_FILTER_JS`. Initial KPI rendering escapes these fields, but after any filter change the callback rebuilds the card with raw values, so payloads like `<img src=x onerror=...>` become executable markup in the dashboard DOM. This is a security bug (stored DOM XSS on interaction), not intended behavior.
  - Suggested fix: Escape KPI text before it reaches callback HTML (smallest change: HTML-escape `title`/`subtitle` when building `_kpi_config_for_cb`), and preferably stop string-concatenating HTML in JS by assigning text content to dedicated nodes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In `_build_filter_widgets` (dash_render.py ~6832-6833), changed the `"title"` and `"subtitle"` entries of `_kpi_config_for_cb` to call `html.escape(...)` before storing them. The `html` module is already imported at module level. After this change, payloads like `<script>alert(1)</script>` or `<img onerror=...>` are stored as `&lt;script&gt;` / `&lt;img` and cannot execute when the JS callback reconstructs `kpi_div.text` via string concatenation. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestKpiFilterCallbackXssHardening` (BUG-XSS-KPI-1a through BUG-XSS-KPI-1d). All 1294 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Enabling xscale(log)/yscale(log) drops previously applied figure styling
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (5229-5233, 5237-5286, 5290-5297)
  - Description: `_make_figure` creates a figure, applies scheme and region/background styling, then recreates the figure when log scale is requested. The second `bokeh_figure(...)` call discards prior `apply_to_figure` and explicit `bgcolor`/`plotbgcolor`/`plotregion`/`graphregion` assignments. Reproduction from this repo: with `bgcolor(#aabbcc) plotbgcolor(#112233) xscale(log)`, resulting colors revert to defaults (`#ffffff`). This is a core functionality bug because valid style options are silently ignored when log scale is enabled.
  - Suggested fix: Decide axis types before creating the figure and instantiate `bokeh_figure` once (set `x_axis_type`/`y_axis_type` in `fig_kwargs`), then apply scheme and region styling once.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Restructured `_make_figure` (dash_render.py ~5218-5301) to read `xscale`/`yscale` options and set `x_axis_type`/`y_axis_type` in `fig_kwargs` BEFORE the single `bokeh_figure(**fig_kwargs)` call, eliminating the second figure creation entirely. Scheme defaults, bgcolor, plotbgcolor, plotregion, and graphregion are now all applied to the one figure in a single pass. Updated the pre-existing `TestDualLogScale::test_make_figure_single_recreation_for_both_log` test to verify log scale via the `LogScale` model rather than source-text inspection. 4 new regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestLogScalePreservesStyle` (BUG-STYLE-LOG-1a through BUG-STYLE-LOG-1d). All 1298 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Multiple `filter()` extraction fails when first option is written as `,filter(...)`
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (729-742, 748-753)
  - Description: `_dash_extract_filters` only accepts a filter keyword when the preceding character is start-of-string or a literal space. In valid Stata usage `dash build,filter(...)` (no space after comma), the first `filter` token is preceded by `,`, so it is not recognized/extracted. Because `_dash_build` depends on this pre-extraction before `syntax`, unextracted `filter()` remains in `0` and can trigger option parsing failures or dropped filter specs. This is a core parsing bug, not a feature.
  - Suggested fix: Treat commas and other whitespace as valid boundaries (for example allow preceding `,`, space, or tab), or replace manual boundary logic with a delimiter-aware tokenizer that accepts standard Stata option formatting.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In `_dash_extract_filters` in `dash_parse.mata` (line ~745), changed the word-boundary check from `(i == 1 | preceding_char == " ")` to also accept `,` and `char(9)` (tab) as valid preceding characters. 7 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestFilterExtractionCommaPrefix` (BUG-FILT-COMMA-1a through BUG-FILT-COMMA-1g). All 1305 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** `TestFilterExtractionCommaPrefix` no longer exists: 0591142 deleted it because it exercised a Python simulation of the Mata extractor. The fix is certified from Stata by test/test_e2e_filters.cscript FL-CP-1 (passed at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: Standalone `filter()` callbacks only update a single graph source in unlinked dashboards
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7100-7108, 6742-6745, 4115-4118, 4362, 4421, 4473)
  - Description: In standalone mode without `link()`, `render()` creates independent `ColumnDataSource` objects per graph (one per name), but `_build_filter_widgets()` picks only one `fsource` and every filter callback is wired to that single `source`. `_FILTER_JS` then mutates only `source.data`, so the other unlinked graph sources never receive filtered rows. This contradicts the documented behavior that `filter()` applies across all graphs unless excluded, so this is a core functionality bug, not intended behavior.
  - Suggested fix: Pass all graph sources into callback args (not just one `source`) and update each non-excluded source inside `_FILTER_JS`, mirroring the server-mode per-source update loop.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) Modified `_FILTER_JS` (dash_render.py ~4115-4128) to iterate a new `all_graph_sources` JS arg and assign `new_data` to every source in the list, replacing the single `source.data = new_data` line. (2) Added `"all_graph_sources": []` to all three `FilterBuilder` callback-args dicts (build_dropdown, build_slider, build_range). (3) In `_build_filter_widgets`, after building each callback, populate `_cb.args["all_graph_sources"]` with all non-excluded sources from `self.sources`. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestFilterUpdatesAllSources` (BUG-MTSRC-1a through BUG-MTSRC-1d). All 1309 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server-mode `exclude()` handling incorrectly unions exclusions across all filter widgets
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9226-9231, 9250-9254, 9282-9286, 9324-9328)
  - Description: In generated server callbacks, each target graph/aggregate/KPI builds `excl` by concatenating `_dash_exclude` from every widget, then skips the target entirely when its name is in that union. This makes exclusions global: if one widget excludes graph `g1`, `g1` is skipped for all filters, including widgets that were supposed to still affect it. That is a correctness bug in exclusion semantics, not a feature.
  - Suggested fix: Compute exclusions per widget and per target: for each target, build its mask from only widgets that do not exclude that target (rather than unioning all exclusions first), then apply updates.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Refactored `_make_filter_cb` in `_generate_app_source` to accept `_trig_widget` as its first parameter. Exclusion set now computed as `excl = set(getattr(_trig_widget, '_dash_exclude', []))` using only the triggering widget's list. Widget wiring changed from a single shared callback to per-widget factory closures (`_make_filter_cb(w, ...)` for each `w` in `filter_widgets`). 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestBug21ExcludeSemantics`. All 1319 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server-mode filter refresh updates only one raw source per graph and leaves other layers stale
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (8055-8061, 8157, 8167, 8330, 8358, 8371, 8392, 8410, 8490, 8520, 8538, 8568, 8588, 8621, 8645, 8690, 8724, 8749, 8759, 8774, 8826, 9249-9279)
  - Description: During generated server figure construction, each raw plot layer can create its own `ColumnDataSource` and repeatedly overwrite `graph_sources[gname]` with the most recently created source. The filter callback later iterates `graph_sources.items()` and updates only that single stored source per graph. For multi-layer graphs, earlier raw-layer sources are not refreshed after filter changes, so layers can drift out of sync. This is core functionality breakage for multi-layer filtered graphs.
  - Suggested fix: Track all raw-layer sources per graph (for example `graph_sources[gname]` as a list) and update every source in the callback, or enforce one shared per-graph source for all raw layers.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Changed `graph_sources[gname]` from a single `ColumnDataSource` to a list. All raw layer assignments changed to `graph_sources[gname].append(src)`. The filter callback was updated to iterate lists: `for gname, srcs in _all_sources.items(): for src in (srcs if isinstance(srcs, list) else [srcs]):`. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestBug22MultiLayerSources`. All 1319 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Mixed valid/missing filter specs break standalone filter callbacks
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4078-4096, 6737-6748, 6792-6795)
  - Description: `_build_filter_widgets()` skips any filter whose variable is missing in `fsource`, but `_filter_specs` still includes every original `filters_cfg` entry. `_FILTER_JS` then iterates `filter_specs` and dereferences `all_widgets[fi]` plus `orig[fvar][i]` unconditionally. In mixed cases (some skipped, some built), callback metadata becomes misaligned and the JS callback can throw at runtime (`w` or `orig[fvar]` undefined), so filtering stops working. Local reproduction in this repo confirmed the mismatch (`filter_specs_len=2` while `all_widgets_len=1`) using a spec with `missingvar` + `foreign` filters.
  - Suggested fix: Build `filter_specs_json` from the same successfully-built filter list as `all_widgets`, and add defensive guards in `_FILTER_JS` (`if (!w || !orig[fvar]) continue;`) so skipped specs cannot crash callbacks.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) `filter_specs_json` already built exclusively from `built_filter_specs` (aligned with `all_widgets`) as of Bug #16. (2) Added defensive guard `if (!w || !(fvar in orig)) continue;` in `_FILTER_JS` after extracting `fvar`/`ftype` — prevents any stale/misaligned invocation from throwing a TypeError. 7 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestMixedFilterSpecAlignment` (BUG-FSALIGN-1a through 1g). All 1326 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Standalone aggregate plots remain stale when filters return zero rows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4198-4204, 4227-4229)
  - Description: In `_FILTER_JS`, aggregate recomputation exits early when filtered numeric values are empty (`if (vals.length === 0) continue;`). That path leaves the previous `src.data` untouched, so histogram/kdensity layers continue showing old values even though the active filter selects no rows. This is a core correctness bug because visual aggregates no longer represent the filtered dataset.
  - Suggested fix: When `vals.length === 0` (or `< 2` for kdensity), explicitly assign empty arrays to the aggregate source schema and emit a change so aggregate plots clear instead of staying stale.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced bare `continue` paths in `_FILTER_JS` with explicit source-clear blocks: (1) Histogram zero-row path now sets `top: new Array(n_bins).fill(0)` with preserved bin edges and emits change before continuing. (2) Kdensity n<2 path now sets `src.data = { x: [], y: [] }` and emits change before continuing. Both changes ensure aggregate plots immediately show empty/zero data rather than stale previous rows. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestAggregateStaleOnZeroRows` (BUG-AGGSTALE-1a through 1d). All 1330 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server dropdown filters use display labels as keys and lose distinct values
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9187-9199, 9235-9238)
  - Description: Generated server code populates `Select.options` with labels only (`['All', ...]`) and stores a reverse map keyed by label text (`widget._dash_opts_map = {o[1]: o[0] ...}`). When two raw values share the same display label, the later entry overwrites the earlier one, so one category becomes unreachable. The same mechanism also collides with the reserved `'All'` sentinel used for “no filter”, making real labels equal to `'All'` unfilterable. Reproduced locally by generating a server app where value labels `1 -> Same` and `2 -> Same` produced `map={'All':'All','Same':'2'}`.
  - Suggested fix: Keep stable raw values as widget option values (for example `(raw, label)` tuples) and use a dedicated sentinel value such as `__all__` instead of human-readable `'All'`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Generated `Select.options` now uses `(raw_value, display_label)` tuples (Bokeh native format) so widget `.value` is the raw value directly — no opts_map indirection needed. Sentinel changed from `'All'` to `'__all__'` (dedicated non-colliding value). Callback updated to check `w.value != '__all__'` and use `w.value` directly for masking (removed `_dash_opts_map` and `raw_map.get()` lookup). 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestServerDropdownDistinctValues` (BUG-DROPDOWN-1a through 1e). All 1335 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server app crashes when `slider`/`range` filter targets a string variable
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9180-9181, 9200-9207, 9211-9217)
  - Description: The generated server builder only checks that the filter variable exists in `df`, then unconditionally computes `float(df[fvar].min())`/`float(df[fvar].max())` for `range` and `slider`. For string columns this raises `ValueError` at document build time and prevents the dashboard from loading. Local reproduction in this repo with `filter("group, type(slider)")` on a string column raised `ValueError: could not convert string to float: 'A'`.
  - Suggested fix: Gate `slider`/`range` to numeric dtypes (skip with warning or coerce safely), and fall back to `dropdown` behavior when the target variable is non-numeric.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(df[fvar])` guard before `float(df[fvar].min()/.max())` in both `range` and `slider` branches of the generated filter-widget construction loop. Non-numeric columns now emit a `UserWarning` and `continue` (skip the widget) rather than raising `ValueError`. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestServerSliderStringFallback` (BUG-SLIDER-STR-1a through 1d). All 1339 tests pass.

- [x] Title: KPI filter callback allows style-attribute injection through unescaped colors
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4180-4184, 6813-6823)
  - Description: In standalone filter callbacks, KPI `color` and `bgcolor` values are copied into `kpi_config_json` without escaping and then concatenated directly into inline-style HTML (`'color:' + k.color`, `'background:' + k.bgcolor`). A crafted color string containing quotes can break out of the `style` attribute and inject event handlers/markup when the callback rewrites `kpi_div.text`. This is a DOM XSS vector independent of the already-documented unescaped title/subtitle path.
  - Suggested fix: Strictly validate KPI color fields to safe CSS color formats (hex/rgb/named whitelist) and/or HTML-escape before callback HTML assembly; safest small fix is to sanitize both fields when building `_kpi_config_for_cb`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `_sanitize_css_color(val, default)` module-level function backed by `_CSS_COLOR_RE` regex that accepts only hex (#RGB/#RRGGBB/#RRGGBBAA), rgb/rgba(), hsl/hsla(), and letter-only named CSS colors. Any other value (containing quotes, semicolons, unknown syntax) falls back to the supplied safe default. Applied to both `color` and `bgcolor` when building `_kpi_config_for_cb` in `_build_filter_widgets`. 7 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py` class `TestKpiColorCssInjectionHardening` (BUG-CSS-KPI-1a through 1g). All 1346 tests pass.

- [x] Title: `if_expr` filtering allows arbitrary code execution through `pandas.DataFrame.query()`
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4968-4979, 8006-8009)
  - Description: Both standalone filtering (`_apply_graph_filter`) and generated server filtering validate `if_expr` with a token blacklist, then execute `df.query(..., engine="python")`. The blacklist does not block `@` variable references plus attribute/function access, so crafted expressions execute arbitrary Python. Confirmed locally with `@pd.io.common.os.system('echo APPLY_POC > /tmp/dash_apply_filter_poc.txt') == 0`, which created the file before the filter was skipped. The same payload also executed when passed through generated server `make_document(...)`. This is a security bug (code execution from expression input), not desired behavior.
  - Suggested fix: Stop using `engine="python"` on user-provided expressions. Smallest safe patch: reject `@` references and callable/attribute patterns (for example identifier-dot chains and identifier followed by `(`), then evaluate only a strict comparison/boolean subset (preferably via an AST whitelist) instead of `query`'s Python evaluator.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced the regex blacklist in `_apply_graph_filter` with a strict AST whitelist validator `_validate_if_expr(expr, columns)`. Accepted node types: comparisons, boolean combinators (`BoolOp`/`BinOp` with `BitAnd`/`BitOr`), unary ops, `Name` (column refs only), `Constant`, `Tuple`/`List`. All other nodes — `Call`, `Attribute`, `Subscript`, `Lambda`, comprehensions — are rejected. `@` is also hard-rejected before parsing. The same AST guard (`_safe_g`) was embedded in the generated server code template, replacing the old `_UNSAFE_G` regex. 8 regression tests added in `test/test_phase5.py::TestIfExprRceHardening` (BUG-RCE-1a through 1h). All 1354 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Standalone filter callbacks corrupt unlinked graphs that do not share identical source schemas
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4110-4169, 6815-6833, 6874-6878, 7218-7235)
  - Description: `_build_filter_widgets` serializes `orig_data_json` from a single `fsource` (first source), while `_FILTER_JS` applies one `new_data` object from that snapshot to every source in `all_graph_sources`. For unlinked graphs with different columns, this drops required columns from non-primary graphs. Repro: with graphs `(x,y1)` and `(x,y2)` plus one range filter, callback args had `orig_keys=['x','y1']`; assigning `new_data` to graph2 removes `y2`. The same design also breaks unlinked `by()` facets: facet sources are split in phase 2b, but `orig_data_json` still comes from one facet source, so filters repaint all facets from a single facet's row universe.
  - Suggested fix: Pass per-source original snapshots (for example `orig_data_by_source_json`) and rebuild filtered data per source using that source's own columns and base rows. Do not broadcast one schema/data payload to all sources.
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) Added `all_graph_sources_orig_json` arg (list of JSON strings indexed parallel to `all_graph_sources`) to all three `FilterBuilder` builder methods' default callback args dicts. (2) In `_build_filter_widgets`, after populating `_graph_srcs_for_cb`, loop over each source and serialize its `.data` snapshot via `json.dumps` into `_per_src_orig_json`, then assign to `_cb.args["all_graph_sources_orig_json"]`. (3) Replaced the single-schema broadcast loop in `_FILTER_JS` (lines 4235-4261) with a per-source loop that parses `all_graph_sources_orig_json[_si]` into `_orig_i`, builds `_nd_i` from `_orig_i`'s columns using the shared `active[]` mask, and assigns only `_nd_i` to that source — preserving each source's unique column set. The primary `new_data` (from `orig`) is retained for KPI updates and the primary `source` fallback path. (4) Updated the pre-existing `TestFilterUpdatesAllSources::test_filter_js_contains_all_sources_loop` test to verify `_srcs[_si].data` and `_srcs[_si].change.emit()` (not `.data = new_data` which no longer appears in the loop). 6 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestFilterPerSourceOrigData` (BUG-29-1a through 1f). All 1360 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Generated server rate limiter keeps unbounded per-IP state and grows memory indefinitely
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7499-7514)
  - Description: Generated `_RateLimiter` stores timestamps in `_store[ip]` but never deletes dictionary keys. Cleanup only pops old timestamps for the current key, so unique IP churn accumulates permanent key entries. Local repro against generated app code: after 10,000 unique IP checks, `_store` size was 10,000; after forcing timestamps stale and invoking `is_allowed` again, key count remained 10,000. This is a resource-exhaustion risk under bot/high-cardinality traffic.
  - Suggested fix: After pruning timestamps, delete `ip` from `_store` when its list becomes empty. Optionally switch to `collections.deque` for O(1) pops and enforce a global cap/LRU eviction on tracked IP keys.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced `defaultdict(list)` with a plain `{}` dict. In `is_allowed`, changed access from `self._store[ip]` (auto-creates entry) to `self._store.get(ip)` with explicit `None`-handling; after pruning, if the list is empty the key is immediately deleted from `_store`. Added `_MAX_IPS = 10000` class attribute: when a new IP arrives and `len(self._store) >= _MAX_IPS`, the oldest key (first in insertion-order dict) is evicted before the new entry is added. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestRateLimiterBoundedMemory` (BUG-30-1a through 1e). All 1365 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server-mode function plots silently drop valid constant expressions
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9013-9014, 9018-9019)
  - Description: In generated server code, function output is converted with `np.asarray(_y, dtype=float).tolist()` and written directly to `ColumnDataSource` as `y_fn`. For constant expressions like `pi` or `3`, `np.asarray(...).tolist()` returns a scalar float, not a vector matching `x_fn`, so `ColumnDataSource(...)` raises and the broad `except Exception: pass` silently drops the function line. Reproduced locally via `test/test_server.py` helpers: `_exec_function_app(..., "pi")` builds a document with zero glyph renderers, while `_exec_function_app(..., "x")` builds one as expected. This is a core functionality bug because constant expressions are valid and already supported in standalone mode.
  - Suggested fix: Mirror standalone `map_function` behavior in generated server code by broadcasting scalar results to `len(_x)` before creating `ColumnDataSource` (for example, if `np.asarray(_y).ndim == 0`, replace with `np.full(len(_x), float(_y))`).
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added two lines after `_y = eval(...)` in the generated function handler (dash_render.py ~9169): `_y_arr = np.asarray(_y, dtype=float)` and `if _y_arr.ndim == 0: _y_arr = np.full(len(_x), float(_y_arr))`, then use `_y_arr.tolist()` in the `ColumnDataSource` — mirroring the standalone `map_function` path which already had this broadcast. Also added `_count_glyph_renderers(doc)` helper in the test file to traverse Bokeh's `GridPlot`/layout tree and count renderers correctly. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerFunctionConstantExpressions` (BUG-31-1a through 1e). All 1370 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server filter callback clears synthetic columns and corrupts source schemas
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (8236-8240, 9431-9436)
  - Description: The generated server callback rebuilds each `ColumnDataSource` by copying only columns that exist in `_gdf`; every other existing source column is forced to `[]`. This breaks any glyph that depends on derived columns (for example `_x_jit`/`_y_jit` from scatter `jitter()`), and it also clears Bokeh’s implicit `index` column while leaving `x`/`y` populated. Local reproduction with a jittered scatter plus dropdown filter produced `BokehUserWarning: ColumnDataSource’s columns must be of the same length` and left `_x_jit`/`_y_jit` empty after filter changes, so points disappear.
  - Suggested fix: In `_make_filter_cb`, preserve per-source schema instead of blanking non-DataFrame columns. At minimum, repopulate `index` from `_gdf.index.tolist()` and recompute/update derived columns (like jitter fields) from filtered base columns before assigning `src.data`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `_orig_src_data` dict in the generated `make_document` (before `_make_filter_cb`) that snapshots every raw source’s `.data` at document build time (before any filter fires). Updated `_make_filter_cb` to accept `_orig_src_data` and use it in the raw source update loop: columns in `_gdf.columns` still use `_gdf[col].tolist()`; the `index` column uses `_gdf.index.tolist()`; all other columns (e.g. `_x_jit`) select entries from the snapshot using `[_snap[col][i] for i in _src_idx if 0 <= i < n_orig]`, preserving their correct row subset after every filter change. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerFilterPreservesDerivedColumns` (BUG-32-1a through 1e). All 1375 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server filter interactions stop enforcing graph-level `if_expr`/`in_expr`
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7992-8009, 9388-9429)
  - Description: Initial server rendering correctly applies per-graph qualifiers into `g_df`, but filter callbacks recompute source data from global `_df_ref` masks only and never reapply each graph’s `if_expr`/`in_expr`. Reproduction: with `if_expr: "foreign == 1"` the initial graph source had 10 rows; after one slider change callback it expanded to 16 rows, proving qualified-out rows were reintroduced. This is core correctness breakage for qualified graphs.
  - Suggested fix: Persist graph-level qualifier metadata and reapply it inside `_make_filter_cb` when deriving `_gdf` for each graph (before updating raw and aggregate sources).
  - Resolution: Fixed. (1) After the figure-building loop in generated `make_document`, added `_graph_qualifiers = {}` that captures each graph’s `if_expr`/`in_expr` from `graph_options` (or top-level graph key). (2) After the facet loop, added `_facet_to_orig = {}` reverse map from faceted gnames to original gnames. (3) Updated `_make_filter_cb` to accept both new parameters and added an inline `_safe_g_cb` AST whitelist validator. After computing `_gdf` for each source, the callback now reapplies the graph’s qualifier using `_gdf.query(if_expr)` (guarded by `_safe_g_cb`), and `in_expr` slice if present. This ensures qualified-out rows are never reintroduced by filter interactions. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerFilterReappliesGraphQualifiers` (BUG-33-1a through 1e). All 1380 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Standalone linked graphs ignore graph-level qualifiers
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7152-7191, 7239-7262)
  - Description: In `render()`, linked graphs are all bound to one shared `ColumnDataSource` built from full `self.df`, while `_apply_graph_filter()` is only used in the unlinked path. Reproduction: with `link("g1 g2")` and `g1.if_expr = "foreign == 1"`, both `g1` and `g2` shared the same full-length source (`len == 20`), so `g1`’s qualifier was ignored. This is core functionality breakage for dashboards that combine `link()` with graph-level `if`/`in` constraints.
  - Suggested fix: For linked graphs, keep the shared source for brushing but apply per-graph masks via `CDSView` (or equivalent per-graph filtered views) derived from each graph’s `if_expr`/`in_expr`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In phase 2 of `render()`, added `_linked_graph_views` dict. For each linked graph, `_apply_graph_filter(g)` is called; if it returns fewer rows than `self.df`, a boolean mask is built over the shared source’s row set and stored as `CDSView(filter=BooleanFilter(...))` in `_linked_graph_views[name]`. In phase 3 figure building, the view is passed as `facet_view` to `_make_figure()` — the shared source (for brush selection) remains unmodified while each graph only renders its qualified rows. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestLinkedGraphQualifierView` (BUG-34-1a through 1e). All 1385 tests pass.

- [x] Title: KPI min/max filter callback can fail on large datasets due spread-argument overflow
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4211-4213)
  - Description: `_FILTER_JS` computes KPI min/max using `Math.min(...vals)` and `Math.max(...vals)`. For large filtered arrays this can throw `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` in JavaScript engines, which aborts callback execution and leaves filters/KPIs stale. Reproduced with Node using an array of 300,000 values, which raised `RangeError` on `Math.min(...vals)`.
  - Suggested fix: Replace spread-based min/max with iterative reduction (loop or `reduce`) to avoid argument explosion regardless of row count.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced `Math.min(...vals)` and `Math.max(...vals)` in `_FILTER_JS` (dash_render.py ~4309-4314) with `vals.reduce((a, b) => a < b ? a : b, vals[0])` and `vals.reduce((a, b) => a > b ? a : b, vals[0])` respectively. These iterative reductions process arrays element-by-element and cannot cause stack overflow regardless of array size, unlike the spread-argument form which explodes call stack on large inputs. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestKpiMinMaxSpreadOverflow` (BUG-35-1a through 1d). All 374 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server `by()` faceting ignores graph-level `if_expr`/`in_expr` during initial render
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9084-9087, 9099-9100, 9233-9243)
  - Description: In generated server apps, the faceting phase rebuilds panels from `work_df = df.copy()` and groups that global frame directly, instead of starting from the graph-qualified subset. This drops graph-level qualifiers during initial facet construction. Reproduction: a server spec with `if_expr: "foreign == 1"` and `by: "foreign"` rendered two panels (`"G1 — 1"` and `"G1 — 0"`) when only the `foreign == 1` panel should exist. This is a core functionality bug because valid graph qualifiers are silently ignored in faceted server output.
  - Suggested fix: Before faceting each graph, apply that graph’s `if_expr`/`in_expr` to produce a per-graph base DataFrame, then run `by()` grouping and total-panel construction from that filtered frame (not global `df`).
  - Resolution: Fixed in two parts: (1) Promoted the `_safe_g` AST whitelist validator and its imports (`import ast as _ast_g`, `_ALLOWED_G`, `_SAFE_NAMES_G`) to `make_document` scope (before the rendering loop) so it is always available without re-definition per graph. (2) In the faceting loop (before `work_df = df.copy()`), added qualifier application: compute `_by_q_df = df.copy()`, apply `in_expr` slice if present, apply `if_expr` via `_safe_g` + `query()` if present, then set `work_df = _by_q_df` so `groupby()` groups only the qualifier-filtered row subset. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerByFacetingRespectsQualifiers` (BUG-36-1a through 1e). All 379 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Dropdown filter sentinel `__all__` collides with legitimate data values
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (4130-4133, 4427-4433, 9330-9337, 9395-9396)
  - Description: Both standalone and generated server dropdown filters hard-code `"__all__"` as the no-filter sentinel and also use raw data values as option values. When real data contains the literal value `"__all__"`, options contain duplicate values (for example `[('__all__', 'All'), ('A', 'A'), ('__all__', '__all__')]`), and callback logic treats any `"__all__"` selection as "no filter". The real category `"__all__"` becomes unselectable, so filtering is incorrect for valid datasets.
  - Suggested fix: Compute a per-widget sentinel that is guaranteed absent from observed raw values (for example, generate `"__all__"`, then add suffixes until unique) and compare callbacks against that generated sentinel instead of a fixed literal.
  - Resolution: Fixed in three places: (1) `FilterBuilder.build_dropdown` (dash_render.py ~4528-4535): computes `_sentinel = "__all__"` and appends `"_"` until the sentinel is absent from `raw_values`; stores as `widget._dash_sentinel`. (2) `_build_filter_widgets` `_filter_specs` builder (dash_render.py ~6990): adds `"sentinel": getattr(_w, "_dash_sentinel", "__all__")` to each spec so JS can check the per-widget sentinel. (3) `_FILTER_JS` dropdown check (dash_render.py ~4216): changed `if (val === '__all__')` to read `spec.sentinel || '__all__'`. For generated server code: (4) dropdown widget construction (dash_render.py ~9567): computes `_uniq_strs`, loops `while _sent in _uniq_strs: _sent += '_'`, stores `widget._dash_sentinel = _sent`. (5) filter callback (dash_render.py ~9645): reads `getattr(w, '_dash_sentinel', '__all__')` into `_w_sentinel` before comparison. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestDropdownSentinelCollision` (BUG-37-1a through 1e). All 384 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Generated server `by(total)` panel drops non-scatter/line/hist plot types
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9251-9266)
  - Description: The generated total-panel renderer only handles `scatter`, `line`, and `histogram/hist` branches. Other supported plot types are not rendered in the `Total` panel, even though they render correctly in per-group facets. Reproduction: with a `bar` plot and `by("foreign, total")`, grouped panels each had one renderer, but the `Total` panel had zero renderers. This is a core functionality break for `by(total)` dashboards.
  - Suggested fix: Reuse the same plot-dispatch logic used for facet panels when building the `Total` panel, so all supported plot types render consistently in grouped and total views.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced the 3-branch dispatch in the Total panel with the full dispatch matching the per-group facet panels (dash_render.py ~9479-9553): added `bar`, `area`, `lfit`, `qfit`, `kdensity`, `lowess`, `lpoly/lpolyci`, `fpfit/fpfitci`, `connected`, `dot`, `spike`, `dropline`, and `mband` branches. Aggregate types use `work_df` instead of `_fac_gdf`. Also registers aggregate sources in `agg_sources` for proper filter-callback recomputation. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerByTotalFullDispatch` (BUG-38-1a through 1e). All 389 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Stata build/serve omit qualifier and by() variables from serialized data
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (655-667), dash.ado (1273-1293, 1486-1505), dash_render.py (4955-4980, 5124-5128)
  - Description: `_dash_extract_vars` only collects plot-variable keys (`yvar`, `xvar`, `var`, etc.) and never collects variables referenced only in graph qualifiers (`if_expr`) or `by()` faceting. Both `_dash_build` and `_dash_serve` rely on that list when constructing `all_vars` for `jsonio out`, so qualifier/facet-only variables can be missing from the exported dataset. When a missing variable is referenced in `if_expr`, `_apply_graph_filter` calls `df.query(...)` and raises `pandas.errors.UndefinedVariableError` (not caught by the current exception list), which aborts rendering. When a missing variable is used in `by()`, `_expand_by_groups` falls back to no faceting (`valid_vars` empty). Local repro (current tree): a spec with `if_expr: "foreign == 1"` and data containing only `mpg`/`weight` fails with `UndefinedVariableError: name 'foreign' is not defined`.
  - Suggested fix: Keep the current extractor for plot vars, but add a second pass in `_dash_build` and `_dash_serve` to append `by()` variable names and identifiers referenced in graph-level `if_expr` before `jsonio out`. Also harden `_apply_graph_filter` to catch `NameError`/`pandas.errors.UndefinedVariableError` and downgrade to warning+skip instead of crashing.
  - Resolution: Fixed in two layers. (1) Stata-side: Added `_dash_extract_qual_vars(json_str, out_macro)` to `dash_parse.mata` (after `_dash_extract_vars`). This new function scans the graph JSON for `"by"` values (tokenising the varlist before the first comma to avoid option keywords like `total`/`missing`) and `"if_expr"` values (replacing operator punctuation with spaces then keeping only `^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$` tokens, filtered against a Python reserved-word list). Both `_dash_build` and `_dash_serve` in `dash.ado` now call `_dash_extract_qual_vars` after `_dash_extract_vars` inside the graph loop, then use `capture confirm variable` to verify each returned token is a real dataset variable before adding it to `all_vars` — this discards expression string literals (e.g. `AMC` from `make == "AMC"`) that look like identifiers but are not variables. (2) Python-side: Added `NameError` to the exception tuple in `_apply_graph_filter` (dash_render.py ~5099), covering `pandas.errors.UndefinedVariableError` which inherits from `NameError`. Any query that cannot execute because a column is absent now emits a `UserWarning` and returns the unfiltered DataFrame instead of crashing. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestQualifierMissingColumnGraceful` (BUG-39-1a through 1e). All 1409 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Windows browser auto-open command uses `start` with incorrect quoting
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (999-1005, 1449-1451)
  - Description: The Windows open path uses `shell start "<path>"`. In `cmd.exe`, `start` treats the first quoted token as the window title, not the target path, so the file-open step can no-op or open an empty shell window instead of opening the generated dashboard. This affects both immediate-render auto-open and standalone `dash build` post-save open behavior on Windows.
  - Suggested fix: Use `shell start "" "<path>"` in both Windows branches (or `shell explorer "<path>"`) so quoted paths are interpreted as the target file, not the title.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In `_dash_render_immediate` (dash.ado ~line 999-1005), replaced the `local open_cmd "shell start"` pattern with direct per-OS open commands so the Windows branch issues `shell start "" "`saving_path'"` (empty title token before the path). Similarly updated the `dash build` Windows open at line ~1461 from `shell start "`saving'"` to `shell start "" "`saving'"`. Both sites now use the correct two-argument `start "" "path"` form. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestWindowsBrowserStartQuoting` (BUG-40-1a through 1d). All 1413 tests pass. **Tests replaced 24sep2026 (WP-24):** `TestWindowsBrowserStartQuoting` no longer exists: it read dash.ado's text, and passed with the Windows branch unable to run and with the path handed to `start` unquoted.  Both callers now open the page through one program, `_dash_open_page`, which takes the platform as an argument and can return its command without running it; test/test_serve_browser.cscript SB-14 and SB-15 hold the whole command for each platform, ordinary and hostile paths (`start "" "<path>"` on Windows), and SB-19 has stub openers receive the path whole, once.

- [x] Title: Server rate limiter collapses proxied users into a single IP bucket
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7521-7522, 7886-7894)
  - Description: Generated server code derives the limiter key from `request.remote_ip` first in `make_document()` and exclusively in `_HealthHandler.prepare()`. In reverse-proxy deployments, `remote_ip` is commonly the proxy address, so distinct end-user clients are merged into one rate-limit bucket. A minimal reproduction of the current expression with `remote_ip='10.0.0.1'` and `X-Forwarded-For='203.0.113.55, 10.0.0.1'` yields `10.0.0.1`, proving forwarded client IPs are ignored when `remote_ip` is set. This is a core availability/security bug because one noisy client can throttle unrelated users sharing the proxy path, and health checks can be rate-limited under the same collapsed key.
  - Suggested fix: Add a shared `_client_ip_from_request(...)` helper in generated source that prefers trusted forwarded headers (`X-Forwarded-For` first element when present) before `remote_ip`, then `'0.0.0.0'`. Use that helper consistently in both `_HealthHandler.prepare()` and `make_document()` so per-client throttling semantics are preserved behind proxies.
  - Resolution: Fixed, then **narrowed**, and this entry is the record of both. The first fix added `_client_ip_from_request(req)` to the generated server source and had it prefer the FIRST token of `X-Forwarded-For` over `remote_ip`, unconditionally. That closed the proxy-bucket collapse and opened a worse hole: `X-Forwarded-For` is set by the client, so any caller could spoof a fresh key on every request and never be limited at all, and the first token is the one furthest from the server and so the most attacker-controlled. Commit `7cff67f` therefore made the header **opt-in**: `_TRUST_FORWARDED_FOR` is `False` by default and the helper returns the peer address, which is the only value the server itself observes. Passing `--trust-forwarded-for` to the generated app turns it on, and then the LAST token is used (the one the nearest proxy appended), validated as an IP address with `ipaddress.ip_address`, and key-spaced under an `xff:` prefix so a forwarded key can never collide with a peer key. So a deployment behind a proxy gets per-client buckets by asking for them, and a deployment without one is not spoofable. `test/test_server.py::TestProxiedRateLimiterBucket` asserts the default, the opt-in, the last-token choice, the IP validation and the key space. One limit remains open and is recorded in `.ultraplan/CHANGELOG.md`: the limiter's `_MAX_IPS` eviction means a single machine that cycles 10,000 fresh forwarded keys can flush its own bucket, measured at 300 sessions inside one 60-second window against a nominal 60. **Closed by WP-19, 23sep2026.** Remeasured first with the same attack against the generated app's own `_RateLimiter` and a fake clock: in default mode 60 granted; with the header trusted, 10,120 sessions in 60 s, and the real client's key flushed so that it got 120 of 120 in one window. The eviction is gone. A key the limiter is not tracking is charged to one shared new-client window (60 a minute, `--new-clients-per-minute`), every key is held to its own 60 (`--sessions-per-client`), all sessions together to 600 (`--sessions-per-minute`), a live key is never evicted, and stale keys are swept; a refusal is logged once a minute per bound, naming the flag. With the header trusted the same attack now gets 59 sessions (the real client's first took one new-client slot) and the real client 60 of 120, not 120; with the default peer-address key the attack gets none and the real client 60, as before. Certified by test/test_server_rate_limit.py (the forwarded-key cycle in both modes, key reuse held to the total, the bounded and swept store, no live eviction, separate health budget, the refusal log, the three flags) and test/test_server.py `TestRateLimiterBoundedMemory', restated for the new contract without weakening a bound. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the limit this entry left open, the `_MAX_IPS' eviction, was closed by 72d94e5 (WP-19), which replaced the eviction with three bounds that never evict a live key: test/test_server_rate_limit.py::TestTheForwardedKeyCycleIsHeldToTheNominalRate.

- [x] Title: `by()` silently falls back to ungrouped data when all facet values are missing
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (5146-5147, 9084-9088)
  - Description: The standalone grouping path drops missing facet keys (`work_df = work_df.dropna(...)`) and then returns `[("", df)]` when `work_df` is empty, which restores the original ungrouped dataset instead of preserving the empty grouped result. Local reproduction in this repo with `by("grp")` and all `grp = None` rows returns one group with all rows (`[(‘’, 2)]`) rather than no non-missing facets. The generated server path has the same behavior pattern: after dropping missing facet keys, `if work_df.empty: continue` skips faceting entirely, leaving the original unfaceted graph active. This is a core correctness bug because `by()` without `missing` should not render all rows when every facet key is missing.
  - Suggested fix: Preserve empty grouped semantics instead of falling back to full data. In `_expand_by_groups`, return an empty grouped frame (for example `[("", work_df)]`) when `work_df.empty` after missing-value exclusion. In server facet generation, handle `work_df.empty` by clearing/emptying that graph’s source (or building an empty facet set) rather than `continue`-ing to the original unfaceted graph.
  - Resolution: Fixed in three places. (1) `_expand_by_groups` (dash_render.py ~5271-5272 and ~5291): changed both `return [("", df)]` paths to `return [("", work_df.copy())]` so callers receive a zero-row DataFrame group instead of the full original dataset when all facet values are missing. (2) `render()` 2b by-faceting path (dash_render.py ~7398-7407): when `len(by_groups) == 1 and by_groups[0][0] == ""` and the group DataFrame is empty (all missing), builds a fresh zero-row source instead of restoring `base_src` (which had all rows from `filtered_df`). (3) Generated server code (dash_render.py ~9335-9343): when `work_df.empty`, iterates `graph_sources.get(gname, [])` and clears each source’s column arrays to `[]` before `continue`, so the original unfaceted source ends up with zero rows rather than showing all data. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestByFallbackOnAllMissingFacets` (BUG-42-1a through 1e). All 1424 tests pass.

- [x] Title: `layout()` accepts zero columns and crashes render with division by zero
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (1213-1216), dash_render.py (6452-6454, 7001-7002)
  - Description: Build-time layout validation accepts `layout("0 0")` and `layout("2 0")` because it only checks the `N M` shape (`^[0-9]+ [0-9]+$`) and not positivity. The renderer then trusts those values: `_compute_grid()` returns `cols_n = 0`, and `_assemble_layout()` computes `rows_n = math.ceil(n_items / cols_n)`, which raises `ZeroDivisionError`. Local reproduction in this repo with a minimal spec using `build_options.layout = "0 0"` raised `ZeroDivisionError: division by zero` during `DashRenderer.render()`.
  - Suggested fix: Enforce strictly positive grid dimensions at validation and render layers. Smallest safe fix is to require `[1-9][0-9]*` for both layout integers in `dash.ado`, and defensively clamp/fallback to auto layout in `_compute_grid()` when parsed rows/cols are less than 1.
  - Resolution: Fixed at both layers. (1) `dash.ado` (line ~1212): changed validation regex from `^[0-9]+ [0-9]+$` to `^[1-9][0-9]* [1-9][0-9]*$` so "0 0", "2 0", "0 3" are rejected with a clear error message at build time. (2) `_compute_grid` (dash_render.py ~6585): added `max(1, ...)` clamp on both parsed integers so renderer-side zero-dimension inputs can never produce a ZeroDivisionError regardless of what Stata passes. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestLayoutZeroColumnsCrash` (BUG-43-1a through 1e). All 1429 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Standalone `slider`/`range` filters crash on string variables
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (276-291, 4483, 4534, 6851-6858)
  - Description: Standalone filter construction sends both `type(slider)` and `type(range)` through `_extract_finite()`, which converts the full column to `dtype=float` without guarding non-numeric values. For string columns this raises `ValueError` and aborts rendering. Local reproduction in this repo with `build_options.filter = "grp, type(slider)"` (or `type(range)`) and `grp` marked as a string variable raised `ValueError: could not convert string to float: 'A'` during `DashRenderer.render()`. This is a core functionality bug because the dashboard fails to load instead of degrading gracefully.
  - Suggested fix: Mirror the generated-server guard in standalone mode: before calling `build_slider()`/`build_range()`, verify the filter variable is numeric (for example via pandas dtype check). If not numeric, skip the widget with a warning or automatically downgrade to `dropdown`.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(pd.Series(...))` guards in `_build_filter_widgets` (dash_render.py ~6986-7010) for both `slider` and `range` branches. When the column is non-numeric, a `UserWarning` is emitted naming the variable and explaining the `type(dropdown)` alternative, then the branch `continue`s. This mirrors the existing server-side guard. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestStandaloneSliderStringColumn` (BUG-44-1a through 1e). All 1434 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Semicolon-delimited multi-filter specs only serialize the first filter variable
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (1303-1323), dash_render.py (5061-5066)
  - Description: `_dash_build` only splits `build_options.filter` on `||` when collecting filter variables for `jsonio out`, so a single filter string written with semicolon separators (for example `foreign, type(dropdown); rep78, type(range)`) contributes only the first variable to `all_vars`. The renderer explicitly supports semicolon separators (`DashRenderer._parse_filter_raw()` normalizes `;` to `||`), so this is not desired behavior. Repro evidence: the current extraction logic yields `['foreign']` for `foreign, type(dropdown); weight, type(range)`, while `_parse_filter_raw()` returns two filter specs for the same string; with data missing the second filter variable, render builds only one widget (`filters_spec_count=2`, `widgets_built=1`).
  - Suggested fix: In `_dash_build`, normalize semicolons to `||` before the filter-variable extraction loop (or split on both delimiters) so every declared filter variable is added to `all_vars` and serialized.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Added `local _filt_work = subinstr(...)` to replace every `;` with `||` in `_filt_work` before the extraction while-loop in `_dash_build` (dash.ado ~line 1318). This mirrors Python's `_parse_filter_raw` which already normalizes `;` → `||`. 4 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestSemicolonFilterSerialization` (BUG-45-1a through 1d). All 1438 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Server `range`/`slider` widgets are initialized with NaN bounds on all-missing numeric columns
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (9351-9357, 9371-9377)
  - Description: Generated server code guards only for numeric dtype, then computes `vmin = float(df[fvar].min())` and `vmax = float(df[fvar].max())`. For numeric columns that are entirely missing, `min()`/`max()` are `NaN`, so widgets are created as `RangeSlider(start=nan, end=nan, value=(nan, nan))` (and similarly for `Slider`). Local repro in this repo: generated app from `filter("flt, type(range)")` with `flt = [None, None, None]` produced `WIDGETS [('RangeSlider', nan, nan, (nan, nan), False)]`. This is a core functionality bug because the filter UI is rendered in an invalid state and cannot provide meaningful filtering.
  - Suggested fix: Before constructing server `range`/`slider` widgets, drop non-finite values and require at least one finite numeric value; if none remain, skip or disable the widget with safe defaults (mirroring standalone `_extract_finite()` behavior).
  - Resolution: Fixed. In the generated server filter-widget construction loop (dash_render.py ~9725-9753), after computing `vmin`/`vmax` for both `range` and `slider` branches, added `if not (math.isfinite(vmin) and math.isfinite(vmax)):` guards that emit a `UserWarning` and `continue`. Also added `if vmin == vmax: vmax = vmin + 1.0` normalization so single-value columns don't produce zero-width sliders, replacing the inline ternary that could produce NaN step values. `math` is already imported in the generated template. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServerNaNBoundsSlider` (BUG-46-1a through 1e). All 1443 tests pass.

- [x] Title: `dash serve` uses a fixed temp app filename, causing cross-run overwrite/race risk
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (1595-1597)
  - Description: `_dash_serve` always writes the generated server app to `"`c(tmpdir)'dash_serve_app.py"`, so every invocation targets the exact same file path. This is a core functionality and safety issue when two runs overlap (for example, concurrent user/automation runs): one run can overwrite the other's app file, leading to serving the wrong dashboard or startup failures during write/read races. Reproduction evidence from this repo: computing the path twice yields the same absolute path (`same_path=True`).
  - Suggested fix: Generate a unique per-run app path instead of a constant filename. Smallest safe change: use a Stata `tempfile` local and append `.py` (or include PID/timestamp/random suffix) so each `dash serve` invocation gets an isolated app file.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Replaced `local serve_app_path "`c(tmpdir)'dash_serve_app.py"` with `tempfile _serve_app_base` / `local serve_app_path "`_serve_app_base'.py"` in `_dash_serve` (dash.ado). This mirrors the pattern already used in the cscript tests and ensures each invocation gets a unique, isolated app file that Bokeh serve can load without cross-run interference. 5 regression tests added in `test/test_server.py::TestServeUniqueAppPath` (BUG-47-1a through 1e). All 433 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the extension-less `tempfile' path this fix settled on is the path 7ddbc08's output check refuses, so every `dash serve' launch aborts at 4ede074. See the open entry "`dash serve' aborts with r(7102) on every launch: the renderer refuses the extension-less app path dash.ado hands it".

- [x] Title: Health endpoint and dashboard sessions share the same rate-limit bucket
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (7516-7523, 7898)
  - Description: Generated server code uses one global `_LIMITER` for both `/health` requests and dashboard session creation (`make_document`). Because both call `_LIMITER.is_allowed(...)`, normal user traffic can consume the same per-IP budget used by health checks, causing `/health` to return 429 even when the service is healthy. This can produce false-negative liveness/readiness signals and unnecessary restarts. Reproduction evidence: in generated app code, 60 allowed requests for one IP make the 61st call to `_LIMITER.is_allowed(ip)` return `False`.
  - Suggested fix: Separate limiter budgets by endpoint (for example `_HEALTH_LIMITER` and `_SESSION_LIMITER`) or exempt `/health` from the shared limiter while keeping stricter limits on session creation paths.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Introduced `_SESSION_LIMITER = _RateLimiter(_RATE_LIMIT_RPM)` (60 RPM) and `_HEALTH_LIMITER = _RateLimiter(_HEALTH_LIMIT_RPM)` (600 RPM) as independent buckets in the generated server template (dash_render.py). `_HealthHandler.prepare()` now calls `_HEALTH_LIMITER.is_allowed()`; `make_document()` calls `_SESSION_LIMITER.is_allowed()`. The `_LIMITER` name is kept as a backwards-compatible alias for `_SESSION_LIMITER`. Existing `TestRateLimiterEnforcement` tests updated to replace the correct limiter per endpoint. 5 new regression tests added in `TestSeparateRateLimitBuckets` (BUG-48-1a through 1e) confirming independent bucket behavior. All 438 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** both classes named above are in test/test_server.py (test/test_server.py::TestRateLimiterEnforcement, test/test_server.py::TestSeparateRateLimitBuckets); WP-19 later replaced the limiter, and test/test_server_rate_limit.py holds its bounds.

- [x] Title: `timeout()` unit mismatch: help documents seconds, implementation enforces minutes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.sthlp (216-217), dash.ado (1476-1478), dash_render.py (9763-9767)
  - Description: The help file states `timeout(#)` stops the server after `#` seconds, but runtime logic treats timeout as minutes (`sleep(args.timeout * 60)`), and validation/error text in `dash.ado` also says minutes. This is a core functionality bug because user-configured stop times are off by 60x versus documented behavior.
  - Suggested fix: Align all surfaces to one unit. Smallest-change fix is to keep minutes in code and update `dash.sthlp` to say minutes; alternatively, switch runtime to seconds and update validation/help text consistently.
  - Resolution: Fixed.
