Updated `dash.sthlp` line 216 to say "minutes" instead of "seconds" to match the implementation (`sleep(args.timeout * 60)` in dash_render.py, and the error message in dash.ado). All three surfaces (help file, .ado error message, argparse help, runtime) now consistently document and enforce minutes. 5 regression tests added in `TestTimeoutUnitConsistency` (BUG-49-1a through 1e) confirming unit consistency across all surfaces. All 443 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the class named above is test/test_server.py::TestTimeoutUnitConsistency.

- [x] Title: `dash_setup` can abort before dependency checks when `packaging` is not installed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (81-85, 100-106)
  - Description: The version-gate code imports `Version` from `packaging` inside the per-package loop (lines 81-85), but `packaging` is only conditionally installed afterwards (lines 100-106). On environments where `packaging` is absent, the first version check raises `ModuleNotFoundError` and exits `dash_setup` early, so dependency setup does not complete. This is a core functionality bug rather than intended behavior because the file explicitly treats `packaging` as a dependency that may need installation, yet uses it before ensuring it exists.
  - Suggested fix: Ensure `packaging` is installed before the loop that performs version checks (move/install guard to run before line 46), or wrap the version-check import in `capture` logic and skip version comparison when `packaging` is unavailable.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Moved the `packaging` install guard to run BEFORE the `foreach pkg` loop (new section 2 in dash_setup.ado). The guard sets a `pkg_has_packaging` flag; the version-comparison block inside the loop is now also gated on this flag as defence-in-depth. If `packaging` cannot be installed, version comparisons are gracefully skipped rather than aborting. The old post-loop packaging install block is removed. 5 regression tests added in `TestDashSetupPackagingOrder` (BUG-50-1a through 1e) verifying static code ordering. All 448 tests pass. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the class named above is test/test_server.py::TestDashSetupPackagingOrder. **Tests replaced 24sep2026 (WP-24):** `TestDashSetupPackagingOrder` no longer exists: it read dash_setup.ado's text, and all five of its tests passed with an uncaptured `import packaging.version' put back before the guard.  dash_setup now runs for real in a sandbox (test/setup_sandbox.py): test/test_setup_honesty.cscript HON-26 fails on that import, HON-27 on the guard moved after the loop, and test/test_program_coverage.cscript PC-18 on the import too, now that the sandbox blocks a package's cached submodules with it.

- [x] Title: Scheme path traversal guard is bypassable via symlinked `.scheme` files
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (762-778, 798, 805)
  - Description: `SchemeParser._resolve_path()` validates only the candidate path string (`abspath`) against allowed directories, but it does not validate the resolved target (`realpath`). A symlink placed in the current working directory (for example `tmp_symlink_test.scheme -> /etc/hosts`) passes the allowed-directory check and is returned; `_parse_file()` then opens the symlink path and reads the external file target. Reproduced locally in this repo with a Python snippet: `_resolve_path('tmp_symlink_test.scheme')` returned a CWD path while `realpath(...)` resolved to `/private/etc/hosts`. This is a security bug because it bypasses the intended directory boundary enforcement.
  - Suggested fix: Resolve each candidate with `os.path.realpath()` before the allowed-directory check, and enforce that resolved paths stay under allowed roots. Keep using `realpath` for cycle detection and open only the validated resolved path.
  - Resolution: Fixed. In `SchemeParser._resolve_path()` (dash_render.py), after the existing `abspath` boundary check, added a second check using `os.path.realpath(candidate)` against `realpath`-resolved allowed dirs. Symlinks whose targets resolve outside CWD or the schemes dir now raise `ValueError("...via symlink outside allowed directories...")`. Symlinks whose targets remain within an allowed dir are still accepted (intra-CWD symlinks work normally). 5 regression tests added in `test_scheme.py::TestSecurity` (BUG-51-1a through 1e) covering external-target rejection, same-CWD symlink acceptance, and source-code verification of the realpath check. All 1468 tests pass.

- [x] Title: Generated Bokeh Server app executes arbitrary Python from a function() expression
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (emitted `_val_node' Call branch; compare the standalone validator's `if node.keywords' guard)
  - Description: The generated app carries its own copy of the function() expression validator. Its Call branch walked `node.args' and never `node.keywords'. A keyword argument's VALUE is an arbitrary expression that Python evaluates before the call, so `function sin(x, out=<expression reaching open() through object.__subclasses__()>)' wrote a file when the document was built. An empty `__builtins__' does not prevent the escape. The standalone validator has always rejected keywords, so the two modes disagreed about what was safe -- and the generated .py is the artefact users are told to keep and re-run.
  - Suggested fix: Reject keyword arguments in the emitted validator as the standalone one does, and reject starred arguments with them.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The emitted Call branch now raises on `node.keywords' and on `_ast.Starred' arguments. test/test_server_function_security.py drives the escape payload through both renderers and asserts neither executes it; 2 of its 14 assertions were red before the fix.

- [x] Title: `dash serve' binds every network interface, and its only gate is a client-supplied header
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_dash_server_app', the Server(...) call and its `address' kwarg); dash.sthlp Server Mode security paragraph
  - Description: The generated launcher passed no `address', which Bokeh reads as "bind everything". Measured: the listening sockets were ('0.0.0.0', port) and ('::', port), reachable from the LAN, while the launcher's own --address help text said the default was loopback. The only access gate was `allow_websocket_origin', which filters the browser-supplied Origin header: measured with raw sockets, a client that omits Origin, or forges `localhost:<port>', completed the handshake. dash.sthlp stated that connections were restricted to the loopback interface. The help also says the generated app embeds the whole dataset.
  - Suggested fix: Default the bind address to loopback, leave --address as the deliberate way to open it, and correct the help to describe what the Origin allow-list is and is not.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_server_app' passes `address=address or '127.0.0.1''. dash.sthlp now says the app binds loopback, that the Origin allow-list stops another website driving the dashboard in the user's browser but is not an access control, that there is no password, and that anything beyond loopback needs a tunnel, proxy or firewall. test/test_security_surface.py asserts both, reading the generated app rather than the builder.

- [x] Title: A .scheme file can break out of dash's own CSS rules
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`OptionTranslator.translate_mcolor' hex passthrough; `SchemeParser._color' -> `css_block')
  - Description: `translate_mcolor' returned any 4- or 7-character string beginning with `#' as a colour, checking only the length. `SchemeParser' interpolates that value into a `<style>' block where `html.escape' leaves `{', `}' and `;' untouched, so a scheme file containing `color background #}a{b;c' closed dash's own `body' rule and opened one of its own. dash.sthlp invites the user to place a custom .scheme file in the working directory, so the file is untrusted input. The KPI colour path was hardened against exactly this; this was the colour path the hardening did not reach.
  - Suggested fix: Validate the passthrough as hexadecimal instead of by length.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The passthrough is now `re.fullmatch(r"#(?:[0-9a-f]{3}|[0-9a-f]{6})", v)'; anything else falls through to the existing ValueError. test/test_security_surface.py drives eight malformed values and an end-to-end .scheme file, and asserts the emitted stylesheet's braces stay balanced.

- [x] Title: An `if' qualifier was rewritten inside its own string literals
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_stata_if_to_pandas')
  - Description: The translator applied its rewrites to the whole expression, so `if make == "Wow!"' became `make == "Wow~"', matched no rows and drew an empty graph. The rewritten expression is still valid, so nothing warned. Measured: `make == "a~=b"' -> `make == "a!=b"', `make == "inlist(a,b)"' -> `make == "((a == b))"'. The sibling `_stata_expr_to_python' tracks quote state and its docstring says why.
  - Suggested fix: Mask quoted literals before the rewrites and restore them afterwards -- masking rather than splitting, because `inlist(make, "AMC", "BMW")' is a legitimate qualifier whose arguments are literals.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_mask_string_literals'/`_unmask_string_literals' replace each literal with a placeholder carrying no operator, comma or identifier. Both are copied into the generated app alongside `_stata_if_to_pandas'. test/test_security_surface.py covers eight literals plus controls that the rewriting outside quotes still happens.

- [x] Title: `function 9^9^9^9' never returns, and Stata's Break key cannot recover it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`map_function' eval; the same eval in the generated server app)
  - Description: Python's integer power is exact and unbounded, so the expression computed a number with billions of digits. Measured past 20 seconds with the GIL held throughout, inside Stata's embedded Python, in one uninterruptible call -- so the only way out is killing Stata. Stata itself evaluates in double precision and overflows the same expression to missing.
  - Suggested fix: Give Python the same arithmetic Stata has by making every numeric literal in the validated tree a float.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_floats_only' walks the already-validated tree and converts integer constants; `9.0 ** 9.0 ** 9.0' is `inf' immediately, so an overflowing expression draws nothing instead of hanging. The generated app does the same. test/test_resource_limits.py runs the expression in a subprocess with a 25-second timeout.

- [x] Title: A long function() expression takes the whole dashboard down with RecursionError
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_validate_expr_node', which recurses once per AST node, and the guard around it)
  - Description: The guard caught `SyntaxError' and `ValueError'. `RecursionError' is neither, so a 50,000-term expression -- about 100,000 characters, which fits inside Stata's command-line limit -- escaped it and lost every graph, KPI and filter in the dashboard, with a message naming nothing.
  - Suggested fix: Bound the expression length before parsing and widen the except clause.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_MAX_EXPR_CHARS' is 5000; over that the layer is drawn empty with a warning that says why. The except clause now also catches RecursionError and MemoryError, and the generated app carries the same bound. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_resource_limits.py::TestAFunctionExpressionAlwaysReturns (a 50,000-term expression is reported, not fatal) and test/test_resource_limits.py::TestTheServedAppHasTheSameBounds (the served validator's bound).

- [x] Title: `histogram width(1e-310)' raises OverflowError and destroys the dashboard
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`GlyphMapper._histogram_grid', `n_bins = int(math.ceil(span / width))')
  - Description: A denormal width makes `span / width' overflow to `inf', and `math.ceil(inf)' raises OverflowError. The exception is not confined to the histogram: it takes every other graph, KPI and filter in the same build with it. In server mode the generated app raised on every session, so the served page was permanently broken.
  - Suggested fix: Check the ratio is finite and within the bin cap before using it.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A width that would need more than `_MAX_BINS' bins, or that produces a non-finite ratio, is reported and the width derived from the data instead. test/test_resource_limits.py covers three denormal widths and asserts an ordinary width still sets the bin width exactly.

- [x] Title: `bar barwidth(-5)' raises inside Bokeh and destroys the dashboard
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`map_bar', the bare `float(options[key])' for barwidth/width)
  - Description: The value was parsed with a bare float() guarded only against ValueError and TypeError, then handed to Bokeh, which raised "Distances must be positive!" -- a message naming neither the option nor the plot -- and lost the whole dashboard. Stata rejects a negative barwidth with a clear r(198).
  - Suggested fix: Read it through `_stat_option_float' with a positive lower bound, as the other numeric options are read.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_MIN_BAR_WIDTH' bounds it; a value that is not a number, not finite or not positive is reported and the computed width used. test/test_resource_limits.py covers -5, -0.1, nan, inf and -inf.

- [x] Title: `mband bands()' is unbounded in one mode and clamped in the other
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`map_mband', `_stat_option_float(..., lo=1.0)' with no hi; the generated app's own `min(n_bands, 1000)')
  - Description: The standalone renderer had no upper bound, so `bands(4000000)' took 7.7 seconds on 74 observations and `bands(1000000000)' allocated 8 GB for the bin edges alone before drawing anything. The generated server app clamped the same option to 1000, so the two modes drew different numbers of bands from the same command.
  - Suggested fix: Give the standalone read the same clamp the served copy uses.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_MAX_MBANDS' is 1000 and both modes use it; over that the count is reported and clamped. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_resource_limits.py::TestMbandBandsIsBounded (an enormous count is clamped and reported) and test/test_resource_limits.py::TestTheBoundsThatAlreadyWorkedAreKept (the served app carries the same `_MAX_MBANDS').

- [x] Title: Every KPI statistic but ten returns a missing value under `dash serve'
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the generated app's hand-written `_compute_kpi'); dash.ado (`valid_stats')
  - Description: `dash kpi' accepts fifteen statistics plus any pNN. The generated app carried a third, hand-written copy that knew ten. Measured on a 74-observation fixture: var 29.178 standalone against nan served, and the same for cv, iqr and skewness, so those cards read `.' under `dash serve' and a number under `dash build'. p25 and p75 disagreed as well -- 17.551 against 17.563 -- because the copy used pandas' linear interpolation where Stata, and every other path in dash, uses its own percentile rule. `count' over a selection filtered down to nothing read `.' rather than 0.
  - Suggested fix: Emit the standalone renderer's own `_kpi_statistic' into the app and delegate to it, as the statistical helpers are already emitted.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_kpi_statistic' is copied in by `inspect.getsource' and `_compute_kpi' delegates to it, so the two modes run the same text. test/test_server_kpi_statistics.py reads dash.ado's `valid_stats' rather than restating it; 13 of its 26 assertions were red before the fix.

- [x] Title: Stata's `jitterseed()' reached none of the five places that read a jitter seed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_build_jitter_source', `map_dropline', `map_scatteri', `_resolve_seed', and the generated app's own copy)
  - Description: `twoway scatter' takes `jitter(#)' and `jitterseed(#)'. `jseed(#)' is dash's own shorthand and was the only spelling any of the five sites read, and dash.ado listed jitterseed on the ignoring-unsupported list. So a command pasted out of a do-file drew jitter from an unseeded generator -- a different cloud of points on every render of the same command -- while reporting the seed as dropped. Reproducibility is the entire purpose of the option. `map_dropline' also parsed the seed with a bare `int(float(...))', so `jseed(abc)' took the render down.
  - Suggested fix: Read both spellings through one helper, with the dash spelling winning when both are given.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_jitter_seed()' is used at all five sites and reports a non-numeric seed as absent. test/test_jitter.py gains twelve assertions, eight red before the fix, including two controls: that the jittered values do not live in the original columns, and that an unseeded jitter really is unseeded.

- [x] Title: dash reported `density()', `color()', `bands()' and `range()' as ignored while honouring them
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (`_dash_pending' blocks in `_dash_parse_plot_opts'); dash_render.py (`_histogram_grid', `map_bar'/`map_area'/`map_histogram', `map_mband', the function branch)
  - Description: The existing measurement renders each pending option at two different values and asserts the output does not change. It could not see these four. `density' NAMES the default histogram y axis, so writing it and omitting it give the same document whatever the renderer does. `bands' was measured on a kdensity, where Stata does not define it, and `range' on a kdensity too; both are live elsewhere. `color' was measured on a scatter, one of the mappers that did not read it, while bar, area, histogram, dropline and rcapsym honoured it. So `dash mband y x, bands(5)' drew five bands and said bands() had been ignored.
  - Suggested fix: Take the four off the list, make `color()' reach scatter and line as Stata's generic series colour does, and implement `range()' for kdensity, which Stata defines and dash read only for function plots.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_with_color_alias()' lets the generic name stand in for `mcolor'/`lcolor' only when the specific one is absent, leaving a bar's own blcolor() alone. kdensity reads range() and reports a malformed pair. A new guard in test_unsupported_report_is_true.py reads the y-axis family out of `_histogram_grid''s own source and asserts no member of it is on the pending list, so a flag that names a default cannot hide there again.

- [x] Title: Sixteen glyph properties differ between a saved dashboard and a served one
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (ServerAppBuilder's emitted style literals); test/test_mode_parity.py
  - Description: The parity guard collected `line_width', `line_dash' and `fill_alpha' and asserted four other properties, on Scatter glyphs only, over an eight-case corpus of nothing but scatters. Widened to every glyph and every property it collects, over line, area, bar, histogram, kdensity, mband, dropline, spike, connected, a fit and marker labels, it failed sixteen times at once: served scatters at alpha 0.7 where the scheme says 0.8, areas 0.4 against 1.0, histograms 0.8 against 1.0, bars outlined white rather than in their own fill, economist lines 1.5 against the scheme's 1.2, mband 1.5 against 2.0, and a connected plot's marker black where the mono scheme asks for #606060. Both by() facet loops held a third set of the same literals.
  - Suggested fix: Emit the scheme's own numbers and read them, and widen the guard so the next drift fails here.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The generated app carries `_SCHEME_FILL_ALPHA', `_SCHEME_BAR_ALPHA', `_SCHEME_AREA_ALPHA' and `_SCHEME_LINE_WIDTH'; mband's own 2.0 in the standalone renderer became the scheme's width too. The parity corpus is now thirteen cases across eleven glyph types and asserts every property it gathers, plus a control that the profile is not empty. 87 parity assertions pass.

- [x] Title: `pcscatter' is parsed as literal coordinates although it takes four variables
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (the varpart and JSON branches that listed pcscatter with scatteri, pci and function)
  - Description: Measured: `dash pcscatter mpg weight mpg price' wrote `"values":[[mpg,weight],[mpg,price]]' -- the variable names as bare identifiers inside a JSON array, which is not valid JSON. The renderer read nothing from it and drew a titled, axed, empty plot region with rc 0 and no warning.
  - Suggested fix: Route it with pcspike, pcarrow and pcbarrow, which is what it is.
  - Resolution: Fixed in both branches, and `_PC_TYPES' in the render loop gained it. test/test_every_plot_type_draws.py reads the supported-type list out of the message dash.ado prints and renders every entry.

- [x] Title: `pci' draws nothing: there is no renderer for its literal coordinates
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`map_pci' delegated to `map_pcspike'; the render loop routed pci nowhere)
  - Description: `twoway pci 20 3000 30 4000' names two points and draws a line between them, and dash.ado writes them correctly as [[20,3000],[30,4000]]. `map_pci' delegated to `map_pcspike', which wants variable names, and the render loop did not route pci there in any case. The result was an empty plot region with rc 0.
  - Suggested fix: Give pci a literal renderer, as scatteri has.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `map_pci_literal' draws the pairs two at a time in the (y, x) order scatteri uses, honours lcolor()/color(), and reports an unpaired last point rather than dropping it in silence. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_immediate_values.py (the segments, labels and refusals of `map_pci_literal') and test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTheUnificationKeepsD2Closed::test_pci_draws_the_same_segments (a Segment in both modes).

- [x] Title: `dropline' ignores every marker option the help says it takes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`map_dropline': size=8 and line_width=1.5 written into the call, no `_apply_marker_opts', a source without the mlabel column)
  - Description: dash.sthlp lists dropline among the plot types accepting mlabel() and msangle(). It honoured neither, nor msymbol(), msize() or malpha(), because the scatter renderer was never passed to `_apply_marker_opts' -- the only place those options are read. mlabel() failed a second way: `_apply_mlabel_opts' was given a source carrying only x, y, y0 and the two plot variables, so the label column was never in it. The two literals are the ones S11 replaced elsewhere; under economist the scheme asks for 10.18 and 1.2.
  - Suggested fix: Pass the dot through `_apply_marker_opts', add the label column to the source, and take the size and width from the scheme.
  - Resolution: Fixed, and `map_scatteri' -- which had the same gap for msangle() and malpha() -- is routed through the same helper. test/test_dropline_marker_options.py carries twelve assertions, eight red before the fix.

- [x] Title: `theme(dark)' has been advertised since the first release and never existed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_THEME_SCHEME_MAP'); dash.sthlp (Dashboard Options table)
  - Description: The map held default, clean, mono, economist and sj. `dark' was in neither the map nor any scheme file, so `dash build, theme(dark)' warned that the name was unknown and drew s2color, which is white -- from the one theme name a user picks precisely because they want a dark dashboard. The table also omitted mono, economist and sj, which work, and had no scheme() row at all although scheme() is in the syntax diagram.
  - Suggested fix: Map dark to s1rcolor, Stata's own dark scheme, which dash ships.
  - Resolution: Fixed. test/test_theme_aliases.py asserts every advertised alias resolves to a scheme that ships, that an unknown name is still reported, and that the help's list and the map agree in both directions. test_scheme_consumption.py's theme test was written against dark being unknown and now tests a name that really is.

- [x] Title: A custom scheme's base name is rejected although the help says to use one
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`SchemeParser._resolve_path': a bare name was looked for only in the package's schemes/)
  - Description: dash.sthlp tells the user to put a .scheme file in the working directory and give its base name. A bare name was resolved only against the package's own schemes/ directory, so the file was reported as an unknown scheme and only `scheme(myscheme.scheme)' found it.
  - Suggested fix: Try the working directory first for a bare name, as the file-reference branch already does.
  - Resolution: Fixed, with the same path validation applied to the new candidate. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** this fix reached the graph-level route only: `_resolve_scheme_name' still refused a bare name at build level before `_resolve_path' could run (GAPS.md F4's bare-name row), and fe3b12a (WP-17) finished it. Certified by test/test_scheme_resolution.py::TestACustomSchemeInTheWorkingDirectory (build and graph level, both modes) and test/test_custom_scheme.cscript CS-01 to CS-05.

- [x] Title: `dash_setup' never performs the Python version check its help describes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (the Python availability step); dash_setup.sthlp
  - Description: The help said step 1 verifies Python 3.8+. The code ran `capture python query', which succeeds for any linked Python including 3.6, then read `sys.version' into a macro and printed it. Nothing compared it to anything. A user on Python 3.7 was told "Python ............. OK (version 3.7.9)" and sent on to fail later inside bokeh, with a message about neither Python nor dash.
  - Suggested fix: Compare the version, numerically and per component, and report a version below the minimum.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_pymin' holds the minimum in one place and `_dash_pyver_ok' compares component by component as numbers -- so 3.10 is newer than 3.9, which is how this check is usually got wrong -- returning 1, 0 or -1 so that an unreadable version is reported as unknown rather than as too old. test/test_setup_python_gate.cscript drives thirteen cases from any interpreter and asserts dash_setup.sthlp states the same minimum the code uses.

- [x] Title: Three test fixtures are not in the repository, so 174 tests skip on a clone
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/fixtures/kpi_truth.json, test/fixtures/color_truth.json, test/fixtures/scheme_visual/truth.json; .git/info/exclude
  - Description: All three are measured against real Stata and the tests that read them skip when the file is absent. Measured, a clean `git archive HEAD' export against the working tree: 2934 passed / 116 skipped against 3056 passed / 33 skipped. So 174 tests -- precisely the Stata-ground-truth checks for KPI statistics, the colour table and rendered geometry -- never ran on a clone, and the suite still exited 0. The cause is a local `*.json' rule under "Common ASCII dataset extensions"; the other six truth fixtures are tracked because they were added with `git add -f'.
  - Suggested fix: Force-add the three, and make a missing fixture fail rather than skip.
  - Resolution: Fixed. test/test_fixtures_are_committed.py asks `git ls-files', not the filesystem, so a fixture that exists only on the author's machine fails there rather than on someone else's clone.

- [x] Title: Eight fills in comprehensive_demo.do render at a hundredth of the intended opacity
  - File Names and Line Numbers: examples/comprehensive_demo.do (fa() at eight sites)
  - Description: falpha() is a percentage, 0-100, as Stata's intensity options are. The example wrote fa(0.4), fa(0.3), fa(0.25) and fa(0.5), so fa(0.4) rendered fill_alpha 0.004 and every shaded band and area in the showcase was effectively invisible.
  - Suggested fix: Write the percentages the option takes.
  - Resolution: Fixed in all eight places (b917c33). examples/feature_tour.do is new and exercises the options no example reached -- bin(), level(), kernel(), bwidth(), legend(), xline(), note(), mlabel(), xlabel(), yaxis(), jitter(), function, a multi-variable varlist, a named scheme, tabs, a filter sidebar and the four KPI statistics the help used to omit. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** no test covered the fix then: it is data in examples/comprehensive_demo.do, and test/test_examples_run.cscript runs every example for rc 0, which fa(0.4) passes as well. **Regression test 24sep2026 (WP-24, GAPS.md L9):** test/test_bug_regressions.cscript BR-01 runs the example as shipped and holds the spec dash parses from it to falpha 40, 25, 50, 40 and 30 four times (BR-01a), and the fills of the page it saves (BR-01b) and of the document the served app builds from the spec `dash serve' would hand it (BR-01c) to fill_alpha 0.40, 0.50, 0.40 and 0.30 four times. Seven of the eight sites draw a fill; b6 puts fa(25) on an mband, which draws a line and no fill, in dash and in twoway alike (measured: twoway's SVG with fc(ltblue%25) on the mband is byte-identical to the one without), and BR-01 holds it to none. With b917c33's change to the example reversed on a copy of the tree, BR-01a finds the fractions and BR-01b and BR-01c every fill drawn at a hundredth (0.004, 0.005, 0.003) in both modes.

- [x] Title: Three dead artefacts in dash_render.py, one of them with three tests attached
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`safe_title'; `if True:' around the hover block; `_pad_grid_children'); test/test_layout.py
  - Description: `safe_title' was computed from the page title and never read. `if True:' wrapped ninety-seven lines of hover construction, which is not a condition. `_pad_grid_children' has never been called by the renderer -- the live path pads its own grid inline -- yet three tests in test_layout.py called it directly, so they certified a duplicate implementation and would have stayed green if the padding the product actually does had been deleted.
  - Suggested fix: Remove all three and point the tests at the live grid.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The three empty-cell tests now read the GridPlot the renderer built, the way test_layout_placement.py does, and one more asserts that five graphs in a 2x2 layout lose none of themselves.


- [x] Title: A parenthesis inside a quoted option value deletes every later plot in flat mode
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (`_dash_split_segs' flat mode, 468 and 473; `_first_comma_pos', 303 and 308)
  - Description: Both scanners gate their double-quote and compound-quote ENTRY branches on `& depth == 0'. Paren mode (409, 414) has no such gate. So in flat (||) mode any '(' inside a quoted option value permanently unbalances `depth', and every subsequent '||' stops being a separator. Measured: `dash scatter mpg weight, title("a ( b") name(bad) || line mpg weight' registers ONE plot, prints "dash: ignoring unsupported option(s): |() line() mpg() weight()" naming four options that do not exist, and returns rc 0. The control without the paren registers both plots and prints nothing. Real twoway accepts the identical line and draws both plots. `legend(label(1 "a ( b"))' is the same shape and is a value a user writes without thinking about it.
  - Suggested fix: Remove the `& depth == 0' clause from the quote-entry branches so flat mode matches paren mode, which is the reference implementation. Factor the state machine into one helper so a fourth copy cannot drift.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The quote state machine is now a single shared helper, `_dash_quote_step', used by all five scanners; the `& depth == 0' clause is gone from both flat-mode quote-entry branches and from `_first_comma_pos', so quoted text is opaque at any depth -- which is what paren mode, the correct reference, always did. Measured after the fix: the command registers both plots and prints no ignoring-unsupported note, and the built dashboard carries 2 GlyphRenderers, structurally identical to the control without the parenthesis. test/test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-01..P1B-12 cover an opening paren, a closing paren (a different mechanism -- the ')' closes the option's own paren and the string's closing '"' then OPENS an unterminated quote), a balanced pair, one and two nesting levels, compound quotes, and the same defect in `_first_comma_pos'.

- [x] Title: The word `filter(' inside a quoted title is harvested as a filter and cut out of the text
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (`_dash_extract_filters', 752-828; the keyword scan at 770-778 and the word-boundary test at 787)
  - Description: The function scans the lower-cased raw command for `filt|filte|filter' with only a preceding-character word-boundary test. It has no quote tracking, and it lower-cases the text first, so the match is case-insensitive as well. Measured end to end: `dash build, title("Q1 filter(foreign) report") saving(b2.html) replace nobrowser' returns rc 0 with no warning, and the rendered dashboard title is "Q1 report" -- `foreign' became a live filter widget the user never asked for and the user's title was silently edited. `note("see FILTER(abc) here")' behaves the same way. It fires on `dash build' and `dash serve' (dash.ado:2368, 2536); `_dash_plot' does not call it.
  - Suggested fix: Track double and compound quotes while scanning and skip any match that falls inside a quoted run, using the same shared helper as the other scanners.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_extract_filters' now tracks quotes with the shared helper and skips any match inside a quoted run; the inner paren matcher is quote-aware too, so a filter whose own label carries a parenthesis is still extracted whole. The scan additionally requires paren depth 0, because filter() is a top-level option -- without that, `tabs(a, filter(x))' harvested `x' and left the user holding `tabs(a, )', which was measured and is now covered. Verified end to end: the dashboard title renders as "Q1 filter(foreign) report" and the document contains zero Select widgets. test/test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-19..P1B-22e.

- [x] Title: Unbalanced and malformed plot specifications are accepted silently
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (`_dash_split_segs': no end-of-scan balance check in either mode; paren mode drops a ')' at depth 0 at 423 and stray text between groups; the top-level-comma break at 435)
  - Description: Neither mode checks `depth', `in_dq' or `in_cq' on exit, so an unclosed '(' or an unterminated '"' yields one segment and no message: `scatter y x, legend(off || line y2 x' and `scatter y x, title("oops || line y2 x' both return one segment silently. Paren mode also `break's at the first top-level comma, so anything after it is treated as graph options regardless of shape. Measured against real twoway: `(a) junk (b)' is r(198) in twoway and rc 0 in dash with `junk' discarded; `(a)) (b)' is r(198) in twoway and rc 0 in dash with the extra ')' ignored; `(a), (b)' is r(198) in twoway and rc 0 in dash with the second group swallowed into the graph-option string.
  - Suggested fix: Error on unbalanced state at end of scan, on a ')' at depth 0, on stray text between top-level groups, and on a '(' opening the text after the graph-option comma -- a graph option can never begin with '('.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Both modes now check quote and paren state at end of scan and exit 198, quote first because an unterminated quote also leaves the depth counter stuck and naming the parenthesis sends the user after the wrong character. Paren mode rejects an unmatched ')', stray text between groups (reporting the whole word) and a plot group written after the graph-option comma; flat mode rejects an unmatched ')' too, which no balance check can catch because it never leaves depth non-zero. All were measured against real twoway first -- each is r(198) there and was rc 0 here. Two legal forms the first cut of this fix wrongly rejected are now guarded by tests: `(a) || (b)' and a top-level if/in qualifier in paren mode, both measured rc 0 in twoway. test/test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-13..P1B-18h. All 12 files in examples/ still run rc 0.

- [x] Title: An unbalanced quote drops every trailing option from the ignored-options note
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (`_dash_option_names', 945-990; the quote toggle at 959)
  - Description: The scanner toggles `inq = !inq' on every '"' with no compound-quote handling, so an odd quote count leaves it stuck inside a quote and every option after that point is swallowed into one token. Measured: input `title("unbalanced quote) xlabel(1)' reports only `title'. This runs on the error-reporting path reached from dash.ado:1353, so the one place that is supposed to tell the user what dash ignored is itself lossy on exactly the malformed input most likely to reach it.
  - Suggested fix: Track compound quotes as the other scanners do, and degrade gracefully -- this function must never lose information or error, because it runs while reporting someone else's error.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The scan is factored into `_dash_option_names_scan', which reports whether it ended inside a quote; when it did, the input cannot be honestly quoted, so a second pass reads '"' as an ordinary character and recovers the option names that would otherwise be swallowed. The quote-aware pass uses the shared helper, so compound-quoted values no longer come apart. No exit() was added anywhere in this path -- it runs while dash is already reporting a problem, and an abort there would replace a diagnostic with a crash. test/test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-23..P1B-25.

- [x] Title: Stata's multi-string text form collapses to a single line
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (graph-level syntax 282 and plot-level 500: TItle/SUBtitle declared `string' where NOTE/CAPtion were `string asis'); dash_render.py (`_parse_textbox_opt', the quoted-run join); scripts/regen_option_surface.py (the ASIS set that generates those declarations)
  - Description: Stata lets a text option take SEVERAL quoted strings, each rendering as its own line. Measured: `twoway scatter mpg weight, title("line one" "line two")' stores mtextq(`""line one" "line two""') in the .gph -- two separate strings -- and draws two lines. dash drew one, for two independent reasons. title() and subtitle() were declared `string', so Stata dequoted at capture and the spec carried `line one line two'; the line structure was destroyed before the renderer ran and no renderer change alone could have recovered it. note() and caption() were already `asis' so their quoting survived, but `_parse_textbox_opt' joined the quoted runs with a SPACE, so they collapsed too. The asymmetry people noticed -- note keeping its quotes in the spec while title did not -- was the symptom, not the defect: the rendered note text was always correct, because _parse_textbox_opt consumed those quotes.
  - Suggested fix: Declare the four display-text options alike, and join the quoted runs with a newline rather than a space.
  - Resolution: Fixed. TItle and SUBtitle are now `string asis' at both declaration sites, and "title"/"subtitle" were added to the ASIS set in scripts/regen_option_surface.py so a regeneration cannot revert it. `_parse_textbox_opt' joins with char 10. A single quoted string joins to itself, so the ordinary case cannot gain a stray break. BokehJS renders the newline as a real line break in both Title and Label -- verified visually in a browser against bokeh 3.9 rather than inferred from the model accepting the character. The build-level title() (dash.ado 2011, 2374) and the KPI title() (1830) are deliberately NOT changed: they feed the HTML <title> element and the dashboard heading, where a line break has no meaning, and they are dash's own options rather than twoway's. test/test_textbox_multiline.cscript covers the Stata half and test/test_textbox_multiline.py the renderer half.

- [x] Title: A comma in the user's own title text truncates it at that comma
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_parse_textbox_opt': the bare _TEXTBOX_SUBOPT_RE.search over the whole value)
  - Description: Stata writes a textbox option as text[, suboptions], and the split was found by searching the entire value for a comma followed by a sub-option keyword. The keyword list is ordinary English -- size, color, just, width, height, style, span, box, margin, position -- so a comma inside the user's own text triggered it. Measured: `title("Sales, size matters")' rendered as `Sales'; `title("Revenue, color coded")' as `Revenue'; `title("A, just testing")' as `A'. The rest of the text was silently discarded, and twoway keeps it. Affects title, subtitle, note and caption alike.
  - Suggested fix: Only treat a comma OUTSIDE the quoted text as the separator, the same quote-awareness the Mata scanners in dash_parse.mata apply to the command line.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_textbox_split_point' walks the value tracking quote state and returns the first comma at quote depth zero that a sub-option keyword follows; an unquoted value with a genuine sub-option still splits. Covered by test/test_textbox_multiline.py class TestCommaInsideTheUsersText, including the multi-string case where a comma appears inside one of several quoted runs.

- [x] Title: `_dash_json_get_str' has never decoded a single JSON escape
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (`_dash_json_get_str', the escape branch); called from dash.ado 1497-1498
  - Description: Every comparison in the escape branch tested a one-character string against the Mata literal "\\", which is TWO characters -- measured, strlen("\\") is 2 and "\\" == char(92) is false. No comparison could ever be true, so the leading backslash of an escape fell through to the plain-character branch and the '"' that followed it was read as the closing quote. The function returned everything up to the first escape. Measured on the value this function was written for -- its own docstring names `"if_expr": "make == \"AMC Concord\""' -- it returned a single backslash of length 1. It is used by the immediate-render path to recover a per-plot if/in qualifier, so a qualifier containing a quoted string was handed to the renderer as a fragment. The whole of its previous coverage was `assert "_dash_json_get_str" in src' in test_server.py: a check that the name exists, not that it works.
  - Suggested fix: Compare against char(92), as the same function already does for char(34).
  - Resolution: Fixed, with a comment recording why the literal cannot be used. test/test_parser_integrity.cscript P1B-27..P1B-30 exercise an escaped quote, an unescaped value, an escaped backslash and a missing key -- the first behavioural tests this function has had.

- [x] Title: The caption is never drawn on any dashboard
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_TEXTBOX_DEFAULTS`, the caption's y offset in the note/caption block)
  - Description: The caption's Label was placed at screen y = -12.0, meant to push it below the note. What actually separates the two is the ORDER they are added in -- add_layout(..., "below") gives each Label a row of its own -- so the offset only pushed the caption outside the row Bokeh had sized for it, where it is clipped. Measured in a browser against bokeh 3.9 with an otherwise identical Label: it renders at y = 0.0, renders at y = 2.0, and does not render at all at y = -12.0. So `caption()' has produced nothing visible for as long as this offset has been there. A model-level test cannot see it -- the Label exists in the document with the right text -- which is why it survived.
  - Suggested fix: Put the caption at the top of its own row, as the note already is.
  - Resolution: Fixed by giving the caption the note's y = 2.0; the rows still stack note-then-caption, matching Stata's ring(4)/ring(5) ordering. Verified visually in a browser: a two-line note and a two-line caption now render as four lines in the right order. test/test_textbox_multiline.py asserts the caption's y is non-negative and that the note is added before the caption -- the drawable property rather than the text, because the text was never the thing that was wrong.

- [x] Title: Every computed layer ignores the rows its qualifier selected
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (GlyphMapper: map_area, map_lfit, map_qfit, map_lfitci, map_qfitci, map_lowess, map_lpoly, map_lpolyci, map_fpfit, map_fpfitci, map_histogram, map_kdensity, map_mspline, map_mband -- each accepted a `view' parameter and never referenced it)
  - Description: An AST scan of GlyphMapper's 38 map_* methods found fourteen that take a `view=' argument and never mention it in the body: they read the whole `source' instead. Measured on data where g==0 is y=x and g==1 is y approx 50+2x, a spec carrying {"type":"lfit","if_expr":"g==0"} produced fitted endpoints 25.5 -> 30.0. That is the FULL-data fit; the subset answer is 1.0 -> 4.0. The qualifier changed nothing and nothing warned, so `dash (scatter mpg weight) (lfit mpg weight if foreign==1)' drew the all-cars regression line labelled as the foreign-cars one. The same defect made LINKED by() panels draw byte-identical curves: measured, a linked two-panel histogram gave [0.0182, 0.0182] in both panels where the correct answers are [0.3333, 0.3333] and [0.1667, 0.1667]. Unlinked by() was correct, because that path gives each panel its own ColumnDataSource; the linked path shares one source and distinguishes the panels with a CDSView, which these mappers discarded. So the same command gave different numbers depending on whether link() was present.
  - Suggested fix: Mask the input arrays before computing. Attaching the view to the returned renderer cannot work: each of these mappers builds its OWN ColumnDataSource from a grid it calculates, so by the time there is a renderer there is nothing left to filter.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `GlyphMapper._view_mask(view, n)' returns the boolean row mask a view selects, understanding BooleanFilter and IndexFilter, returning None for a view it cannot read, and warning rather than applying a mask whose length does not match the source. `GlyphMapper._masked(source, view, *varnames)' returns the float arrays for those columns with the mask applied and NaN rows dropped together, which is the pairing every call site previously did inline. Twelve mappers now call it; map_histogram and map_kdensity reach their input through the module-level _extract_finite, which gained an optional `view=' -- deliberately defaulted to None so its other two callers, the slider and range FILTER BUILDERS, keep seeing the unfiltered column, since a slider's bounds come from the whole variable and not from whatever a layer is showing. test/test_computed_layer_rows.py asserts, for each of the fourteen, that the qualified result differs from the unqualified one AND equals the same mapper run on a pre-filtered source -- correctness rather than merely "it changed" -- plus that no view and an all-true mask are both unchanged, and that linked and unlinked by() now agree. test/fixtures/qualified_stat_truth.json records what Stata returns for all fourteen layers on auto.dta with and without `if foreign==1', cross-checked against the plotted .gph serset, for the statistical-fidelity work that comes later.

- [x] Title: Seven layers attach a row filter to a source they rebuilt, and draw the wrong rows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py. Affected, and fixed: map_bar, map_spike, map_pcscatter, map_rscatter, map_pcspike, map_pcarrow, map_pcbarrow. NOT affected, and left alone: map_rbar, map_rcap, map_rcapsym.
  - Description: These seven do consume their view, but they attach it to a ColumnDataSource they rebuilt after dropping NaN rows, while the view's BooleanFilter is sized to the ORIGINAL source. When any plotted variable has a missing value the two lengths differ and the mask lands on the wrong observations. Measured end to end through DashRenderer with `spike y x if g==1' on six rows whose first y is missing: the rebuilt source holds five rows, the mask holds six, and the figure draws x = [2, 3, 4] where the qualifier selects [2, 3] -- an extra spike from a row the user excluded. auto.dta's rep78 has five missing values, so this is reachable with the shipped example data. map_rscatter is worse than the others: it stacks y1 and y2 into one array, so its rebuilt source has twice the rows the mask describes.
  - Suggested fix: Mask the input before rebuilding, as the fourteen computed layers now do, and stop attaching the view to a source that is already filtered. Each has a different shape, so they need individual treatment rather than one substitution.
  - Resolution: Fixed, and the reported set was wrong in both directions -- measured, not argued. What decides it is not whether a mapper builds its own source but whether that source is SHORTER than the mask describes. THREE OF THE SEVEN NAMED WERE NOT AFFECTED: map_rbar, map_rcap and map_rcapsym put the full-length arrays into their source, missing values included, so a 6-entry mask meets a 6-row source and Bokeh simply draws nothing for a segment with a NaN coordinate -- measured across all nine renderers the three of them produce. Attaching the view is correct there and they are unchanged. THREE THAT WERE NOT NAMED WERE AFFECTED: map_pcspike, map_pcarrow and map_pcbarrow pass a dropna'd DataFrame straight to ColumnDataSource rather than a dict literal, so the regex that produced the report passed over them; each gives a 5-row source against a 6-entry mask on the same frame. The real set is map_bar, map_spike, map_pcscatter, map_rscatter, map_pcspike, map_pcarrow and map_pcbarrow. Each now masks its input before rebuilding and no longer attaches the view: map_bar takes the raw mask through _view_mask because its xvar may be a STRING column and _masked casts to float, and masking before dropna also means the bar width is derived from the rows actually plotted; the four pc* mappers mask the four-column frame before dropna; map_rscatter masks the three columns before stacking, and deliberately does not use _masked, because that drops a row when ANY named column is NaN and would discard the y2 point of an observation whose y1 alone is missing -- the per-entry test it already had keeps that point. test/test_view_alignment.py asserts for all seven that no excluded row is drawn and that the qualified draw equals the pre-filtered draw, keeps the other three under a guard that they stay aligned, and carries the end-to-end spike reproduction. An AST diff confirms the change touches exactly those seven methods.

- [x] Title: A bar or cap width is derived from rows the qualifier excluded
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (map_rbar, map_rcap, map_rcapsym -- each derives a glyph width from the x spacing of the WHOLE column while drawing only the selected rows)
  - Description: These three size their glyphs from the spacing between distinct x values. They took that spacing from `source.data[xvar]' in full, unmasked, while the glyph itself correctly drew only the rows the view selected -- so adding a qualifier changed how WIDE the bars were as well as which ones appeared. Measured on a frame whose selected rows are spaced 10 apart and whose excluded rows are spaced 1 apart: `map_rbar' through a view produced width 0.8 where plotting the same three rows directly gives 8.0, an order of magnitude and plainly visible; `map_rcap' gave a cap half-width of 0.2 against 2.0. This is NOT the stale-mask defect recorded in the entry above -- these three keep every row in their source, so the mask lines up and the rows drawn are correct. It is the other half of the same principle, and it was surfaced by the asymmetry the previous commit created: map_bar was changed to derive its width from the masked rows and these three were left deriving theirs from all rows, for the identical concept.
  - Suggested fix: Apply the view's mask to the x column before taking the spacing, leaving the glyph source and its view attachment alone -- those are already correct here.
  - Resolution: Fixed in all three. Each now masks the x column with `_view_mask' before computing the distinct-value grid; the source still carries every row and still has the view attached, because that part was never wrong. test/test_view_alignment.py class TestADerivedWidthComesFromTheRowsDrawn asserts for map_bar, map_rbar, map_rcap and map_rcapsym that the width obtained through a qualifier equals the width obtained by handing the mapper the same rows directly, on data where the two differ tenfold. An AST diff confirms the change touches exactly those three methods. Note recorded while measuring this, not acted on: /Applications/Stata/ado/base/t/twoway_bar.sthlp documents twoway bar's default barwidth as a constant 1 in xvar units, NOT derived from x spacing, so dash's `0.8 * min(gap)' is a divergence from Stata rather than a reimplementation of it -- the audit note at .ultraplan-archive-20260921/audits/hardcode-render-0001-2600.md entry M18 claims the opposite and is wrong. That is a twoway-parity question for the fidelity work, separate from this consistency defect.

- [x] Title: `dash serve' returns r(198) for every option it documents
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (`_dash_serve', the forwarding of `0' to _dash_serve_spec)
  - Description: `dash serve, port(5006)' -- the example dash.sthlp gives -- returned "invalid 'port'" and r(198). So did timeout(), nobrowser, and every build option the help says serve inherits. Only the bare form ran. `0' still carries the leading comma that -syntax- leaves on it, so forwarding it after options of our own put TWO commas on one line and everything past the second was read as a positional argument. _dash_build fixes exactly this at its own forwarding site with `: subinstr local' and has done for some time; the serve path never got the same treatment. Nothing caught it because test_serve_options.cscript calls _dash_serve_spec DIRECTLY, bypassing the single line the defect lives on -- its own header says it covers "everything dash serve does except the launch", and its five PASS banners are labelled "dash serve ..." while none of them runs `dash serve'. Every other serve case in the suite was a `capture' asserting _rc == 198 on an error path, so the happy path had no coverage at all.
  - Suggested fix: Strip the leading comma before forwarding, as _dash_build does.
  - Resolution: Fixed with `: copy local' then `: subinstr local', which work on the macro's VALUE so neither expands the user's title() text a second time, and which replace only the first occurrence -- the leading comma. Verified by launching a real server: `dash serve, port(5099) timeout(1) nobrowser' answered on 5099 within six seconds, /health returned {"status": "ok"}, and the root served the Bokeh document titled "dash". The documented build-then-serve sequence was verified too: after `dash build, filter(foreign, type(dropdown))' the remembered filter is carried into the served spec. test/test_serve_launch.cscript drives `dash serve' as a COMMAND, which is what was missing; it runs against an empty registry so option parsing happens first and a correctly-parsed command stops at the registry check, distinguishing "the options parsed" from "invalid 'port'" without holding the suite for the sixty seconds a real launch would cost (timeout() is measured in minutes). **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the options still parse (test/test_serve_launch.cscript passes at 4ede074), but the launch this entry verified now aborts with r(7102), since 7ddbc08. See the open entry "`dash serve' aborts with r(7102) on every launch: the renderer refuses the extension-less app path dash.ado hands it".

- [x] Title: Every successful `dash build' prints an unexplained "file not found"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (the two spec writers: `file open ... using "`spec_json_path'", write replace text' in _dash_render_immediate, and the same line on `specpath' in _dash_prepare_spec)
  - Description: Both spec writers target a freshly declared tempfile, so `write replace' always has nothing to replace and always emits its note. Measured on a bare `dash build' with no capture and no quietly: "(file /var/folders/.../S_86564.000002 not found)" immediately before "dash: dashboard saved to bare.html". An unexplained "not found" printed by a command that succeeded reads as an error, and the path it names is an internal tempfile the user has never heard of. Located by `set trace' rather than by reading: the message comes from the `file open' line itself, not from any of the three `capture confirm file' sites that looked like candidates.
  - Suggested fix: Write the spec quietly; there is never anything to replace.
  - Resolution: Fixed at both sites (81ebbc5). Confirmed absent from a bare `dash build' afterwards. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** no test covered it then: test/test_render_log_clean.cscript bounds the immediate route's log at 60 lines, which one extra line passes, and no test read a `dash build' log for the note. **Regression test 24sep2026 (WP-24, GAPS.md L9; ROADMAP WP-03's AC-03.4):** test/test_bug_regressions.cscript BR-02 runs `capture noisily dash build' (BR-02b), the immediate route's `dash scatter ..., saving()' (BR-02c, the other writer) and `capture noisily dash serve' up to its launch, against a stub renderer that writes the app and exits (BR-02d), each under a named log, and requires the command to succeed and its log to hold no "not found" once Stata's wrapped lines are rejoined; BR-02a first shows the search finding the note itself, made as the defect made it, by a fresh tempfile opened with `write replace'. With `quietly' taken off the writer in _dash_prepare_spec on a copy of the tree, the dash build and dash serve logs each hold one note and BR-02b fails; taken off the writer in _dash_render_immediate instead, BR-02c fails.

- [x] Title: `dash serve' accepts saving(), replace and server, and writes no file
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_prepare_spec declares SAVing, REPLACE and SERVER and reads none of them; they reach it through the trailing * of _dash_serve_spec)
  - Description: These three describe writing a FILE, which is what `dash build' does and what `dash serve' does not. dash.sthlp lists serve's surface as port(), nobrowser(), timeout() and the dashboard options, and none of the three is on it. They were accepted and vanished, so `dash serve, saving(out.html)' served a dashboard, wrote nothing, and said nothing.
  - Suggested fix: Name them rather than dropping them.
  - Resolution: Fixed by reporting them, with the check placed in _dash_prepare_spec rather than in _dash_serve_spec. The first attempt declared the three in _dash_serve_spec's own syntax and was wrong: test_serve_options.py::test_serve_spec_declares_only_its_own_options requires that program to forward the build surface through syntax's catch-all rather than enumerate it, because enumerating it is how the two option lists drifted apart before. That test caught the mistake. _dash_prepare_spec already declares all three for dash build's sake and already knows which command called it through cmdname(), so the check adds no new enumeration anywhere. test/test_serve_launch.cscript SRV-13..15b assert the note appears for each of the three and stays silent when none is given.

- [x] Title: A KPI silently takes a graph's name, and the graph vanishes from the dashboard
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_kpi had no registry check of any kind; _dash_register_graph had one but did not retire the superseded item's globals; _dash_prepare_spec's graph loop skips any entry carrying a KPI marker)
  - Description: Graphs and KPI cards share one registry and one namespace. _dash_register_graph has always refused a duplicate unless name(x, replace) was given. _dash_kpi checked nothing. So `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)' followed by `dash kpi mean(mpg), name(g1) title("Avg")' returned rc 0 at every step and said nothing -- and the entry then held BOTH the graph's JSON and the KPI's marker. At build time the graph loop passes over any entry with a KPI marker, so the graph was dropped: measured, the built file contained the KPI's text and ZERO graph models, with the build returning rc 0. A second KPI of the same name overwrote the first just as quietly -- measured, ${dash_kpi_k2_stat} went from "mean" to "max" with nothing said. The reverse direction was already safe, because _dash_register_graph consults the registry and a KPI is in it; but a graph replacing a KPI kept the KPI's marker, so the graph it accepted would then have been skipped.
  - Suggested fix: Give _dash_kpi the same collision check and the same name(x, replace) handling, and have both registrars retire whatever held the name before.
  - Resolution: Fixed. _dash_kpi now splits a ", replace" sub-option off the name before validating it, refuses a name already in the registry without it, and both registrars call the new _dash_drop_item to retire the previous holder. The globals to drop are resolved in Mata by _dash_item_globals against what Stata actually holds, because `macro drop dash_kpi_g1*' would also take g10's -- the name grammar lets g1 and g10 be registered at once -- and enumerating the suffixes instead would go stale, which is the failure _dash_clear records in its own comment. _dash_prepare_spec's graph loop now names an entry that somehow holds both rather than passing over it silently; that state is no longer reachable through either registrar, but the registry lives in ordinary Stata globals and a do-file that sets dash_* by hand can still produce it. test/test_registry_collision.cscript covers all fifteen cases, including that replacing k1 leaves k10 untouched. dash.sthlp now states that graphs and KPI cards share one namespace and that the registry is held in globals which are not meant to be set by hand.

- [x] Title: RETRACTED -- "A graph-level option written inside a plot segment is dropped in silence"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_plot's graph-option accumulators and the segment loop that fills them)
  - Description: THE DEFECT THIS ENTRY DESCRIBED DOES NOT EXIST, and the entry is kept only so the mistake is on the record. It claimed that `dash (scatter mpg weight, title("A")) (line mpg weight), name(p2)' registers a graph whose JSON has no title key. Re-measured, it registers title "A". The four shapes the entry cited all keep their value: p1 (two segments naming title) -> "B", f1 (the same in || form) -> "B", p2 (one segment) -> "A", p3 (after-comma only) -> "G". The original measurement was taken from a probe that ran `dash clear' between the registrations and read the globals only at the end, so every graph but the last had been wiped before it was looked at; the empty value was the clear, not the parser. Registering under distinct names without clearing shows all of them present. The claim was propagated into dash.sthlp, into this entry, and into commit ab19c74's message before it was checked.
  - Note on the descriptions this entry corrected: GAPS.md K3 and ROADMAP.md TR-04.4 say "last-write-wins across segments -- a later segment can override an earlier value". That half is CORRECT, and this entry's previous contradiction of it was wrong. The other half of K3 -- that a later segment "can never clear" an earlier value -- IS wrong: `title("")' in a later segment blanks it, and twoway agrees.
  - Suggested fix: None for the reported symptom. Correct the false statements, and fix the one real divergence found while measuring (below).
  - Resolution: Retracted, and one real divergence fixed. GROUND TRUTH, read out of the `mtextq' of a saved .gph rather than assumed: `twoway (scatter y x, title("A")) (line y x, title("B"))' -> "B"; one segment naming it -> "A"; `title("")' in a later segment -> empty; and `twoway (scatter y x, title("INSIDE")) (line y x), title("AFTER")' -> "AFTER". dash matched twoway on the first three and diverged on the fourth, giving "INSIDE": _dash_plot parses the after-comma options into the g_* accumulators FIRST, and the segment loop then overwrites them through c_local. Fixed by saving each after-comma value into a `_gafter_<key>' local at parse time and restoring it once the segment loop has finished, so only keys the after-comma parse actually set are restored and a graph naming an option only inside a segment keeps that value. test/test_graph_option_scope.cscript covers all eight cases; GOS-01..04 exist specifically so the retracted claim cannot be made again -- they assert the option IS honoured -- and GOS-05/06 were measured failing before the fix and passing after. dash.sthlp's passage, which told users the option was dropped and pointed at this entry as a known defect, has been replaced with the measured behaviour.

- [x] Title: A `net install`ed dash ignores every scheme it shipped
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (SchemeParser._SCHEME_SEARCH_DIRS and _resolve_path); dash.pkg (the fourteen `f schemes/<name>.scheme' lines)
  - Description: Measured by running a real `net install' from a local source into a clean PLUS. Stata files by BASENAME and EXTENSION, not by the path in the manifest, so the `schemes/' component is dropped and the files land as PLUS/e/economist.scheme and PLUS/s/<name>.scheme, while dash_render.py goes to PLUS/py/ -- an extension subdirectory, not a letter one. Stata's own stata.trk records the same destinations, so this is not an artefact of the staging. _SCHEME_SEARCH_DIRS is (dirname(__file__)/schemes, dirname(__file__)), which on that tree is PLUS/py/schemes and PLUS/py; neither holds a .scheme file. Loading the installed copy and resolving all fourteen built-in names returned is_fallback=True, name='s2color', with two UserWarnings each: 0 of 14 resolved. End to end from Stata, `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1) scheme(s1color)' then `dash build' returned rc 0, wrote a 1,227,573-byte dashboard, printed FOUR raw Python UserWarning blocks, and the HTML carried s2color's #eaf2f3 background twice with s1color's white background nowhere. The default scheme fails identically, so every installed build warns, and Stata 18+ defaults c(scheme) to stcolor, which differs from the fallback in nine of fifteen measured properties. Nine scheme test files missed it because all nine run in the development tree, where dash_render.py sits beside schemes/; test_packaging.py stages a manifest-only install but its assertions (`scheme is not None', `default_color()' starts with `#') are satisfied by the fallback, so it passed on a tree where no scheme file was readable at all.
  - Suggested fix: Let the resolver look in the install's letter directory, without widening what else it can read.
  - Resolution: Fixed in dash_render.py. `_PLUS_ROOT' is dirname of the package directory, but ONLY when that directory is named `py' -- in the repo and in a loose copy it is None and the whole mechanism is inert. `_installed_letter_dir(name)' returns a directory only for one of the fourteen literal _BUILTIN_NAMES and only for a name carrying no separator, so no caller string can steer the join. The lookup itself is an isolated block AFTER the candidate loop and does NOT extend the `allowed' list: `allowed' also governs the CWD candidate, which the caller fully controls, so adding <PLUS>/s to it would let a symlink named s2color.scheme planted in the working directory point at any file in <PLUS>/s, reopening BUG-51 for exactly the fourteen names this was meant to help. That over-broad variant was built and measured: it made 25 files under the test PLUS reachable, the whole PLUS subtree once CWD was inside it, and the entire home tree when dash_render.py was not under py/. The narrow version leaves the symlink-reachable set byte-identical to before, and a tampered install (a shipped scheme symlinked out of its own directory) warns rather than being read. The "searched ..." message now names <PLUS>/<letter> as well, so a genuinely broken install points at the right place. Verified end to end against the real installed tree: the same two commands now emit ZERO warnings and the s2color background is absent from the HTML. test/test_installed_layout.py, INST-01..16, 52 cases, 37 of which failed at HEAD; INST-05 is the BUG-51 regression in the only layout that can break it.

- [x] Title: Three copies of the renderer search end at a path that cannot exist
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_render_immediate, _dash_build and _dash_serve each held the same block, ending `local render_py "`c(sysdir_plus)'d/dash_render.py"')
  - Description: The renderer installs to PLUS/py/dash_render.py; PLUS/d holds dash.ado, dash.sthlp, dash_parse.mata and the two dash_setup files and no .py at all. The middle branch derives the same wrong directory by assuming the renderer sits beside dash.ado. Neither is reached on a healthy install -- measured, `findfile dash_render.py' returns rc 0 and resolves PLUS/py/dash_render.py, because findfile DOES search the extension subdirectory -- so this is not a live failure for installed users. What made it worth removing is that nothing confirmed the file existed before the path was handed to `runpy.run_path', so when the branch WAS reached the user got a Python FileNotFoundError naming a Stata internal directory, r(7102), and no message from dash. `dash serve' printed "starting Bokeh Server on port NNNN. Stata is blocked until the server stops" first and died after, so the user believed a server had started.
  - Suggested fix: One locator, with only the branches that can succeed, and a dash-authored error otherwise.
  - Resolution: Replaced by a single `_dash_find_render' rclass program holding the two candidates that work -- findfile dash_render.py, then beside dash.ado with a `confirm file' before it is returned -- and exiting 601 with "dash: cannot locate dash_render.py. / Not on the ado-path and not beside dash.ado. / Reinstall dash, or put its directory on the ado-path." Candidates under $PLUS/py or $PERSONAL/py were considered and rejected: dash executes whatever this returns, `confirm file' is an existence check rather than a trust check, and those are trees the shipping code never ran from, so adding them would widen what dash executes. test/test_render_locate.cscript, LOC-01..09, including that exactly one place in dash.ado looks for the renderer, so the three copies cannot be recreated.

- [x] Title: theme() is the one spec string interpolated into JSON unescaped
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_build's master_json line: `"theme":"`theme'"' beside `"scheme":"`_scheme_esc'"')
  - Description: Every other user string on that line goes through _dash_json_str first. theme() did not. Measured on ordinary two-command builds: theme("a\q"), theme("C:\temp\x"), theme("a\") and a theme() containing a double quote each gave r(7102) with a Python JSONDecodeError traceback and no message from dash; the identical values in scheme() all gave rc 0. theme("a\b") gave rc 0 and was WORSE -- \b is a valid JSON escape, so two characters the user typed reached the renderer as a single backspace. A Windows-style path is the obvious way to hit this by accident. Rule 16 exists to prevent exactly this. The other raw interpolations on the same line were measured and are not affected: link(), layout() and filterposition() are validated upstream and return r(198), and tabs() was already escaped.
  - Suggested fix: Route theme() through _dash_json_str like its neighbours.
  - Resolution: Fixed; theme() now goes through _dash_json_str into `_theme_esc'. test/test_theme_escaping.cscript, THM-01..09, covers the four aborting values, the silently-corrupting one, a theme() shaped like JSON that tried to add a spec key, and a source check that the raw interpolation is gone.

- [x] Title: A served dashboard built on the fallback scheme says nothing about it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (ServerAppBuilder._glyph_layer_lines emitted `_s.is_fallback = False' as a literal); dash_render.py (_FALLBACK_PROPS and the comment claiming it mirrors s2color)
  - Description: Two things, and the ROADMAP's account of the first was wrong in mechanism. The hardcoded literal suppresses nothing at build time -- SchemeParser has already warned in the BUILDER's process, so whoever ran `dash build, server' was told. The defect is that the generated app is silent: the parent's parsed properties are baked in, and an app built where no .scheme file could be read is byte-indistinguishable in provenance from a healthy one. `is_fallback' also had no reader anywhere in the codebase -- grep over dash.ado, dash_parse.mata, scripts/ and test/ returned nothing -- so correcting the literal alone would have changed nothing observable. Second, and separately: _FALLBACK_PROPS carried 23 properties against s2color's 118 and its comment said "Mirrors s2color so the output stays recognisable". Measured, twelve public attributes differed -- draw_major_vgrid True to False, grid_line_width 1.5 to 0.8, area_alpha and bar_alpha 1.0 to 0.8, heading_font_size 14pt to 13pt, text_font_size 11pt to 10pt, note_font_size 8pt to 9pt, symbolsize 'medium' to '', and graphsize_x/y and legend_cols/legend_position to None. Recognisability is the only reason that dict exists.
  - Suggested fix: Emit the provenance and a notice naming the scheme that was REQUESTED, and make the fallback keep its own promise.
  - Resolution: ServerAppBuilder now keeps `scheme_requested' alongside the resolved scheme and emits `_DASH_SCHEME_IS_FALLBACK' and `_DASH_SCHEME_REQUESTED' into the app, with the rebuilt SchemeParser taking is_fallback from the first rather than the literal False, and a warnings.warn guarded on it. The notice names the REQUESTED scheme on purpose: SchemeParser.fallback sets name to the literal "s2color", so a message built from `self.scheme.name' would tell the user they got what they asked for. _FALLBACK_PROPS gained s2color's own values for the non-colour half -- grid, graphsize, gsize, linewidth, numstyle, clockdir, symbolsize, intensity and the three draw_major_* flags -- read from the shipped schemes/s2color.scheme. test/test_scheme_fallback_provenance.py, FBP-01..09: FBP-01 compares EVERY public attribute rather than the twelve that happened to differ, so the next one cannot slip through.

- [x] Title: dash.pkg carries no Distribution-Date, so adoupdate can never see a release
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.pkg (no `v' line, no `d Distribution-Date:' line, `d Version: 1.0.0  25mar2026'); dash_setup.ado, dash.sthlp, dash_setup.sthlp (all 1.0.0) against dash.ado and dash_parse.mata (2.0  27mar2026)
  - Description: adoupdate compares the `d Distribution-Date:' line and nothing else; dash.pkg had none, so an installed dash could never be reported out of date. Six shipped files declared two different versions, and the manifest's is the one `net describe' and `ado describe' show, so an installed dash reported 1.0.0 while its code was 2.0.
  - Note on the descriptions this corrects: GAPS.md B4 and ROADMAP.md TR-05.6 say to add `v 2' and attribute the adoupdate failure jointly to the missing `v' line and the missing date. Both halves are wrong, and were read rather than measured. `v' is the manifest's FILE FORMAT version -- the same field stata.toc sets to 3, maximum 3, with `v 4' being r(610) -- not the package's version, so `v 2' would declare an older format. And `v' is not written into stata.trk at all, so it cannot take part in an update comparison; only the date can.
  - Suggested fix: Add the date, sync the versions, and add `v 3' as conformance rather than as the fix.
  - Resolution: dash.pkg now leads with `v 3' and carries `d Version: 2.0  27mar2026' and `d Distribution-Date: 20260327'; dash_setup.ado, dash.sthlp and dash_setup.sthlp were moved to 2.0  27mar2026 so all six agree. test/test_pkg_metadata.py, PKG-01..08, asserts the date exists, is yyyymmdd, is a plausible date and names the same day as the `d Version:' line; that the format version is 3 and matches stata.toc's; and that every shipped file states the same version. PKG-04 is deliberately separate from PKG-01 so the two reasons are not conflated again.

- [x] Title: The Python version gate never fired, because `version 16.0' broke the comparator
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (_dash_pyver_ok, the `return scalar ok = ...' / `exit' pairs inside its forvalues loop); reached from dash_setup, which declares `version 16.0'
  - Description: Found while fixing J1, and it is the more serious half. `_dash_pyver_ok' compares two dotted versions component by component and is the whole of dash's Python minimum check. Called from a certification script, which runs under the session's own version, it is correct -- test_setup_python_gate.cscript's fourteen cases all pass. Called from `dash_setup', which declares `version 16.0', it returned 1 (at or above) for EVERY input. Measured, the same program through a `version 16.0' wrapper and through one with no version line: 2.4 >= 3.0 gave 1 against 0; 3.7.9 >= 3.8 gave 1 against 0; 0.9 >= 1.5 gave 1 against 0. Traced inside the loop, at i=1 for "2.4" against "3.0" it computed gnum=2 and wnum=3, took NEITHER branch, and carried on to i=2 -- so `return scalar' and `exit' inside the forvalues took effect under neither, and every input fell through to the final `ok = 1'. The parsing itself was fine: the same trace showed gparts "2 4", wparts "3 0", ng 2, nw 2. So a user on Python 3.7 was told "Python ............. OK (version 3.7.9)" and the branch J1 is about -- the one that prints an error and forgets to exit -- was never reached at all. The test passed throughout because no case called the comparator the way production does.
  - Suggested fix: Decide once inside the loop and return once after it, and cover the calling context the tests were missing.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_pyver_ok' now sets a `decided'/`answer' pair inside the loop and issues its single `return scalar ok' after it; the loop body is guarded on `decided' rather than exiting. Re-measured through both wrappers, all six pairs now agree: 2.4>=3.0 -> 0, 3.7.9>=3.8 -> 0, 3.8.0>=3.8 -> 1, 3.10>=3.9 -> 1, 1.23.5>=1.23 -> 1, 0.9>=1.5 -> 0. test_setup_python_gate.cscript gains seven cases that call it through a `version 16.0' program, which is the gap that let this survive; test_setup_honesty.cscript covers the rest.

- [x] Title: dash_setup reports a failure and then declares success
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (the below-minimum Python branch, which has no `exit'; the optional-package `continue'; the `packaging could not install' branch; the below-minimum package branch; and the unconditional closing banner)
  - Description: dash_setup ended, unconditionally, with "dash_setup complete. All dependencies are satisfied." Four branches reached it after reporting a failure. Every `exit' in the file was on another branch -- no Python at all, a required pip install that failed, and the two jsonio ones. The command whose entire job is to tell the truth about the environment was the one command that could not, which inverts CLAUDE.md rule 2.
  - Suggested fix: Count failures and warnings, and branch the summary on them.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `dash_setup' now keeps n_fail/n_warn plus the names of what went wrong, and the banner moved into `_dash_setup_summary', which prints the success line only when there were no failures, prints a distinct "dash_setup did not complete: N unmet requirement(s)" and exits 198 when there were, and prints a middle form naming the optional items when there were only warnings.
