A failure is something that stops dash working; an optional package that could not be installed is a warning, because it costs four plot types rather than the command. The jsonio and required-package exits now route through the same program so the message and the exit code come from one place. It is a SEPARATE program precisely so a certification script can drive it: while the banner was four inline `display' lines, "the summary must reflect what happened" could not be tested without pip-installing into the user's environment. test/test_setup_honesty.cscript HON-01..05, and HON-18 asserts the success line is written in exactly one place.

- [x] Title: The version gate reported "OK (version unknown)" for versions it knew
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (the `if "`pkgver'" != "unknown" & `pkg_has_packaging'' gate and its `else')
  - Description: The gate had four possible states and two messages. Whenever `packaging' was unavailable the `else' printed "OK (version unknown)" for every package -- although `_dash_pkg_version' had already retrieved the real version. Measured on this machine it returns bokeh 3.8.0, numpy 2.3.5, pandas 2.3.3, scipy 1.16.3 and statsmodels 0.14.5, so "version unknown" was false and "OK" was unverified rather than true: a bokeh 2.4 was reported OK. The `Version(...)' call had no `capture' either -- measured, an unparseable string gives r(7102) from inside dash_setup with no message of its own.
  - Suggested fix: Four states, four messages, and never "OK" for something that was not checked.
  - Resolution: Fixed by `_dash_setup_gate', which returns r(verdict) in {ok, below, unverified, unknown} and is captured. dash's own `_dash_pyver_ok' is the fallback when packaging is absent, NOT a replacement: measured against packaging on nine pairs it agrees on six and disagrees on three, all pre/post-release forms (0.13.0rc1>=0.13 gives 1 against packaging's 0; 3.0b1>=3.0 the same; 3.0.post1>=3.0 gives -1 against 1), because PEP 440 puts a release candidate before its release. So the fallback may report `below', where it is conservative, but never `ok' -- an at-or-above answer it produced alone is reported as `unverified'. A below-minimum required package is now counted as unmet and the exact upgrade command is printed, rather than being upgraded behind the user's back: the package is already importable, so something else in their environment may depend on the version they have. test_setup_honesty.cscript HON-06..13.

- [x] Title: dash_setup accepted any option, and never checked dash's own files
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado (no `syntax' line; no check of dash_render.py or the schemes; two sections both numbered 3)
  - Description: `dash_setup, garbage(1)' ran the whole setup and ignored the option in silence -- dash_setup.sthlp's Syntax section is the bare command. Separately, setup checked Python, five Python packages and jsonio and never asked whether dash ITSELF was reachable, which is exactly how the `net install' whose fourteen schemes all fell back to built-in defaults was reported as "All dependencies are satisfied" (see the scheme entry above). pip also received a bare package name while the minimums declared beside it never reached it, so a fresh install could land below the minimum and then be reported as below it; a permission-denied install was never retried with --user; and the `packaging' install discarded pip's output, so a failure there gave no reason.
  - Suggested fix: Declare the surface, check dash's own files, and give pip the minimum.
  - Resolution: `syntax' added -- measured, a bare `syntax' rejects both an option and a stray argument with r(101), Stata's "options not allowed", not r(198). `_dash_setup_check_files' added and called before the summary: it locates dash_render.py the way dash.ado's own locator does, then asks the RENDERER whether it can read a scheme, because `findfile s2color.scheme' succeeds on the very install where the renderer could not read a single one -- a findfile check would have passed on the broken tree it exists to catch. pip now receives `<pkg>>=<min>' built from the minimum declared beside it, and a failed install is retried once with --user before being reported. The `packaging' install now prints pip's tail like the package loop already did. The duplicate section number is corrected; the sections run 1-6. test_setup_honesty.cscript HON-14..17.

- [x] Title: dash reached Python with no check, and its Mata library with none either
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_do_render and _dash_serve both go straight to `python:'; the top-level loader ends `capture run "`_dash_matalib'"' and discards the code); dash_setup.ado (`capture python query' as the availability probe)
  - Description: CLAUDE.md rule 15 asks for the environment to be verified at start-up. dash verified one of its three external dependencies. jsonio was checked, and well -- "dash: jsonio is required but not found. Run dash_setup first." -- and Python and the Mata library were not checked at all. `c(python_exec)' appeared NOWHERE in either .ado. A user with no linked Python got a raw Stata Python error naming neither dash nor the remedy, from a line a few lines below the jsonio check that does it properly. For the Mata library, measured on three staged trees: with dash_parse.mata absent the first command died with "<istmt>: 3499 _dash_check_utf8() not found" and not one word about a missing file; with nothing compiling, a page of Mata syntax errors and then the same 3499; and with one unrelated function broken, a bare "command real is unrecognized" followed by `dash scatter mpg weight' returning RC 0 and reporting "graph 'b1' registered". GAPS J5 names `_dash_split_segs()'; the function actually reached first is `_dash_check_utf8()', and the rc-0 case is not described there at all.
  - Suggested fix: Probe Python by configuration, and verify the Mata functions dash calls.
  - Resolution: `_dash_python_ok' takes the value as an argument and `_dash_require_python' passes it `c(python_exec)', exiting 198 with a message modelled on the jsonio one. It is split that way because the failing state cannot otherwise be reached on a machine that HAS Python: c() is read-only and `set python_exec' refuses a path that does not exist (measured, r(601)). It is called at the entry to _dash_do_render and before `dash serve' announces its port. dash_setup's `capture python query' is replaced by the same configuration probe; `python query' is kept for REPORTING the version, which is what it is for. test/test_env_guards.cscript ENV-01..07, registered as runner 34, and test_cscript_hygiene.py now applies its "do not probe Python by running it" rule to dash.ado and dash_setup.ado, which is how dash_setup kept that probe -- the rule had been enforced for .cscript files only.

- [x] Title: ROADMAP TR-07.4's fix would have been a regression
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_require_mata, and the top-level loader's `capture run')
  - Description: TR-07.4 asks for the return code of the Mata `run' to be checked, on the grounds that discarding it hides a failed compile. Measured, that code does not carry the question:
    state                         run rc    functions present
    healthy, first load                0                  yes
    healthy, loaded a second time   3000                  yes
    nothing compiles                3000                   no
    one unrelated function broken    199                  yes
    file not found            (not called)         not by this
    A benign SECOND load returns the same 3000 as a total compile failure, so refusing to run on a non-zero code would break every user -- and every certification script in test/ -- that loads dash_parse.mata itself before dash.ado. Implemented literally, TR-07.4 is a regression.
  - Note on a second wrong turn, recorded because it was measured: the first replacement checked that the functions were present and VETOED when they were not. That failed test_examples_run.cscript, which runs `clear all' before each of the eleven shipped examples. Measured, the library is routinely absent at the entry to a command through no fault of the install: `dash clear' never needed it and succeeds without it, `clear all' drops it, and dash.ado's own top-level loader does not always run before the first command when the file is reached through the ado-path -- `mata describe' returned 111 immediately after a successful `dash clear' from the ado-path and 0 only after a later reload.
  - Suggested fix: Repair before reporting.
  - Resolution: `_dash_require_mata' checks one function cheaply, and on a miss loads dash_parse.mata itself -- findfile, then beside dash.ado -- exactly as the top-level loader does, before re-checking all ten functions dash calls and reporting only if they are still absent. So it cannot false-positive, it fixes a library cleared mid-session rather than merely complaining about it, and the genuinely broken install gets "dash: dash's Mata library could not be loaded. / Missing: ... / dash_parse.mata is not on the ado-path and not beside dash.ado. / Reinstall dash, or run dash_setup." with r(198). Verified by hand against all three staged trees. test_env_guards.cscript ENV-08..11 covers the reload, the repair and the double-load case; the unrepairable branch is not asserted there because a cscript cannot take its own package off the ado-path -- under the runner the directory is on it twice -- and the script says so.

- [x] Title: The UTF-8 gate accepted overlong encodings, surrogates and codepoints above U+10FFFF
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata (_is_utf8); dash.ado (the gate was called at one entry point only)
  - Description: `_is_utf8' checked that the LEAD byte was in range and that the bytes after it were 0x80-0xBF. RFC 3629 does not say that: the allowed range of the SECOND byte depends on the lead byte, and that is precisely what separates a well-formed sequence from a malformed one. Measured, every one of these was accepted -- E0 80 AF (overlong U+002F), E0 9F BF (overlong U+07FF), F0 80 80 80 (overlong U+0000), F0 8F BF BF (overlong U+FFFF), ED A0 80 and ED BF BF (UTF-16 surrogates), F4 90 80 80 (above the Unicode maximum) -- and each then reached Python and ended the render with r(7102). An overlong encoding is a filter-bypass primitive: it spells a character that a validator reading the bytes naively does not recognise as that character, which is why the RFC forbids them rather than discouraging them. Separately, the gate was called at exactly one entry point, `_dash_plot', so KPI titles, build titles and subtitles, tabs() and filter labels had no gate at all.
  - Suggested fix: Apply RFC 3629 section 4 verbatim, and call the gate wherever text enters.
  - Resolution: Rewritten with the per-lead second-byte ranges (C2-DF / E0 A0-BF / E1-EC / ED 80-9F / EE-EF / F0 90-BF / F1-F3 / F4 80-8F). Verified by DIFFERENTIAL TEST against Python's own decoder rather than against a hand-written table -- a sequence is well-formed exactly when bytes(seq).decode("utf-8") does not raise. A sweep of 41,976 sequences, every lead/second pair at all four lengths, produced ZERO mismatches. test/fixtures/utf8_cases.txt commits the 2,245 of those that distinguish the rules (every single byte; every lead 0xC0-0xF7 against second bytes on and just outside each range boundary, in two-, three- and four-byte forms; plus random sequences), regenerable with scripts/gen_utf8_cases.py. The gate is now called from _dash_kpi, _dash_prepare_spec, _dash_build, _dash_serve_spec and _dash_serve as well. test/test_utf8_validation.cscript UTF-01..12, and test_adversarial.cscript P8-86..88 drive a surrogate through build title(), KPI title() and tabs() end to end.

- [x] Title: JSON strings carried raw control bytes, so a stray character aborted the build
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_json_str escaped backslash, quote, TAB, LF and CR and nothing else)
  - Description: JSON forbids every unescaped byte below 0x20 inside a string. Five were escaped and twenty-seven were not, including \b (8) and \f (12), which have short forms in the grammar. Measured: `dash build, title("bad" + char(1) + "title")' and the same through the immediate route both ended in r(7102), a Python JSONDecodeError, with no message from dash. A control character pasted in with text from a web page is the ordinary way to reach this.
  - Suggested fix: Escape them.
  - Resolution: The five short forms plus \u00XX for every other C0 byte. ESCAPING rather than rejecting is deliberate: the user story asks for "either a clean dash error or a correct dashboard", and a correct dashboard is the better of the two -- the character now survives into the title instead of stopping the build. ROADMAP AC-08.2 asks for an error; this delivers the other half of the same sentence. char(0) is unreachable because a Stata string cannot carry one. Note for the next person: Stata has no hex display format -- `: display %02x' is r(120) -- so the digits come from a digit string. test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-01..08 walks all thirty-one bytes, and test_adversarial.cscript P8-89 asserts the build succeeds rather than merely not crashing.

- [x] Title: Every Windows saving() path on the immediate route wrote to the wrong file
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_render_immediate's `strpos(..., "\"saving\":\"")' slice)
  - Description: _dash_plot writes saving() into the spec through _dash_json_str, which doubles backslashes, and _dash_render_immediate read it back by slicing the raw document at the first quote after "saving":" -- so it took the ESCAPED text and used it as a filesystem path. Measured: `dash scatter mpg weight, saving("<dir>a\b.html") replace' returned rc 0, and `confirm file "<dir>a\b.html"' gave r(601) -- the file was written to "<dir>a\\b.html" instead. The command reported success and put the user's dashboard somewhere they did not ask for. The same slice truncated any path containing a quote, since \" is a quote in the document but not in the value.
  - Suggested fix: Read it as JSON.
  - Resolution: Replaced by `_dash_json_get_str', the reader dash already uses for every other spec value, which handles the escapes correctly (its escape branch was itself fixed earlier in this session). test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-09 asserts the file the user named exists and JSE-10 that the doubled one does not.

- [x] Title: The path validator refused my..report.html and accepted -e.html
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (_dash_validate_path: `regexm("\.\.")', no leading-hyphen check, no `~' handling); dash_render.py (main() validated its --output not at all)
  - Description: Three things, all measured. The traversal rule matched the two characters ANYWHERE, so `saving("my..report.html")' -- an ordinary file name -- gave r(198), while catching nothing the segment rule does not. A file name beginning with `-' was accepted, although dash opens what it writes with `shell open "<path>"' and `open -e.html' is parsed as a flag rather than a file. And `~' was never expanded, so `saving("~/dashtilde.html")' ended in r(7102), a Python traceback. Separately, dash_render.py is also a command-line program, and its --output reached main() with nothing checked at all, so a path Stata refuses was writable by invoking the renderer directly.
  - Suggested fix: Make `..' a segment rule, reject a leading hyphen, expand `~/', and mirror the checks on the Python side.
  - Resolution: `..' is now `(^|[/\\])\.\.($|[/\\])'; a leading `-' on the basename is refused with the `./name' remedy named; a leading `~/' is expanded from $HOME, and a `~' elsewhere is left alone. The validator publishes the normalised path as `_dash_vpath' and all three call sites read it back -- it cannot write through its argument, which is a value rather than a macro name. dash_render.py gains `_validate_output_path', mirroring those rules plus a control-character check and the expected extension for the mode. The mode is checked BEFORE the path, because the expected extension depends on it; test_server.py::test_main_raises_on_serve_mode caught the first version reporting the wrong thing for mode "serve". test_path_validation.cscript PV-13..19.

- [x] Title: The server is a second implementation of every plot type, and it disagrees with the first in 132 ways
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (ServerAppBuilder's emitted `if/elif ptype == '...'` chain, ~36 branches) against GlyphMapper's 39 `map_*` methods
  - Description: The whole GlyphMapper class is already embedded in the generated app by `_inspect.getsource(GlyphMapper)`, and `_DASH_MAPPER` is constructed from it, so all 39 methods are AVAILABLE there. Measured, `grep -oE '_DASH_MAPPER\.map_[a-z_]+' dash_render.py | sort -u` returned exactly one name: `map_scatter`. The other 38 plot types were reimplemented inline. A systematic comparison of every server branch against its mapper counterpart -- 37 types, each compared and then adversarially re-checked -- found 132 non-cosmetic differences, 28 of them S1. Only `scatter`, which already delegates, had none.
  - Note on ROADMAP TR-09.1's premise: WP-09 says "This is cheaper than it looks ... The server simply does not call 37 of them." The first half is wrong. Calling them is not a substitution: in 30 of the 37 types the two implementations differ in ways a user can see, and in several the SERVER does something the mapper does not, so a straight swap would lose behaviour. The measured inventory is the real work list.
  - Direction of the differences, which is what decides the order of work: most are `mapper_does_more`, and for those unification is a pure gain -- e.g. a bar chart with a string x variable currently takes the served app down with an uncaught TypeError that `map_bar` catches; `barwidth(-5)` reaches Bokeh and raises "Distances must be positive!" where `map_bar` clamps and warns; `lcolor(none)` draws an opaque BLACK line in the served document where Stata and the saved document draw nothing; every Stata one-letter `msymbol()` abbreviation is silently ignored server-side because the branch carries a 9-name table against the translator's 50, which under a mono scheme makes two overlaid series identical. The `server_does_more` ones are the blockers: `map_area` does not sort by x before drawing the polygon and the server branch does, so on unsorted data the mapper draws a self-intersecting zig-zag -- a standalone bug the comparison surfaced; `map_spike` builds its own x0/y0/x1/y1 source where the server keeps the original columns plus `index`, which the app's filter callback and its brush-to-KPI wiring both read by name; and the pc branches register their source for live recomputation, which is caller plumbing rather than mapper behaviour and has to stay in the caller.
  - Suggested fix: Close the `server_does_more` gaps in the mappers first -- they are standalone bugs too -- then swap type by type with test_mode_parity.py as the gate.
  - Resolution (partial): The machinery is in and six types are through it. `_dash_glyph_dispatch`, `_dash_pc_dispatch`, `_dash_add_glyph`, `_dash_add_pc_glyph` and `_dash_layer_source` are module-level functions taking the mapper as a parameter, and the emitter copies them into the app with `inspect.getsource` -- the same mechanism `_shared_stat_lines` already used for the statistical helpers, and for the same stated reason. `DashRenderer._add_glyph`/`_add_pc_glyph` now delegate to them, so there is one dispatch rather than two. The three plot-type categories moved to module level for the same reason. `dot`, `qfit`, `lfitci`, `qfitci`, `fpfit` and `fpfitci` -- the six whose only differences were `mapper_does_more`, so the swap can only gain -- now call the shared dispatch, joining `scatter`. An unknown type warns instead of drawing nothing (AC-09.3). The remaining 30 types are NOT swapped: each needs its `server_does_more` or `both_differ` items resolved first, and the inventory says which. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** the partial resolution here was completed by 878529c and 5422974 (WP-09): every plot type now dispatches through `GlyphMapper' in both modes. Pinned by test/test_server.py::TestTheServerDispatchesThroughTheSharedTable and test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTheUnificationKeepsD2Closed.
  - Note on a mistake worth recording: the first attempt at the swap located branch boundaries with `elif ptype == 'x':` only. The main chain writes area as `elif ptype in ('area',):`, which that pattern does not match, so a neighbouring replacement ran past its own end and swallowed the area branch. Three tests caught it immediately -- test_server.py's two area-option tests and test_mode_parity's area case -- and the work was reverted and redone with a detector that matches both forms and then asserts that every branch present before is present after.

- [x] Title: Four path-drawing mappers joined their points in dataset order
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (map_area, map_rline, map_rarea, and map_tsrline through map_rline)
  - Description: Bokeh's `line` and `varea` join points in the order the ColumnDataSource gives them, and Stata's twoway line/area/rline/rarea draw along x. `map_line` and `map_connected` sorted; these four did not. Measured on x = [3, 1, 2, 5, 4], every one of them returned that order unchanged, so `dash area y x`, `dash rline y1 y2 x`, `dash rarea y1 y2 x` and `dash tsrline y1 y2 x` drew self-intersecting shapes on any dataset not already sorted by x -- silently, because every point is present and correct. Found while comparing the two render modes for WP-09: the generated server app sorts in each of its own inline branches, so these are places where the two disagreed and the SERVER was right. They are bugs in the SAVED dashboard, independent of the unification.
  - Suggested fix: Sort, without disturbing the shared source.
  - Resolution: `map_area` already built its own ColumnDataSource, so it sorts before building it. `map_rline` and `map_rarea` draw straight from the passed source, which is shared with other layers and may carry a view whose IndexFilter is in the original row order -- sorting that in place would corrupt both. They now take the row ORDER through a new `_masked_order` helper and build their own source from it, exactly as `map_area` does, and pass no view on because the rows are already the selected ones. `_masked_order` returns indices rather than arrays so a non-numeric column can ride the same selection: `_masked` coerces everything to float, and the first version of this fix dropped the mlabel column, which `test_glyphs.py::TestMlabelOnRangePlots` caught immediately. test/test_polyline_ordering.py, 21 cases: the ordering itself, that no row is lost or invented, and that x keeps its pairing with y (sorting one column and not the other is how this kind of fix goes wrong, and it is invisible on y = x**2 unless you look). `rcap`, `rspike`, `rbar` and `rcapsym` are deliberately not covered: they draw one independent mark per row, so their order cannot be seen.

- [x] Title: Four mappers lost the plot's line options whenever the curve could not be computed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (map_lowess, map_mspline, map_mband, map_fpfit -- each returns an empty ColumnDataSource on its bail-out path)
  - Description: Each of these computes a curve and falls back to an empty renderer when it cannot -- no statsmodels, fewer than two points, an x that does not vary. Four of them returned that renderer WITHOUT applying the plot's line options, so `lwidth(thick)' was silently dropped for the layer. Measured with a constant x, which makes every one of them bail: map_lowess, map_mspline, map_mband and map_fpfit gave line_width 1 where 2.5 was asked for; map_lpoly, map_lfit and map_qfit were already correct. This is not cosmetic: statsmodels is an OPTIONAL dependency, so on a machine without it EVERY lowess layer takes this path. The generated server app applied the width regardless, which is how test_server.py::TestServerLineWidthAllTypes::test_lowess_applies_lwidth caught it the moment lowess started going through the mapper.
  - Suggested fix: Style the empty renderer too.
  - Resolution: All four now call `_apply_line_opts' before returning the empty renderer. test/test_polyline_ordering.py covers all seven of the degrading mappers, so the three that were already correct cannot regress either.

- [x] Title: map_rbar ignored bfcolor(), blcolor(), lwidth() and lcolor()
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (map_rbar called _apply_fill_opts and never _apply_line_opts, and read neither bfcolor nor blcolor)
  - Description: map_bar handles the bar-specific fill and line overrides and then calls both appliers; map_rbar had only `_apply_fill_opts'. Measured: bfcolor(red), blcolor(navy) and lwidth(thick) each left the glyph at the scheme defaults, while fcolor(green) worked -- so a range bar honoured one of the four colour options a bar honours. The generated server app read all four in its own branch, which is how test_server.py's rbar cases caught it the moment rbar started going through the mapper.
  - Suggested fix: Do what map_bar does.
  - Resolution: map_rbar now applies bfcolor and blcolor, then `_apply_fill_opts' and `_apply_line_opts' in that order, so blcolor keeps priority for the line -- the same sequence and the same reason as map_bar.

- [x] Title: A served pci layer drew nothing, because it read variable names that do not exist
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `elif ptype in ('pci', 'pcscatter')' branch read y1var/x1var/y2var/x2var from the plot)
  - Description: `pci 20 3000 30 4000' is an inline coordinate list: dash.ado writes it into the spec as `values', not as four variable names. The standalone caller has always used `map_pci_literal' for it, with a comment recording that `map_pci' forwards to map_pcspike, which wants variable names, so nothing was ever drawn. The served branch read exactly those four names, which the command never sets, so a pci layer in a served dashboard drew nothing at all -- silently, with rc 0.
  - Suggested fix: Use the same method the standalone caller does.
  - Resolution: The combined branch was split; pci now calls `_DASH_MAPPER.map_pci_literal(fig, plot.get('values', []), opts, color_idx=pi)' and pcscatter takes the pc source builder plus its agg_sources registration. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTheUnificationKeepsD2Closed::test_pci_draws_the_same_segments.

- [x] Title: A served marker label was drawn twice, and drawn on layers the saved dashboard does not label
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted "Common mlabel block", which ran after the plot-type dispatch)
  - Description: Once WP-09 routed every plot type through `GlyphMapper', the mapper drew each layer's `mlabel()' itself -- and the generated app still ran a hand-written block over the top that added a second `LabelSet' at the same coordinates. Measured with `mlabel(foreign)': line, connected, dot, dropline and tsline each carried TWO label sets when served and one when saved; lpoly, lpolyci, fpfit, fpfitci and mband carried one when served and NONE when saved, because the block's exclusion list was written before those types delegated. Two label sets at one point render as doubled, slightly smeared text.
  - Suggested fix: Delete the block. `_dash_layer_source' already carries the label column into the layer source for the mapper to read.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The block is gone, not narrowed: `GlyphMapper' is the only thing in either mode that draws a marker label. 51 regression cases in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestMlabelIsDrawnExactlyAsTheSavedDashboardDrawsIt' -- one per plot type comparing the two modes, plus a "labelled once" case for each of the eight types that carry labels, plus a source-level guard that the generated app holds no second implementation.

- [x] Title: A per-plot `if'/`in' qualifier was not read at all in server mode
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted plot loop had no `plot.get('if_expr')'); dash.ado (1785-1805, which promotes the first segment's qualifier and leaves every segment carrying its own)
  - Description: `dash.ado' promotes the FIRST plot segment's qualifier to the graph level and leaves each segment carrying its own, because the saved renderer applies them per layer. The generated app read only the graph-level one, so every later segment's qualifier was discarded. Measured on ten observations, five foreign: `dash (scatter mpg weight) (scatter mpg weight if foreign==1)' drew 10 and 5 points when saved and 10 and 10 when served -- a wrong picture with no warning and nothing visibly wrong with it.
  - Suggested fix: Read the qualifier per layer, through the same whitelist the graph-level one uses.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_qualified_frame' is one module-level reader that both modes run; it translates Stata's `if' grammar, validates it against `_validate_if_expr''s AST whitelist and warns rather than silently drawing the wrong rows when it cannot apply it. The emitted plot loop now narrows `g_df' per layer when the layer's qualifier differs from the graph's, which is the test the saved renderer makes. A linked graph keeps drawing from the shared source: `_dash_layer_source' turns the shorter frame into a CDSView over it. The app's own `_safe_g' whitelist is gone; it is now an alias of `_validate_if_expr', so one node set governs both modes. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestAPerPlotQualifierIsRead', including an injection case proving the per-plot path goes through the same gate.

- [x] Title: A served KPI ignored its own `if'/`in' and showed the unconditional number
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `val = _compute_kpi(df, kstat, kvar)', and the two callback recomputes)
  - Description: `_compute_kpi' was handed the whole frame. `dash kpi mean(mpg) if foreign==1' therefore served the mean over every observation, where the saved dashboard -- and Stata's own `summarize' -- report the mean over the five that qualify. The same was true of both recompute paths, so the card stayed wrong when a filter moved or a selection changed. A wrong number presented as a right one is worse than a missing card.
  - Suggested fix: Apply the KPI's own qualifier before computing the statistic, at all three sites.
  - Resolution: Fixed at all three: the initial build, the filter callback and the brush callback each restrict the frame with `_dash_qualified_frame' first. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestAKpiQualifierIsApplied', including an injection case.

- [x] Title: A served layer that drew nothing said nothing
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted plot loop had no equivalent of the saved renderer's empty-layer report)
  - Description: GAPS.md D6. The saved dashboard has warned since WP-02 when a layer adds no data to a non-empty graph. The generated app had no equivalent, which is why every divergence GAPS.md D2 lists was silent -- a served `pci' produced a titled, axed, empty plot region and rc 0.
  - Suggested fix: Report it in the served app too, in the same words.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_layer_drew_anything' and `_dash_report_empty_layer' are module-level and copied into the app, and the saved renderer calls them too, so the two modes cannot word the report differently. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestALayerThatDrewNothingSaysSoInBothModes', including a sweep over all 38 plot types asserting that neither mode reports a layer the other drew.

- [x] Title: Only five of the 38 plot types had a hover tool when served, and hover() was ignored
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (HoverTool was imported into the generated app and never instantiated); dash_render.py `_make_figure' (the saved renderer cleared the mappers' own tools only when hover() was given)
  - Description: The only tooltips a served figure carried were the ones `map_scatter' and `map_line' add for themselves. Measured on a bare plot of each type: bar, histogram, area, dropline, lfit, qfit, and every range type had NO tooltip when served and one when saved; `hover(foreign)' was ignored entirely. The saved renderer had its own defect at the same spot -- it built a comprehensive tool and removed the per-layer ones only when `hover()' was given, so a plain `dash scatter mpg weight' carried TWO HoverTools with identical tooltips, which renders as two tooltip boxes.
  - Suggested fix: Build the figure's hover in one place, for both modes, and clear what came before unconditionally.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_build_hover' is module-level, copied into the app, and called by both renderers. It clears existing HoverTools first, decides its rows from the columns the figure's renderers actually carry, attaches the comprehensive tool to the renderers that can answer it and falls back to the generic bin or x/y tooltip only when none can. `hover()`'s columns are carried into the served layer sources (`_dash_layer_source' / `_dash_range_source' gained `extra_cols', and the NaN drop still names only the plotted variables, so an extra tooltip column cannot delete rows), and `map_bar', `map_spike', `map_dropline' and `map_rcap' carry the caller's other columns through their derived sources via the new `GlyphMapper._carry_cols'. Measured after: all 38 types, bare and with `hover(foreign)', produce identical tooltips in both modes. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestEveryFigureCarriesTheSameHover'.

- [x] Title: The served legend named every layer after the y variable
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `_leg_lbl' computation and the `_leg_bounds' rebuild)
  - Description: Stata keys a computed layer "Fitted values" and a confidence band "<level>% CI"; the saved dashboard has done so since WP-02. The generated app wrote `var_labels.get(yvar) or yvar' for all 38 types, so a served `lfitci' was keyed "mpg" for both the band and the line and there was no way to tell them apart. It also built a Legend model for `lowess', `scatteri' and `pci', where the saved dashboard builds none, and styled the keys with the scheme's TICK label size rather than its text size -- 10pt against 11pt, and those two differ for 13 of the 14 shipped schemes.
  - Suggested fix: Share Stata's keying rules, and follow the saved renderer's rule for whether a legend exists at all.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_legend_key' and `_dash_legend_entries' are module-level, copied into the app, and used by `DashRenderer._rebuild_legend' as well; `_DASH_FIT_TYPES' and `_DASH_CI_TYPES' moved with them. The served app rebuilds keys only when the mapper created a legend, which is the saved renderer's own rule. `_SCHEME_CHROME' gained `text_font_size' and `_dash_style_legend' uses it. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTheLegendSaysTheSameThing', covering all 38 types plus the CI split, the level and a non-default scheme.

- [x] Title: layout(name:0,0) placed the item when saved and was discarded when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (`_placement_map' accepted both forms; the emitted `_dash_placements' required every index to be at least 1)
  - Description: The saved renderer accepts both the 1-based form `dash.sthlp' documents and the 0-based form `dash.ado''s regex admits, and normalises them. The generated app had a second parser that dropped any placement with a zero index, without a word -- one spec with two meanings.
  - Suggested fix: One parser.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_placement_map' is module-level, takes the set of valid names, and is what both `DashRenderer._placement_map' and the app's `_dash_placements' call. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestLayoutPlacementMeansOneThing', including a source-level guard that the app carries no second parser.

- [x] Title: A served KPI's fmt(%12.0fc) was identical to fmt(%12.0f)
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `_format_kpi')
  - Description: The generated app's formatter matched `%[w].[d][fge]', ALWAYS comma-grouped an `f', ignored the `c' suffix that asks for grouping, had no `%9.0g' default, never consulted the variable's own Stata display format, and fell back to `round(value, 4)'. So the `c' suffix meant nothing when served, and a card with no `fmt()' showed a different number of digits in the two modes.
  - Suggested fix: Use the saved renderer's formatter.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_effective_kpi_fmt' and `_dash_format_kpi_value' are module-level and copied into the app; `DashRenderer._effective_kpi_fmt' and `_format_kpi_value' delegate to them, and the app's `_format_kpi' is a two-line call that now also receives the variable and the statistic. The dataset's numeric display formats are resolved at build time into `_NUM_FORMATS'. `_DATE_VALUED_STATS' moved to module level with them. Regression tests in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTheKpiCardIsFormattedTheSameWay'.

- [x] Title: Under layout(bs) an item tabs() did not name was drawn ABOVE the tabs
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_render_bootstrap_html' (both the sidebar and the horizontal-filter page bodies)
  - Description: `dash.sthlp' says an item `tabs()' does not name is "still drawn, below the tabs". The plain grid and server mode do that; the Bootstrap layout emitted the grid section before the tabs section, so the same sentence described two behaviours depending on `layout()'.
  - Suggested fix: Emit the tabs section first.
  - Resolution: Fixed in both page bodies. Regression test in `test/test_server_layer_defects.py::TestTabsAreWhereTheHelpSaysTheyAre'.

- [x] Title: Only level() was validated; every other option value went to the renderer verbatim
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_parse_plot_opts' (the level() block at 830-842 was the only check), `_dash_plot' (no graph-level check at all), `_dash_plot_opts_to_json' (every value serialised as a string)
  - Description: GAPS.md E5. Measured 22sep2026 through Stata, forty probes, thirty-six of them bad values: only level(abc) and level(150) were refused; seventeen bad values drew with rc 0 and no message at all (msize(abc), msymbol(nonsense), mcolor(notacolour), jitter(-3), jitter(abc), lwidth(abc), lpattern(bogus), mlabposition(99), yaxis(3), colornlevels(0), bgcolor(notacolour), hover(bogus), fintensity(abc), mlabsize(abc), msangle(abc), bands(0), jitterseed(abc)); thirteen drew after a Python warning substituted a value. twoway, measured the same day over 248 probes, refuses 60 of them (yaxis(0|10|abc|1.5), bin(0|abc|2.5), bands(0|2000), degree(-1|x), n(0|abc), msangle(400), lwidth(-1), xsize(0|101), kernel(bogus) ...), and prints a note where a style name is unknown.
  - Suggested fix: One declarative table applied in Stata, following twoway's rc where it has one and refusing a word where a number-only option got one twoway would have swallowed.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_option_rules' holds the table (`option=kind[,p1[,p2[,p3]]][@type]'; kinds num, int, pos, style, istyle, enum, numlist, var, varlist, byvarlist, format), `_dash_validate_value' judges one value and `_dash_validate_opts' applies the plot rules, and the graph rules against the segment's own values, once per segment right after `_dash_fold_bw'; `_dash_plot' applies the graph rules (xsize, ysize, aspectratio, hover, by) once per graph to the after-comma winners. A refusal is one line, `dash: opt() must be ... (got: V)', r(198), or r(111) for a variable that does not exist. The bounds that are dash's own, where twoway accepts the value: yaxis/xaxis 1..2, degree 0..3, bin 1..1000, n 2..10000 and lowess bwidth (0,1], whose message carries a second sentence saying so; and colornlevels 2..256, jitter 0..100, level strictly inside 0..100, barwidth > 0, aspectratio >= 0, jitterseed/jseed >= 0 and kdensity bwidth > 0, whose message does not. mlabposition and fintensity are unbounded style kinds: a name or any number passes, and the renderer notes 13 or 150 and draws the default, as twoway does. The level() block moved into the table. test/test_option_validation.cscript (OV-nn), test/test_option_validation.py and test/test_adversarial.cscript P9-nn certify it; test/fixtures/value_truth.json records twoway's own rc per value, and test/test_option_validation.py checks each rule's kind against it (a style kind rests on a twoway note, a refusing kind on a twoway refusal).

- [x] Title: KPI fmt() failed with r(111) "not found" instead of a format error
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_kpi' (the `local kpi_display : display `fmt' `kpi_value'' at 2410; the syntax declared FMT(string) and nothing checked it)
  - Description: fmt() was interpolated into an evaluating context. Measured 22sep2026: fmt(bogus) and fmt("HACKED" %9.2f) both end in r(111), `HACKED not found': syntax strips the quotes and Stata resolves the token as an identifier. Not an injection vector, but the user who mistypes a format is told a variable is missing, while level() beside it gets a clear message.
  - Suggested fix: Validate it as a numeric Stata display format before any computation, and say so when it is not one.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Beside the update() check, both of them moved above the name(k, replace) block so nothing is destroyed before every value is proven, `_dash_kpi' passes fmt() through a character gate (a single leading %, then only letters, digits and . , : ! / + - _, at most 60 characters) and `confirm numeric format', measured to accept %9.2f %9.0gc %-12.3e %09.2f %9,2fc %12.0fc %tdCCYY-NN-DD %tcHH:MM:SS %21x %16H %tc %td %tm %9.2fc %-9.2f %10.0g %8.0e and to refuse %12s bogus 9.2f %9.2 %9.2f_n %9.2f%9.2f %c % %%9.2f. Failure is `dash kpi: fmt() must be a numeric display format such as %9.2f or %12.0fc (got: V)', r(198). `: display' stays the formatter on the proven token (strofreal(x, "bogus") returns "" in silence, so it is no validator), and its `if' is compound-quoted. Certified in test/test_option_validation.cscript and test/test_adversarial.cscript P9-nn.

- [x] Title: scatteri msize() or msymbol() with an unknown value aborted the whole render
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_scatteri' (5908-5911: bare translate_msize and translate_msymbol calls); the same call inside the emitted make_document
  - Description: Every other marker layer wrapped translate_msize and translate_msymbol in try/except and kept the scheme's default; map_scatteri called them bare, so `scatteri 20 3000, msize(abc)' or `msymbol(nonsense)' raised ValueError out of the mapper, through the layer loop, and would have ended the render with r(7102) via dash.ado's Python call (read off the bare runpy call, not run through Stata; the ValueError is pinned by test_option_validation.py); served, the failure happened in make_document. twoway draws the default with a note.
  - Suggested fix: The same warn-and-default path as every other layer, in both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. map_scatteri resolves both through `_dash_style_note' and the scheme default, so a bad value can no longer raise; a name passes the Stata table untouched, as twoway passes it, and is reported here. test/test_option_validation.py covers the abort.

- [x] Title: dropline jitter(abc) aborted the render
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_dropline' (5701-5704: `float(options.get("jitter", 0) or 0)' with no guard)
  - Description: scatter and scatteri guarded jitter(); dropline did not, so `dropline mpg weight, jitter(abc)' raised ValueError and lost the render (r(7102) saved, read off dash.ado's bare runpy call rather than run through Stata; in make_document served). jitter(-3) on dropline was clamped to 0 in silence.
  - Suggested fix: Read it through `_stat_option_float', which warns and defaults, and refuse it in Stata first.
  - Resolution: Fixed. map_dropline reads jitter through `_stat_option_float(options, "jitter", 0.0, "dropline", lo=0.0, hi=100.0)'; the Stata table's row `jitter=num,0,100' refuses jitter(abc) and jitter(-3) before Python runs, `dash: jitter() must be a number between 0 and 100 (got: abc)'. test/test_option_validation.py and test/test_option_validation.cscript.

- [x] Title: A negative jitterseed() aborted the render in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_jitter_seed' (2497-2522: int(float(raw)) with no sign check), `_apply_jitter' (2408) and `_bootstrap_ci' (2894, via `_resolve_seed'), where numpy.random.default_rng raises
  - Description: jitterseed(-1) or jseed(-1) passed through to np.random.default_rng, which raises ValueError "expected non-negative integer", uncaught in both modes, on any jittered layer and on the bootstrap bands of lpolyci and fpfitci; jitterseed(2.5) was truncated in silence. twoway accepts jitterseed(abc|-1|2.5) with rc 0 and no note.
  - Suggested fix: A non-negative integer or nothing, said aloud.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_jitter_seed' warns `dash: jitterseed(V) must be a non-negative integer; drawing unseeded.' and returns None, so default_rng is never handed a negative value; the Stata table's rows `jitterseed=int,0' and `jseed=int,0' refuse it before Python runs. test/test_option_validation.py and test/test_option_validation.cscript.

- [x] Title: A style name dash did not recognise was replaced in silence where twoway prints a note
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `OptionTranslator.parse_color_alpha', `_apply_line_opts', `_apply_marker_opts', `_apply_fill_opts', `_apply_mlabel_opts' (every `except ValueError: pass' around translate_msymbol, translate_msize, translate_lwidth, translate_lpattern and translate_mcolor), the direct translate_mcolor sites in map_dropline, map_rcapsym, map_scatteri and the pc mappers
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026 in both modes: mcolor(notacolour), msymbol(nonsense), msize(abc), lwidth(abc), lpattern(bogus), msangle(abc), fintensity(abc), mlabsize(abc) and mlabposition(99) all drew with rc 0 and no message. twoway draws the same default and says `(note: named style abc not found in class linewidth, default attributes used)' (36 of its 188 accepted probes carry that note). The help promises nothing is dropped without being named.
  - Suggested fix: One helper, one sentence, both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_style_note(option, value, what)' is module-level, copied into the served app beside `_stat_option_float', and every fallback goes through it: `dash: lwidth(abc) is not a recognised line width; using the scheme's default.', one note per option, value and kind per render in both modes (a module-level `_DASH_NOTED' set consulted by the helper and cleared by `_dash_reset_notes()' at the start of DashRenderer.render() and of the served make_document, so rcapsym's spine and dot, connected's line and marker border and every by() panel say it once where the first cut said it two or four times), naming the option; what is one of colour, marker symbol, size, line width, line pattern, angle, clock position, fill intensity. parse_color_alpha takes the option name so the colour note names mcolor, mfcolor, mlcolor, lcolor, fcolor, blcolor or bfcolor as written. The Stata table deliberately does not gate these (twoway does not either). test/test_option_validation.py asserts exactly one warning naming the option, in both modes, for each style option it covers, `connected lcolor(notacolour)' with and without by(foreign), `rcapsym mcolor(notacolour)' and `area color(notacolour)' among them.

- [x] Title: mlabel() with several names, an abbreviated by() and an unknown hover() variable were dropped or left unexpanded without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (no `confirm variable' for mlabel(), colorvar() or hover(); the `capture confirm variable' before the jsonio varlist dropped a missing by() name in silence), dash_render.py `_apply_mlabel_opts' (3530-3531: returns when the name is not a column), `_expand_by_groups' (one unfaceted panel when no by() name is a column), `_dash_build_hover' and `_collect_graph_vars' (hover() names not in the data dropped)
  - Description: twoway expands and confirms these names: mlabel(nosuchvar) and by(nosuchvar) are r(111), by(for) means by(foreign), mlabel(make foreign) is accepted. dash carried the text verbatim to the renderer: `make foreign' is no column, so the layer was drawn without labels; by(for) arrived as a name with no column and took the path measured for by(nosuchvar), one unfaceted panel with no message in both modes; hover(bogus) was split on whitespace and every unknown name dropped in silence. (A hover-only variable that is never serialised is the separate open entry below.)
  - Suggested fix: Expand and confirm them in Stata, as twoway does, and name what is refused.
  - Resolution: Fixed on the Stata side by the table's `var', `varlist' and `byvarlist' kinds: mlabel() and colorvar() must name exactly one existing variable (`dash: mlabel() must name one variable (got: make foreign)'), hover() one or more, by() one or more before its comma; each is expanded with unab and written back normalised, so by(for) reaches the renderer as foreign; a time-series expression, which twoway draws and dash cannot, is `dash: mlabel() must name a variable, not a time-series expression (got: L.mpg)', r(198); a name that does not exist is `dash: by(): variable X not found', r(111); and an empty by() head is refused. In the renderer, an mlabel() list from a spec-JSON caller uses the first name and warns once naming the extras. test/test_option_validation.cscript and test/test_option_validation.py.

- [x] Title: A time-series expression in colorvar() or mlabel() emptied every option of its layer
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_validate_value' (the var/varlist/byvarlist branch: `capture tsunab' after `capture unab' in the first cut of WP-15)
  - Description: tsunab and fvunab are sclass and begin with sreturn clear; called from inside the validator while the segment's s() was live, they wiped it. Measured 22sep2026 on tsset data: `dash (scatter mpg weight, colorvar(L.price) jitter(5) msize(large) mcolor(red)) , name(p)' registered with rc 0 and an empty options object (colorvar, jitter, msize and mcolor all gone), and `mlabel(L.mpg) jitter(5)' the same; without tsset the same command was r(111). `_return hold' does not preserve s(); `confirm variable' and `tsrevar ..., list' keep it.
  - Suggested fix: Never call an sclass ado from inside the validator; refuse a time-series expression by name, since dash cannot draw one.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The variable kinds use `capture unab' alone; a head it cannot expand but `capture tsrevar ..., list' (rclass) can is refused, `dash: colorvar() must name a variable, not a time-series expression (got: L.price)', r(198), and otherwise the first token failing `confirm variable' is `dash: colorvar(): variable X not found', r(111). Defence in depth: `_dash_validate_opts' snapshots every s() macro before each value and restores any that came back empty. Cert cases in test/test_option_validation.cscript (tsset and not, colorvar() and mlabel(), the other options of the segment asserted to survive).

- [x] Title: A refused fmt() or update() on `dash kpi ..., name(k, replace)' destroyed the old KPI and corrupted the registry
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_kpi' (the `_kpi_already'/`_dash_drop_item' replace block ran before the update() check and, in the first cut of WP-15, before the fmt() gate)
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026: register `dash kpi mean(mpg), title(t) name(k) fmt(%9.2f)', then `dash kpi mean(mpg), title(t) name(k, replace) fmt(bogus)' gives r(198) with the right message, but dash_kpi_k, dash_kpi_k_fmt and dash_json_k were already empty while dash_graph_registry still listed k; the next `dash build' died in a Python JSON traceback (illegal trailing comma), r(7102). update(bogus) did the same before WP-15. Graphs had the order right: a refused value on name(g, replace) left the old graph intact.
  - Suggested fix: Prove every value before anything is destroyed.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The update() check and the fmt() gate both run above the replace block. Cert case in test/test_option_validation.cscript: the sequence above, then the old KPI's globals asserted intact and a following `dash build' rc 0.

- [x] Title: A graph option written inside a layer escaped validation whenever a later value won
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_plot' (the graph-rule loop read only the surviving g_<opt> after the after-comma restore loop)
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026: `dash (scatter mpg weight, xsize(500)) , xsize(5) name(p)' rc 0 with xsize 5 in the JSON, where twoway is r(198); `(scatter mpg weight, by(nosuch)) , by(foreign)' rc 0 where twoway is r(111); the same for hover(nosuch) and aspectratio(-1) overridden by a later segment. The reverse orders were refused, because the bad value won.
  - Suggested fix: Validate the graph rules per segment as well as per graph.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_validate_opts' also runs the graph rules against the segment's own s(g_<opt>) and sreturns the normalised value; the per-graph pass in `_dash_plot' still validates the after-comma winners. Cert cases in test/test_option_validation.cscript (xsize and by).

- [x] Title: plotregion(lwidth(abc)) aborted the saved render and was silent when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_extract_region_lwidth' (995-998 at the WP-15 baseline: a bare translate_lwidth inside the except branch) and the served figure string's float() in a silent except
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026: `dash scatter mpg weight, plotregion(lwidth(abc)) saving(c.html)' gives ValueError "Unrecognised Stata line width: 'abc'", a RuntimeError from the figure body and r(7102); served, the same value drew with no message. twoway draws with its named-style note. Pre-existing at the baseline, and plotregion() is deliberately not gated in Stata, so it reached users.
  - Suggested fix: The note-and-default path, in both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Both readers note `dash: plotregion(lwidth(abc)) is not a recognised line width; using the default.' and draw the scheme's width. test/test_option_validation.py renders it in both modes with one note.

- [x] Title: msize(1e400) aborted the render in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `OptionTranslator.translate_msize' (`max(1, int(float(v)))' caught ValueError only) and `translate_lwidth' (returned inf in silence); dash.ado `_dash_validate_value' (the style kind took a token real() maps to missing as a name)
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026: `dash scatter mpg weight, msize(1e400) saving(c.html)' gives OverflowError "cannot convert float infinity to integer", r(7102), in the saved and the served app alike; `line ..., lwidth(1e400)' drew with line_width inf and no message. real("1e400") is missing, so the Stata table passed the token as a style name. Pre-existing at the baseline.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse in Stata what only looks like a number, and never let a non-finite number through the translators.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A style-kind token matching ^[-+.0-9eEdD]+$ whose real() is missing is refused as `must be a name or a number' (r(198)), as twoway's invalid syntax; translate_msize and translate_lwidth raise ValueError on a non-finite number, so every caller's note-and-default path fires. test/test_option_validation.py renders msize(1e400) and lwidth(1e400) in both modes with one note each; test/test_option_validation.cscript refuses msize(1e400).

- [x] Title: One bad style value was noted twice per layer and again per by() panel
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_apply_line_opts' and `_apply_marker_opts' (both read lcolor, lwidth and color on connected), `map_rcapsym' (the spine and the dot both read mcolor), and every mapper called once per by() panel
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026 in both modes: `connected mpg weight, lcolor(notacolour) by(foreign)' printed the same note four times, `connected lcolor(notacolour)' twice, `rcapsym ..., mcolor(notacolour)' twice, `scatter mcolor(notacolour) by(foreign)' twice. twoway prints its note once, and the contract asked for one warning naming the option.
  - Suggested fix: One note per option, value and kind per render.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A module-level `_DASH_NOTED' set is consulted by `_dash_style_note' and cleared by `_dash_reset_notes()' at the start of DashRenderer.render() and of the served make_document; both names are emitted into the app. test/test_option_validation.py asserts one note for connected lcolor with and without by(foreign), rcapsym mcolor and area color, in both modes.

- [x] Title: Graph-level colours, xline() styles, legend(size()) and area color() were replaced in silence
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `map_area' (color()), `map_bar' and `map_histogram' (color() beside blcolor()), the standalone figure body and the served `_resolve_color' (bgcolor(), plotbgcolor(), plotregion(color()/lcolor()), graphregion(color())), `_dash_reference_lines' (xline()/yline() lpattern() and lwidth()), `_dash_apply_legend' (size()), `_resolve_kpi_color', and the served figure string's bare float() on aspectratio(), xsize() and ysize()
  - Description: Measured 22sep2026: `area mpg weight, color(notacolour)', `bar ..., color(notacolour) blcolor(red)', `bgcolor(notacolour)', `plotbgcolor(notacolour)', `plotregion(lcolor(notacolour) color(notacolour))', `graphregion(color(notacolour))', `xline(3000, lpattern(bogus) lwidth(abc))', `legend(size(abc))' and a KPI's color(notacolour) all drew the default with no message in both modes, where twoway notes each; the reference-line lcolor warning used its own wording. The served figure read aspectratio(), xsize() and ysize() through a bare float() in a silent except where the standalone body warned, and the standalone body said the aspectratio message twice.
  - Suggested fix: Every fallback through `_dash_style_note', naming the option as written.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Each site notes the option as the user wrote it (`plotregion(lcolor)', `xline(lpattern)', `legend(size)'), the KPI colour with the `dash kpi' prefix; the served strings read aspectratio(), xsize() and ysize() through `_stat_option_float(..., "graph")' like the standalone body, which now emits one aspectratio message. test/test_option_validation.py covers each in both modes.

- [x] Title: Every dashboard drew a minor grid Stata never draws, on both axes, under every scheme
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `SchemeParser.apply_to_figure' (2365 at the WP-16 baseline, 52c473c: `grid.minor_grid_line_color = self.minor_grid_color' with no yesno guard) and the served `_dash_apply_scheme' (14617, the same line hand-copied); schemes/*.scheme (no `yesno draw_minor_hgrid' or `draw_minor_vgrid' in any of the 14 files); scripts/extract_scheme_truth.py and test/fixtures/scheme_truth.json (never extracted them)
  - Description: GAPS.md G3. Measured 22sep2026 on real Bokeh figures, all 14 schemes, both modes: minor_grid_line_color was set on both axes everywhere -- #505050 under s1color, s1mono, s2color, s2gcolor, s2mono, sj, stcolor, stcolor_alt, stgcolor, stmono1, stmono2 and stsj, #808080 under s1rcolor, #ffffff under economist -- at Bokeh's minor_grid_line_alpha 1.0 with five minor ticks per major, so the lines drew. Stata's own chains declare `draw_minor_hgrid no' and `draw_minor_vgrid no' in all 14 (scheme-s2color.scheme:2118 and :2122, restated by economist at :343 and, through its `vertical_nolinetick' axis style, `draw_minor_nlt_vgrid no' at :367); the four schemes that draw no major grid either (s1color, s1mono, s1rcolor, stmono1: `draw_major_vgrid no' at scheme-s1color.scheme:128) got a dark grid on both axes where Stata draws nothing. The translated files carried no draw_minor key, the parser had no attribute, the fixture no entry, and no test read minor_grid_line_color off a figure (test_scheme_grids.py and the parity profile look at the major grid only), which is why the fidelity suite passed with the grid on the page.
  - Suggested fix: Extract the effective per-axis minor flags beside the major ones, parse them, and colour the minor grid only where the flag is yes -- one implementation for both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `scripts/extract_scheme_truth.py' records the effective per-axis `draw_minor_hgrid'/`draw_minor_vgrid' (through the axisstyle indirection, so economist's y axis reads `draw_minor_nlt_vgrid'), the 14 shipped schemes/*.scheme carry them, `SchemeParser' parses them with a default of no and matching `_FALLBACK_PROPS' entries, and `apply_to_figure' sets minor_grid_line_color only when the axis's flag is yes and None otherwise, at the width of `linewidth minor_grid'; the served app calls that same `apply_to_figure' in place of its hand-copied body. Regression, written before the fix: test/test_scheme_rendered_output.py asserts minor_grid_line_color is None on both axes for all 14 schemes in both modes, and major None too under s1color, s1mono, s1rcolor and stmono1; test/test_scheme.py TestApplyToFigure gains test_draws_no_minor_grid_where_the_scheme_draws_none (None on both axes under s2color); test/test_scheme_fidelity.py checks the new yesno keys parsed against the fixture for all 14.

- [x] Title: Scheme text sizes and colours, legend placement and marker colours were parsed and never drawn
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `SchemeParser._init_properties' (title_color 2145, p_mark_colors 2160, heading_font_size 2224, note_font_size 2227, legend_position and legend_cols 2266-2268 at the WP-16 baseline), `apply_to_figure' (2337-2370: axis font sizes only), `_dash_apply_legend' (9027-9055: chrome and the user's keyword placement only), `_DASH_TEXTBOX_DEFAULTS' and `_dash_text_annotations' (10018-10045: literal 13, 11 and 12px), `default_color' (2274-2292: never indexes p_mark_colors); the served `_SCHEME_CHROME' (14555-14574) exported legend_position and legend_cols that nothing read
  - Description: GAPS.md G4. Measured 22sep2026, all 14 schemes, both modes: fig.title was Bokeh's #444444 at 13px everywhere (s1rcolor asks for white on its black plot, s2color for dknavy #1e2d53 at 14pt); the subtitle, note and caption Labels were 13px #444444, 11px #555555 and 12px #555555 dash literals everywhere (Stata's note textbox is `gsize small_body', 8pt, in `color small_body'; s1rcolor's are white); axis titles and tick labels were Bokeh's #444444 everywhere, grey on black under s1rcolor; the legend sat top_right, vertical, ncols auto everywhere while the schemes say 6 o'clock in 2 columns (s2color and eight others), 3 o'clock in 1 (stcolor, stgcolor, stmono1, stmono2) or 12 o'clock in 3 (economist); p_mark_colors was never read, invisible only because no shipped scheme declares a `color p#mark' different from `p#'. Two G4 claims were stale at this baseline: the served legend already went through the shared `_dash_apply_legend', so the `lg.location = 6' ValueError no longer existed (the attributes were simply unread in both modes), and the served app already built a real SchemeParser from `_SCHEME_PROPS', so glyph colours, symbols, patterns, alphas and marker sizes were already in parity. dash's `note_font_size' also read the wrong key: every scheme has `gsize note', but Stata's note textbox uses `gsize small_body'.
  - Suggested fix: Consume each directive where Stata consumes it -- the textbox styles heading, subheading, body, small_body, key_label, axis_title and tick_label -- and the legend's clock position and column count as the default placement an explicit legend() still beats.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `apply_to_figure' sets the figure title's colour and size from `color heading' and `gsize heading' and the axis-title and tick-label colours from `color axis_title' and `color tick_label' (an explicit title(, size() color()) runs after and wins); `_dash_text_annotations' takes the scheme, so subtitle() draws at subheading size and colour, note() at small_body size and colour, caption() at body size and colour, with `_DASH_TEXTBOX_DEFAULTS' kept for spec-JSON callers that pass no scheme; one module-level `_dash_scheme_chrome(scheme)' builds the legend chrome for both modes, label size and colour from key_label, and `_dash_apply_legend' places the legend from `clockdir legend_position', `numstyle legend_cols' and `legend_rows' only when the user gave no position(), ring() or keyword (6 below and horizontal, 12 above, 3 right, 9 left; cols() and rows() still win); `default_color' reads p_mark_colors for the marker plot types; the served `_dash_apply_scheme' is `_DASH_SCHEME.apply_to_figure' and the unread served constants are deleted. `note_font_size' stays parsed for fidelity and is read for nothing, because Stata reads nothing from it either. Regression: test/test_scheme_rendered_output.py reads title, Label, axis and legend values off real figures for all 14 schemes in both modes and checks legend(position()) still wins; test/test_mode_parity.py's profile now records the minor grid, axis text colours, ncols, nrows and the label colour.

- [x] Title: A numeric gsize, linewidth or symbolsize was converted on an invented scale
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `SchemeParser._gsize' (2024-2033 at the WP-16 baseline: `max(6, int(val * 1.2))'), `_lwidth' (2083-2093: a bare number taken as pixels at 2088), `_GSIZE_PT' (1762-1779: minuscule 2pt, vhuge 24pt), `_markersize_px' (2294-2319: symbolsize keywords scaled through the text ladder)
  - Description: GAPS.md G5. Stata's relative unit is fixed: 100 is the graph's smaller dimension, so on the four-inch reference graph one unit is 2.88pt (`gsize-medium.style' is 3.8194 and its own comment says 11pt; `gsize-vhuge.style' says 28pt, `gsize-minuscule.style' 1pt). Measured 22sep2026: economist's `gsize heading 6.0' became 7pt and its dashboard title drew smaller than its 11pt subtitle in both modes, where Stata means 17.28pt (1.57 times the subtitle at any graph size); economist's `linewidth p 1.2', four times medium, drew at 1.2px where every other scheme's medium drew at 1.5px, so the scheme with the heaviest lines had the thinnest; `minuscule' was 2pt and `vhuge' 24pt; symbolsize keywords went through the gsize ladder, so `large' markers were 10.18px (1.27 times medium) where Stata's symbolsize-large is 7pt against medium's 4.4pt (1.59), and stcolor's `small' 5.82px where the symbolsize ratio gives 5.45px.
  - Suggested fix: One ladder per style class, from the style files, and the numeric branches on the same reference.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_gsize' numeric is `val * 2.88' pt with no floor and no truncation (6.0 gives 17.28pt, 0.1 gives 0.288pt); the two table entries are 1pt and 28pt; `_lwidth' numeric is `val * 5.0' px, so medium .30 is 1.5px as before and economist's 1.2 is 6.0px; a `_SYMBOLSIZE_PT' ladder (zero 0, vtiny 0.29, tiny 1.0, vsmall 1.5, small 3.0, medsmall 3.6, medium 4.4, medlarge 5.6, large 7.0, vlarge 9.0, huge 14.0, vhuge 16.0, ehuge 32.0) anchored at medium 4.4pt = 8px gives stcolor's small 5.45px and economist's large 12.73px; test/fixtures/scheme_truth.json records the three ladders and the formula under _meta.units. Not changed: the linewidth keyword ladder `_LWIDTH_MAP', whose ratios are not Stata's (a follow-up, recorded in ROADMAP WP-16). Regression: test/test_scheme_rendered_output.py (economist's 17.28pt title larger than its 11pt subtitle, its 6.0 line wider than s2color's 1.5, marker sizes per scheme, the unit functions); test/test_scheme_coercion.py's numeric pins restated.

- [x] Title: KPI cards were #f8f9fa on #dee2e6 whatever the scheme, and xlabel(, grid) drew Bokeh's grey
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._make_kpi_div' (12362-12369 at the WP-16 baseline: inline `background:#f8f9fa; ... border:1px solid #dee2e6'), the served `_dash_kpi_card' (14910-14917, the same string), the JS card rebuild in `_FILTER_JS' (8163-8168), `css_block' (2398-2399: a `.db-kpi-card' rule attached to no card Div in either mode), `_dash_apply_axis_spec' (9379, inside the helper `_dash_apply_axis_options' calls: `g.grid_line_color = "#e5e5e5"')
  - Description: GAPS.md G6. Measured 22sep2026, all 14 schemes, both modes: every card carried the inline background and border, a light card on s1rcolor's black page and #f8f9fa where economist's legend box is #c6d3df. `css_block()' emitted the right rule, but the inline shorthand wins and, because a Bokeh 3 Div renders in a shadow root, neither the page's <style> nor the header Divs' InlineStyleSheet reached a card Div (0 of 1 card Divs carried a stylesheet); the card was also the one place the two modes disagreed, and only in whitespace. `xlabel(, grid)' on the ten schemes whose x grid is off coloured it #e5e5e5, Bokeh's default, instead of the scheme's major_grid colour (#e0e0e0 s1color, #ffffff economist, #505050 s1rcolor, #eaf2f3 s2color, #e8e8e8 s2mono); the four with it on kept the scheme colour only because the None guard did not fire.
  - Suggested fix: One card builder for both modes and the JS rebuild, background and border from the class rule, the class rule delivered to the Div, an explicit bgcolor() still inline; the grid branch given the scheme's colour.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_kpi_card_html(title, value, subtitle, color, bgcolor, name, scheme)' is module-level, used by `_make_kpi_div' and the served `_dash_kpi_card' and mirrored by the JS rebuild: the div keeps class db-kpi-card and its layout-only inline properties, carries no inline background or border unless bgcolor() was given (then `background:<explicit>' is inline and wins, as an explicit option should), the value colour is color() if given and otherwise the scheme's `color text', and the title and subtitle are `color text' plain; each card Div in both modes gets `stylesheets=[InlineStyleSheet(scheme.css_block())]', so `.db-kpi-card { background-color: <color legend>; border: 1px solid <color legend_line>; color: <color text>; }' applies (economist #c6d3df on #c6d3df, s1rcolor black with white text). `_dash_apply_axis_options' takes the scheme's grid colour, so `xlabel(, grid)' under s1color draws #e0e0e0. Regression: test/test_scheme_rendered_output.py (no inline background unless bgcolor() is given, the stylesheet text, economist's #c6d3df, an explicit bgcolor(#ecf0f1) still inline, the xlabel(, grid) colour); the inline-background and #212529 value-colour pins in test/test_kpi.py, test/test_kpi_security.py and test/test_option_validation.py restated to the class rule and the scheme's `color text' (the seven pins in test_kpi_security.py had been missed and were 7 failed, 25 passed against the tree until the review caught them).

- [x] Title: layout(bs) with a KPI or a filter widget was a blank page
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_render_bootstrap_html' (12967 in the WP-16 tree: `_head_m = re.search(r"(?s)<head>(.*?)</head>", _dummy_html)'; the same line at 52c473c)
  - Description: Pre-existing, S1, found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026); not a WP-16 regression. The Bootstrap page took Bokeh's script and style tags by scraping the head of a `file_html' dummy with a non-greedy regex, and Bokeh 3.9.0's inlined bokeh-widgets.min.js (its DOMPurify) contains the literal string `<head></head>' 189,588 characters into the 378,283-character bundle. So every layout(bs) page whose document needs the widgets bundle -- a KPI Div, a filter widget -- had the widgets script cut there and never closed, with dash's `</head><body><script>' landing inside the JavaScript. Measured on the WP-16 tree and on `git show HEAD:dash_render.py' alike: 3 `<script' opens against 2 `</script>' closes, no `/* END bokeh-widgets.min.js */' marker, the first divergence from the on-disk bundle at character 189588 exactly at `<head>', `Uncaught SyntaxError: Invalid or unexpected token' in the browser and a white page. The same bs spec without a KPI rendered. The checked-in sample pages (test/bs_responsive.html and its siblings: 4 opens, 4 closes, END marker present) were made with a bundle that lacked the string, which is why no test noticed. WP-16's claim that the card's InlineStyleSheet reaches the bs page held only at the JSON level (`.db-kpi-card { background-color: #c6d3df' is in the embedded models).
  - Suggested fix: Stop regex-scraping file_html: take the head up to the LAST `</head>', or build it from `INLINE.render_js()' and `render_css()' directly.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_render_bootstrap_html' takes the head up to `_dummy_html.rfind("</head>")'. Regression, written first: test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py renders layout(bs) with a KPI and asserts `html.count("<script") == html.count("</script>")' and that `/* END bokeh-widgets.min.js */' is in the page.

- [x] Title: A scheme-placed legend sat outermost on its side and right-aligned, and legend(rows()) inherited the scheme's columns
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_apply_legend' (9316-9337 in the WP-16 tree: the scheme placement called `_dash_move_legend', i.e. `fig.add_layout(legend, side)', left `legend.location' at Bokeh's `top_right', and applied the scheme's `ncols' unconditionally before the user's cols()/rows() at 9347-9357; the explicit position() path at 9304-9308 likewise), the standalone call order (`_dash_text_annotations' at 12367 before `_rebuild_legend' at 12528) and the served template's (16156 before 16310)
  - Description: Found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026), both modes, identical in each, so the parity guard was green. Order: with the scheme's placement the legend was the outermost item on its side -- under s2color and the nine other 6 o'clock schemes `fig.below = [LinearAxis, Label(note), Label(caption), Legend]', so the note and caption sat between the plot and the legend; under economist (12 o'clock) `fig.above = [Label(subtitle), Legend]', which Bokeh reverses, so the legend sat between the title and the subtitle. Stata's rings are the other way round: scheme-s2color.scheme:1946-1951 `legend_ring 3', `note_ring 4', `caption_ring 5', `subtitle_ring 6', `title_ring 7', the legend nearest the plot. Pre-existing for an explicit legend(position(6)) or (12); WP-16 made it the default look of eleven schemes. Alignment: a legend in the below or above panel is positioned by its `location', and Bokeh's default `top_right' put it flush right under the plot (`left: calc(100% - margin)'), where Stata centres a 6 or 12 o'clock legend. Rows: `legend(rows(1))' with three series under s2color drew a two-by-two grid, because the scheme's `ncols' 2 stayed set beside the user's `nrows' 1 and Bokeh wraps at ncols -- HEAD gave `('center', 'vertical', 'auto', 1, 3)', the tree `('below', 'horizontal', 2, 1, 3)' -- and eight series with rows(2) kept ncols 2 where HEAD had auto; twoway treats rows() and cols() as alternatives. test_scheme_rendered_output.py asserted only the side.
  - Suggested fix: Insert the legend right after the axis of the panel it is placed into, before every Label, centre it there, and let a user rows() or cols() alone reset the other dimension to auto.
  - Resolution: Fixed. When the scheme or a user position() puts the legend into `fig.below' or `fig.above', `_dash_apply_legend' inserts it after that panel's axis models and before everything else (index 0 would put it between the frame and the tick labels) instead of `add_layout' and sets `legend.location = "center"'; the keyword paths (legend(top) is top_center and so on) are unchanged; a user rows() alone sets `ncols = "auto"' and cols() alone `nrows = "auto"', so the scheme's columns never survive a user rows(). Regression, written first, in test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py: the Legend's index in the panel below every Label's, both sides, both modes; `location == "center"' for the s2color default and for legend(position(6)); three series with legend(rows(1)) under s2color give `ncols "auto"' and `nrows 1'. test/test_mode_parity.py's corpus gains "legend(rows(1)) with three series under s2color".

- [x] Title: color p#mark coloured the spike, rcap, rspike, pcspike and pcarrow segments and the line halves of dropline and rcapsym
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `default_color' and `_MARKER_PLOT_TYPES' (2405-2423 in the WP-16 tree, keyed on the "scatter" alias) and the mappers that asked for "scatter" for a Segment glyph: `map_spike' (4325), `map_rspike' (5731), `map_rcap' (5796), `map_dropline' (5999), `map_rcapsym' (6083), `map_pcspike' (6276), `map_pcarrow' (6482)
  - Description: Found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). TR-16.5 made `default_color' read `p_mark_colors' when asked for "scatter", and those seven mappers ask for "scatter" for their Segments, so the rcap, rspike, spike, pcspike and pcarrow lines and the line halves of dropline and rcapsym took the marker colour. Stata keeps `color p#mark' apart from `p#line', `p#other' and `p#otherline' (scheme-s2color.scheme:458-473) and its pstyle help calls these "other" plots; the WP-16 design promised that only the marker types change. Invisible under the 14 shipped schemes, where p#mark equals p#; measured with a user scheme (`color p1 #111111', `color p1mark #22aa22', `color p1line #3333ff'), a HEAD copy against the tree: the rcap, rspike, spike, pcspike and pcarrow Segments went from #111111 to #22aa22, dropline's Segment and Scatter both to #22aa22, rcapsym's three Segments to #22aa22; the scatter, scatteri, dot, rscatter and pcscatter markers to #22aa22 as intended; line, rline, area and bar unchanged.
  - Suggested fix: Gate the p_mark_colors lookup on the glyph, not on the "scatter" alias.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `color p#mark' is read for marker glyphs only; the spike, rcap, rspike, pcspike and pcarrow Segments, the line half of dropline and the segments of rcapsym ask `default_color' with their own type name, which falls to `p_colors'. Regression, written first, in test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py with a temporary user .scheme file the test writes (`color p1 #111111', `color p1mark #22aa22'): the scatter, scatteri, dot, rscatter and pcscatter markers #22aa22; the spike, rcap, rspike, pcspike and pcarrow segments and the dropline and rcapsym segments #111111; the dropline and rcapsym markers #22aa22; both modes.

- [x] Title: A tiny or huge numeric gsize in a user scheme aborted the whole figure
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `SchemeParser._gsize' (2098 in the WP-16 tree: the numeric branch `f"{val * 2.88:g}pt"')
  - Description: Found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). `%g' switches to scientific notation below 1e-4 and at or above 1e16, and Bokeh's FontSize validator (CSS_LENGTH_RE) rejects `2.88e-05pt', so a user scheme with `gsize body 0.00001' (or an absurd 1e20) failed the render with `RuntimeError: failed to build figure 'g1': ... '2.88e-05pt' is not a valid font size value', where 52c473c floored to 6pt and drew. Measured: 0.00001 gave `2.88e-05pt', rejected; 0.00003 `8.64e-05pt', rejected; 0.0001 `0.000288pt', accepted; 1e20 and 123456789 rejected; every size in the 14 shipped schemes validates.
  - Suggested fix: Format with fixed decimals and validate the point size, falling back to the default with the existing warning.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_gsize' numeric formats as fixed decimals, three places, trailing zeros dropped (3.8194 x 2.88 = 10.999872 is "11pt"), and a point size outside (0, 1000] falls back to the default with the `dash scheme:' warning shape.
