Regression, written first, in test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py: 0.00001 gives the default and a warning; 6.0 gives "17.28pt"; 3.8194 gives "11pt" (11.0 formats as "11pt").

- [x] Title: linewidth axisline was carried and never drawn, and a hostile clockdir, legend_cols, legend_rows or linewidth in a user scheme passed in silence
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_FALLBACK_PROPS' (1709-1712 in the WP-16 tree: `linewidth axisline' and `color foreground' keys with no parser attribute), `SchemeParser._init_properties' (no range check on `clockdir legend_position', `numstyle legend_cols'/`legend_rows' or any linewidth), `_dash_apply_legend' (9316-9322 and 9329-9337: any clock value through `_DASH_CLOCK_SIDE.get(clock, "right")', any cols/rows applied); scripts/extract_scheme_truth.py, schemes/*.scheme and test/fixtures/scheme_truth.json (`axis_line_width', `foreground_color' extracted for nothing)
  - Description: Found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). Two directives WP-16 added to the extractor, the fixture, all 14 scheme files and the fallback dict had no SchemeParser attribute and no consumer: `linewidth axisline' never reached `axis.axis_line_width' (Bokeh drew its 1px default) and `color foreground' was read by nothing, while every other new attribute had a reader in apply_to_figure, _dash_text_annotations, _dash_scheme_chrome or css_block. And a user scheme's hostile placement values passed without a word where a bad colour warned: measured on a hostile .scheme, `clockdir legend_position 99' parsed and became "right", `numstyle legend_cols -2' and `legend_rows 1e3' parsed (a thousand rows), `linewidth major_grid -4' became width 0.0, an invisible grid; the three colour injections in the same file were refused with the `dash scheme:' warning and css_block held only hex, so no CSS injection was possible.
  - Suggested fix: Parse and apply the axis line width in both modes; range-check the clock, the counts and every width with the same warning shape as `_color'.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `axis_line_width = _lwidth("linewidth axisline", thin)' is parsed and `apply_to_figure' sets each axis's `axis_line_width' from it; `color foreground' stays a carried directive with no attribute, deliberately -- the second stated exception beside `note_font_size', because no Bokeh property answers to it. `_init_properties' range-checks `clockdir legend_position' (1 to 12), `numstyle legend_cols'/`legend_rows' (at least 0) and every linewidth (at least 0), each refused with the `_color'-style warning and the default used. Regression, written first, in test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py: economist's and s2color's `axis_line_width' equal the fixture's `linewidth axisline' through `_lwidth' in both modes; a hostile .scheme with legend_position 99, legend_rows 1000 and `linewidth major_grid -4' draws three warnings and the defaults. test/test_mode_parity.py's `_chrome' profile records `axis_line_width'.

- [x] Title: The JS-rebuilt KPI card lost the value div's id after a filter move
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_FILTER_JS' `dash_apply_kpis' (8383-8392 in the WP-16 tree; the same omission at 52c473c) against `_dash_kpi_card_html' (7963: `<div id="kpi_val_{_n}" ...')
  - Description: Pre-existing, cosmetic, found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). After a filter move the card the JavaScript rebuilt was byte-identical to `_dash_kpi_card_html''s output -- class, the inline style with or without `background:', both colours, both modes' defaults, measured over 18 scheme-by-option combinations including bgcolor(navy) to #1a476f, gs10 and a refused colour -- except that its value div carried no `id="kpi_val_<name>"'. Live in the browser (s1rcolor, the Select moved to 14): before, `id="kpi_val_k1"'; after, none; everything else equal. The served app rebuilds through the Python builder at its four call sites, so it kept the id. Nothing in dash_render.py or dash.ado reads the ids, only tests do, but the WP-16 design said the rebuild must produce the same markup.
  - Suggested fix: Carry an escaped name in the JS config and emit the id in the template.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `kpi_config' carries the escaped name and the JS template emits `id="kpi_val_<name>"', so the rebuilt markup equals `_dash_kpi_card_html''s. Regression, written first, in test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py: the initial card text diffed against a JS-template rendering with the same config.

- [x] Title: A served by() panel title was HTML-escaped; the saved one was not
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, the served template's by() panel title (16376 and 16400 in the WP-16 tree: `html.escape(go["title"])') against the saved path (13587: the parsed title text `_dash_figure_title' puts on `fig.title.text')
  - Description: Pre-existing, not a WP-16 regression, found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). In the served app a by() panel's title was built from the raw option text passed through `html.escape', so `title("By") by(foreign)' gave the Bokeh Titles `&quot;By&quot; -- Domestic' and `&quot;By&quot; -- Foreign' where the saved page's were `By -- Domestic' and `By -- Foreign'; a Bokeh Title is plain text, so the served page showed the literal `&quot;'. Measured with `dash (scatter mpg weight)(connected mpg weight, sort), name(b1) title("By") by(foreign)' built under scheme(economist) served and saved, and identically against a `git archive HEAD' copy of the package. Non-by() titles were clean in both modes. The parity guard missed it because no by() case in its corpus carried a title.
  - Suggested fix: Use the parsed title text in the served template; a Bokeh Title needs no HTML escaping.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The served by() panel title uses the parsed title text -- what `_dash_figure_title' puts on `fig.title.text' -- not `html.escape(go["title"])'. Regression, written first: test/test_scheme_review_fixes.py asserts both modes' panel titles are equal for `title("By") by(foreign)', and test/test_mode_parity.py's corpus gains "by() with a graph title".

- [x] Title: A two-variable plot type given one variable registered clean and died at build with a Python KeyError
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (the varpart parser that serves the two-variable plot types: no variable count) and dash_render.py `map_bar' (4188 in the WP-16 tree: `_bar_cols = {xvar: source.data[xvar], ...}' with `xvar' empty)
  - Description: Pre-existing, not a WP-16 regression, found by the adversarial review of WP-16 (22sep2026). Measured with the console binary before the fix: `dash bar price, name(a1)', `dash scatter mpg, name(a2)', `dash line price if foreign', `dash lfit price', `dash dot price' and `dash spike price in 1/10' each printed `dash: graph ... registered.' and returned 0, and a `dash build' then died with a traceback ending `File dash_render.py, line 4188, in map_bar: ... KeyError: '''' and `RuntimeError: dash_render: failed to build figure 'bar1': ''', r(7102); the same against a HEAD copy of the package. twoway refuses at parse time: `twoway bar price' and `twoway scatter mpg' return r(102), and `twoway bar' with no variable says `varlist required', r(100), measured with the console binary. With valid twoway syntax (`dash bar price mpg') the same dashboard built clean under economist and s1rcolor in both modes.
  - Suggested fix: Mirror twoway: refuse at registration with twoway's own rc, naming the type, in the varpart parser that serves the two-variable types; leave the one-variable types (histogram, kdensity, tsline, function and the rest) untouched.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A two-variable plot type given one variable is refused at registration with `dash: <type> requires a y and an x variable (got: <varlist>)' and twoway's own rc, r(102); histogram, kdensity, tsline, function and the other one-variable types are untouched. Regression, written first: test/test_option_validation.cscript OV-135 to OV-141 through a new `_ov_refused' helper (rc, the message naming the type and the variables given, not the ignoring note, nothing registered): `dash bar price' r(102) naming bar, `dash bar price mpg' 0, `dash scatter mpg' r(102) naming scatter, `(scatter mpg weight) (bar price)' r(102) naming bar (one short segment refuses the whole command), `line price if foreign' r(102) naming line (an if-clause is not a variable), `histogram mpg' and `kdensity mpg' 0.

- [x] Title: A misspelt scheme() or theme() drew s2color with rc 0 and a raw Python warning, on every entry point
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 0112221: `_dash_prepare_spec' (2969; layout(), link() and filterposition() are validated before the master spec is written at 3369, while scheme() and theme() are only escaped at 3357-3365 and passed through), `_dash_parse_graph_opts' (539; a graph-level scheme() is one of the keys written verbatim at 1669), `_dash_validate_value' (1257; no `scheme' kind, and theme() under no kind at all). dash_render.py at 0112221: `_resolve_scheme_name' (8941-9031; the unknown-name branch 9018-9029 warns `is not a known theme or scheme; using s2color' and blanks the name), `resolve_or_fallback' (1736; a second warning, `scheme 'X' was not found for graph ...; using s2color', from the per-graph swap at 12317-12325)
  - Description: GAPS.md G8, and WP-05's AC-05.3 ("scheme(nosuchscheme) fails loudly in both modes"), which that package left to this one. No entry point validated the name; every policy was Python's, and Python's was warn-and-fall-back. Measured 22sep2026 with the console binary (scratchpad wp17/probe/probe.log and probe2.log): `dash scatter mpg weight, scheme(nosuchthing) saving(a3.html, replace)' returned rc 0, wrote a3.html in s2color (`#1e2d53' heading, `#eaf2f3' background) and printed two raw UserWarning blocks, each naming dash_render.py and a line number -- one from `_resolve_scheme_name' for the page and one from the per-graph swap for the figure; `dash build, saving(B3.html) scheme(nosuchthing) replace' rc 0, one warning, s2color; `theme(nosuchtheme)' the same; `scheme(d3colors)', one of the 129 third-party `scheme-*.scheme' files in PLUS/s on this machine, which dash deliberately does not read, the same. twoway's own rc for `scheme(nosuchthing)' is 0 with `(note: scheme nosuchthing not found, using s2color)', and `set scheme nosuchthing' is r(111) `scheme nosuchthing not found' (measured the same day); dash had copied the first where a dashboard wants the second, because a dashboard is a saved file the user looks at later, so a wrong scheme behind a warning that scrolled past is a wrong deliverable.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse an explicitly requested name in Stata, before any Python runs, in WP-15's message shape and on every entry point (the immediate render, a registered graph, a per-segment scheme(), `dash build' and `dash serve'); keep the fallback for the inherited c(scheme) only.
  - Resolution: Fixed (WP-17). `_dash_validate_value' gains the kind `scheme': accepted when the lower-cased value is one of the twenty shipped names or the six theme aliases handed back by the one list in `_dash_shipped_schemes', else when it carries no `/', `\' or `..' and `confirm file' finds it, as written or with `.scheme' appended, in the working directory; otherwise `dash: scheme() must be a shipped scheme, a theme, or a .scheme file in the working directory (got: V)', r(198). A shipped name given with the extension, `scheme(s2color.scheme)', is accepted on every entry point without a file in the working directory, so the extension form the help documents for a file of your own works for a shipped name too. The graph rule token `scheme=scheme' puts the immediate route, a registered graph and a per-segment scheme() through the existing validation loops, and `_dash_prepare_spec' validates scheme() with the same kind and theme() as an `enum' of the six themes beside its layout() and filterposition() checks, for `dash build' and `dash serve' alike, before anything is written. The Python gate stays as the second line of defence for a spec built without dash.ado. Certified, written before the fix, by test/test_custom_scheme.cscript CS-06 (scheme(nosuchthing) on the immediate route: r(198) naming scheme()), CS-07 (on `dash build'), CS-08 (theme(nosuchtheme): r(198) naming theme()), CS-09 (scheme(../mytheme): r(198)) and CS-13 (the `dash serve' spec path: r(198)); test/test_scheme_resolution.py pins the Stata list against `_BUILTIN_NAMES' and the extractor's `SCHEMES'.

- [x] Title: A custom scheme named without its extension styled the figure and not the page on `dash scatter', and nothing on `dash build'
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 0112221: `_resolve_scheme_name' (9018-9029: a name that is neither a theme alias nor one of `_BUILTIN_NAMES' is rejected unless it ends in `.scheme', before `SchemeParser._resolve_path' can run), `_resolve_path' (1910; its bare-name branch at 1930-1931 tries `<cwd>/<name>.scheme' first and was added for exactly this route), `_make_figure' (12317-12325: the per-graph swap calls `resolve_or_fallback' directly, bypassing the gate, and does find the file)
  - Description: GAPS.md F4's bare-name row, ROADMAP AC-17.1. dash.sthlp said: put its .scheme file in the working directory and give its base name without the extension. Measured 22sep2026 with the console binary and a `mytheme.scheme' in the working directory that is a copy of s1rcolor (black background, white heading): `dash scatter mpg weight, scheme(mytheme) saving(a1.html, replace)' rc 0, the warning `'mytheme' is not a known theme or scheme; using s2color', and a1.html carrying the FIGURE in mytheme (`background_fill_color":"#000000' twice) under a page whose CSS is s2color's (`.db-title { color: #1e2d53; ... }', `#eaf2f3'): half applied, the figure black and the header navy on a light page. `scheme(mytheme.scheme)' gave a2.html with `.db-title { color: #ffffff; ... }' and no s2color colour anywhere. On `dash build, saving(B1.html) scheme(mytheme) replace' the page and figure were both s2color with the same warning (B1.html: `#1e2d53' twice, `#000000' nowhere); `scheme(mytheme.scheme)' worked (B2.html); a graph registered with `scheme(mytheme)' and built with no scheme was the half-applied page again (D4.html), and with `scheme(mytheme.scheme)' the whole page (D5.html). So one documented name behaved three ways on three entry points, and the working case was the one the help did not describe.
  - Suggested fix: Before warning, ask the resolver whether the name resolves; let the Stata gate accept a bare name it can `confirm file' in the working directory.
  - Resolution: Fixed (WP-17). `SchemeParser.can_resolve(name)' answers whether `_resolve_path' would succeed, without a warning or an exception (a name that fails containment is False), and the unknown-name branch of `_resolve_scheme_name' asks it first, so a bare working-directory name, a `.scheme' file name and a shipped name all pass untouched and only a name that resolves to nothing reaches the warning. On the Stata side the `scheme' kind accepts the bare name when `<name>.scheme' is in the working directory and passes it through unchanged. The page chrome and the figure now come from the same file in both modes. Regression, written before the fix: test/test_scheme_resolution.py in a temporary working directory holding `mytheme.scheme' (s1rcolor with `color background #123456'): build-level scheme(mytheme) and scheme(mytheme.scheme) and graph-level scheme(mytheme), in both modes, give a figure whose border_fill_color is #123456 and a `css_block()' carrying #123456, with no warning; test/test_custom_scheme.cscript CS-01 and CS-02 (the immediate route, bare and with the extension: rc 0, no warning, #123456 in the page), CS-03 and CS-04 (`dash build'), CS-05 (a graph-level scheme(mytheme) then a build with no scheme: #123456 present and s2color's #1e2d53 absent).

- [x] Title: Every unschemed build under the certification runner warned that s2color8 was not a known scheme
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 0112221: `SchemeParser._BUILTIN_NAMES' (1624-1631: fourteen names, s2color8 absent), `_resolve_scheme_name' (9018-9029: the session scheme takes the same unknown-name branch as an explicit one, with the same words); dash.ado at 0112221 (2646-2648 and 3356-3369: `c(scheme)' forwarded as `session_scheme'); cscript.ado:35 (`set scheme `c(scheme_cscript)'', which is s2color8); test/test_render_log_clean.cscript (asserts on `__builtins__', `DashRenderer', `GlyphMapper' and a line count, so a UserWarning line passed)
  - Description: Recorded by the WP-16 review as a follow-up. `cscript' sets the session scheme to s2color8 for every certification script, dash forwards c(scheme) when no scheme() or theme() was given, and s2color8 was not among the fourteen translations, so the renderer warned `dash: 's2color8' is not a known theme or scheme; using s2color' and drew s2color -- close to s2color8, which is `#include s2color' with the "8" colour variants substituted (`ltbluishgray8' 225 230 240 for the background and grid against `ltbluishgray' 234 242 243, `navy8' 39 63 111 for p1 against `navy' 26 71 111, `bluishgray8' for the boxes and p15), but not it. Measured 22sep2026: the runner's log of the WP-16 verification run (test/run_all_cscripts.log, 41 passed) carries the line 49 times across 13 of the 41 scripts -- 15 in test_varlist_expansion, 8 in test_examples_run, 6 in test_e2e_serve, 4 each in test_adversarial and test_e2e_single, 3 in test_filter_label, 2 each in test_e2e_kpi and test_e2e_multi, 1 each in test_e2e_filters, test_option_surface, test_render_log_clean, test_serve_options and test_server_build; `set scheme s2color8' then `dash scatter mpg weight, saving(d1.html, replace)' or a `dash build' with no scheme reproduces it outside the runner (probe.log D1, probe2.log D2), and `set scheme d3colors', a third-party scheme, gives the same line with that name; `twoway ..., scheme(s2color8)' draws it, rc 0. The words did not say where the name came from, so a user who typed no scheme() read a message about a name they never typed, with no way to tell it from a typo of their own; and the one certification script whose job is a clean render log tolerated it.
  - Suggested fix: Ship s2color8 with the other five user-selectable StataCorp schemes dash lacked, and make the session-scheme warning say the name was inherited and why that can happen.
  - Resolution: Fixed (WP-17). s2color8 is one of the six new translations (s2manual, s1manual, s2gmanual, s2color8, sttrans, stgcolor_alt; `_BUILTIN_NAMES' and `_dash_shipped_schemes' are twenty), so an unschemed build under cscript draws s2color8 and warns nothing. A session scheme dash cannot translate (129 third-party files in PLUS/s on this machine) is still one warning and s2color, never an error, and the warning now says why: `dash: 'X' is not a known theme or scheme; it was inherited from the session (c(scheme)) because no scheme() or theme() was given, or came from a spec built without dash.ado; using s2color. Themes: ... Schemes: ...', one line. Certified by test/test_custom_scheme.cscript CS-10 (`set scheme zzdashtest', a file in the working directory Stata finds and dash does not translate, then a build with no scheme: rc 0, exactly one UserWarning line, mentioning the session, the page in s2color), CS-11 (`set scheme s2color8' then a build: rc 0 and no warning) and CS-12 (every one of the twenty names on `dash build, scheme()': rc 0, no warning); test/test_scheme_resolution.py (a spec with scheme(nosuchthing): exactly one warning carrying the inherited-session sentence; each of the twenty resolves with no warning in both modes). test/test_render_log_clean.cscript, which tolerated the line, now asserts that no UserWarning line appears in the render log, closing the WP-16 review's second follow-up.

- [x] Title: theme() took any string, so a mistyped or hostile value reached Python and drew s2color with rc 0
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 0112221: `_dash_prepare_spec' (3357-3369: theme() escaped through `_dash_json_str' and written into the master spec, checked against nothing); dash_render.py at 0112221: `_THEME_SCHEME_MAP' (8926-8938, the whole of theme()'s meaning: default, clean, mono, dark, economist, sj), `_resolve_scheme_name' (9018-9029); test/test_theme_escaping.cscript (THM-01..06 asserted rc 0 for the hostile values, by design at the time)
  - Description: WP-05 closed theme()'s escaping hole -- `theme("a\q")', `theme("C:\temp\x")', `theme("a\")', `theme(`"a"b"')', a JSON-shaped theme() and `theme("a\b")' had reached the master spec raw, for r(7102) and a Python traceback or a silently mangled value -- by escaping the value like every other spec string, and test_theme_escaping.cscript pinned that with rc 0 as the assertion, its header saying "theme() takes an arbitrary string". It does not: theme() is an alias table of six names, and every other value went to Python only to be warned about and replaced by s2color. Measured 22sep2026 with the console binary: `dash build, saving(B4.html) theme(nosuchtheme) replace' rc 0, `dash: 'nosuchtheme' is not a known theme or scheme; using s2color', B4.html in s2color (probe2.log). The Windows path THM-02 exists for, the accident most likely to happen, therefore produced a dashboard in a scheme the user did not ask for instead of a refusal, and the JSON-shaped value of THM-05 was escaped and then handed to a resolver that had to be trusted to do nothing with it.
  - Suggested fix: Validate theme() in `_dash_prepare_spec' as an `enum' of the six names, beside layout() and filterposition(), before the spec is written; keep the escaping for the values that pass.
  - Resolution: Fixed (WP-17). `_dash_prepare_spec' validates theme() as an `enum' of clean, dark, default, economist, mono, sj (matched without regard to case, as WP-15's kind is) and scheme() with the `scheme' kind before anything is written, for `dash build' and `dash serve' alike, so the hostile values are refused at the gate with `dash build: theme() must be one of clean, dark, default, economist, mono, sj (got: V)', r(198), no Python traceback and nothing written. test/test_theme_escaping.cscript THM-01..06 are restated to r(198) with the message naming theme(), no `Traceback' in the captured output and no file; THM-07 (the shared escaping helper is still the one site), THM-08 (an ordinary theme()) and THM-09 (an empty theme()) unchanged; test/test_custom_scheme.cscript CS-08 pins theme(nosuchtheme) on `dash build'.

- [x] Title: sttrans's twenty-percent colour opacity was stripped by the extractor and dropped by the parser, so its palette drew opaque
  - File Names and Line Numbers: scripts/extract_scheme_truth.py at 0112221 (`ColorResolver.resolve': a `%NN' suffix on a scalar colour recorded in `unresolved' as `opacity suffix %20 ... not folded into hex' and the opaque hex written); schemes/sttrans.scheme (WP-17: `color p1 #1a476f%20' and the other fourteen p# colours with their line, bar, mark and lineplot twins, 120 `%20' values); dash_render.py at 0112221: `SchemeParser._color' (2108-2122, resolving every scheme colour through `OptionTranslator.translate_mcolor', which discards a `%NN' suffix by design; `parse_color_alpha' at 1318 reads the suffix and was not asked); /Applications/StataNow/ado/base/s/scheme-sttrans.scheme (`#include s2color', `color p1 "26 71 111 %20"' and so on)
  - Description: Stata's sttrans declares every `color p#' with a `%20' opacity suffix (Paul Tol's muted palette at twenty percent) and draws it so: measured 22sep2026 with the console binary, `twoway scatter mpg weight, scheme(sttrans)' exported to SVG carries its markers at fill-opacity 0.20 (scratchpad wp17/ownerA/). When WP-17 extracted the six schemes dash lacked, the extractor stripped the suffix into its `unresolved' list and wrote the opaque hex, so the first translation would have drawn the palette solid; the extractor was taught to keep it (`resolve_with_opacity'), and the shipped file writes `#1a476f%20'. The renderer then dropped what the file carried: `SchemeParser._color' resolves a scheme colour through `translate_mcolor', which strips a `%NN' suffix, so the figure took the hex alone and drew sttrans opaque while every other property of the scheme was right -- a translation faithful in the file and not on the page, in both modes. The help said only that Stata draws the palette translucent, which read as a difference dash accepted.
  - Suggested fix: Carry a colour opacity the scheme file declares into the glyph alpha, ahead of the alpha dash derives from the scheme's intensity directives and behind a user's own mcolor(x%NN) or malpha(); keep the fixture's `p_colors_opacity' as the truth it is checked against.
  - Resolution: Fixed (WP-17). The scheme parser reads a `%NN' suffix on a scheme colour and the declared opacity is carried into the glyphs in both modes for the layers that take a scheme alpha -- scatter and connected markers, line, connected, bar, area and histogram -- a colour opacity the scheme declares wins over the intensity approximation, and a user's `mcolor(x%NN)' or `malpha()' still wins over the scheme. The range, spike, fit, confidence-band, density, function, scatteri, pci, dot and rscatter layers take the scheme colour but apply no scheme alpha and still draw sttrans opaque; routing each through `default_alpha' is a follow-up. test/test_scheme_resolution.py draws sttrans in both modes (scatter, line, connected, bar, area, histogram at 0.2; `mcolor(red)' keeps the scheme's 0.2 as Stata's SVG does; `mcolor(red%50)' 0.5) and pins every other scheme's alphas unchanged; test/test_scheme_fidelity.py pins that the extractor records the opacity rather than flagging it and that the shipped sttrans file keeps `%20'; the help's sttrans row says dash carries the opacity.

- [ ] Title: The generated server app has no date handling at all
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `_load_spec_and_data', which coerces every non-string column with pd.to_numeric; no `_stata_date_kind' or `_stata_dates_to_datetime' reaches the app)
  - Description: The saved renderer reads `build_options["formats"]', recognises Stata's date formats, converts those columns to datetimes and draws a DatetimeAxis with date tooltips. The generated server app does none of it. Measured on a four-row %td fixture: the saved dashboard drew a DatetimeAxis with the tooltip `@{date}{%F}', the served one drew a LinearAxis with `@{date}' and the underlying Stata day numbers (15340, 15341, ...). So `dash serve' on a time series shows a raw integer x axis. It is in no GAPS.md entry; REMAINING.md records it under "08-04 ... Remaining, item 1", which also lists `numeric_formats 0' -- that half is closed, WP-10 resolves the dataset's numeric display formats into the app as `_NUM_FORMATS' and the served tooltip now uses them.
  - Suggested fix: Copy the date detection and conversion into the app the way WP-09 and WP-10 copied the glyph dispatch, the qualifier reader and the hover builder -- one implementation, run by both modes -- and pass the resulting `date_vars' to `_dash_build_hover', which already takes it.
  - Resolution: Not fixed. Found while measuring WP-10's hover parity; the tooltip's numeric formatting was closed there (`_NUM_FORMATS'), the date half was not, because it is a feature the server does not have rather than a place where the two modes word the same feature differently. Still open, measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 (WP-23), because the served figure still converts no date: a line of mpg over a %td `when' draws a DatetimeAxis with the tooltip `@{when}{%F}' saved, and a LinearAxis over the raw day numbers 15340, 15341, ... with `@{when}' served. Both helpers the file-name note says are missing do reach the app now -- `_stata_dates_to_datetime' for the CSV download alone (WP-21's `_dash_csv_payload') and `_stata_date_kind' for reading an axis format() -- but nothing converts the columns a figure draws, so the note is out of date on the names and not on the effect. test/test_mode_parity.py::TestDatesAreAKnownServerGap still sees its five gap comparisons fail (5 xfailed).  Narrowed 23sep2026 by WP-23: the marker labels of a date variable and the titles of date by() panels are written as dates by the served app too (test/test_help_claims_5.py `TestADateVariablesLabels` and `TestThePanelTitles::test_a_date_is_titled_alike_in_both_modes`).  Still open because the served axes, tooltips and KPI values still show Stata's day numbers.

- [ ] Title: hover() is dropped by the mappers that rebuild their source from the plotted variables alone
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (GlyphMapper, the map_* methods that build a derived ColumnDataSource)
  - Description: The figure's hover tool reads the variables `hover()' names out of the renderer's own source. A mapper that rebuilds its source from the plotted columns alone leaves them behind, so `hover(foreign)' has nothing to read and the tooltip falls back to the plot variables. WP-10 fixed the four types this was measured on -- bar, spike, dropline and rcap, via the new `GlyphMapper._carry_cols' -- and both modes behave identically for every type. The remaining types that build a derived source (the fits, the smoothers, the histogram, kdensity, rscatter, the pc* family) still drop it, in BOTH modes, so this is a coverage gap rather than a divergence.
  - Suggested fix: Carry the caller's other columns through every derived source with `_carry_cols', or decide per type that the tooltip genuinely cannot answer for a fitted curve's rows and say so.
  - Resolution: Not fixed beyond the four types named. Recorded so the gap is not mistaken for parity work already done. Still open, measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 (WP-23), because 26 of the 40 plot types still drop hover() in both modes, a wider set than this entry names. With hover(foreign), 14 types carry `foreign' on every glyph the tooltip reads (bar, connected, dot, dropline, pcarrow, pcbarrow, pccapsym, pcspike, rbar, rcap, rconnected, rspike, scatter, spike -- the pc family has joined them); 13 keep a tooltip of the plotted variables only (area, histogram, kdensity, lfit, line, pcscatter, qfit, rarea, rline, rscatter, scatteri, tsline, tsrline); and 13 carry no hover tooltip at all (fpfit, fpfitci, function, lfitci, lowess, lpoly, lpolyci, mband, mspline, pcarrowi, pci, qfitci, rcapsym). The two modes agree for every type, so this is still coverage, not parity.

- [x] Title: by() drew twenty of the 38 plot types as empty panels when served, and the eighteen it drew were not the graph
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted by()-facet block: two reduced `if/elif' chains, one for the per-group panels and one for the Total panel)
  - Description: GAPS.md D4. The generated app built its by() panels with two hand-written chains that handled eighteen plot types; the other twenty fell off the end with no `else' and no warning, so `by(foreign)' on a rarea, an lfitci, a pci, a function or any pc* served a titled, axed, EMPTY panel per group. Measured on all 38 types under by(foreign) before the fix: every one differed from the saved dashboard. The eighteen that drew carried no hover tool, a visible Bokeh-default legend at 13px keyed "lfit mpg", no confidence band for lpolyci or fpfitci, 200 fitted points for fpfit where the saved dashboard draws five, and hardcoded marker sizes (size=6) whatever the scheme said.
  - Suggested fix: TR-11.1 -- delete the chains and route every panel through the one dispatch.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The per-graph loop body is now an emitted function, `_dash_figure_for(g, gname, g_df, _fac_title)', and the 38 per-type branches WP-09 left standing (each the same eleven lines with a different name in them) are one family dispatch, `_dash_draw_layer', in the saved renderer's own order: literal, function, pc, range, else. The driver loop calls `_dash_figure_for' once per graph, or once per by() panel with that panel's share of the QUALIFIED frame, and the Total panel is one more call with the whole of it. The old block is deleted, not narrowed. Measured after: 0 of 38 types differ under by(foreign); the Total panel, two by-variables, missing, a linked faceted graph, mlabel() and hover() per panel all agree. 124 cases in test/test_server_facets.py.

- [x] Title: The saved dashboard grouped the UNQUALIFIED frame under by()
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer.render(), the two `_expand_by_groups(g, base_df=self.df)' call sites (linked and unlinked)
  - Description: The reverse of GAPS.md D3. `dash scatter mpg weight if price > 4500, by(foreign)' faceted every observation when SAVED and only the qualified ones when served. Measured on ten rows with six qualifying: saved 5 + 5, served 2 + 4; Stata draws the six. The base source built in phase 2 was already qualified; the grouping simply started from self.df instead of it, so an `if' on a faceted graph was honoured by the served app (BUG-36) and ignored by the saved one.
  - Suggested fix: Group the frame `_apply_graph_filter(g)' returns.
  - Resolution: Fixed at both call sites. Regression tests in test/test_server_facets.py::TestAFacetedGraphHonoursItsQualifier (if, in, total, linked), each comparing the two modes as well.

- [x] Title: A served computed layer was not recomputed after a filter move; it became a row-index slice of the old curve, and a served line came out unsorted
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted plot loop after WP-09 registered every mapper-built source in graph_sources and none but rscatter/pcscatter in agg_sources; the emitted raw-source refresh '# 2. Replace raw source data')
  - Description: A regression of WP-09's, found here because facets ride the same path. Once computed layers went through the mapper, the served app stopped registering them for live recompute. The mapper-built fit source landed in graph_sources, where the raw-source refresh, finding `x'/`y' are not dataframe columns, "restored" them by the filtered ROW INDEX. Measured after one filter move: a 100-point lfit became ten points of the OLD line; kdensity 300 -> 10; lpoly 92 -> 10; a served `line' and `area' were drawn in dataset order, crossing themselves -- the scribble WP-09 had fixed for the initial draw. An audit of the fifteen `_compute_<type>' twins the old recompute dispatched to found lowess returning x/y for a glyph bound to x_lw/y_lw, lfitci and qfitci ignoring n(), no registration site writing the plot options at all, and five branches unreachable.
  - Suggested fix: Refresh a mapper-built source by running the mapper again.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A layer whose mapper drew from a source it built registers a `redraw' closure: the same `_dash_draw_layer', the filtered frame (with the graph's and the layer's own qualifiers reapplied), a throwaway figure, and the fresh data copied onto the live sources. The raw-source refresh skips those sources. The `_compute_<type>' twins are no longer called by the recompute path (they remain emitted; removing them is WP-26's). Verified against the saved renderer run on the filtered rows, for all seventeen affected types, per facet, and for a qualified layer: test/test_server_facets.py::TestAServedComputedLayerRecomputesWhenAFilterMoves; the six text-level `*_filter_callback_recomputes' tests in test_server.py were restated the same way.

- [x] Title: A served histogram or kdensity ignored its qualifier
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_layer_source' (the univariate branch)
  - Description: A univariate layer -- histogram, kdensity -- was handed the graph's initial source, built from the whole dataset before any qualifier, whenever the plot had no x variable. Measured: `histogram mpg if foreign==1' binned every observation when served, three bars where the saved dashboard draws two, for a plot-level and a graph-level `if' alike.
  - Suggested fix: Build the univariate layer's source from the frame it was given.
  - Resolution: Fixed in `_dash_layer_source': a layer with a y variable and no x variable draws the qualified frame. Covered by the by() parity sweep (a faceted histogram is exactly a qualified one) and by test_server_facets.py.

- [x] Title: A by() sub-option dash does not implement was discarded without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_parse_by_raw' and the emitted facet block's own regex copy
  - Description: Stata's by() takes some thirty sub-options; dash implements cols(#), total and missing. `_parse_by_raw' regex-matched those three and discarded everything else, so `by(foreign, rows(2) compact)' faceted as though the user had typed `by(foreign)'. ROADMAP BR-11.2.
  - Suggested fix: TR-11.2 -- name what is ignored.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_parse_by' is one module-level parser for both render modes (it also holds the one copy of the injection check both had); it tokenises the sub-option string respecting parentheses and quotes, so `holes(1 3)' is one option, accepts Stata's abbreviations for the three it implements, and names every other sub-option in one warning: "dash: by() sub-option(s) not implemented and ignored: rows(2) compact". The saved renderer's `_parse_by_raw' and `_expand_by_groups' and the served app's driver all call it. The served app's facet grid also now uses the cols(#) it parses: it read a `by_cols' graph option nothing writes and never the value it had just parsed. Tests: test_server_facets.py::TestAnUnimplementedBySuboptionIsNamed; cert test/test_by_suboptions.cscript (BYS-01..06) reads the warning back out of the Stata log, re-joining Stata's wrapped lines in Mata.

- [x] Title: A by() panel of an integer-valued float is titled "3.0"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_facet_label'
  - Description: `by(rep78)' titles its panels "3.0", "4.0", "5.0" in both render modes where Stata titles them 3, 4, 5. A value with a value label is fine (the label is used) and a variable with a Stata display format is fine (the format is used); a bare float with no format prints through str(). Not a divergence -- the two modes agree -- so it is recorded here rather than fixed in WP-11.
  - Suggested fix: Print an integer-valued float without the ".0", the way `_format_stata_numeric' does for %9.0g.
  - Resolution: Not fixed. Still open, measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 (WP-23), because the title still falls back to str(), and it reaches users through dash.ado: `sysuse auto', `dash scatter mpg weight, by(rep78)' and `dash build' title the panels 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 4.0 and 5.0. rep78 carries %8.0g, a general format `_stata_numeric_pattern' does not translate, so the variable's own format never reaches `_dash_facet_label'; a double carrying %9.0g reads the same. Under a fixed format such as %9.0f the saved page titles the panels 3, 4 and 5 while the served app still says 3.0, 4.0 and 5.0 -- a separate entry at the end of this file records that divergence.  Fixed 23sep2026 by WP-23: `_dash_facet_label` titles a panel with its value label, else with the number as Stata writes it -- 3, 4 and 5, in both modes, whatever the display format, as twoway titles them (measured from twoway's own graphs, test/test_help_claims_5.cscript HC5-07).  Certified by test/test_help_claims_5.py `TestThePanelTitles` (both modes).

- [x] Title: dash_setup silenced every later dash warning in the same Stata session
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado `_dash_setup_check_files' (the single-line `python:' scheme probe)
  - Description: The probe that asks the renderer whether it can read a .scheme file ran `warnings.simplefilter("ignore")' in Stata's embedded Python, to hide the probe's own fallback warning, and never removed it. Python's warning filters are interpreter-wide and the interpreter lives as long as the Stata session, so after `dash_setup' -- the first thing a new user is told to run -- no warning dash raises was printed again: not the empty-layer report, not the by() sub-option report, not the unknown-scheme report. Measured in one session: a render printed two warning lines before the filter and zero after. Found because test_setup_honesty.cscript calls the probe and test_by_suboptions.cscript, four scripts later in the same session, could not see the warning it was written to read.
  - Suggested fix: Silence the probe only.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The filter list is saved before the probe and restored after it, on the same line (a `with' block cannot follow `;' in the one-line `python:' form the ado-path loader requires). Regression: test_by_suboptions.cscript BYS-06 runs the probe and then a by(rows(2)) render in the same session and reads the warning back out of the log.

- [x] Title: The parity guard covered 17 of 38 plot types, no cross-cutting feature, and seven style properties
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/test_mode_parity.py (CORPUS, _glyph_profile, _annotations)
  - Description: GAPS.md D8. The standing guard between the two render modes rendered 17 plot types, set none of by(), link(), filter(), tabs() or a KPI, and compared seven glyph style properties and a few annotation fields -- not the rows a glyph draws, not its coordinates, not the tools, not the tooltips, not the legend, not the axes, not the widgets, not the KPI cards, not where anything sits in the document. Every divergence GAPS.md D2-D6 records was invisible to it.
  - Suggested fix: ROADMAP WP-12.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The corpus covers every plot type the code dispatches -- the list is derived from the dispatch tables by `_dash_plot_type_names', cross-checked against dash.ado's Supported: text and the Mata abbreviation table, so a type cannot be added without a parity entry -- and every cross-cutting feature: by() with its sub-options, link(), all three filter() types, tabs(), KPIs with their qualifiers and formats, layout() placement, graph and per-plot qualifiers, mlabel(), hover(), colorvar(), legend() sub-options, xlabel()/ylabel(), yaxis(2)/xaxis(2), level(), schemes, title and subtitle. The profile is the whole document: the layout tree, every widget's title/options/bounds/step, every KPI card's value, and for every figure its glyphs with rows and coordinates drawn, annotations, legend (items, size, visibility, placement, orientation, click policy), tools with tooltips and scope, axes and chrome, plus the warnings each mode raises. A second class fires a filter widget in the served document and compares the result to the saved renderer given the equivalent qualifier, for eleven layer types, facets, KPIs and a linked pair. The legitimate differences are three, each looked up by name where it is tolerated, and the two known date gaps are asserted to STILL fail so closing them is noticed. Twelve divergences the wider profile found on the day it was written are the entries below.

- [x] Title: The served legend hid a layer on click; the saved one did not
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted legend block set `_lg.click_policy = 'hide''; DashRenderer._rebuild_legend left Bokeh's default)
  - Description: Found by the widened parity profile on 36 of 38 plot types. Nothing in dash.sthlp promises a clickable legend and Stata's legend is static. The same block also knew only `off', the four bare keywords, label() and cols()/rows(): measured, legend(position(6) rows(1)) was below the plot and horizontal when saved, top-right and vertical when served; order(2 1) did nothing served; size(small) was 7px saved and 11pt served; legend(on) on a one-series graph was visible saved and hidden served; and the served keys were HTML-escaped where the saved ones are the raw text Bokeh draws on the canvas.
  - Suggested fix: One legend implementation.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_apply_legend' (with `_dash_move_legend' and the clock tables) is module-level, copied into the app, and is the body of DashRenderer._rebuild_legend; the saved renderer's second keyword/label/cols block and the app's own block are deleted. click_policy is Bokeh's default in both modes. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_annotations over the corpus's legend entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: a clickable served legend, put back, failed 156 comparisons).

- [x] Title: A served histogram titled its y axis with the variable; the saved one titled it nothing; Stata writes "Density"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer._make_figure (axis titles), the emitted figure body (its own y fallback to plots[0]['var'] and html.escape of xtitle())
  - Description: Measured by exporting Stata's own graphs to SVG: `twoway histogram mpg' titles y "Density" and x with the variable's label; percent/frequency/fraction give "Percent"/"Frequency"/"Fraction"; `twoway kdensity mpg' titles y "kdensity mpg" and x "x". Neither mode did any of that, and they disagreed with each other. The served path also html.escape'd a user's xtitle(), so xtitle("R&D spend") read "R&amp;D spend" -- Bokeh draws an axis title on the canvas.
  - Suggested fix: One axis-title rule, with Stata's defaults.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_axis_labels' is the rule for both modes; `_dash_histogram_ymode' is the one place the histogram scale is decided, for the bars and the title alike. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_axes over the histogram and kdensity entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 9 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: xlabel()/ylabel() were Stata's full grammar when saved and a bare numlist when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `_parse_axlabel'/`_parse_axangle', 14 lines)
  - Description: The saved renderer parsed a(b)c, #N, minmax, none, value "label" pairs, format(), labsize(), angle(), grid/nogrid and the xtick()/ytick() fallback; the app read a bare numlist and angle(). Measured: xlabel(2000(1000)4000) fixed the ticks when saved and left BasicTicker when served, silently.
  - Suggested fix: One parser, one applier.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_parse_axis_spec', `_dash_parse_axis_label_opt', `_dash_apply_axis_spec' and `_dash_apply_axis_options' are module-level and run by both; the DashRenderer staticmethods are aliases. minmax now labels the extremes of the rows the graph draws in both modes. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_axes over the xlabel() and ylabel() entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 6 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: yaxis(2)/xaxis(2) built a second axis when saved and none when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the generated app contained no `extra_y_ranges' or `y_range_name' at all)
  - Description: A layer asking for the secondary axis was served on the primary range. dash.sthlp documents yaxis(2) with ytitle2(), ylabel2() and yscale2().
  - Suggested fix: Share the saved renderer's secondary-axis setup.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_add_secondary_axes' builds the range and axis from the rows the figure draws and `_dash_assign_axis_ranges' puts a layer's renderers on it, in both modes and for every by() panel. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_axes over the yaxis(2) and xaxis(2) entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 4 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: colorvar() coloured by the variable when saved and by the scheme when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `_dash_draw_layer' carried mlabel() and hover() columns into the layer source and nothing else; the emitted `_DASH_MAPPER' was built with no variable or value labels)
  - Description: `_apply_colorvar' is a mapper method and ran in both modes, but returns when its column is absent from the source -- and the served layer source never carried it. A served discrete colour key would also have read "0"/"1" where the saved one reads the value labels, because the app's mapper had no metadata. A latent third defect: the invisible swatch renderers a discrete key is built from were about to be registered for redraw, and a filter move that emptied a category would have raised.
  - Suggested fix: Carry the option's columns; attach the metadata; skip the swatches.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_DASH_OPT_VAR_KEYS'/`_dash_option_columns' name the columns a layer's options need (the saved `_collect_plot_vars' reads the same tuple); `_dash_attach_labels' gives either mode's mapper the dataset's labels and formats; `_dash_is_swatch' keeps a colour key's swatches out of the legend keys and the redraw registry. The served shared source for link() carries those columns and the hover() columns too, which is what a linked hover(price) and a linked mlabel(rep) were missing. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_glyphs and test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_annotations over the colorvar() entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 8 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: A served confidence band ignored the session's level and was labelled with it anyway
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (DashRenderer._make_figure_body injected the level into the layer's options; the emitted `_dash_draw_layer' passed the options through untouched; the emitted legend read bo['level'])
  - Description: Under `set level 90' a served lfitci, qfitci, lpolyci or fpfitci drew a 95% band keyed "90% CI". Measured: the served band was identical to the 95% baseline while the saved one was narrower.
  - Suggested fix: Let the mapper own the default, so both callers resolve it in one place.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_session_level' validates c(level) for both modes; GlyphMapper takes `default_level' and the four band mappers resolve `level()' against it; the saved injection is gone and the served app sets the mapper's level from the spec. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_glyphs and test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_annotations over the level(90) entries (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 8 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: A dropdown's option values were 0/1 when saved and 0.0/1.0 when served; a threshold slider lost its title's inequality
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py FilterBuilder.build_dropdown/build_slider/build_range and the emitted widget blocks, two implementations of one widget
  - Description: The saved page normalises a whole-number float to its integer text because its JavaScript compares String(v); the app used str(v). The served slider was titled "price" where dash.sthlp says "varname >= slider value" and the saved widget says so. The served range/slider guard also warned and skipped an all-missing column where the saved renderer builds a disabled widget.
  - Suggested fix: One widget builder, one match rule.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_filter_widget', `_dash_filter_title', `_dash_option_value' and `_dash_dropdown_mask' are module-level and run by both; the served callback dispatches on the widget's own kind rather than on isinstance, so a date widget (not a Slider subclass) is no longer silently ignored once the app can build one. test_server.py's BUG-37-1d, which asserted the names of the app's old sentinel loop, is restated as the behaviour it was protecting.

- [x] Title: The saved renderer's filter widgets and snapshot read the FIRST graph's source, not the dataset
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer._build_filter_widgets (`fsource' = the first graph source; `_orig_data' built from it)
  - Description: Under by() the first source is one facet; under a graph-level `if' it is the qualified rows. Measured with by(foreign) on ten rows: the range slider's bounds came from five rows, the dropdown offered "Domestic" and nothing else, and the snapshot the JavaScript filters and recomputes KPIs from held five rows -- so a KPI recomputed after a move was a statistic of half the data. The served app read the whole frame for all three, which is how the parity guard's widget comparison found it.
  - Suggested fix: Read the dataset.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The builders take the variable over the whole frame and the snapshot is the whole frame with its row identities; the JavaScript already masks every source by identity, so nothing else changes. Regression tests in test/test_filters.py::TestTheFilterReadsTheWholeDataset.

- [x] Title: A KPI card sat before its graph when saved and after it when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer.render() (`all_names' = KPIs then graphs, whatever the registry); the emitted app used registry order
  - Description: dash.ado appends every name() -- graph or KPI -- to ONE registry in the order the user issued it, and dash.sthlp describes layout(auto) in terms of registered items. The KPIs-first rule was a Bootstrap hero-row policy that had leaked into every saved layout.
  - Suggested fix: The registry, as typed, in both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_layout_order' is the rule for both; `_dash_kpi_keys' resolves a registered KPI to its card or its per-facet cards. dash.sthlp's layout(auto) now says so. A do-file that registers its KPIs after its graphs will see the cards move below them in the saved dashboard. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_document_tree over "a KPI registered before its graph" and "a KPI registered between two graphs" (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 11 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: The dashboard header was two classed Divs when saved and one classless Div when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted header: one Div, hard-coded sans-serif and #666, and doc.title set to already-escaped text)
  - Description: The saved plain grid emits an h2.db-title and a p.db-subtitle so the scheme's CSS styles them; the app emitted neither class and escaped the document title twice.
  - Suggested fix: One header builder.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_header_fragments' (also used by the Bootstrap page) and `_dash_header_models' build the same two Divs in both modes, with the scheme's css_block() attached to the Divs as an InlineStyleSheet -- a Bokeh 3 Div renders inside a shadow root, so a stylesheet in the page head would not reach it. Not verified in a browser in this work package: the models are compared, not the rendering. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression test test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_document_tree (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 3 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: A faceted graph inside tabs() was two panels when saved and one nested grid when served
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer._build_tabs_widget (flattened `_facet_map' into the tab's own grid, ignoring by(, cols(#))); the emitted `_dash_cell' nested them
  - Description: Under the plain grid both modes already nested a faceted graph's panels in a sub-grid that honours cols(); the saved tabs path did not.
  - Suggested fix: One cell resolver for the grid and the tabs.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_layout_cell', `_dash_tab_panels' and `_dash_parse_tabs' are module-level and run by both; the app's own tabs parser is deleted. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_document_tree and test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree::test_same_number_of_figures over "a faceted graph in a tab" (ROADMAP.md WP-12 AC-12.2: 121 failures when put back).

- [x] Title: An immediate render saved a dashboard with no figure and rc 0, for one commit of this branch
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_layout_order' (introduced in WP-12); dash.ado's immediate master_json (line ~2070)
  - Description: `dash scatter mpg weight, saving(x.html)' writes ONE graph with no name key and no registry; the saved renderer keys its figure ''. The ordering helper that replaced the KPIs-first rule dropped an empty name, so all_names was empty, no cell was placed, and the file was written with the dataset embedded and nothing drawn -- rc 0. The Python suite, whose every spec is named, passed; the certification suite failed test_e2e_single (P1C-17, "Mileage (mpg)" absent from the HTML) and test_varlist_expansion (V2-01/V2-03, whose colour census found no series). Never committed: found and fixed before this work package's commit.
  - Suggested fix: Keep the empty name.
  - Resolution: Fixed; a graph with no name is placed under '' as before. The parity corpus now carries the immediate path's own spec shape verbatim (no name, no registry, no kpis key, layout "1 1"), so both modes are compared on the JSON dash.ado actually writes and the completeness check requires that entry to exist. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree over the "an unnamed immediate graph" entry, and test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheCorpusIsComplete::test_every_cross_cutting_feature_has_an_entry, which requires that entry.

- [x] Title: The served document title was escaped twice
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted `doc.title = html.escape(title)'); test/test_server.py test_xss_title_escaped
  - Description: Bokeh's page template escapes the document title itself. Measured: a raw "R&D <b>" renders as "R&amp;D &lt;b&gt;" in the page's <title>, and the pre-escaped text the app set renders as "R&amp;amp;D &amp;lt;b&amp;gt;" -- the browser tab showed "&amp;". The XSS test pinned the model-level escape ("Layer 2") rather than the page.
  - Suggested fix: Set the raw title and assert on the rendered page.
  - Resolution: Fixed with the header work: the title is raw on the model, the header builder escapes it once for the Div, and the test now reads the rendered <title> and requires no tag and no double escape.

- [x] Title: A weight expression was accepted and dropped without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado _dash_parse_varpart_std/_1v/_3v/_4v (the weight clause and its `sreturn local _weight'); _dash_process_segment (`sreturn clear' before the option parse); _dash_report_ignored
  - Description: GAPS.md A3. The parsers stored the weight type in s(_weight) and the comment beside them said it was reported as ignored; `sreturn clear' destroyed it before the report was assembled. Measured: `dash scatter mpg weight [aw=price], name(w1)' -> rc 0, registered, no note. Nothing in dash weights anything, so a user pasting a weighted twoway command got an unweighted picture and no warning.
  - Suggested fix: Carry the type across the clear and name it.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The type is saved before the clear and appended to the segment's ignored list as a bracketed token; the reporter prints `[aweight]' without the `()' it gives options. One note per command names each distinct type once, never the variable. Certified by test_option_surface.cscript AC-13.1a-c; dash.sthlp says so. Honouring weights in the computed layers is WP-18.

- [x] Title: link() and filter() written on a graph were stored and read by nothing; saving() on a named graph wrote nothing
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado _dash_parse_graph_opts/_dash_parse_plot_opts pending blocks; _dash_plot (the route decision)
  - Description: GAPS.md E2. All three were accepted with rc 0 and named nowhere. The renderer reads link() and filter() only from build_options; a named graph never reaches the saving() extraction. Measured: name(g1) link(g1) then dash build gave one source and no note; name(g2) filter(foreign, type(dropdown)) gave no widget and did not serialise the variable; name(g3) saving(named.html) registered g3 and wrote nothing.
  - Suggested fix: Report; honouring is a feature with semantics to decide.
  - Resolution: Fixed. link() and filter() are on both pending lists and named in the note, with an Unsupported entry in the help explaining that they belong to dash build; saving() on a named graph is named at the route decision, because the immediate route honours it. Certified by test_option_surface.cscript O2-08 (each named once), O2-09 (named, nothing written) and O2-10 (the immediate route still honours it, no note), and by the converse test in test_unsupported_report_is_true.py.

- [x] Title: nodraw was reported as ignored while it was honoured, and dash build ignored it while the help said it did not
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (the two `_dash_pending' nodraw lines; _dash_render_immediate's launch decision; _dash_build's launch); dash.sthlp (the Unsupported entry for nodraw, the overview example); test/fixtures/rejected_options.json (the nodraw disposition)
  - Description: GAPS.md E1. `dash scatter mpg weight, nodraw' printed "ignoring unsupported option(s): nodraw()" and then kept the browser shut, which is nodraw honoured. The help's Unsupported entry admitted as much and argued for naming it anyway. On dash build a registered graph's nodraw was never read, while the help's nobrowser row said it had the same effect there. test_unsupported_report_is_true.py could not see either: it diffs rendered output, and nodraw's effect is an ado-side shell call.
  - Suggested fix: Take it off the lists; make the help's claim true; give the launch decision a seam a script can call.
  - Resolution: Fixed. nodraw is off both lists; `_dash_browser_wanted' decides the launch from saving(), $dash_nobrowser and the spec's nodraw and returns r(open) without launching; `_dash_registry_nodraw' lets dash build honour a registered graph's nodraw. The help entry is gone, the overview example uses play() and text(), the fixture disposition is "honoured-nobrowser", and the generator's inventory no longer classifies nodraw as IGNORE. Certified by test_nodraw_honoured.cscript ND-01 to ND-05; pinned in test_unsupported_report_is_true.py (never pending; the launch decision still reads it).

- [x] Title: bw() meant barwidth() on every plot type, so `lpoly ..., bw(400)' silently drew the default bandwidth
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado _dash_parse_plot_opts (the unconditional fold), now _dash_fold_bw
  - Description: GAPS.md E4. twoway accepts bw() only as an abbreviation of bwidth(), and only on lpoly, lpolyci, lowess and kdensity (rc 0); everywhere else it is r(198). dash folded bw() to barwidth() before the plot type was known. Measured: `dash (lpoly mpg weight, bw(400))' wrote {"barwidth":"400"}, drew the same 193-point rule-of-thumb curve as with no bandwidth at all, and said nothing; bwidth(400) drew a different 200-point curve.
  - Suggested fix: Resolve the alias by plot type.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The parser sreturns bw() untouched and `_dash_fold_bw', called once the type is known, gives it twoway's meaning on the bandwidth types, dash's documented barwidth() reading on the bar family, names it in the note elsewhere, and returns twoway's r(198) beside bwidth(). test_alias_semantics.cscript runs twoway first for every type and certifies dash against its rc; test_abbreviations.cscript's R3 case for bw moved to a bar context.

- [x] Title: Sub-options of legend(), the axis options, the textbox options, xline() and filter() vanished without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_apply_legend', `_dash_parse_axis_spec'/`_dash_apply_axis_options', `_parse_scale_opt' and the old `_report_unhandled_scale_opts', `_dash_axis_labels', the saved and emitted reference-line and note/caption blocks, `_parse_filter_raw'
  - Description: GAPS.md E3. dash.sthlp promises that nothing is ever dropped without being named; by() kept it since WP-11 and nothing else did. Measured 22sep2026 in both modes: legend(span stack holes(1)), xlabel(, valuelabel), ylabel(, labcolor(red)), yscale(reverse noline titlegap(2)), xline(3000, lstyle(foreground)) and filter(price, type(bogus)) all rendered with rc 0 and no warning. xtitle("Weight", orientation(vertical)) titled the axis "Weight, orientation(vertical)".
  - Suggested fix: One reporter, by()'s wording, from shared code.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_report_suboptions' names the tokens as written, once per graph per render (a by() panel does not repeat its graph's), and is fed by `_dash_legend_unsupported', `_dash_textbox' (title, subtitle, note, caption, xtitle, ytitle -- size() and color() honoured, the rest named), `_dash_parse_axis_spec' (labcolor(), labgap(), tlength(), valuelabel ...), `_dash_scale_spec' (noline, titlegap(), off ...; the five-token warning is subsumed), `_dash_reference_lines' (lstyle(), noextend, axis()) and `_dash_parse_filter_raw' (stray tokens). test/test_suboption_warnings.py (both modes) and test/test_suboption_reporting.cscript certify it; the parity guard compares the warnings.

- [x] Title: A filter() label containing __ or an ordinary word made the whole widget vanish, silently
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py DashRenderer._parse_filter_raw (the `_UNSAFE_F' pattern applied to the whole group text)
  - Description: A group whose text contained ; ` $ \ __ or the words import, eval, exec, open, getattr was discarded whole with rc 0 and no message. Measured: filter(price, type(range) label("R&D __ spend")) and label("open the door") produced no widget in either mode, from Stata too. Sink analysis: the label reaches only a Bokeh widget title (entity-escaped on the way into the page) and, in the served app, a repr() Python literal; filter_specs_json and the filter JavaScript carry varname, type and sentinel only. Nothing in the label is evaluated anywhere, so the gate protected nothing.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse only what a sink needs refused, and say so.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_parse_filter_raw' is one parser for both modes: the variable name and every exclude() name must be Stata names, type() must be one of the three widgets; each refusal is a warning naming the group and the reason, an unknown type() names the accepted types, a variable not in the data is named too, and any other token is named as an unimplemented sub-option. The label may be any text but a semicolon, which separates groups on the Stata side as well; the help says so. label("Size (in)") also survives now: the old label regex stopped at the first ")". **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_suboption_warnings.py::TestAFilterIsRefusedAloud::test_a_benign_label_produces_the_widget (both modes) and test/test_suboption_reporting.cscript SR-07 (from Stata; passed at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: ylabel(#5, grid) was silently ignored
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_parse_axis_spec' (the sub-option strip)
  - Description: Stripping a recognised sub-option out of the value left its comma behind, so `#5, grid' arrived at the keyword forms as `#5,', matched none of them, fell through to the numlist parse and produced no ticks -- with no warning. Found when the new reporter named `#5' as an unknown token.
  - Suggested fix: Strip the trailing comma.
  - Resolution: Fixed; ylabel(#5, grid) is a count form again (cc3c4ac). **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** test/test_suboption_warnings.py::TestTheHonouredOnesStayQuiet checks only that ylabel(#5, grid) draws no warning, which the silent defect satisfied (reverted today it fails, because the reporter cc3c4ac added names the dropped `#5' -- the warning, not the axis), and the parity corpus compares two modes that share one parser. **Regression test 24sep2026 (WP-24, GAPS.md L9):** test/test_bug_regressions.py::TestYlabelCountWithAGridIsHonoured holds ylabel(#5, grid), in both modes, to twoway as measured under s1color (its live .gph records `.yaxis1.major.suggest_ticks 5' and `draw_major_grid(yes)'; its SVG draws a #E0E0E0 gridline at each of its four y labels, and none without grid): the y axis asks Bokeh for 5 ticks, as ylabel(#5) does, and the grid is drawn at the axis's ticks in #e0e0e0, and not at all without grid. test/test_bug_regressions.cscript BR-03 measures twoway live and holds the saved page and the served document, built through Stata, to it. With `.rstrip(",")' taken out of _dash_parse_axis_spec on a copy of the tree, both fail in both modes (desired_num_ticks 6, not 5). Where the ticks fall is Bokeh's choice and is not asserted: measured in the browser, dash draws 15(5)40 on auto's mpg, the pre-fix default ticker the same, and twoway 10(10)40.

- [x] Title: title() sub-options, xscale(range()) and the caption's position were served differently from saved
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the emitted figure body: title from `_parse_textbox_opt(...)[0]' with no style; xrange/yrange keys dash.ado never writes; the emitted caption Label at y = -12)
  - Description: Measured while widening the reporter: title("T", size(large) color(red)) was 15px red saved and 13px grey served; xscale(range(1500 4500)) bounded the saved axis and left the served one on its data range; the served caption sat at y = -12, where the saved renderer's own comment records that a Label is clipped and never drawn. None was in the parity corpus, and the parity profile did not record a Label's position.
  - Suggested fix: One text for each, and the profile made to look.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_figure_title', `_dash_style_axis_titles', `_dash_scale_spec'/`_dash_apply_scale_ranges', `_dash_text_annotations' and `_dash_reference_lines' are module-level and run by both modes; the served twins `_textbox_size_px'/`_textbox_color_of' and the emitted reference-line and annotation blocks are gone. The parity profile records a Label's position and units, and the corpus carries each case. **Reconciled 23sep2026 (WP-23):** regression tests test/test_mode_parity.py::TestTheTwoModesAgree (test_same_chrome, test_same_axes, test_same_annotations) over "graph title with sub-options", "xscale(range()) and yscale with an unimplemented sub-option" and "note and caption".

- [x] Title: Under layout(bs) the served app draws the auto grid, without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py ServerAppBuilder (no Bootstrap path); DashRenderer._render_bootstrap_html
  - Description: dash.sthlp's server-mode table says "Bootstrap layout(bs) ... No (falls back to a grid)", so this is documented, and the parity guard records it as a legitimate difference (no Bokeh layout root to compare; per-figure toolbars kept on the Bootstrap page and merged into the grid's when served). Nothing warns the user who builds a server app from a bs spec. ROADMAP WP-21 TR-21.4 decides what the served page should do.
  - Suggested fix: A warning from ServerAppBuilder, or the layout.
  - Resolution: Not fixed; recorded. **Closed 23sep2026 (WP-23): fixed by 31e76e0 (WP-21, TR-21.4).** The served app draws the saved page's rows and spans on a 12-column GridBox, from the reader both modes share (`_dash_parse_bs_layout', `_dash_bs_grid'). Measured with dash's classes on layout(bs) of two graphs and four KPI cards: the renderer of 31e76e0's parent (4f6a314) served a 2 x 3 GridPlot in registry order and said nothing; 4ede074 serves one 12-column GridBox with the four cards at span 3 in row 0 and the two graphs at span 6 in row 1, as the saved page lays them out. Regression test: test/test_served_bs_layout.py (110 tests; 109 of them fail against 4f6a314's renderer), with test/test_mode_parity.py's layout(bs) entries comparing the served grid to the saved page cell by cell.

- [x] Title: mspline was a different estimator from Stata's, and read neither bands() nor n()
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `GlyphMapper.map_mspline' (6985-7057: np.argsort 7024, duplicate-x averaging 7028-7036, scipy `PchipInterpolator' through every observation 7047, `np.linspace(min, max, 200)' 7048); the served app's dead textual copy `_compute_mspline' (16012-16025); dash.ado:1251 (`n=int,2,10000', which refuses the `n(1)' twoway accepts)
  - Description: GAPS.md A4, first finding. Stata's `twoway mspline' is `as_median_bands, bands(B)' then `as_spline, splinepts(n)': the cross medians (median x, median y) of B equal-width bands over the observed x range, K of them non-empty, and a cubic spline through those K knots evaluated at the knots plus n equally spaced points between each adjacent pair, (K-1)*n + K points (twoway_mspline_parse.class:34-37; derived_serset.class:51-63). B defaults to round(min(10*log10 N, sqrt N)) raised to min(N, 2) -- readable in _gs_default_bands.ado:5-17, the rule the old fixture's `unverified' block said was in no .ado -- and n to 10. dash drew a PCHIP interpolant through every distinct-x/mean-y observation on a fixed 200-point grid, and `bands()' and `n()' arrived in the options dict (dash.ado:976, :1720) and were read by nothing. Measured 23sep2026 against the sersets of the saved .gph (the spline is serset 2; serset 1, the cross medians, is created and dropped by derived_serset.reset, so `serset set 1' is _rc 111): max abs error 14.0 mpg on auto default (y range 29), 9.2 at bands(8), 11.5 at bands(8) n(5), 5.3 at bands(4); point counts auto default 89 (K 9), bands(5) 45, bands(5) n(4) 21, n(20) 169, bands(20) 177 (K 17 non-empty), nlsw88 default 342 (K 32 of 34: two bands empty), sp500 default 166, census bands(20) 122 (K 12). A natural cubic spline through the K knots reproduces every recorded curve to 4.1e-6 abs (auto default), 2.3e-6 (bands 8), 1.2e-6 (bands 4) and 9.65e-6 (nlsw88), the sersets being float32; not-a-knot 2.76, clamped 1.62, PCHIP-through-knots 0.96 and Akima 1.02 were rejected by the same measurement. twoway mspline accepts aw fw pw (iw r(101)) and they are inert: `mspline [fw=rep78], bands(4) n(1)' equals the unweighted spline to 7.6e-7. Pinned by test/fixtures/stat_truth.json `mspline' (20 probes, curves whole), `mspline_knots' (the mband cross medians, equal point for point), `mspline_checks', `rules_wp18.mspline.estimator' and `rules_wp18.mspline.bands_default'.
  - Suggested fix: Factor the cross-median loop out of map_mband, fit a cubic spline through the K knots with the end condition the fixture picks out (natural, at fe3b12a's measurement), evaluate it at (K-1)*n + K points, honour bands() and n(), draw nothing when K < 2, and let dash.ado accept n(1) on mspline.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; the coordinator's verification run in ROADMAP.md WP-18 is the record): map_mspline is Stata's estimator through the shared cross-median helper and a numpy cubic spline, so scipy is no longer needed for it; bands() and n() are honoured, `n=int,1,300@mspline' admits n(1) and, as twoway does, refuses n(301); one or two knots draw the knots alone (the coordinator's review, entries below). Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestMsplineAgainstStata' (all 20 probes: point count, knots equal the mband section, curve rtol 1e-6 or the documented residual), the parity corpus entry `mspline bands(5) n(4)', and test/test_statistical_options.cscript (`mspline, n(1)' rc 0).

- [x] Title: fpfit could never fit a repeated-power model, so it chose the wrong powers where Stata chooses (p, p)
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `GlyphMapper._fp_transform' (5357-5365, no repeated-power branch), `_fit_fp2' (5367-5408: `combinations_with_replacement' 5388 yields (p, p), `valid.sum() < 4' 5393, `lstsq' 5397), `_FP_POWERS' 5354; the served app's second textual copy of `_FP_POWERS'/`_fp_transform'/`_fit_fp2' (15961-15994)
  - Description: GAPS.md A4, second finding. Royston-Altman's degree-2 model with a repeated power p is b1*x^p + b2*x^p*ln x (ln^2 x when p = 0; fracgen.ado:193-212), and `fracpoly regress' searches all 36 pairs p1 <= p2 from {-2 -1 -.5 0 .5 1 2 3} by deviance (frac_154.ado:286-368). dash built the pair (p, p) as [1, z, z], rank 2 of 3 (measured for all eight powers on auto), so lstsq returned the minimum-norm FP1 solution and the pair could never win on RSS: a third of Stata's candidates were unreachable. Measured 23sep2026: `fracpoly regress' picks a repeated pair on auto price on mpg (-2,-2; deviance 1353.51 against the best degree-1 model's 1356.93, the linear 1373.08 and the null 1391.43), auto mpg on weight (-2,-2), nlsw88 wage on ttl_exp (2,2) and the synthetic y = 3 x^2 ln x + N(0, .5^2) with seed 20260923 (2,2); dash chose (-2,-1) on auto (fit 9.0e-3 of the y range from Stata's serset) and (1,3) on nlsw88 (2.7e-2), and evaluated nlsw88 on a 1000-point linspace where Stata predicts at all 1546 distinct x (`_FP_GRID_MAX' 1000 at :122). With the repeated-power column the RSS argmin equals Stata's pair and the fit matches the serset to 1.5e-7 on all four datasets. Pinned by `fpfit_repeated' (7 fits: powers, e(b), deviances, the `twoway fpfit' curve), `fpfit_repeated_data' (the synthetic sample), `fpfit_repeated_pairs_found' (4) and `rules_wp18.fpfit.repeated_powers' in test/fixtures/stat_truth.json.
  - Suggested fix: Build the design Stata builds, keep the 36-model search, predict at every distinct x, and remove the reason for the grid cap (the bootstrap band, below).
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): the repeated-power design is in `_fit_fp2', the prediction grid is every distinct sorted x (`_FP_GRID_MAX' raised from 1000 to 20000, past nlsw88's 1546), and the served app's dead copy is deleted after a grep for call sites. Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestFpfitRepeatedPowers' (the 7 fits: chosen powers equal Stata's, curve rtol 1e-6) -- acceptance AC-18.2.

- [x] Title: fpfit dropped every x <= 0 and never scaled x, so it lost rows Stata keeps and picked the wrong powers on census
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `_fp_transform' (5361-5363: x <= 0 -> NaN, rows dropped per pair), no scaling anywhere in `_fit_fp2' (5367-5408); `_fp_grid' (7520-7550: unique POSITIVE x, `len(unique) > _FP_GRID_MAX' -> linspace)
  - Description: GAPS.md A4, third finding, and one it did not see. frac_xo.ado:81-106: when min(x) <= 0, x is shifted by (the smallest positive spacing of the sorted x) - min(x); then, on EVERY fit, x is divided by 10^(sign(log10 s) * int(abs(log10 s))) with s the range of the (shifted) x, before the powers are chosen. Predictions are invariant to the scale; the selection is not, numerically. Measured 23sep2026: census death on pop, raw pop 4e5 to 2.4e7, so pop^3 is ~1e22 and the (1,3) design has condition number 3.6e21: lstsq returns RSS 7.39e10 at (1,3) against 1.67e9 after scaling by 1e7 (e(fp_sfac)), and dash chose (0,.5) where Stata chooses (1,3), the fit 3.9e-2 of the y range from Stata's serset -- no repeated power involved, no x <= 0 involved. Shift cases, both rc 0 in `twoway fpfit' and `twoway fpfitci': auto xs = weight - 3000 (min -1240, 35 of 74 non-positive, spacing 10) -> e(fp_shft) 1250 = 10 - (-1240), e(fp_sfac) 1000, powers (0,0), 64 points, fit 13.18 to 30.90; auto xz = mpg - 12 (min exactly 0, 2 non-positive, spacing 1) -> shift 1, scale 10, powers (-2,-2), 21 points; dash would have dropped 35 and 2 rows. The rule recomputed from the data equals e(fp_shft) and e(fp_sfac) on both (self-check `fpfit_shift_rule_reproduces_e_fp_shft_and_e_fp_sfac' true); with shift, scale and the repeated-power design the fit matches Stata to 1.5e-7 on auto, census, sp500 and nlsw88. Pinned by `fpfit_shift' (2), `fpfit_shift_curve' (4: fpfit and fpfitci curves, whole), `fpfit_shift_rule_check' and `rules_wp18.fpfit.origin_shift_and_scale'.
  - Suggested fix: Prepare x as frac_xo does (shift when min(x) <= 0, always scale), fit and predict on the prepared x, keep every observation.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): `_fp_prepare' applies the shift and the scale; `_fp_grid' returns every distinct x.
