Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestFpfitShift' (the 2 cases and the fpfitci stdp band) and, for the census selection, `TestFpfitRepeatedPowers' -- acceptance AC-18.3; dash.sthlp no longer says fpfit requires positive x.

- [x] Title: lowess was statsmodels' smoother, not Stata's, and ignored mean, noweight, adjust and logit
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `GlyphMapper.map_lowess' (4986-5061: bwidth 5012-5015, statsmodels import 5017-5020, the call `lowess(y, x, frac=bw, return_sorted=True)' 5041); the served app's dead copy `_compute_lowess' (15899-15929); dash.ado 757-816 (no Mean, noWeight, Adjust or LOgit declaration, so all four fell through the `*' at 816 into the unknown-option note)
  - Description: GAPS.md A4 (the estimator) and A5 (the four switches). statsmodels' lowess takes the k = int(frac*N) NEAREST points by distance, runs three robustness iterations (bisquare reweighting), and gives every member of a tied-x group the same fit. Stata's ([R] lowess Methods and formulas; lowess.ado) sorts by x with ties in dataset order (stable), takes the index-symmetric window i-k..i+k with k = int((N*bw - .5)/2) truncated at the ends, weights by tricube on d/(1.0001*max d in the window), fits a weighted line evaluated at x_i (a weighted mean with `mean'; unit weights with `noweight'), runs no iterations, then `adjust' multiplies the smooth by mean(y)/mean(smooth) (lowess.ado:203-209) and `logit' clamps the smooth to 1/N and 1 - 1/N where it is below .0001 or above 1 - .0001 and takes ln(s/(1-s)) (lowess.ado:213-221), adjust before logit; a bwidth() outside (0, 1) is reset to .8 (lowess.ado:75-78). Measured 23sep2026: dash's default curve is off by 1.08e-1 (auto), 1.19e-1 (census), 1.92e-1 (sp500) and 3.67e-2 (nlsw88) of the y range, 3.13 mpg on auto; turning the iterations off alone leaves 0.62 mpg; six of auto's ten tied-x groups carry different Stata values (spread up to .0325 mpg) where statsmodels' are identical. `mean' and `noweight' were accepted, named in the note and drawn as the default: 1.46e-1 and 1.05e-1 on auto, 4.36e-1 for `mean' on census. The Stata rule reimplemented in the probe reproduces all 20 recorded curves (4 datasets x default, bwidth(.2), bwidth(.8), mean, noweight) to 1.5e-10 abs (census, y ~ 1e5; 4.8e-14 relative). Weights: twoway lowess and lowess refuse every weight type, r(101) `weights not allowed' (lowess.ado:9; twoway_lowess_parse.class:35), where dash accepted `[aw=price]' with a note. Pinned by `lowess' (20), `lowess_more' (20: default, mean and noweight at full resolution with obs_index for the tie order on all four datasets; default, logit, adjust and logit adjust on auto foreign on weight and nlsw88 union on wage, with mean_y_all_obs, mean_y_smoothed_obs and the clamp constants), `lowess_full_resolution_check', `lowess_adjust_check', `lowess_logit_check' and `rules_wp18.lowess.adjust', `.lowess.logit', `.lowess.tie_order', `.lowess.weights'.
  - Suggested fix: Implement Stata's smoother in numpy, with the four switches, and refuse weights on lowess in Stata with twoway's rc.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): `GlyphMapper._stata_lowess' is the smoother in both modes, statsmodels is no longer imported for it, `mean', `noweight', `adjust' and `logit' are declared in dash.ado as twoway declares them and honoured, and a weight on lowess is r(101) `weights not allowed'. Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestLowessIsStatasSmoother' (full-resolution default, mean and noweight on 4 datasets at rtol 1e-8 of the y range; the logit and adjust cases), test/test_unsupported_report_is_true.py (each of the four changes the document), the parity corpus (`lowess mean', `lowess noweight logit adjust') and test/test_statistical_options.cscript (the refusal).

- [x] Title: lpoly with degree(1) or more squared the kernel weight
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `GlyphMapper._compute_lpoly_fit' (5108-5172; `np.polyfit(x, y, deg, w=w)' at 5167 with w the kernel weight K)
  - Description: Not in GAPS.md; found while measuring A4. numpy.polyfit's `w' multiplies the UNSQUARED residual, so w = K minimises sum K^2 r^2 where local weighted least squares minimises sum K r^2; the weight had to be sqrt(K). Measured 23sep2026 at Stata's own bwidth (auto r(bwidth) 70.6429): the degree(1) curve at Stata's x is off by 2.8e-2 (auto), 7.6e-5 (census), 2.9e-2 (sp500) and 3.5e-2 (nlsw88) of the y range; with sqrt(K) it is 1.3e-15, 2.7e-17, 4.4e-14 and 9.4e-16. degree(0) has no polyfit and was exact (5.6e-15), which is why the existing fixture test passed. Pinned by `lpoly' (auto:degree1 and the other degree(1) probes at r(bwidth)) and recorded in scratchpad wp18/probe/fidelity.json `diagnostics.lpoly_degree1_weighting'.
  - Suggested fix: w = sqrt(K), and w = sqrt(K * w_i) once observation weights arrive.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): the polyfit weight is the square root of the local weight. Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestLpolyDegreeOne' (auto degree(1) at Stata's bwidth, rtol 1e-6).

- [x] Title: lpoly drew a point where Stata leaves it missing: one observation in the window was enough
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `_compute_lpoly_fit' (~5152: a grid point is skipped only when the kernel-weight sum is below 1e-12)
  - Description: Not in GAPS.md; found while measuring A4. Stata's lpoly returns missing at a grid point when fewer than two observations have positive kernel weight (measured in scratchpad wp18/probe/lpoly_null.log on auto at bwidth 5, 20 and 40, degree 0 and 1 alike; epanechnikov support |x - x0| < h*sqrt(5)); dash drew a one-observation point where Stata has none (twoway then joins its line across the missing points: its serset on auto bwidth(40) has 11 missing of 51 and the exported SVG one unbroken path). Measured 23sep2026 at the fixture's 11 sampled grid points: auto bwidth(5) Stata null at all 11, dash finite at 5; census default dash finite at 2 where Stata is null, sp500 default 2, nlsw88 default 1; the rule reproduces 15 of 16 probes -- census kernel(gaussian), with |z| up to 3e5 (bwidth 71 on a range of 2.3e7), has 8 Stata nulls against 5 predicted by double underflow (|z| < 38.6) and 4 by dash's threshold, so Stata's positive-weight cutoff for the gaussian kernel at extreme z is not pinned. Pinned by `lpoly' and `lpoly_twoway' (twoway lpoly's serset keeps only the non-null points: 46 of 50 on auto) and fidelity.json `diagnostics.lpoly_null_rule'.
  - Suggested fix: Leave a grid point missing when fewer than two observations have positive kernel weight.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): the null rule is Stata's. Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestLpolyNullRule'.

- [x] Title: fpfitci drew a bootstrap percentile band where Stata draws a t band on the standard error of the prediction
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at fe3b12a: `GlyphMapper.map_fpfitci' (5479-5546: `_fit_fp2' 5522, the bootstrap 5531-5535 through `_bootstrap_ci' 3282-3332 with `_N_BOOT_DEFAULT' 200 at :110 and the seed from `_resolve_seed' 7601, default 42); `_FP_GRID_MAX' (:122), whose stated reason was the bootstrap's cost
  - Description: Not in GAPS.md; found while measuring A4. twoway__fpfit_serset.ado:31-50: se from `fracpred se, stdp', tval = invttail(e(df_r), (100-level)/200) with df_r = N - 3 unweighted, band yhat -/+ tval*se; `stdf' and `stdr' are refused on fpfitci (r(198), measured). dash resampled the fit 200 times (36 models each) and drew the percentile band, which moves with the seed and cost 0.17 s at N = 74. Measured 23sep2026 against the fixture's fpfitci sersets: lower/upper off by 1.1e-1 (auto), 2.3e-1 (census), 1.17 (sp500: more than the y range at the first sample, x = 4103, where Stata's band is 1023 to 1238 around a fit of 1130) and 1.1e-1 (nlsw88) of the y range; the width ratio dash/Stata ran 0.67 to 4.72 (medians 0.85, 1.17, 1.01, 1.06). The analytic stdp band with df_r = 71 and t = 1.99394 reproduces auto's lower and upper to 6.3e-8. Pinned by `fpfitci' (the pre-existing section) and `fpfit_shift_curve' (`...:fpfitci' fit, lower, upper, whole); the sp500 sample is in fidelity.json `diagnostics.fpfitci_sp500_band'.
  - Suggested fix: The t band on the analytic stdp of the FP design, N - 3 degrees of freedom, HC1 under pweights; keep the bootstrap for lpolyci only.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): fpfitci's band is analytic, df N minus the rank the design supports (see the regression entry below); `_bootstrap_ci' remains for lpolyci and the qfitci LinAlgError fallback. Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestFpfitShift' (the fpfitci stdp band) and test/test_statistical_defaults.py's restated fpfitci assertions. lpolyci's bootstrap band is recorded in ROADMAP.md WP-18 as a follow-up.

- [x] Title: A weight was named in the note and discarded on every layer, including the twelve twoway weights, and accepted where twoway refuses it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at fe3b12a: `_dash_parse_varpart_std' (1059-1068), `_1v' (1073-1082), `_3v' (1087-1096) and `_4v' (1101-1110), whose `syntax ... [aweight fweight pweight iweight]' sets `exp' to `= price' and sreturns only the type; `_dash_process_segment' (1976-1996: the type saved across `sreturn clear' and appended to the ignored note as ` [aweight]'; the option validation at 1983-1987 skipped on a weight-only segment; the plot JSON at 2096, 2107 and 2111 carrying no weight); dash_parse.mata `_dash_extract_vars' (761-805, key tuple 768-772 with no weight key, so the weight variable was never exported); dash_render.py `_DASH_OPT_VAR_KEYS' (10438: colorvar and mlabel only) and every computed mapper, none of which read a weight
  - Description: GAPS.md A3, the half WP-13 left: the note was true and the picture was unweighted. Measured 23sep2026: `dash scatter mpg weight [aw=price], name(w1)' rc 0, note `[aweight]', `"options":{}'; `dash lfit mpg weight [aw=price]' the same, unweighted; `[iw=price]' on scatter rc 0 with the note where twoway is r(101) `iweights not allowed' on every plot type; `[pw=price]' on histogram rc 0 with the note where twoway is r(101); `[aw=price+1]' rc 0 with the expression discarded. What twoway accepts, measured as an 18 x 4 grid on auto and recorded in `weight_acceptance' (72 probes) and `weight_acceptance_table': aweight, fweight and pweight on lfit, qfit, lfitci, qfitci, fpfit, fpfitci, mspline and mband; aweight and fweight on kdensity, lpoly and lpolyci (pweight r(101) `pweights not allowed'); fweight only on histogram (aweight and pweight r(101)); NOTHING on lowess (all four r(101) `weights not allowed', lowess.ado:9, twoway_lowess_parse.class:35); iweight nowhere (r(101) everywhere). So the plan's premise that lowess honours weights was false, histogram's `[aw]' is an error, not a feature, and kdensity's and lpoly's `[pw]' too. Semantics recorded from the weighted commands (`weighted', 55 probes over auto [aw=price] and [fw=int(price/1000)], census [aw=pop], sp500 [aw=volume], nlsw88 [aw=hours]; `weighted_data'; `weighted_fit_check', 15 of 15 true): regress with aweights normalises the weights to sum N (df_r = N - 2), fweights replicate (N = sum w) and must be non-negative integers (r(401)), pweights give the aweight point estimates with a robust variance; the weighted lfit, qfit and lfitci sersets equal the weighted regress on all 15 probes (auto [aw=price] lfit slope -0.0054960, intercept 37.6987); kdensity's bandwidth uses the weighted sd and percentiles with N = sum w for fweights (auto [fw=int(price/1000)] r(bwidth) 1.3973377; [aw=price] 1.9746142) and the density is sum w K / (sum w h); lpoly normalises aweights to mean 1 and uses fweights raw with local weight K_i w_i (nlsw88 [aw=hours] N 2242, r(bwidth) .730943); histogram counts by fweight with N = sum w (auto [fw=int(price/1000)] N 417, 20 bins of width 1.45); fracpoly takes the weight into the deviance search (census [aw=pop] powers (2,2)); mspline and mband accept aw, fw and pw and change nothing. twoway accepts a weight EXPRESSION (`[aw=price+1]' rc 0); dash cannot evaluate one before the data leave Stata. Pinned by the sections named and `rules_wp18.weights.accepted_by'.
  - Suggested fix: Carry the weight type and the variable through the plot JSON into both modes, honour it on the twelve layers with each type's own semantics, refuse in Stata exactly what twoway refuses with its rc and wording, require the expression to be one existing variable, and keep the WP-13 note on the glyph layers.
  - Resolution (WP-18, 23sep2026; verification in ROADMAP.md WP-18): the parsers sreturn the expression, `_dash_process_segment' refuses per the acceptance table with r(101) and twoway's words, expands and confirms the variable (r(198) with a `generate' hint for an expression, r(401) for a non-integer fweight), and splices `"weight":"<type>","wexp":"<var>"' into the plot's options only when a weight is present, so the unweighted JSON is byte for byte what three tests pin; `_dash_extract_vars' exports the variable on both routes; `_DASH_OPT_VAR_KEYS' carries it into either mode's source and `_layer_weights' hands each mapper (w, type). Certified by test/test_statistical_truth.py `TestWeightedLayers' (every weighted probe: lfit, qfit and lfitci coefficients and curves, lpoly, fpfit powers and curve, histogram fweight heights, kdensity bandwidth and curve), test/test_statistical_options.cscript (accepted per type with no note and the JSON carrying weight and wexp; iw everywhere, lowess any, histogram aw/pw, kdensity/lpoly/lpolyci pw r(101); `[aw=price+1]' r(198) with the hint; `[aw=pri]' expanded; `[fw=gear_ratio]' r(401); a weighted immediate render with the weight column in the data), and the parity corpus (a weighted lfit and a weighted kdensity) -- acceptance AC-18.5.

- [x] Title: A weight written as a one-variable range, `[aw=price-price]', was read as price
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment', the WP-18 weight block (`capture unab _wx : `macval(_seg_wexp)'' before the fix, now at 2119-2121)
  - Description: Found by the coordinator's review of WP-18 before it was committed. The one-variable rule was enforced with `unab', which expands a VARLIST, not a name: `price-price' (and `price - price') is the range from price to price, one variable, so `dash lfit mpg weight [aw=price-price]' registered `"wexp":"price"' and drew the price-weighted fit, rc 0. twoway evaluates the expression price-price, which is zero for every observation (measured 23sep2026, `twoway lfit mpg weight [aw=price-price], nodraw' rc 0), so the picture was silently wrong. A wildcard never got that far: `syntax' itself refuses `[aw=p*]' and `[aw=pr~e]' with "p* invalid name", r(198), as twoway does. Non-integer frequency weights were also checked against Stata: twoway refuses one even on an observation its missing y excludes (r(401)), so checking on the if/in sample, as dash does, is twoway's rule.
  - Suggested fix: Require the expression to be one Stata name (`confirm name', which takes Unicode names) before `unab' expands an abbreviation.
  - Resolution: Fixed in WP-18. `capture confirm name' runs first and `unab' only on a name, so a range, a wildcard or any other expression gets the r(198) refusal with the `generate' hint. Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-33b (`[aw=price-price]' r(198) "must name one variable"), SO-33e (`[aw=price - price]'), SO-33c/SO-33d (`p*', `pr~e': syntax's own "invalid name", r(198)) and SO-30u (a Unicode variable name is still accepted and exported); SO-33b was written before the fix and failed with rc 0 and graph so33b registered; SO-33e was added with the fix.

- [x] Title: mband and mspline put an observation on a band edge in the band below
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._cross_medians' (np.linspace edges and `x >= lo, x < hi')
  - Description: The coordinator's review of WP-18, 23sep2026. twoway puts an observation on an edge in the band ABOVE it -- band floor((x - min) * B / range), the top band closed. np.linspace's edges put it below whenever the edge is not exactly representable: x = 0, .1, ..., 1 with bands(10) has its edge at .3 as 0.30000000000000004, so .3 and .6 joined the bands below and dash drew 8 knots where Stata draws 10; on 200 draws of round(runiform(), .01), bands(20), 6 of the 20 knots moved (up to 0.53 in y). The four fixture datasets rarely put a value on an edge, which is why the mband fixture passed.
  - Suggested fix: The floor rule, clamped to the top band.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestCrossMediansAgainstStata' (the tenths case, x = k/3 with bands(9), and test/fixtures/mband_band_edges.json: the 200 values and Stata's plotted knots at bands(20) and bands(7)); every stat_truth mband and mspline record still reproduces.

- [x] Title: Two or three observations drew no mband and no mspline, and two knots drew a spline
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_mband_default_bands' (`max(1, ...)'), `GlyphMapper.map_mband' (a `len < 4' guard), `GlyphMapper.map_mspline' (K < 2 drew nothing; K = 2 drew (K-1)n + K points)
  - Description: The coordinator's review. _gs_default_bands.ado raises the default band count to min(N, 2), so two observations are two knots; dash used max(1, ...), and mband refused anything under four rows. twoway draws N = 2 as (1,3),(2,5) and N = 3 as (1,3),(2.5,4.5); bands(1) is the single knot (5.5, 30.5) and bands(2) its two knots -- with one or two knots Stata's spline serset is the knots alone, where dash drew twelve points along the chord. mband's second empty exit also skipped the line styling.
  - Suggested fix: The min(N, 2) floor, no row guard on mband, the knots alone below three.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestDefaultBandCount', `TestFewKnots' (both layers: N = 2, N = 3, bands(1), bands(2), three knots a spline again); test/test_glyphs.py and test/test_phase4_plots.py restated.

- [x] Title: mspline accepted n() above 300, and the help called bands()' 1..200 dash's own cap
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_option_rules' (`n=int,1,10000@mspline'); dash.sthlp's option-value paragraphs and the bands() row
  - Description: The coordinator's review. twoway mspline refuses n(301) with "n must be between 1 and 300", r(198), and twoway mband/mspline refuse bands(0) and bands(250) with "bands must be between 1 and 200", r(198) -- so the 1..200 cap is twoway's, not dash's, and the WP-18 text saying twoway has none was wrong. dash accepted mspline n(400) with rc 0.
  - Suggested fix: `n=int,1,300@mspline', a renderer ceiling of 300, and the help corrected.
  - Resolution: Fixed; the n() refusal on mspline no longer adds dash's "evaluates up to 10000 points" sentence. Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-87 (n(300) rc 0), SO-88 (n(301) r(198)) and SO-88d (no dash-only bound named), test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestMsplineNCeiling' and test/test_option_validation.py's contract.

- [x] Title: histogram, discrete dropped the values past its last edge
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_histogram' (np.histogram on the discrete edges) and the page's JavaScript recount in `_FILTER_JS' (values past the last edge skipped)
  - Description: The coordinator's review. twoway__histogram_gen.ado:200-203 bins by floor((v - min)/h) and folds a value one past the last bin back into it. With discrete the grid is min - h/2 and int((max - min)/h + .5) bins, so a width() coarser than the data's spacing leaves the maximum past the last edge: `histogram mpg, discrete width(3) frequency' is 2 12 21 13 12 6 4 1 2 1 (N 74) in Stata, and dash lost the 41-mpg car and drew every density bar 74/73 too tall; 0, 1, 2.6, 2.6 is 1 1 2 in Stata and was 1 1 0.
  - Suggested fix: Count discrete bars by the floor rule, clamped to the grid, in Python and in the page's recount.
  - Resolution: Fixed; the recount's config carries `discrete'. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestHistogramDiscreteTopBar' (Stata's heights, frequency and density, and the fold keeping an fweight) and `TestFilterJavaScript' (the real recount run under node, weighted and not).

- [x] Title: A weighted percentile missed a tie the float running sum lands just below, and moved kdensity's bandwidth 9%
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_stata_weighted_percentile' (`np.searchsorted(cum, P)' with no tolerance)
  - Description: The coordinator's review. On x = 1..8, 30, 60 with aw = .15 .7 .15 .35 .2 .1 .35 .7 .35 .35, `summarize [aw], detail' gives p25 = 2.5 (P = .85 equals the running sum of the first two weights) and kdensity's bandwidth 2.3152252. The binary running sum is a hair below .85, so dash took p25 = 3.0 and a bandwidth of 2.10475, the density 4.2% high at x = 1 -- and the page's own `dash_wpctl', which has a tolerance, moved the curve on the first filter move.
  - Suggested fix: The same tolerance on both sides of the tie test.
  - Resolution: Fixed with dash_wpctl's rule. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestWeightedPercentile.test_a_tie_the_float_running_sum_misses_is_still_a_tie'.

- [x] Title: A negative aweight was accepted on kdensity, lpoly and lpolyci, and the renderer said Stata excludes such rows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment' (the negative check inside the fweight branch only); dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._layer_weights' (the note "as Stata excludes them")
  - Description: The coordinator's review. twoway kdensity, lpoly and lpolyci refuse a negative aweight, "negative weights encountered", r(402), over the plot's own if/in sample (a negative weight on a row whose y is missing refuses; one outside the if does not -- measured). dash registered the command and drew the positive rows. The renderer's note was true only for lfit, qfit, lfitci and qfitci.
  - Suggested fix: Run the negative check for aweights on those three, and word the renderer's note per layer.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-81..84 (and SO-85/86: lfit and fpfit keep rc 0).

- [x] Title: stdf and stdr were drawn with an aweight or a pweight, where twoway refuses them
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_validate_opts'; dash_render.py `map_lfitci', `map_qfitci'
  - Description: The coordinator's review. regress's predict gives no stdf or stdr after weighted or robust estimation (regres_p.ado): twoway lfitci/qfitci with an aweight say "not possible with weighted data", r(135), and with a pweight "option stdf not allowed after robust estimation", r(198). dash drew a band twoway never produces ([21.36, 37.37] at x = 1760 on auto [aw=rep78], stdf). An fweight is fine.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse them in Stata with twoway's codes; in the renderer draw the fit with no band and a note.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-75..80 and test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestWeightValueRules.test_stdf_with_an_aweight_draws_the_fit_and_no_band'.

- [x] Title: A histogram start() above the variable's minimum was accepted and the rows below it silently dropped
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment'
  - Description: Predates WP-18; found by the coordinator's review. twoway__histogram_gen.ado:102-107 refuses a start() above the minimum of the plot's sample, "option start() may not be larger than minimum of mpg", r(198), discrete or not. `dash histogram mpg, start(15)' drew 66 of the 74 cars, rc 0 (measured by the review).
  - Suggested fix: The same check, over the plot's own sample.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-89..92 (written with the fix; the review's measurement is the pre-fix failure).

- [x] Title: A histogram of one repeated value drew three bars of width |v|/3
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._histogram_grid' (`span = abs(a_max) or 1.0')
  - Description: Predates WP-18; found by the coordinator's review. twoway__histogram_gen.ado:98-100 makes the width 1 when every value is equal and no width() is given: v = 5 on 10 rows is one bar [5, 6], density 1, whatever bin() says.
  - Suggested fix: Width 1 in that case.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestHistogramOfOneValue'.

- [x] Title: lowess adjust used the wrong mean of y, and the running line was drawn where Stata has none
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_lowess', `GlyphMapper._stata_lowess'; the mapper had no view of the data beyond the layer
  - Description: The coordinator's review, four defects. (1) lowess.ado:207 runs `summ y, mean' with no if, so adjust divides by the mean of y over every observation in memory: `twoway lowess mpg weight if foreign, adjust' uses 21.2973 (all 74 cars), where dash used the foreign cars' 24.78; off by up to 4.13 mpg, and by() panels likewise. (2) A window whose x are all equal (k = 0, or ties) has no line: Stata's running line is missing there -- 495 of 2244 on nlsw88 wage on grade, bwidth(.2) -- where dash drew the window's mean. (3) lowess.ado:89 smooths only N >= 3 with a range of at least 1e-30; dash drew two rows, and a constant x of 0.1 (std 2.8e-17) as a vertical line. (4) A smooth whose mean is zero makes adjust missing everywhere; and logit turns a missing smooth into 1 - 1/N (Stata's `ys > 1 - small' is true for missing): ln 19 at the missing tied rows of a 20-row sample, ln 73 throughout for adjust logit on a zero y.
  - Suggested fix: Give the mapper the data in memory (GlyphMapper.dataset, set by both modes), return NaN for a line without spread, Stata's gate, and adjust/logit as measured; drop what is still missing before drawing (twoway joins across it).
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestLowessEdgesAgainstStata' (twoway's own adjusted serset under if foreign at rtol 1e-9, the missing-x ratio .9773663, the tied-window serset, the logit values, the gate, the zero-mean smooth) and `TestStataLowess'.

- [x] Title: lpoly's grid followed the plot's sample instead of the dataset, a window with too few distinct x gave an arbitrary fit, and lpolyci's bootstrap mixed grids
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._compute_lpoly_fit', `map_lpoly', `map_lpolyci'
  - Description: The coordinator's review. (1) twoway_lpoly_parse.class:56 sets n = _N when the DATASET has fewer than 50 rows, overriding n(), and lpoly.ado:283-290 caps n() at _N: `twoway lpoly mpg weight if foreign==1' draws on a 50-point grid over the 22 foreign cars (28 non-missing points, qualified_stat_truth.json), where dash used 22; n(100) on auto is 74, not 100. (2) Where the positive-weight observations take no more distinct x than the degree, Stata drops the top powers; numpy's polyfit returned a least-norm answer with an uncaught RankWarning (`lpoly price rep78, degree(1) bwidth(.3)': 32 of 50 points off, 745 at x = 1.327). (3) lpolyci's bootstrap evaluated each resample on a grid over the resample's own range, so the percentile band mixed x positions (124 of 200 resamples on another grid, edges moved up to 11.3 mpg).
  - Suggested fix: Twoway's grid rule on the dataset's row count, the degree the window supports, and every resample on the drawn grid.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestLpolyGridAgainstStata' (the 28-point foreign serset for lpoly and lpolyci at rtol 1e-9, n(100) on 74 rows, under 50 rows) and `TestLpolyDegreeAgainstStata' (Stata's degree(1) and degree(2) curves on rep78). The band is still a bootstrap, not Stata's (a follow-up recorded in ROADMAP.md WP-18).

- [x] Title: The fitted lines' bands lost their precision on a date-time x, and a two-valued x got the wrong model
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_wls' (`cov = s2 * inv(X'WX)'), `_dash_fitci_se', `map_qfit' (np.polyfit)
  - Description: The coordinator's review. inv(X'WX) squares the condition number: fpfitci on hourly %tc came out at 0.09 to 1.2 times Stata's half-width, 5-minute %tc 0.82 to 1.21 with df 285 against Stata's 286 (Stata omits a term). An x with two values makes the design singular: fpfitci used df N - 3 and inverted a singular matrix (a zero-width band at x = 10 where Stata has [8.404, 10.482]), and qfitci/qfit returned an arbitrary least-norm quadratic, where Stata omits x^2 and draws the line, band and all.
  - Suggested fix: Omit an exactly collinear later column as regress does, decide the rank on the raw design as lstsq does, and keep the covariance in SVD form -- on a centred and scaled copy of the column space when the design is full rank.
  - Resolution: Fixed; qfit now draws qfitci's own regression. Certified by test/fixtures/fp_edge_truth.json through test/test_statistical_helpers.py `test_fpfit_and_fpfitci_edge_cases_against_stata' (14 cases: hourly and 5-minute %tc, three %td, two two-valued x, N = 2 and 3, collapsed fweights, constant y, a negative aweight, non-integer fweights, x offset by 1e6 -- fit and band against Stata's plotted points; 12 of them failed before the fix), `TestWls' (the collinear column omitted; an ill-conditioned design against Stata's own regress, which omits a term there: fit within 1.1e-5 of the span, stdp 2.3e-5 -- dash truncates the SVD where regress drops a column), and `TestQuadraticFitsAgainstStata'. On a date-time or offset x Stata's choice between two nearly equal FP models rests on its own rounding (on the offset x it picks (-1,-1); the residual sums of squares to 60 digits rank (-2,-2), dash's, first), so those cases carry a tolerance of 1e-3 of the curve's span; the measured difference is at most 5e-4.

- [x] Title: fpfit needed four observations, fitted a constant y, and drew negative or non-integer weights that Stata cannot fit
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `map_fpfit', `map_fpfitci' (`len(x) >= 4'), `GlyphMapper._fp_fit', `GlyphMapper._layer_weights'
  - Description: The coordinator's review. twoway fpfit draws N = 2 (22, 17 on auto in 1/2) and N = 3, and collapsed data with fweights (df 15); fpfitci draws the line with no band where df is 0. A constant y makes fracpoly fail (r(498)) and twoway draws nothing; so do a negative weight of any type (fracpoly r(402)) and a non-integer fweight -- on qfit and qfitci too ("regress could not fit model"). dash drew all of them.
  - Suggested fix: Two observations, no band at df 0, None for a constant y, and the weight-value rules per layer in _layer_weights.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by the edge-case fixture above and test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestWeightValueRules' (written with the fix; the review's measurements are the pre-fix failures).

- [x] Title: qfitci with three usable observations took the whole dashboard down
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `map_qfitci' (`_t_ppf(df=0)' raised ValueError; only LinAlgError was caught)
  - Description: The coordinator's review; new in WP-18's working tree. `dash (scatter mpg weight) (qfitci mpg weight if foreign==1 & rep78==3)' was r(7102), and so was a by() panel of three cars; twoway draws the fit and leaves the band missing.
  - Suggested fix: No band when df < 1.
  - Resolution: Fixed, in lfitci and fpfitci too. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestQuadraticFitsAgainstStata.test_three_observations_draw_the_fit_and_no_band'.

- [x] Title: The page's kdensity recount left the old curve on screen when the filtered values were all equal
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_FILTER_JS' (`if (!(h > 0)) continue;')

  - Suggested fix: Clear the source.

- [x] Title: A string weight variable in a hand-written spec crashed the render; the served app still carried three weight-blind statistical copies
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._layer_weights'; the served template's `_compute_histogram', `_compute_lfit', `_compute_qfit'
  - Description: The coordinator's review. dash.ado refuses a string weight as twoway does (r(109) "type mismatch"), but a hand-written spec reached the float conversion and took the render down. The three served copies were called by nothing and ignored a weight; TR-18.6 deleted the other dead statistical copies and missed them.
  - Suggested fix: Name a non-numeric weight and draw unweighted; delete the copies.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Certified by test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestAStringWeightIsNotFatal'; test/test_server.py P4-62, P4-63, P4-70 and P4-72 restated against the served layers; test/test_statistical_helpers.py `TestTheServedAppCarriesEveryName' checks that the app defines every module-level name GlyphMapper reads (it caught _qfit_design and two weight tables). `_compute_rscatter' and `_compute_pcscatter' are dead too and are left for WP-26.

- [x] Title: Served app, linked graphs with by(): a filter move overwrites the shared source with each panel's rows in turn
  - File Names and Line Numbers: the served app's filter callback (ServerAppBuilder)
  - Description: Found by the coordinator's review of WP-18; not a weight defect and outside WP-18. g1 scatter, g2 `lfit, by(foreign)', link(g1 g2), a range filter on price set to 4000-11385: the g1 scatter ends with 5 rows (the foreign==1 rows) instead of 9.
  - Suggested fix: Refresh the shared source once from the filtered frame, not once per panel.
  - Resolution: Not fixed; recorded. **Closed 23sep2026 (WP-23): fixed by 7c2c918**, which records the same defect in its own entry below ("A served filter move wrote each linked graph's own rows into the link group's one source in turn ..."). This entry's own case, measured with dash's classes on the same ten rows -- g1 a scatter, g2 `lfit, by(foreign)', link(g1 g2), the price range moved to 4000-11385 -- draws g1 with 5 rows, the foreign==1 ones, under the renderer of 7c2c918's parent (1169200), and with 9, every row the filter keeps, at 4ede074. Regression test: test/test_served_linked_views.py (69 tests, every one failing against 1169200's renderer: the 34 that compare the rows drawn after a move, and the rest on helpers that renderer lacks), with the linked cases of test/test_mode_parity.py::TestAFilterMoveAgrees.

- [ ] Title: A weight on a literal type answers with a different rc from twoway's; kernel() abbreviations twoway takes are refused
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_parse_varpart_any'; `_dash_option_rules' (`kernel=enum,...')
  - Description: Found by the coordinator's review of WP-18; predates it. `function y=x [aw=w]' is r(101) in dash and rc 0 in twoway; `scatteri 1 2 [aw=w]' r(101) against twoway's r(198). twoway lpoly accepts kernel(gau), kernel(ep), kernel(rec) (the class file's EPanechnikov GAUssian RECtangle ...); dash refuses every abbreviation. A weight clause written outside the parentheses, `(lfit mpg weight [fw=halfint]) if foreign==1', is caught by the outer-qualifier defect REMAINING.md records: the qualifier is dropped, so the fweight check runs on every row and refuses where twoway accepts.
  - Suggested fix: WP-22 (twoway parity).
  - Resolution: Not fixed; recorded. Still open, measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 (WP-23) with the console binary, because none of the three has changed and WP-22, the package named above, did not take them up: `twoway function y=x [aw=w]' is rc 0 and dash r(101); `twoway scatteri 1 2 [aw=w]' is r(198) and dash r(101); twoway lpoly takes kernel(gau), kernel(ep), kernel(rec), kernel(bi), kernel(cos), kernel(tri) and kernel(par) with rc 0 where dash refuses each with r(198) (kernel(epan2) and kernel(gaussian) pass in both); and `(lfit mpg weight [fw=halfint]) if foreign==1', halfint 1 on foreign cars and 0.5 elsewhere, is rc 0 in twoway and r(401) in dash, while the same qualifier inside the parentheses is rc 0 in both.  Partly fixed 23sep2026 by WP-23: `twoway function` takes aw, fw and pw and dash now does (rc 0), and the three immediate types refuse a weight with r(198) as twoway does (test/test_help_claims_6.cscript HC6-48).  Still open, measured on the WP-23 integration tree, because two of the three remain: kernel(gau) is r(198) in dash where twoway lpoly takes it, and `(lfit mpg weight [fw=halfint]) if foreign==1`, halfint 1 on the foreign cars and 0.5 elsewhere, is r(401) in dash where twoway runs it -- the fweight check does not read the graph's own qualifier.

- [ ] Title: lowess and fpfit cost more than they need to on large data, and a served filter move reruns them unthrottled
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._stata_lowess' (one window per observation), `_fp_fit' (36 fits at up to 20000 grid points)
  - Description: Measured by the coordinator's review: _stata_lowess takes 4.8 s at 20k rows, 28.6 s at 50k and 116 s at 100k (Stata: 0.84 s at 20k, 22.2 s at 100k; statsmodels, which it replaced, 8.75 s at 20k -- no regression); fpfit 1.6-2.1 s and ~125 MB at 1e6 rows. The served app repeats both on every filter move, per layer and by() panel, with nothing throttling the callbacks.
  - Suggested fix: WP-19 (resource limits): a vectorised window for lowess and a debounce on the served recompute.
  - Resolution: **Half fixed by WP-19.** The served recompute is throttled: a slider recomputes when its drag ends (`value_throttled'), and every filter and brush callback runs through `_dash_event_throttle', a token bucket per callback per session (10 at once, then 2 a second; faster changes fold into one trailing recompute with the latest values). Certified by test/test_served_throttle.py (31 tests; a 50-step drag recomputes once, 1,000 dropdown changes recompute 10 times and schedule one trailing call). The estimators' own cost is unchanged and this entry stays open for it: lowess is O(N x window) by its definition -- every observation fits its own window, and Stata itself takes 22 s at 100k -- so blocking the window loop in numpy would remove Python's per-observation overhead (roughly 2x where the windows are small) without changing the order of the cost. Still open, measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 (WP-23), because the estimators' own cost is unchanged: `GlyphMapper._stata_lowess' takes 5.1 s on 20,000 rows and `_fp_fit' 2.5 s on 1,000,000 (4.8 s and 1.6-2.1 s in the measurement above, on another run). The throttle half stays fixed: `_dash_event_throttle' is present and test/test_served_throttle.py passes.

- [x] Title: A do-file that began `version 16' or `version 17' could not name a graph or a KPI card
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_register_graph' and `_dash_kpi' (the name check in each)
  - Description: Found while probing for WP-19, 23sep2026. Both registrars checked a name with `regexm("`name'", "^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]{0,31}$")'. regexm() follows the caller's user version, c(userversion), which dash's own `version 16.0' does not set, and below 18 it runs Stata's old regex engine, which does not know bounded repetition, so the pattern matched nothing. Measured on Stata 19.5: `version 16' or `version 17' at the top of a do-file, then `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)', gave "dash: invalid graph name 'g1'" and r(198); 18 and 19.5 accepted it. name() is how a dashboard holds more than one graph, and version 16 is dash's own declared minimum.
  - Suggested fix: Check the grammar without bounded repetition, and the length on its own.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$' and a strlen() check, in both registrars. Certified by test/test_caller_version.cscript CV-01..CV-03 and CV-06..CV-10 (graph and KPI names under user versions 16, 17 and the running one; a dashboard built end to end under 16). The whole certification suite was also run with the caller at `version 16' (run_all_cscripts.do with `version 16' after `clear all'): 44 of 44 passed, so nothing else the suite reaches depends on the caller's version.

- [x] Title: A graph name of 23 to 32 characters, or a KPI name of 19 to 32, passed the name check and failed on Stata's global
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_register_graph', `_dash_kpi'
  - Description: Found by writing the test above. The check allowed 32 characters, but the name becomes part of globals -- dash_json_<name>, dash_spec_<name>, dash_kpi_<name>_stat -- and a global's name is at most 32 characters. `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g2345678901234567890123)' (23 characters) passed dash's check and failed on Stata's own "dash_spec_g2345678901234567890123 invalid name", r(198), where the message had promised 32. A 19-character KPI name set its first globals before failing on dash_kpi_<name>_stat.
  - Suggested fix: Allow what the globals allow.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A graph name is at most 22 characters (dash_json_ is 10) and a KPI name at most 18 (dash_kpi_ and _stat are 14), refused by dash's own check, in its own words, before any global is set; the help says so. Certified by test/test_caller_version.cscript CV-04 and CV-05 (dash's wording and no global left behind -- both fail on the old code with Stata's bare message) and CV-11 (the longest names build a dashboard).

- [x] Title: A trusted X-Forwarded-For token became a bucket key of any length, and one address spelled two ways was two clients
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, the generated app's `_client_ip_from_request'
  - Description: Found by WP-19's review of the limiter; reachable only with `--trust-forwarded-for'. The token became the key once `ipaddress.ip_address' accepted it, and that accepts an IPv6 zone -- `fe80::1%<anything>' -- at any length and with any content: a 60,012-character key and a control character were both taken. The key also kept the token's own spelling, so `2001:db8::1' and `2001:0db8:0::1', or `::ffff:203.0.113.9' and `203.0.113.9', were two clients. Since WP-19 neither gets past the limiter -- a key it is not tracking shares the new-client window -- but each multiplied what it must hold: up to 6,000 live /health keys of up to 64 KB apiece.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse a token longer than an address or carrying a zone; key on the address's canonical spelling.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A token longer than 45 characters (the longest address) or containing `%' falls back to the peer address, and the key is `xff:' and the parsed address's own spelling, an IPv4-mapped address folded to IPv4. Certified by test/test_server_rate_limit.py `TestAForwardedTokenIsOneBoundedKeyPerAddress' (six cases; four failed before the fix).

- [x] Title: Inside every certification script $dash_nobrowser was empty, so a dash build without its own nobrowser could open a browser window
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/run_all_cscripts.do (sets the global), test/cert_helpers.do, and the certification scripts that build dashboards
  - Description: Found during WP-19. The runner sets `global dash_nobrowser 1' so that no dash build in the suite opens a window, but every script's first command is `cscript', and cscript drops every global macro -- measured 23sep2026 on StataNow 19.5: `global dash_nobrowser 1' then `cscript "probe"' left it empty, where `clear all', `clear *', `clear' and `program drop _all' all kept it. So the setting never reached the scripts. Thirteen of them build dashboards with the global unset -- test_abbreviations, test_caller_version, test_e2e_filters, test_e2e_kpi, test_e2e_multi, test_e2e_serve, test_e2e_single, test_json_escaping, test_option_surface, test_registry_collision, test_render_locate, test_serve_options, test_theme_escaping -- protected, where at all, only by an explicit `nobrowser' option on each build. Under the console binary `xdg-open' is absent and nothing opened; a run from the Stata GUI would have opened a window per unprotected build.
  - Suggested fix: Set the global again after `cscript': in cert_helpers.do, which most scripts load straight after it, and in each script that builds without loading it.
  - Resolution: Fixed. cert_helpers.do sets `global dash_nobrowser 1', which covers the seven scripts that load it right after cscript, and the six that build without loading it set it after their cscript line. test/test_cscript_hygiene.py now reads every script as Stata does (comments, `///' continuations, display lines, Python and Mata blocks, program bodies, and commands handed to helpers or held in macros) and fails any `dash build', or immediate plot with neither name() nor saving(), that runs while the global is unset (`TestNoScriptBuildsWithTheBrowserAllowed', 14 of whose checks failed before the fix; `TestTheDetectorReadsStataAsStataDoes' certifies the reading). Four scripts first listed as building -- test_parser, test_parser_integrity, test_serve_launch, test_varlist_expansion -- only mention dash build in comments, render with saving(), register with name() or run dash serve on an empty registry, and are left alone.

- [x] Title: `dash serve' ignored $dash_nobrowser and a registered graph's nodraw, and opened a browser window
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_serve' (the launcher flag), `_dash_serve_spec' (r(nobrowser)), `_dash_build' (the same decision, inline)
  - Description: Found while fixing the certification scripts' browser setting, 23sep2026. dash.sthlp says setting $dash_nobrowser "suppresses the window for a whole session", and `dash build' and the one-line form honour it, as they honour a registered graph's nodraw. `dash serve' decided from its own nobrowser option alone: `_dash_serve_spec' returns r(nobrowser) from the option, and `_dash_serve' passed --no-browser only when that was "nobrowser". Measured through a stub renderer that records its launch arguments: `global dash_nobrowser 1' then `dash serve, port(5099)' launched without --no-browser, and dash_render.py's launcher then adds --show and opens a window.
  - Suggested fix: One rule for both commands -- the option, the global or a registered graph's nodraw keeps the window shut.
  - Resolution: Fixed. `_dash_dashboard_browser' is the one rule: r(open) is 0 when the command's nobrowser option was given, $dash_nobrowser is set, or a registered graph carries nodraw, and r(flag) is the --no-browser `dash serve' hands the launcher. `_dash_serve' and `_dash_build' both decide through it, and dash.sthlp says so in both options tables. Certified by test/test_serve_browser.cscript: SB-01..05 the rule itself; SB-06..11 `dash serve' end to end, with a stub dash_render.py placed first on the ado-path so that no server starts (SB-07, the global set and no option, failed before the fix); SB-12 the real renderer resolves again afterwards, and the stub comes off the ado-path even when a check fails. test/test_packaging.py's launcher-guard check looked for the global's name within twelve lines of each `shell open'; the name moved into the shared program, so the check now requires a call to one of the two decision programs beside each launcher (`_dash_browser_wanted', `_dash_dashboard_browser') and `test_each_decision_reads_the_switches' checks that each reads $dash_nobrowser and nodraw -- both fail when a guard or a switch is taken away.

- [x] Title: dash_setup ran a package's version string, and its renderer's path, as Python code
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado `_dash_setup_gate' (the Version() comparison), `_dash_setup_check_files' (the scheme probe), `_dash_pkg_version' (the metadata query)
  - Description: WP-20 (TR-20.4), reproduced 23sep2026 before any change. The version gate interpolated the installed version -- distribution metadata, untrusted -- into a `python:' line as `Version("`got'")': the value `1.0") == __import__("os").system("touch M") or Version("1.0' created the marker file and returned the verdict "ok". The scheme probe interpolated the renderer's path as `r"`render_py'"': a dash_render.py in a directory whose NAME closed the string and called sfi.Macro.setGlobal ran that call, and the probe returned rc 0. The metadata query interpolated the package name the same way, guarded only by a regex.
  - Suggested fix: Read every value inside Python with sfi.Macro.getLocal; never splice it into the source.
  - Resolution: Fixed. All three read their values with sfi.Macro.getLocal, so they reach Python as strings that are never parsed; the name regex is kept as defence in depth. Every other `python:' line in dash_setup.ado and dash.ado was audited: dash.ado's paths go through _dash_json_str, port() and timeout() are validated as digits, and `import `pkg'' names come from a fixed list. Certified by test/test_setup_injection.cscript (INJ-01..10: hostile versions with quotes of both kinds, a backtick, `$', a newline and 5,000 characters, and a hostile renderer directory -- no marker file, rc 0, never "ok"; INJ-01 failed before the fix) and test/test_cscript_hygiene.py, which forbids a macro inside a Python string literal on any `python:' line of either ado file outside a reasoned allowlist, and pins the three closed sites.

- [x] Title: The layout(bs) page ran BokehJS outside its Content-Security-Policy
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, DashRenderer's Bootstrap page head
  - Description: Found by WP-20 (TR-20.2). A <meta> policy governs only what the page parses after it, and the Bootstrap page wrote it after _extracted_head, where BokehJS is inlined, so the whole of BokehJS ran before the policy took effect. The default page already put the policy first.
  - Suggested fix: Put the policy first in <head>.
  - Resolution: Fixed with the stronger policy WP-20 adopts: the meta is the first element of <head> on both saved pages. Certified by test/test_csp.py (the meta precedes every script and style on both pages); checked in Chromium, where a fetch from the page was refused under connect-src 'none'.

- [x] Title: The function-expression whitelist let every literal through, so a string or a tower of True values reached eval
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_function' -> `_validate_expr_node' (the Constant branch)
  - Description: Found by WP-20's pinning of the existing defences (TR-20.5), 23sep2026. The validator's Constant branch was `pass  # numeric literals are always safe', and accepted every literal. A str or bytes constant reached eval -- `'%50000000d' % 1' allocated 50 MB -- and a power tower of bool constants, `(True+True)**(True+True+True)**...', was computed exactly and held the render past 15 s: _floats_only, the coercion that turns 9^9^9^9 into inf, acts on int and float and deliberately skips bool. Both render modes run this one method.
  - Suggested fix: Accept only real numeric literals.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The Constant branch refuses bool, str, bytes, complex, None and Ellipsis, so the layer is drawn empty with a warning; int and float pass on to _floats_only. The bool tower now returns in 0.5 s drawing nothing, and ordinary expressions are unchanged. Certified by test/test_security_surface.py (the whitelist pinned in both modes: each literal kind refused and never reaching eval, attribute and dunder access, subscripts, lambda, comprehensions, calls to names outside the whitelist, starred arguments, walrus and f-strings; the `__subclasses__' escape writes no file).

- [x] Title: A variable label on a slider or range filter could put live markup into the page through TeX, and a KPI or header title through MathJax
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_filter_title' (slider, range and date filter titles), `_dash_header_models' and the KPI card Div in both modes, `_dash_build_hover' (tooltip fields)
  - Description: Found by WP-20 (TR-20.3), 23sep2026, in the bundled BokehJS source of both installed versions (3.8.0, which Stata's configured Python carries, and 3.9.0). `AbstractSliderView._update_title' writes a title that holds a TeX delimiter pair with `innerHTML = process_tex(title)', and `process_tex' copies the text around the TeX through raw; a served page always loads MathJax, and a saved one loads it as soon as a model holds a TeX pair, so a variable label such as `\(x\)<img src=x onerror=...>' on a range filter put a live <img> into the page, where the page's own `script-src 'unsafe-inline'' let its handler run. A Div runs its text through MathJax unless `disable_math' is set, and MathJax's html package makes `$$\href{javascript:...}{x}$$' in a KPI or dashboard title a live link. A tooltip field is `@{name}', which BokehJS parses as HTML after filling it, so a column name from a hand-written data file that closed the brace early left markup in the tooltip (Stata cannot produce such a name).
  - Suggested fix: Keep user text out of every TeX and HTML path it can reach.
  - Resolution: Fixed. A slider-family filter title passes through `_dash_inert_tex', which breaks every `$$', `\(', `\)', `\[' and `\]' pair with a zero-width WORD JOINER, so neither Bokeh nor BokehJS reads it as maths and the reader sees the same glyphs; a dropdown title, which BokehJS always renders as text, is left as written. Every Div dash writes -- the header and the KPI cards, saved and served -- sets `disable_math'. A column whose name is not a plain Stata name is left out of the tooltip, and named. Certified by test/test_label_sinks.py (TestSliderTitles, TestTeX, TestInertTex, TestAHostileColumnNameCannotOpenATooltipField, in both modes).

- [x] Title: Legend labels, dropdown options, facet titles, tick labels and colour-bar titles showed HTML entities instead of the user's text
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_apply_legend' (label overrides), the colorvar Legend and ColorBar titles, `_dash_facet_label', `_dash_filter_widget' (option labels), `_dash_apply_axis_spec' and `_dash_add_secondary_axes' (tick label maps)
  - Description: Found by WP-20's sink inventory. Each of these was html-escaped, but BokehJS renders every one as text -- a DOM text node (legend labels and title, Select options) or canvas text (facet titles, tick labels, colour-bar titles) -- so the escaping reached the screen: `R&D' as `R&amp;D', a value label `Men's' as `Men&#x27;s', and a per-facet KPI card, which is HTML and escapes its title, escaped the facet label a second time. The tick-label maps were also spliced into the CustomJSTickFormatter's code.
  - Suggested fix: Pass the raw text to every text sink, and user text to a formatter as data.
  - Resolution: Fixed. Each is carried raw, and the tick-label maps travel in the formatter's args, parsed by fixed code. The safety of the raw text rests on BokehJS's text rendering, which test_label_sinks.py walks sink by sink in both modes and pins to the Bokeh version it was verified on (TestInventory fails on any sink it does not classify). Three tests that pinned the escaping were restated to the text contract with the reason in each: test_graph_options.py `test_legend_label_xss_is_carried_as_text', test_filters.py `test_filter_values_are_text_and_never_code', test_phase5.py `test_by_xss_in_facet_label_is_canvas_text'.

- [x] Title: Every message that repeated the user's text drew it as SMCL, and one ran it as display directives
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_validate_value' (every option-value refusal), `_dash_process_segment' (the plot type), `_dash_validate_path' (the extension and leading-dash refusals), the scatteri pairing refusal, the weight-expression refusal, the unsupported-option note, the immediate route's .gph note and "saved to" line, `_dash_prepare_spec' (link() and exclude() refusals), `_dash_build' (the "already exists" refusal and the saved and server-app lines), `_dash_serve_spec' (port() and timeout())
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix (AC-20.3), measured 23sep2026 at e932446. P19-24..26 had fixed the filter, graph and KPI name refusals; the other 23 echoes still expanded the user's text into a `display' line. `display' reads its text as SMCL, so `jitter({bf:X})', `kernel({bf:X})', `mlabel({bf:X})', `by({bf:X})', `scheme({bf:X})', `theme()', a KPI `fmt()', `link()', `exclude()', `port()', `timeout()', a plot type and a scatteri coordinate each printed a bold X in the refusal, `{hline}' drew a rule, and `{stata ...}' and `{browse ...}' drew links a click would follow; a saving() path, whose braces are legal in a file name, was shown as some other name in "saved to" and in the server launch line. The .gph note (`saving(x.gph)' is written as x.html) was displayed before the path was validated, inside plain quotes, so a double quote in the name ended the string: `saving(`"p" _dup(3) "INJ.gph"')' printed INJ.html three times -- the user's text run as display directives, the P9-11..14 class.
  - Suggested fix: One helper that prints a message with the user's text read by value and shown as written; the .gph note after validation.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The Mata function `_dash_say' prints before + value + after with every piece through `_dash_smcl_text', which writes { and } as {c -(} and {c )-} and shows C0, DEL and C1 control characters as ? (a terminal reads ESC and CSI sequences as commands, and the console binary writes to one); the value is read with st_local(), so it is never expanded into the line. All 23 echoes use it, the plot-type token is read into Mata by value, and the .gph note is shown only after `_dash_validate_path' has refused the quote. Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-01..13 and P20-29 (each failed before the fix), test/test_echo_text.cscript ECH-01..04 (the helper's escaping, controls and style guard), and test/test_security_surface.py `TestUserTextIsEchoedAsText', which fails if any `display' in dash.ado expands a local that holds user text (it flags all 23 sites in e932446's dash.ado).

- [x] Title: port() and timeout() took digits followed by a newline, and timeout() took 400 digits
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_serve_spec'
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix, measured 23sep2026. Both were checked with regexm("^[0-9]+$"), and StataNow's engine also matches `$' before a trailing newline: `timeout(7<LF>)' was accepted with rc 0 and its newline handed to the one-line `python:' statement that launches the server; `port(5007<LF>)' was refused only by the range check, because real() of it is missing. A 400-digit timeout() was accepted, and the launcher, multiplying it by 60 into a float, died with OverflowError after binding its port.
  - Suggested fix: Prove both are digits byte by byte; bound timeout().
  - Resolution: Fixed. The Mata function `_dash_is_digits' accepts ASCII digits and nothing else, so a sign, a decimal point, e-notation, hex, full-width digits and a trailing newline are refused with the "must be a positive integer" and "must be a non-negative integer in minutes" messages; timeout() is at most nine digits and goes to the launcher as a plain integer; a port of more than nine digits is out of range without being read. dash.sthlp says both are digits only. Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-14, P20-19 and P20-26 and test/test_echo_text.cscript ECH-05.

- [x] Title: A backslash at the end of layout() broke the dashboard spec
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec' (the master spec line)
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix, measured 23sep2026. layout() was the one build option written into the spec raw, on the belief that its check had refused any value that needed escaping; the check reads only the start of the value (bs..., or name:r,c...), so `layout("bs\")' passed it and its backslash escaped the closing quote of the JSON string: r(7102) and a JSONDecodeError traceback.
  - Suggested fix: Escape layout() like every other string on the line.
  - Resolution: Fixed. layout() goes through _dash_json_str, and so do link() and filterposition(), which are proven to be registry names and top or left beforehand, so no value on the line goes in raw. Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-17 (failed before the fix).

- [x] Title: EXCLUDE() in upper or mixed case skipped the registry check and excluded nothing
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec' (the exclude() scan)
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix, measured 23sep2026. The scan searched for "exclude(" case-sensitively, while the renderer reads a filter's sub-option names in any case, so `filter(mpg, type(range) EXCLUDE(nosuch))' built with rc 0 and excluded nothing -- the typo the check exists to catch, in silence.
  - Suggested fix: Search in any case.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The scan searches the lower-cased text (lower() changes A-Z only, byte for byte, so positions carry over). Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-24 (failed before the fix).

- [x] Title: A layout() grid of any size was built, cell by cell, in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec' (layout validation); dash_render.py `_dash_cap_spec', `_dash_layout_cells', `_dash_placement_map', `DashRenderer._compute_grid', the served app's grid block
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix, measured 23sep2026. The saved page pads its grid to rows x cols cells and the served template does the same on every session: a 4000 x 4000 grid of two graphs peaked at 1.5 GB, and layout(99999 99999) -- ten billion cells -- could not finish. Once the renderer bounded the grid, a second gap showed: both grid readers parsed "rows cols" with int() on str.split(), which reads 1_000, +5 and full-width digits and splits at an ideographic space, while the cell count that bounds the grid read none of those, so layout(1_000 1_000) of a spec that did not come through dash.ado went unmeasured and was built as a 1,000 x 1,000 grid.
  - Suggested fix: Hold a grid's cells to the graphs limit, in dash.ado and in the renderer, with one reading of the layout everywhere.
  - Resolution: Fixed. dash build and dash serve refuse a grid whose cells (rows x cols, or for placements the largest row by the largest column) exceed the graphs limit, with r(198) and a message naming dash_max_graphs; the Mata `_dash_layout_cells' counts the cells. The renderer applies the same count to any spec before anything reads it and draws a larger grid as layout(auto) with a warning. Every reader -- `_dash_grid_dims' (new, used by `_compute_grid' and the served app), `_dash_layout_cells' and `_dash_placement_map' -- reads ASCII digits, names and blanks only. dash.sthlp documents the bound. Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-25, test/test_adversarial_matrix.py `TestLayoutGridBound' (the lax spellings failed before the fix: `_compute_grid' returned (1000, 1000) and (99999, 99999)), and test/test_echo_text.cscript ECH-07..09, which checks that the Mata and Python counts agree on 33 layouts.

- [x] Title: The renderer could be made to recurse, allocate or overflow without bound by a scheme, an if qualifier, an axis label, a format, a limit or a function
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `SchemeParser._parse_file', `_validate_if_expr' and `_dash_qualified_frame', `_dash_parse_axis_spec', `_parse_stata_numeric_fmt' and `_dash_format_kpi_value', `_dash_limits', `_dash_function_args'
  - Description: Found by WP-20's adversarial matrix, each measured 23sep2026 at e932446. A chain of 1,500 distinct .scheme files joined by #include raised RecursionError out of SchemeParser, which resolve_or_fallback does not catch. An if qualifier of about 490 !s (497 characters) made pandas' evaluator raise RecursionError, which neither the whitelist check (SyntaxError only) nor the qualified-frame filter caught: r(7102) and a traceback on both routes and in every served session. xlabel()'s a(b)c form expanded every tick into a list -- 10^7 ticks took 1 GB and xlabel(0(0.000001)1000000) could not finish -- and a non-finite endpoint or a 5,000-digit #N raised ValueError. A format such as %9.999999999f, which dash.ado does not check inside xlabel(), built a pattern of a billion zeros, and a hand-edited KPI fmt the same (1,000,000,001 characters). A spec's limits re-check accepted "²" (str.isdigit() is true) and read "٣" as 3. A function plot's n of Infinity raised OverflowError.
  - Suggested fix: Bound each, and catch what escapes.
  - Resolution: Fixed. #include nesting stops at 32 files with a warning; the qualifier walk is iterative with a depth bound of 300, and RecursionError and MemoryError are caught where the tree is parsed and where pandas evaluates it, so the qualifier is reported and skipped; an axis may ask for at most 2,000 ticks (a larger request keeps the default ticks, with a warning), an increment with a non-finite endpoint or a zero step adds no ticks, and #N is clamped to 2,000; a numeric format's width is 1 to 2,045 with fewer decimals than its width, as `confirm numeric format' requires, or it falls back; a limit must be one to nine ASCII digits; a function's n that cannot be read keeps the default of 300. Certified by test/test_adversarial_matrix.py `TestSchemeIncludes', `TestIfQualifierExhaustion', `TestAxisLabelBounds', `TestNumericFormatBounds' and `TestHostileSpec', and test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-20.

- [x] Title: dash_setup displayed an installed package's version as SMCL and passed it on inside quotes it could end
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_setup.ado `_dash_pkg_version'
  - Description: Found while finishing WP-20's TR-20.4, 23sep2026. The version gate now reads its values by getLocal, but the version `_dash_pkg_version' returns -- from a distribution's metadata, untrusted -- was still handed back as written: dash_setup displays it, where {bf:X} is SMCL and {stata ...} a link a click runs, and passes it to `_dash_setup_gate' inside double quotes. Measured with a fake distribution whose metadata says `Version: 1.0{bf:INJMARKV}"x': `_dash_pkg_version' failed with "too few quotes".
  - Suggested fix: Keep only a version made of the characters versions are spelled with.
  - Resolution: Fixed. The query keeps a version only when re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9.+!_-]{1,64}") accepts it -- Python's fullmatch, not regexm, whose `$' also matches before a trailing newline -- and reports anything else as "unknown". Certified by test/test_setup_injection.cscript INJ-11 (failed before the fix), which puts two fake distributions on sys.path: the ordinary version comes back as written, so the hostile one's "unknown" is not the fake going unseen.

- [x] Title: A layout() dash could not read, or that named an unregistered item, was drawn some other way in silence -- and differently in each mode
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec' (layout validation); dash_parse.mata `_dash_layout_refusal'; dash_render.py `_dash_cap_spec', `DashRenderer._compute_grid', `DashRenderer._parse_bs_layout'
  - Description: Found while closing WP-20, measured 23sep2026 at f2ecdd4 in both modes. dash.ado checked only the START of layout() (bs..., or name:r,c...), so the renderer was handed values it could not read: `layout(g1:1,1 junk)', `layout(g1:1,1\)', `layout(g1:1,1,g2:1,2)' and `layout(bs\)' were drawn as layout(auto) or layout(bs) with no word. A placement naming no registered item was worse: `layout(nosuch:1,1)' made the saved page size its grid for the unknown name -- a 3 x 1 grid, the two graphs stacked above an empty cell -- while the served app, whose reader drops unknown names, drew 1 x 2, and `layout(g1:1,1 nosuch:2,2)' was 3 x 2 saved and 2 x 1 served. `layout(bs: ...)' dropped an unknown name, clamped a span of 13 or 99 to 12 and read `k1=x' as 6, all silently ("unknown tokens are silently dropped", its docstring said). link() and exclude() have always refused a name the registry does not hold.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse in dash.ado what cannot be drawn as written; report and correct it in the renderer for a spec that did not come through dash.ado.
  - Resolution: Fixed. dash build and dash serve refuse, with r(198), a layout() that is not one of its forms whole, a placement or Bootstrap name the registry does not hold ("layout() name 'x' not found in registry."), and a Bootstrap span that is not a whole number from 1 to 12; the Mata `_dash_layout_refusal' reads the value, and `_dash_layout_scan' returns the placement names the grid count reads. The renderer's `_dash_layout_check', run by `_dash_cap_spec' in both modes before anything reads the spec, reports each problem once, draws an unreadable layout as auto, and leaves an unknown placement out -- so both modes draw the same grid -- and says what a Bootstrap spec's reader will drop or clamp. dash.sthlp states the rules. Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-30 (the 15 refused values each built with rc 0 before the fix; the 8 valid ones build) and P20-17, restated (layout("bs\") is now refused, so the spec's escaping of layout() is pinned statically in test_security_surface.py `test_the_spec_line_escapes_every_build_option'); test/test_echo_text.cscript ECH-10..11; test/test_adversarial_matrix.py `TestALayoutThatCannotBeReadIsReported' (20 of its 31 cases failed before the fix, among them the mode-parity check).

- [x] Title: A saved page's range and slider filters compared a missing value as 0, so the two modes filtered different rows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_FILTER_JS` (the row mask, now `_DASH_FILTER_MASK_JS`'s `dash_filter_mask`)
  - Description: Found by WP-21, measured 23sep2026 at ed41e8a.  The saved page's filter snapshot carries a missing value as JSON null, and the callback compared it with JavaScript's `<` and `>`, which read null as 0: `range(-1, 10)` kept every missing row, `range(5, 10)` dropped it, a slider at -5 kept it and at 5 dropped it -- and NaN, which compares false both ways, always passed.  The served app compares in pandas, where NaN fails every comparison, as it does in dash's own `if` translation (`x >= 5` is false for NaN).  So the same filter state left different rows in the two modes, and their KPI cards disagreed.
  - Suggested fix: One rule in both modes: a missing value fails a range or slider filter.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The mask is one JavaScript function, `dash_filter_mask`, which the filter callbacks and the saved page's CSV download both call; a value that is null, undefined or NaN fails every range and slider filter and never matches a dropdown.  Certified by test/test_export.py `TestTheSavedFilterMaskFailsMissingValues`, which runs the shipped `_FILTER_JS` under node (5 of its 8 cases failed before the fix), and by `TestTheFileHoldsTheFilteredRows::test_the_modes_agree_on_every_filter_state`, which runs both modes' downloads on the same filter states and requires identical files.

- [x] Title: An untouched range filter dropped every missing value as soon as any filter moved
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `dash_filter_mask` (saved), `_dash_frame_filter_mask` (served; the recompute's mask, moved out of the app template)
  - Description: Found by WP-21, 23sep2026.  A range slider starts at its full extent and a slider at its minimum, and both masks applied every widget on every recompute, so moving a dropdown also applied the untouched range filter -- which, once a missing value fails a range (above), silently dropped every observation with a missing value from the KPI cards, and would have dropped them from a CSV download before anyone touched a filter.  In the served app this was already so: its pandas comparison always failed NaN.
  - Suggested fix: A filter at its full extent restricts nothing, as a dropdown at "All" restricts nothing.
  - Resolution: Fixed in both masks: a range whose value spans its start and end, or a slider at its start, is skipped, missing values included; bounds that cannot be compared leave the widget filtering.  The served recompute and the served download share `_dash_frame_filter_mask`.  dash.sthlp says so under Exporting.  Certified by test/test_export.py `TestAFilterAtItsFullExtentRestrictsNothing` (both failed before the fix) and the download tests, where the unfiltered file holds the missing-value row in both modes.

- [x] Title: A saved dashboard with a filter failed to render when a date variable held a missing value
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._make_source`
  - Description: Found by WP-21, measured 23sep2026 at ed41e8a: `ValueError: Out of range float values are not JSON compliant: nan`, and no page.  `_make_source` built every column with `tolist()`, so a date column became Timestamps with a `NaT` for each missing value, and Bokeh writes `NaT` in a list as a float NaN, which JSON refuses.  Without a filter that source never reached the document; the filter callbacks' arguments carry it in.
  - Suggested fix: Keep a date column an array.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  A datetime column goes into the source as its datetime64 array, which Bokeh sends as binary, `NaT` as NaN, which BokehJS reads as a missing value; every other column is built as before.  Certified by test/test_dates.py `TestAMissingDateDoesNotBreakAFilteredPage` (all three cases failed before the fix: the page renders under a range and a dropdown filter, and the missing date stays missing).

- [x] Title: Under plain layout(bs), a KPI card placed in tabs() was drawn twice on the saved page
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_auto_bs_layout` (was `DashRenderer._auto_bs_layout`)
  - Description: Found by WP-21 while giving the served app the saved page's Bootstrap rows, 23sep2026, and confirmed against ed41e8a.  The automatic layout placed every KPI card in its first row whether or not the grid held it, so a card tabs() had already placed was drawn in its tab and again in the grid.  examples/feature_tour.do, whose six cards all sit in tabs under plain `bs`, takes that path.
  - Suggested fix: Lay out only the cards the grid holds.
  - Resolution: Fixed in the one rule both modes now share: `_dash_auto_bs_layout` places only KPI cards that are in the grid's names.  Certified by test/test_served_bs_layout.py `test_a_card_the_grid_does_not_hold_is_left_out` and `test_auto_bs_leaves_a_tabbed_kpi_card_in_its_tab`, which fail against the old rule.

- [x] Title: A name repeated in layout() was placed once per repeat, or kept its last place, without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_parse_bs_layout`, `_dash_layout_check`; dash_parse.mata `_dash_layout_refusal`
  - Description: Found by WP-21, measured 23sep2026.  `layout(bs: g1=6 g1=6 ...)` placed g1 once for every repeat: the saved page embedded the item again each time, with the same element id -- 100,000 repeats made an 18.1 MB page -- while the served grid kept the first, so the two modes disagreed; and the served app read the whole spec on every session (a 4 MB spec: about 3 s and 300 MB each).  A repeated placement, `layout(g1:1,1 g1:2,2)`, kept its LAST place, because the placements are read into a dictionary.  dash.ado let both through.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse a name placed twice; keep the first place in a spec that did not come through dash.ado.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  dash build and dash serve refuse a layout() that places a name more than once, in either form ("layout() places 'g1' more than once."), with r(198).  The shared Bootstrap reader keeps a name's first place in both modes, and `_dash_layout_check` reports a repeat and keeps each placement's first place.  dash.sthlp states the rule.  Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-31 and test/test_echo_text.cscript ECH-11 (both failed before the fix), and test/test_adversarial_matrix.py `TestARepeatedLayoutNameIsPlacedOnce` (4 of its 5 cases failed before the fix; the served grid already kept the first place).  Three tests in test/test_served_bs_layout.py that recorded the old placing -- g1's empty second copy on the saved page -- are restated to the shared first-place rule, under which both modes draw the same grid.

- [x] Title: Under layout(bs) the saved page drew a by() graph's panels in one row, ignoring cols(), where the served grid honoured it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._render_bootstrap_html` (the grid items)
  - Description: Recorded as not done by WP-21, measured 23sep2026 at 31e76e0.  The saved Bootstrap page built a by() graph's cell itself, `gridplot([[every panel]], toolbar_location="right")`, while every other grid -- the saved page's grid and tabs, and every served grid, the layout(bs) GridBox included -- lays the panels out through `_dash_layout_cell`, in the columns `by(, cols(#))` asks for and ceil(sqrt(n)) without it.  So the two modes drew one dashboard differently: `by(rep, cols(2))` of four panels was one row of four on the saved page and two rows of two served; `by(foreign, cols(1))` one row of two against a column of two; `by(rep)` one row of four against two rows of two; and the saved cell's toolbar sat on the right where the served cell's sits above.  The parity guard could not see it: its reader of the saved bs page could name only figures and KPI cards, so its corpus held no by() graph under layout(bs).
  - Suggested fix: Build the saved bs cells through `_dash_layout_cell`, as the served bs grid does.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_render_bootstrap_html` builds every cell through `_dash_layout_cell` and still names the cell's save after the graph (`_dash_name_save(cell, name)`), so a by() cell has the served cell's panels, rows, columns and toolbar.  A dash build through Stata with `by(rep78, cols(2))` under `layout(bs: g1=12 / g2=6)` puts rep78's five panels in two columns.  Certified by test/test_served_bs_layout.py `TestAByGraphsCellHonoursCols` (all seven cases failed before the fix) and a new test/test_mode_parity.py entry, "layout(bs: ...) with a by(, cols(2)) graph", whose document-tree check failed before the fix: the parity reader now names every component the saved page embeds, and its tree compares each grid's toolbar side.  dash.sthlp says where a by() graph's toolbar sits under layout(bs), and that the panels are arranged the same way in every layout and in both modes.

- [x] Title: by(, cols(#)) was unbounded: a large # cost time and memory in proportion, in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_facet_cols`, `_dash_layout_cell` (both copied into the served app)
  - Description: Found while fixing the entry above, measured 23sep2026 at 31e76e0.  Nothing bounded # -- not dash.ado, not the parser, not the renderer -- and `_dash_layout_cell` padded the panels' last row with None up to # columns before handing it to gridplot().  Four panels under layout(auto): cols(3,000,000) took 0.33 s and 28 MB, cols(30,000,000) 1.8 s and 237 MB -- linear, so cols(1000000000) is about a minute and 8 GB for one render, paid again by every served session.  gridplot() skips a None, so the page was the same at every # past the panel count: the padding drew nothing.  The saved layout(bs) page was spared only because it ignored cols() (the entry above), and routing it through the shared resolver would have exposed it too.
  - Suggested fix: Never more columns than panels.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_facet_cols` returns at most the number of panels, in both modes (the app carries the same function), and what is drawn is unchanged: a cols() past the panel count still draws one row.  cols(1000000000) now renders in 0.18 s, as cols(4) does, and a dash build through Stata with `by(rep78, cols(1000000000))` took 0.33 s.  Certified by test/test_resource_limits.py `TestByColsCannotOutgrowThePanels` -- the bound, the widest row each mode hands gridplot() (1,000,000 cells for four panels before the fix, under layout(auto), tabs() and every served layout), and the unchanged drawing -- registered with the by() surface's exhaustion tests in test/test_adversarial_matrix.py.
