- [x] Title: A graph or KPI named like a name dash generates -- g1__by__0 beside a by() graph g1 -- took that piece's place without a word, in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_register_graph` and `_dash_kpi` (the name checks); dash_render.py `_dash_cap_spec`, with the new `_dash_named_once`, `_dash_reserved_name`, `_dash_listed_names` and `_dash_item_name`
  - Description: Found while fixing the layout(bs) facet cell (679050e), measured 23sep2026.  The renderer stores what it generates under names built from the user's -- a by() graph g1's panels are g1__by__0, g1__by__1, ... among the figures (saved and served), a KPI's per-panel cards the same among the cards, and the saved layout(bs) page keys its tab strip __tabs__ and its filters __fw_0__, __fw_1__ -- while dash.ado accepted any Stata name.  With g1 `by(foreign)` and a plain graph named g1__by__0, both modes drew two figures for three graphs, the plain graph in place of g1's first panel, under layout(auto), layout(bs) and with tabs(); through Stata, dash build drew the plain graph and g1's Foreign panel, and not its Domestic one.  A KPI named k1__by__0 replaced k1's first per-panel card on the saved page; through Stata -- where a KPI has per-panel cards only when its update() value is also a by() graph's name, here a by() graph named filter -- k1's cell drew its own two cards and the other KPI's two, and the other KPI's cell drew k1's first card a second time.  A KPI named g2__by__0 made `_dash_kpi_keys`, which matches by prefix, take graph g2 for a card, so the automatic layout(bs) drew g2 in the card row, in both modes.  A graph named __tabs__ replaced the saved bs page's tab strip, hiding the tabbed graphs, and one named __fw_0__ was replaced by the first filter.  Nothing was reported, and `_assert_every_graph_was_placed` could not see it: the figures held one entry per key.  A spec that did not come through dash.ado and named two items alike -- two graphs, two cards, or a graph and a card -- likewise drew one of them, in both modes.
  - Suggested fix: Keep two underscores in a row for dash's own names, since every name it generates holds them; refuse a repeated name in a spec that did not come through dash.ado.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  dash.ado refuses a graph or KPI name holding two underscores in a row, with or without replace, with r(198) and a message echoed through `_dash_say` ("dash: graph name 'g1__by__0' holds two underscores in a row, which dash keeps for the names it generates, such as a by() graph's panels.").  For a spec that did not come through dash.ado, `_dash_named_once`, run first in `_dash_cap_spec` -- the one pass both modes run on a spec before anything is built from it; the served app is handed the result -- refuses such a name and a name an earlier item already has (graphs are read before cards), aloud and at build time, in one bounded message per reason and kind; a refused item takes no place under the graphs limit.  dash.sthlp states the rule.  Certified by test/test_reserved_names.py (51 of its 57 tests failed before the fix, among them every collision above in both modes; the adversarial cases are registered under name_option) and test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-32 (failed before the fix: name(g1__by__0) was registered).

- [x] Title: A by() graph named after a KPI's update() mode split the KPI into a card per panel on the saved page only
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._build_kpi_html` (the per-facet branch, removed), `_dash_kpi_keys`, `_dash_layout_cell`
  - Description: Found by the name-collision work (9f48cf7) and measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7.  `_build_kpi_html` read a KPI's `update` value as the name of a by() graph -- `update_graph = kpi.get("update", "")`, then `if update_graph in self._facet_map` -- and drew one card per panel when it matched.  dash.ado only ever writes filter, brush or both there (its default is filter), and did so before these cards were written, so they were reachable only by accident: `dash scatter mpg weight, by(foreign) name(filter)` beside `dash kpi mean(mpg), title(K1) name(k1)` drew cards k1__by__0 and k1__by__1 (19.60 and 23.40 on the test data) on the saved page, while the served app, which builds no per-panel cards, drew one card k1 (21.50).  The filter callback carried the per-panel cards with no variable, so they never recomputed; over a LINKED by() graph they showed the placeholder (next entry); exclude(k1) did not reach them.  Nothing documented them.
  - Suggested fix: Key per-panel cards on nothing: a KPI is one card in both modes, and update() names a mode, never a graph.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The per-facet branch is gone, so a KPI is one card in both modes, recomputed like any other; `_dash_kpi_keys` returns a KPI's own card, and `_dash_layout_cell` places it.  dash.sthlp says a KPI is one card and a by() graph does not split it, and shows a card per group with if.  Certified by test/test_keyword_names.py `TestAKpiIsOneCard` (every case failed before the fix, the card's filter recompute included), a new test/test_mode_parity.py entry, "a KPI beside a by() graph named filter" (its document-tree and KPI-card checks failed before the fix), and test/test_e2e_kpi.cscript P2-78 (failed before the fix: the built page held kpi_val_k1__by__).  test_graph_options.py's P6-24 tests, test_label_sinks.py's per-facet sinks and two test_reserved_names.py cases, which pinned the per-panel cards, are restated to the one-card rule.

- [x] Title: On the saved page a per-panel KPI card over a linked by() graph always showed the placeholder
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._build_kpi_html` (the per-facet branch, removed)
  - Description: Read by the name-collision work and measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7: a KPI whose update value named a by() graph that was also in link() drew its per-panel cards as the placeholder dash, because a linked by() graph's panels are views over the shared source and get no source of their own for the card to compute on; unlinked, the same cards showed 19.60 and 23.40.
  - Suggested fix: None beyond the entry above.
  - Resolution: Fixed by the entry above: there are no per-panel cards, and the one card shows its value over a linked by() graph too.  Certified by test/test_keyword_names.py `test_over_a_linked_by_graph_the_card_shows_its_value`, which failed before the fix (both cards showed the placeholder).

- [x] Title: link(_all) was read one way on the saved page and another in the served app, and a graph could be named _all
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._build_sources_and_figures`, `ServerAppBuilder` (the linked-names planning and the app template), the new `_dash_link_names`; dash.ado `_dash_register_graph`, `_dash_kpi`, the link() check in `_dash_prepare_spec`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7.  The saved renderer linked every graph whenever _all stood among link()'s names, the served app only when _all was the whole value, so `link(_all g2)` over graphs g1 g2 g3 linked all three when saved and none when served.  dash.ado refused `link(_all g2)` with "link() name '_all' not found in registry", but registered a graph named _all, after which `link(_all g2)` built -- all three graphs linked when saved, _all and g2 when served.  The served app also read `;` as part of a name where the saved page split on it.
  - Suggested fix: One rule for both modes; keep _all out of the names.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_link_names` reads link() for both modes (copied into the app): its names, or every graph when one of them is _all, with `;` read as a separator.  dash.ado refuses a graph or KPI named _all with r(198) and refuses `link(_all ...)` beside other names for what it is ("link(_all) links every graph; give it alone, or name the graphs to link."); the renderer refuses a graph or card named _all aloud in a spec that did not come through dash.ado (`_dash_named_once`).  dash.sthlp says so.  Certified by test/test_keyword_names.py `TestLinkAll` (the mixed forms, the graph named _all and the shared rule failed before the fix) and test/test_registry_collision.cscript REG-16 (failed before the fix: name(_all) registered), registered under name_option.  test/test_linking_filters.py's guard that _all is expanded in one place is restated to the one shared function.

- [x] Title: A placement layout whose first name begins with bs was refused by dash.ado and drawn as layout(bs)
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata `_dash_layout_refusal`; dash_render.py `_dash_is_bs_layout`, `_dash_layout_check`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7.  Every reader took a layout() value that starts with the letters bs for the Bootstrap form, so `layout(bsales:1,1 g2:1,2)`, a placement of a graph named bsales, was refused by dash build ("layout() must be 'auto', ...") and drawn as layout(bs), with a report that it was a malformed bs value, by both render modes; `layout(g2:1,1 bsales:1,2)` built as written.  A graph named bs could not be placed first at all.
  - Suggested fix: A value that reads whole as placements or a grid is not the Bootstrap form.
  - Resolution: Fixed, by one rule in both languages: a value is the Bootstrap form only when it starts with bs and does not read whole as placements or a grid (`_dash_layout_cells` in Python, `_dash_layout_scan` in Mata), so bs, bs: and bs: name=span are unchanged and a placement naming bsales, or bs itself, is a placement.  The app carries `_dash_layout_cells` for it.  dash.sthlp says so.  Certified by test/test_keyword_names.py `TestAPlacementNamingABsGraph` (both modes; the placements failed before the fix) and test/test_e2e_multi.cscript P1B-71 (failed before the fix: dash build refused the layout).

- [x] Title: filter() exclude() of an unlinked by() graph did not exclude its panels, in either mode
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer.render()` (the filter callbacks' sources and aggregates), the served app's `_on_filter_change`, the new `_dash_excluded`
  - Description: Read by the name-collision work and measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7.  A by() graph's panels have sources keyed g1__by__0, g1__by__1 (and aggregates keyed the same), and both modes matched exclude() against those keys alone, so `filter(price, type(range) exclude(g1))` filtered g1's panels as though nothing were excluded: served, a price move took them from 5 and 5 rows to 3 and 1, as with no exclude(), and the saved page's callback carried both panel sources to filter.  A plain graph was excluded correctly.
  - Suggested fix: Match a panel to its graph's name.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_excluded` matches a key or the by() graph it is a panel of, in both modes (the saved page from its facet map, the served app from its panel-to-graph map), for raw sources and aggregates alike.  dash.sthlp says excluding a by() graph excludes its panels.  Certified by test/test_keyword_names.py `TestExcludeAByGraph` (scatter and histogram, both modes, and the shared rule; every case failed before the fix).

- [x] Title: filter() exclude() did not protect a graph on the saved page when its source was the filter callback's primary: the first registered graph, a graph after an unlinked by() graph, or an excluded link group
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_FILTER_JS` (the fallback to the primary `source`), `FilterBuilder` (the dropdown, range and slider callbacks' arguments), `DashRenderer._build_filter_widgets` (the callbacks' wiring)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at f92ef65 (first seen under node at 9f48cf7), sysuse auto, graphs g1 and g2, one filter with exclude(g1).  In Google Chrome 153 (headless, the saved page's own scripts): with g1 registered first, selecting Foreign cut the excluded g1 from 74 rows to 22, as it cut g2; with g1 registered second, g1 stayed at 74.  Under node, the same with a price range of 5000-8000 (74 to 23) and an mpg slider at 25 (74 to 19); the served app left g1 at 74 in either order.  `_FILTER_JS` writes every source in `all_graph_sources`, which leaves excluded graphs out, and then fell back to its primary `source` -- the first source registered -- with `if (!_srcs.includes(source))`, true only when every graph drawing from it is excluded, so it wrote exactly the source exclude() protects, and wrote the whole dataset's filtered rows into it: an excluded g1 with `if price > 6000` went from its 23 rows to all 22 foreign cars.  The same exposed a graph registered after an unlinked by() graph (whose own source gives way to its panels') and an excluded link group: link(g1 g2) exclude(g1 g2) filtered both on the saved page and neither served.  The fallback came with d63c5e6, whose test excluded g2, never the primary.
  - Suggested fix: Tell the callback which graph sources exclude() protects and never write one; keep the fallback only for a source no graph owns.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_build_filter_widgets` passes each filter callback `excluded_sources` -- every graph source an exclude() names (a by() graph's panels through `_dash_excluded`) that no filtered graph shares -- and `_FILTER_JS` writes the primary only when it is in neither list; the three builders declare the argument.  The served app already skipped excluded graphs and is unchanged; both modes show the same rows in every case measured, and in Chrome the excluded g1 stays at 74 whichever order the graphs came in.  An excluded member of a link group whose other members are filtered is still filtered with them, in both modes: they draw from one source.  Certified by test/test_filter_exclude.py (41 tests; 25 failed before the fix; the served and excluded-second cases passed before and pin the right answer), registered under build_options and served_widget_model_changes.

- [x] Title: name(g1, replace) stored the graph under the name "g1, replace", so layout(), tabs(), link() and exclude() naming g1 missed it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_plot` (the graph_options assembly), `_dash_register_graph`, and the new `_dash_name_split`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7 and again at f92ef65 (Stata 19.5 MP, batch).  Stata's name(string) captures "g1, replace"; `_dash_plot` wrote that text into graph_options "name" before `_dash_register_graph` split ", replace" off and registered g1, so the registry said g1 (rc 0) while `$dash_json_g1` said "g1, replace".  The same happened on a new name, in every spelling Stata allows around the comma, in both paren forms and with by(), whose panels were keyed "g1, replace__by__0".  Both renderers key items by the stored name, so beside a g2: layout(g2:1,1 g1:1,2) warned that g1 "is not a registered graph or KPI card" and drew it below g2; layout(bs: g1=6 g2=6) drew it alone at width 12 and saved its PNG as dashboard; tabs(A: g1) built no tabs and tabs(A: g1 / B: g2) only B; link(g1 g2) left two sources; exclude(g1) did not protect it; a plain build drew it after g2.
  - Suggested fix: Split ", replace" off name()'s text by one rule before the graph options are assembled, and register with the same rule.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_name_split` holds the rule `_dash_register_graph` always used, unchanged; `_dash_plot` writes the split name into the stored graph and still hands name()'s own text to `_dash_register_graph`, which splits it the same way, so the stored and registered names cannot differ and replace behaves as before.  Certified by test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-33 (every check failed before the fix: 11 stored names, 9 pages read back, 3 build logs), test/test_registry_collision.cscript REG-17 (failed before: the rule was not defined on its own) and test/test_caller_version.cscript CV-02 (failed before), registered under name_option.

- [x] Title: A KPI's field globals were other names' globals: a graph or KPI named a_stat took KPI a's statistic, and replacing KPI a deleted KPI a_b
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_kpi` (the field globals), `_dash_prepare_spec` (the KPI test and the field reads), `_dash_drop_item`; dash_parse.mata `_dash_item_globals` and the new `_dash_kpi_fields`; dash.sthlp (what a name may be)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7 and again at f92ef65 (Stata 19.5 MP, batch; pages read from their Bokeh document, filters run under node).  A KPI's fields were dash_kpi_<name>_stat, _var, _val, ... -- the globals of another legal name, <name>_stat -- read through `${dash_kpi_<name>} != ""` and retired by the prefix dash_kpi_<name>_*.  So KPI a beside a graph a_stat, in either order, built (rc 0, "'a_stat' is registered as both a graph and a KPI; drawing the KPI") with card A, a blank card and no figure, and the advised name(a_stat, replace) drew the graph but deleted KPI a's statistic (card A then showed the raw 21.2972972972973); KPI a beside a KPI a_stat set KPI a's statistic to "1" in silence (card A raw, and 0 under a Foreign filter where Stata's summarize gives 24.7727); and replacing KPI a deleted all of KPI a_b's globals while the registry kept a_b, so the AB card vanished without a word -- or the next build died with r(7102): a JSONDecodeError on a graphs array that began "[, {" after a graph was registered, or an illegal trailing comma after KPI a was replaced by a graph.
  - Suggested fix: Part a KPI's name from its fields with two underscores, which no name may hold since 9f48cf7; keep the fields to three letters so an 18-character name still makes a global of at most 32 characters; match exact global names only.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  A KPI's fields are dash_kpi_<name>__<field> (sta var val dsp fmt tit sub col bg upd if in, listed once in `_dash_kpi_fields()`): 9 + 18 + 2 + 3 = 32, measured to be Stata's limit, and the 18-character KPI limit is unchanged.  `_dash_item_globals` returns exact names only, the build's KPI test is `== "1"` wherever it is made, and dash build refuses, with r(198), a registry entry that holds neither a graph nor a KPI and a KPI with no statistic or variable (a hand-set marker, or a KPI an older dash left in the session).  dash.sthlp describes the globals.  Certified by test/test_registry_collision.cscript REG-18 to REG-28 (each failed at 9f48cf7), registered under name_option and renderer_spec_and_data_json; the cert cases that read these globals by hand (REG-07/08/11/13/15, CV-05, CLR-01 to CLR-04, A8, OV-125, P19-02) are moved to the new names.

- [x] Title: pcarrow added its arrowheads to the figure one at a time, so drawing took quadratic time
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_pcarrow`
  - Description: Found by the WP-22 review, 23sep2026: map_pcarrow, which pcarrowi now draws through as well, called `add_layout()` once per arrow, and each call copies the figure's annotation list -- 1,000 arrows took 1.45 s and 8,000 took 46 to 85 s, for a list as long as the data, or for pcarrowi the macro limit, allows.
  - Suggested fix: Add the arrowheads in one change of the annotation list.
  - Resolution: Fixed: one assignment, with the same output; 8,000 arrows draw in 6.4 s.  Certified by test/test_pccapsym_pcarrowi.py `test_the_arrow_heads_are_added_in_one_change`, which counts the changes rather than timing them and failed before the fix.  pcbarrow still adds its heads one at a time (ROADMAP WP-22, recorded for later).

- [x] Title: Text anywhere in a command granted name()'s replace, so a re-used name overwrote a registered graph or deleted a KPI card
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_register_graph` (the whole-command search, lines 3395-3402 at ad13a0f) and `_dash_kpi` (its own copy of the split regex, lines 3774-3783 at ad13a0f); `_dash_name_split`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f (Stata 19.5 MP, batch).  When name()'s own text held no replace, `_dash_register_graph` searched the whole lower-cased command for `name\([^)]*,[ \t]*replace`.  So after `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)`, a second `dash scatter price weight, name(g1)` whose title(), subtitle(), note(), caption(), text(), axis title, legend label or if-string held "name(x, replace)" or "rename(v, replace)" -- or which carried an uppercase NAME(x, replace) option that dash itself reported as unsupported -- returned rc 0 and stored price over g1 without a word, in both paren forms and across || segments; a graph given KPI k1's name with "name(k1, replace)" in its title deleted the card.  twoway refuses every one with r(110) "graph g1 already exists", and the help says a re-used name needs name(g1, replace).  The search came with the first commit (25mar2026), meant to read name()'s value; since name(string) captures name()'s whole text on every route (27mar2026) and the split reads it (18sep2026), it decided only commands name()'s own text said no to: text elsewhere, or a second name() option dash does not read, which twoway refuses as well.  `_dash_kpi` did not search, but kept its own copy of the split regex.
  - Suggested fix: Decide a replace from name()'s own text only, through `_dash_name_split`, for graphs and KPI cards alike, and remove the whole-command search.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The search is gone and `_dash_kpi` reads name() through `_dash_name_split`, so one rule decides a replace for a graph and a card; name()'s own replace still replaces on every route.  Certified by test/test_registry_collision.cscript REG-29 to REG-36 (REG-29 to REG-33 and REG-36 failed before the fix; REG-34 and REG-35, name()'s own replace on eight routes and a KPI's spaced replace, pin what must keep working), registered under name_option.  Found on the way and left as it is: dash accepts a command with two name() options, which twoway refuses, and names the second as an unsupported option.

- [x] Title: The saved page judged a filter's widget from one graph's rows: a graph whose own if/in selected no rows dropped range and slider filters, and a dashboard of KPIs alone dropped every filter
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `DashRenderer._build_filter_widgets` (at ad13a0f: the missing-variable test at 17363, the slider and range type tests at 17403 and 17423, the snapshot test at 17465), `_is_datetime_column` (284, removed), the new `_dash_filter_rangeable`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f, in both modes and through dash.ado with the console binary (sysuse auto).  The saved page tested the filter variable's type in the filter callback's primary source -- the link group's, else the first registered graph's -- which holds one graph's rows.  With g1 carrying `if price > 1000000`, with the only graph selecting no rows, or with a by() graph followed by one selecting no rows, that column was empty, and an empty column has no numeric type (`pd.Series([])` is object), so filter(price, type(range)) and type(slider) built no widget and warned "filter 'price' type 'range' requires numeric data; skipping widget".  With no graph at all there was no primary source, so every filter of a KPI-only dashboard was refused as "the variable is not in the data".  The served app, which reads its whole frame, built every one of these.  Already right: the dropdown's options and the range and slider bounds (read from the whole frame), %td widgets (an empty date column stays datetime64), a first graph whose rows are all missing on the variable, and link groups.
  - Suggested fix: Judge whether a filter is built, and its type, from `self.df[fvar]`, the frame every callback masks, by the numeric-or-datetime test the shared widget builder applies; give a graph-less dashboard's callbacks a source nothing draws.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_build_filter_widgets` tests the variable in the data (`_dash_filter_rangeable`, the test `_dash_filter_widget` applies), computes the callbacks' primary source once for the callback alone, gives a dashboard without graphs an empty placeholder source, and builds the snapshot whenever a widget is built.  `_is_datetime_column`, used only by the old tests, is removed.  Certified by test/test_filter_widget_data.py (21 tests, every one failing before the fix): both modes are held to one widget set, one set of options and one range across seven dashboard shapes, a KPI-only card is recomputed under node to the same values in both modes, and the new test is held to the builder's; registered under build_options and if_in_qualifiers.  Found on the way and left as they are: a served widget whose variable has an empty label is titled '' where the saved page uses the name; a saved %td dropdown labels its options 2001-01-02 00:00:00 rather than in the display format.

- [x] Title: A served filter move wrote each linked graph's own rows into the link group's one source in turn and never recounted a view, so by() panels, graphs with their own if/in, and every other linked graph drew the wrong rows
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, the app template's `_make_filter_cb` / `_on_filter_change` (20159 onward at ad13a0f); the new `_dash_view_positions`, `_dash_linked_views`, `_dash_refresh_linked` and `_dash_own_redrawn_views`, and ServerAppBuilder's copied-helper list
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f on sysuse auto (served: make_document and the widget's registered callback, and again on apps Stata built with dash build, server; saved: `_FILTER_JS` under node).  link(g1 g2) with g1 by(foreign) and a price range of 5000-8000 drew panels of 9 and 0 rows and g2 9 served, against 14, 9 and 23 saved; g1 `if price > 6000` registered last, dropdown Foreign, drew g1 2 and g2 9 served against 9 and 22 saved; g1 `in 1/30` drew 22 served against 0 saved; a total panel, a qualified by() graph, an excluded member, a second move and a move back to All were wrong as well, and a linked qualified line, connected line or jittered scatter kept a view counting rows of its unfiltered copy.  A plain linked pair and unlinked graphs agreed.  `_on_filter_change` wrote each member's and each panel's own rows into the one shared source in turn, so the last one written decided what every linked graph showed, and it never recounted the views' IndexFilter positions, which then named rows the view does not own or pointed past the source's end.
  - Suggested fix: Write the shared source once per move with every row the filters keep, and recount each view from the rows its graph, panel or layer owns, read before any move, as `_FILTER_JS` rebuilds each view's filter from its members; draw a redrawn copy whole.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The views over the shared source and the rows each owns are read once when the callbacks are wired (`_dash_linked_views`); a move writes the shared source once with the filtered rows and points every view at the rows it owns that the move keeps (`_dash_refresh_linked`, which orders the two writes so that no view names a row past its source's end); a renderer drawing a redrawn copy through a view gets a filter of its own that a move opens to the copy's rows (`_dash_own_redrawn_views`).  Unlinked graphs, excluded graphs, the aggregate redraw and KPI cards are unchanged.  The served app now shows the saved page's rows in every case measured, the Stata-built apps included.  Certified by test/test_served_linked_views.py (69 tests, all failing at ad13a0f) and four new TestAFilterMoveAgrees cases in test/test_mode_parity.py (their glyph comparisons failed at ad13a0f); the corpus entry "a linked pair, dropdown", which set no link(), now links its pair.  Registered under served_widget_model_changes and if_in_qualifiers.

- [x] Title: `_dash_extract_qual_vars` never stepped over a JSON escape, so the variables a qualifier names after its first string literal were not exported
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata `_dash_extract_qual_vars` (the escape tests `ch == "\\"` at lines 1533 and 1573 at ad13a0f, section 6b); the new `_dash_qual_str`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f.  The reader meant to skip each escape in the JSON string it scans compared one character with the Mata literal "\\", which is two characters (strlen("\\") is 2 and "\\" == char(92) is 0), so no escape was ever stepped over: the backslash of \" was kept and the quote after it ended the value.  For a qualifier `make != "AMC Concord" & foreign == 1` in a KPI, a plot or a graph, only make was returned and foreign was not exported, so with the qualifier stored as dash stores it both modes warned "if_expr filter skipped" and drew all 74 cars instead of 22, and a KPI's value was wrong the same way.  The reader also grew each value a character at a time, quadratic in its length (48.6 s for 1,000,000 characters), which only its early stop hid.  Through the commands users type the bug was latent: two quoting bugs in dash.ado lose or cut a quoted qualifier before this reader sees it (recorded separately).
  - Suggested fix: Find an escape by its byte, 92; turn each escape into blanks (\uXXXX is six bytes); read each value in linear time and list each name once through a hash.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_qual_str` reads one JSON string value from the text's bytes, blanking every escape and raw control byte, in linear time (1,000,000 characters in 0.89 s); `_dash_extract_qual_vars` reads by() and if_expr through it and lists each name once, first seen first, through an asarray.  The tokenizing is otherwise unchanged.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-11 to JSE-19 (JSE-11 to JSE-18 failed at ad13a0f; JSE-19 guards the rewrite against a value that names nothing) and test/test_mata_backslash_literal.py, a static guard that no character in dash_parse.mata's Mata code is compared with a literal holding a backslash -- the third time the file has had this bug; registered under if_in_qualifiers.

- [x] Title: scatteri and pci wrote their immediate values into the spec as typed, so a malformed token registered and the build died on a JSON error; and none of the immediate types read labels, clock positions, missing values or a trailing record as twoway does
  - File Names and Line Numbers: at ad13a0f: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment` (the pcarrowi reader's call, 2451-2477, and the scatteri/pci token-by-token encoder, 2748-2795); dash_parse.mata section 2c (`_dash_pc_immediate`, `_dash_imm_number` at 831); dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_scatteri` (7904), `map_pci_literal` (8102), `_pc_immediate_records` and `map_pcarrowi` (8540)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f with the console binary, against twoway's own parsers (twoway_scatteri_parse.class, twoway_pci_parse.class) and the SVG each command draws.  scatteri and pci were written into the spec token by token, checked only for an even count: `dash pci 0 0 0 abc` registered, rc 0, as [[0,0],[0,abc]] and `dash scatteri .5 3000` as [[.5,3000]], neither of them JSON, and the build stopped on a JSONDecodeError, r(7102); so did `. 3000`, `.a 1`, `1d3 2`, `0x10 1`, and pcarrowi's `-1e400 0 0 1`, written [[-,0,0,1]].  twoway refuses the first ("invalid point", r(198)) and draws the others, a missing value drawing nothing.  Beside that: `scatteri 41 2040 "VW Diesel"`, the example in twoway's own help, was labelled "VW"; `scatteri 1 2 (13)` drew "(13)" as a label where twoway refuses it; `scatteri 1 2 (9) "A" 3 4`, `scatteri 1 2 3`, `pci 0 0 0 1 0 0 1` and `pci 0 0 0 1 "A"` were refused where twoway draws them, dropping a short trailing record without a word; and pcarrowi drew a label whose clock position is not a whole number at 3 o'clock, where twoway draws it at 12.
  - Suggested fix: Read all three types with one Mata copy of twoway's parse loop, writing every coordinate as a JSON number or null and every label escaped, refusing what twoway refuses with r(198), dropping what it drops with a note; draw the records through shared GlyphMapper helpers in both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_immediate(vals, w)` in dash_parse.mata section 2c reads scatteri (two coordinates) and pci and pcarrowi (four) as twoway's parsers do -- gettoken's tokens, a clock position only where one may stand, a label after it, "." and .a-.z and numbers past a float's range as null -- in linear time, refusing "invalid point", "invalid position" and a list with no whole record with r(198) and naming a dropped trailing record, where twoway is silent.  dash.ado sends all three types through it; pci's records now have pcarrowi's shape, and a label on pci draws nothing, as in twoway, with a note.  `GlyphMapper._immediate_records` and `_immediate_labels` read the records and draw the labels for scatteri and pcarrowi, each at its own clock position, else mlabposition(), else 3 o'clock, and a non-integer clock at 12, in both modes.  dash.sthlp's Plot types section describes the immediate values.  Two twoway accidents stay refused and are said so: a numlist range such as 2/2, which twoway evaluates as a division, and 1.0X+3ff.  Certified by test/test_parser.cscript P1A-60 to P1A-67, test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-34 to P20-36, test/test_immediate_values.py (33 tests) and two test/test_mode_parity.py corpus entries, every one failing at ad13a0f; registered under the new immediate_scatteri_pci_values surface and immediate_pc_values.  Found on the way and left as it is: a dashboard of immediate graphs alone is refused by dash build with "no variables identified across registered graphs", r(198), where twoway draws it.  **Reconciled 24sep2026:** a dashboard of immediate graphs alone is built now, saved and served, with data in memory or none; see the entry "`dash build` refused a dashboard of immediate graphs alone, and the one-line form refused one with no data in memory, where twoway draws both".

- [x] Title: A plot or graph if holding a double quote was dropped at registration, silently, and the qualifier's own text chose which of its caller's locals was overwritten
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment` (`_dash_json_str "`if_expr'" _jif` in plain quotes, line 2807 at ad13a0f) and `_dash_json_str` (472)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f and again at f7b28a2 (Stata 19.5 MP, batch, Stata's own row counts as the oracle).  The plot's qualifier was handed to `_dash_json_str` in plain quotes, so `args` split it at its first inner quote: the text before it became the value and the word after it the NAME of the local the result was stored under.  `dash scatter mpg weight if make != "AMC Concord" & foreign == 1` stored "if_expr":"" at the plot and the graph level and exported only mpg weight, and the saved page, the served app and the immediate route each drew all 74 cars instead of Stata's 22, without a word; so did the first plot of a two-segment graph and a compound-quoted literal.  A literal holding <, { or ' stopped with r(198) "_< invalid name" and the like.
  - Suggested fix: Hand the qualifier over in compound quotes, and read `_dash_json_str`'s text by value.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The qualifier is passed in compound quotes and `_dash_json_str` reads its argument by value (next entry); a qualifier holding a string literal is stored whole at both levels and both modes draw Stata's rows.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-20 to JSE-24 and, for literals holding Python code, SMCL, markup, JSON punctuation and 20,000 characters -- each data, giving Stata's 22 rows in both modes -- JSE-32 to JSE-36 (every one failing at f7b28a2); registered under if_in_qualifiers.

- [x] Title: `_dash_json_str` lost or cut any text holding a compound-quote close, so compound-quoted titles, notes, legend labels, by() titles, filter labels and qualifier literals were stored empty or cut
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_json_str` (472-513 at ad13a0f); dash_parse.mata `_dash_json_get_str` (1783 at ad13a0f) and the new `_dash_json_str_args`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f.  `_dash_json_str` read its text with `args` and escaped it with subinstr() calls in compound quotes; once a double quote became \", a compound quote in the text ended those quotes early, and the text came back empty or cut with rc 0: title(`"A "B" C"') and note() of the same form stored "", legend(label(1 `"L "q""')) stored label(1 ), by(foreign, title(`"By "origin""')) stored foreign, title(), a compound-quoted filter() label stored label(), and make == `"AMC"' & foreign == 1 stored make ==  & foreign == 1.  `_dash_json_get_str`, which reads such text back, decoded only a few escapes and grew its value a character at a time.
  - Suggested fix: Escape the text by value in Mata, writing the backtick, the dollar sign and the apostrophe as JSON escapes so the result can be expanded inside compound quotes, and decode every JSON escape on the way back in linear time.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_json_str` reads its `0' by value through `_dash_json_str_args`, which escapes with `_json_escape` (every control character, and the backtick, dollar sign and apostrophe as `, $ and '); `_dash_json_get_str` decodes every JSON escape, \uXXXX included, in one pass.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-22, JSE-27, JSE-28, JSE-29, JSE-35 and JSE-37 (each failing at f7b28a2); registered under graph_text_options, text_plot_options, by_option and build_options.

- [x] Title: A KPI's if was read back cut at its first double quote, so its variables were not exported or dash build failed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec` (`local _kifx = "${dash_kpi_<name>__if}"`, line 4269 at ad13a0f)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f.  The KPI's qualifier was read back through an expression in plain quotes, which ended at its first double quote: for `dash kpi mean(price) if make != "AMC" & rep78 == 3` rep78 was not exported, and both modes showed 6165.26 with a "filter skipped" warning where Stata's summarize gives 6429.23; with the literal "AMC Concord", dash build stopped with r(198) "invalid 'Concord'".
  - Suggested fix: Read the global whole, by copy.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `: copy global` and a length test.  Both modes show Stata's value (6429.23 and 6509.59).  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-25, JSE-26 and JSE-32 (each failing at f7b28a2); registered under kpi.

- [x] Title: The qualifier reader ignored ~ and Unicode variable names, so `if ~foreign` and an if on a Unicode-named variable exported no variable
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata `_dash_extract_qual_vars` (the operator list and the name pattern ^[a-zA-Z_][a-zA-Z0-9_]*$)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at f7b28a2.  The list of operators the reader blanked held ~= but not ~, Stata's logical not, and its name pattern was ASCII-only, so `if ~foreign` (Stata: 52 rows) and an if on a variable named with an e-acute (Stata: 22) named no variable to export, and both modes drew all 74 cars.
  - Suggested fix: Split on every ASCII byte a Stata name cannot hold, keep every byte of 128 or more, which is part of a UTF-8 letter, and pass over tokens that start with a digit; the callers confirm that each name exists.
  - Resolution: Fixed in the reader: the Unicode-named qualifier now draws Stata's 22 rows in both modes, and `if ~foreign` exports foreign.  The renderer still refuses `~x` and `!x` on a numeric column (pandas cannot invert a float), so that qualifier is still skipped with a warning and draws 74 rows: recorded for a separate fix.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-30 and JSE-31 (each failing at f7b28a2).

- [x] Title: lcolor(), lwidth() and lalpha() restyled the markers' outlines, where twoway's line options never touch a marker
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._apply_marker_opts` (the lcolor, lwidth and lalpha blocks at 4347, 4362 and 4426 at ad13a0f), `map_pccapsym` (its own filter of those options)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 9f48cf7 and again at ad13a0f from twoway's SVG export (StataNow 19.5, console binary).  On scatter, connected, dot, dropline, rscatter, rcapsym, rconnected, pcscatter, pccapsym and scatteri, twoway draws every marker the same with lcolor(red), lcolor(red%50) or lwidth(vthick) as without them; only mlcolor() and mlwidth() change a marker's outline (lalpha() is not a twoway option at all).  dash's marker applier took the outline colour, width and opacity from those line options on every marker type but pccapsym, which filtered them out itself -- so lcolor(red) on a connected plot drew red-rimmed markers.
  - Suggested fix: Read a marker's outline from mlcolor() and mlwidth() only; keep naming an unknown lcolor() or lwidth() style, as twoway notes one even on a plot with no line.
  - Resolution: Fixed, in both modes: `_apply_marker_opts` reads the outline from mlcolor()/mlwidth() and malpha() alone, still resolves lcolor() and lwidth() so that an unknown name is noted once, and applies nothing from them; `_apply_line_opts` styles the lines as before, and pccapsym's own filter is gone.  Certified by test/test_marker_label_parity.py (failing at ad13a0f) and test_twoway_option_parity.py.

- [x] Title: mlabel() drew labels on plot types twoway leaves unlabelled, labelled the wrong ends of range plots, and none on types twoway labels
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py GlyphMapper `map_line` (also tsline), `map_area`, `map_spike`, `map_rarea`, `map_rbar`, `map_rspike`, `map_rcap`, `map_rline` (also tsrline), `map_rconnected`, `map_rcapsym`, `map_rscatter`, `map_pcscatter`, `map_pcarrow`, `map_pcbarrow`; dash.ado `_dash_fold_bw` and the new `_dash_fold_mlab`; dash.sthlp Marker Label Options
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f against twoway's SVG export, ten observations per type.  twoway labels each point of scatter, connected, bar, dot and dropline; both ends of rcapsym, rconnected and rscatter (twenty labels); the first point, (y1, x1), of pcscatter, pccapsym, pcarrow and pcbarrow; and draws no label, without a note, on line, spike, area, rarea, rbar, rspike, rcap, rline, tsline and tsrline; mspline and mband refuse mlabel() ("too many variables specified", r(103)); histogram and function never read it, so mlabel(nosuchvar) is rc 0 there.  dash labelled all ten of the unlabelled types (the range ones at their top end), labelled rcapsym's top end only and each rconnected end twice, drew no label on rscatter, pcscatter, pcarrow or pcbarrow, accepted mlabel() on mspline and mband, and refused mlabel(nosuchvar) on histogram and function.  The help's list of the types mlabel() works on was wrong accordingly.
  - Suggested fix: Draw the labels where twoway draws them and nowhere else, refuse mlabel() on mspline and mband, and drop it unread on histogram and function.
  - Resolution: Fixed in both modes: the ten unlabelled types draw none; rcapsym and rscatter label both ends and rconnected each end once; pcscatter, pcarrow and pcbarrow label the first point; `_dash_fold_mlab` refuses mlabel() on mspline and mband with r(103) and drops it on histogram and function before the value rules; dash.sthlp's Marker Label Options says where labels are drawn and what they say.  Certified by test/test_marker_label_parity.py (146 tests, 145 failing at ad13a0f) and test/test_twoway_option_parity.cscript TP-08 to TP-12 (failing at ad13a0f), with mode-parity corpus entries; registered under text_plot_options.

- [x] Title: mlabel() of a value-labelled variable drew its codes, and a missing value as "NaN"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._apply_mlabel_opts` (the label text, 4890 at ad13a0f) and `_mlabformat_column`; the new `_mlabel_value_labels`, `_mlabel_column_kind`, `_mlabel_missing`, `_mlabel_number_text` and `_mlabel_value_text`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at ad13a0f.  twoway labels a value-labelled variable's points with its value labels (Domestic and Foreign for sysuse auto's foreign), an unlabelled value of it with its number as Stata writes a number into a macro (2 under %9.2f is "2", 0.5 is ".5", a %td value its day number), and a missing value with "."; .a draws its label.  dash drew the codes, 0 and 1, and a missing value as "NaN", or as nothing under a display format.  jsonio writes every missing value as null and keys value labels by the variable's name.
  - Suggested fix: Write the text twoway writes: the value label, else the number as Stata writes it, else ".".
  - Resolution: Fixed in both modes: the label text is derived once per source into a column named by the variable alone (so a served filter redraw keeps the column the LabelSet reads), value labels are looked up by label-set name and then by variable name, an unlabelled value is written as Stata's %18.0g writes it, and a missing value is "." in every format.  Certified by test/test_marker_label_parity.py, whose adversarial cases (a label keyed by another spelling of the code, markup in a value label, a huge label set, a long number) are registered under data_content.

- [x] Title: mlabangle(400) was accepted on pccapsym, where twoway refuses it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_option_rules` (the mlabangle rule's type list, 1369 at ad13a0f)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026: twoway refuses a marker-label angle outside -360..360 with "textangle must be between -360.000 and 360.000", r(198), on every type that draws marker labels, pccapsym among them; the rule's type list left pccapsym out, so dash accepted it.
  - Suggested fix: Add pccapsym to the rule's type list.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  pcarrow and pcbarrow stay off the list on purpose: twoway prints the message there and returns rc 0, drawing the arrows without their labels.  Certified by test/test_twoway_option_parity.cscript TP-04 (failing at ad13a0f).

- [x] Title: `dash serve' aborts with r(7102) on every launch: the renderer refuses the extension-less app path dash.ado hands it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_serve' (`tempfile serve_app_path', handed to `--output' with no suffix, 4938-4952 at 4ede074); dash_render.py `_validate_output_path' (21269-21316, `expected = ".py" if mode == "server"'), called from `main' (21354); the check came in with 7ddbc08 (WP-08, TR-08.8)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 with the console binary (sysuse auto, `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)', then `dash build, filter(foreign, type(dropdown)) ... nobrowser'): `dash serve, port(5487) timeout(1) nobrowser' printed "dash serve: starting Bokeh Server on port 5487" and then a Python traceback ending "ValueError: dash_render: --output must end in .py for mode 'server' (got '.../St71750.000003')", r(7102); nothing listened on the port. dash.ado passes the bare tempfile name on purpose -- its comment says the app runs as `python <path>', which needs no suffix, and a suffixed name leaked files -- and 7ddbc08 then made `main' require `.py' in server mode, so every launch aborts, the bare `dash serve' included. Nothing ran the real launch: test_serve_launch.cscript stops at the empty-registry check, test_serve_browser.cscript launches a stub renderer, and the Python tests run the generated app directly (GAPS.md C1, C2).
  - Suggested fix: Make the two sides agree -- let `main' accept the launch path's app file whatever its suffix when `--launch' is given (keeping the segment, leading-hyphen and control-character checks), or give dash.ado a registered `.py' tempfile it erases itself -- and first add a certification case that runs `dash serve, port(N) timeout(1) nobrowser' against the real renderer and waits for its exit.
  - Resolution: Not fixed. Still open (found 23sep2026 by WP-23 while measuring ROADMAP.md AC-03.1) because the fix is in dash.ado and dash_render.py, outside that documentation change, and CLAUDE.md rule 8 wants its failing test first.  Fixed 23sep2026 by WP-23: on the --launch route the renderer accepts a .py name, or a name no file holds yet, so it can never overwrite an existing file that is not a .py; `dash build, server` still needs .py, and the `..`, leading-dash and control-character checks hold on every route.  The app file stays a registered Stata tempfile, erased on success, on error and after Ctrl+C (measured: SIGINT to Stata's process group stopped the server, dash serve returned rc 0 within 0.7 s and the tempfiles were gone).  Certified by test/test_help_claims_4.py `TestTheOutputPathRule` and `TestTheRealHandoffServes` (the renderer run exactly as `_dash_serve` runs it: /health answered in 1.6 s, SIGINT stopped it at 2.0 s) and test/test_help_claims_4.cscript HC4-19 to HC4-21 (a real `dash serve, timeout(1)`: /health answered 200 while it blocked, rc 0 after 61 s, no copy of the app left); HC4-19 failed at 4ede074.

- [x] Title: A served by() panel title ignores the by() variable's display format
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, the app template's facet driver (`_panels.append((_dash_facet_label(by_vars, _key, val_label_names, val_labels), ...' at 20451 at 4ede074), against `DashRenderer._facet_label' (16065-16081), which also passes the numeric formats, the date variables and a date formatter
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074 with dash's classes (a scatter of mpg on weight, by(rep), rep holding 3, 4 and 5 as floats): with no format, or %8.0g, both modes title the panels 3.0, 4.0 and 5.0 (the "3.0" entry above); with `formats: {rep: %9.0f}' the saved page titles them 3, 4 and 5 and the served app 3.0, 4.0 and 5.0. The served call passes the value labels only, so a variable's display format -- and a date's -- never reaches a served panel title, although the app carries the formats (`_NUM_FORMATS', WP-10). The parity corpus's by() entries use foreign, which has value labels, so no comparison sees it.
  - Suggested fix: Pass the app's numeric formats (and, once the served app handles dates, the date formatter) to `_dash_facet_label' as the saved renderer does, and add a by() entry on a formatted numeric variable to test/test_mode_parity.py's corpus, written first.
  - Resolution: Not fixed. Still open (found 23sep2026 by WP-23 while measuring the "3.0" entry above) because the fix is in dash_render.py, outside that documentation change, and CLAUDE.md rule 8 wants its failing test first.  Fixed 23sep2026 by WP-23, by another route than the one suggested: twoway titles an unlabelled by() value with its number whatever the display format (measured, test/test_help_claims_5.cscript HC5-07), so neither mode reads the format now and both title 3, 4 and 5.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_5.py `TestThePanelTitles::test_a_display_format_plays_no_part` (both modes).

- [x] Title: A refused weighted command left its weight in s(), so the next dash command, written with no weight, was weighted by it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment` (the varpart dispatch, about line 2440 at 4ede074) and the varpart parsers `_dash_parse_varpart_std`, `_1v`, `_3v` and `_4v` (1102-1165 at 4ede074), which set s(_weight) and s(_wexp) only when a weight is given
  - Description: Found by WP-23's run of every help example and measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074.  s() is not cleared when an sclass program starts, and the segment's `sreturn clear` came after the weight checks, so a command refused in between -- lowess or histogram refusing the weight, a weight expression, a non-integer or negative fweight, tsline with no time variable -- left its weight behind, and dash clear does not reach s().  After `dash lowess mpg weight [aw=price]` (r(101)), `dash lfit mpg weight, name(g1)` registered an lfit weighted by price; after `[aw=price+1]` was refused, every later weighted type was refused with that expression's message; and a later scatter's note named `[aweight]` -- the help's own example sequence at dash.sthlp 1526 and 1538 showed it.
  - Suggested fix: Clear s() before the varpart dispatch.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `sreturn clear` before the dispatch.  Certified by test/test_statistical_options.cscript SO-93 to SO-96 (each a refused weighted command, then the same kind of command with no weight; all failing at 4ede074) and test/test_help_examples.cscript, which runs the help's examples in one session as a user would.

- [x] Title: A third t(), b(), l() or r() after t1title() and t2title() was dropped in silence at graph level
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_parse_graph_opts` (at 4ede074: `T() B() L() R()` declared at 567-568, and the t/b/l/r fold at 582-585 and again at 606-609)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23.  `dash (scatter mpg weight), t1title(A) t2title(B) t(C)` printed "ignoring unsupported option(s): t1title() t2title()" and never named t(); the same options at plot level, in the paren form and after || named it.  Stata's syntax binds a spelling to the first declaration that admits it and is still empty, and T1title() and T2title() admit t before T() does, so the explicit T() was reached only by a third spelling on one side -- and the fold then discarded that value.
  - Suggested fix: Remove `T() B() L() R()` and the fold; T1title() to R1title() take t() to r() directly.
  - Resolution: Fixed, with the duplicated generator NOTE comment and the fold copies (GAPS M4).  Certified by test/test_alias_semantics.cscript AS-06b (failing at 4ede074) and test/test_abbreviations.py `test_no_declaration_is_shadowed_by_an_earlier_one`, `test_no_fold_is_repeated_in_a_parser` and `test_every_fold_reads_an_option_its_parser_declares`.

- [x] Title: An unknown size() or color() inside title(), xtitle() or ytitle() replaced the scheme's style with a hard-coded one
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_figure_title` (14259-14273 at 4ede074), `_dash_style_axis_titles` (14276-14291), `_textbox_size` and `_textbox_color` (1573-1594)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23, in both modes.  The note said the unknown style was ignored, but the renderer then drew 16px or 13px and #000000: `title(T)` draws the scheme's 14pt, while `title(T, color(bogus))` under s1rcolor drew black text on a black background.
  - Suggested fix: Resolve with a default of None and set the property only when the value resolves.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_2.py `TestAnUnknownStyleInsideAStructuredOption` (10 of 24 failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: The console binary on a Mac opened dashboards with xdg-open, which macOS does not have
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_render_immediate` (3727-3729 at 4ede074) and `_dash_build` (4779-4781)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23.  Console Stata on a Mac reports c(os) "Unix" (c(machine_type) "Mac (Apple Silicon)"), so dash chose xdg-open: "/bin/bash: xdg-open: command not found", no window, and in the one-line form no line saying where the file was written.
  - Suggested fix: Use open when c(os) is MacOSX or c(machine_type) begins "Mac".
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_2.cscript HC2-30b and HC2-34f, which run the opener against stub open and xdg-open scripts put first on the PATH (HC2-29 refuses to run unless both resolve to the stubs).  A console run on a Mac now really opens a window unless `$dash_nobrowser`, nobrowser or nodraw says not to, so every batch run keeps setting `$dash_nobrowser` (test/test_cscript_hygiene.py enforces it for the cert scripts).

- [x] Title: A filter() label holding ";", "||" or the word "exclude(" stopped dash build or was refused
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec` (the exclude scan at 4245-4287 and the `_filt_work` loop at 4391-4434 at 4ede074); dash_parse.mata's new filter reader (`_dash_filter_split`, `_dash_filter_items`, `_dash_filter_refusal`, `_dash_filter_setvars`)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23.  The joined filter groups were split at every "||" and ";", and every "exclude(" was taken for the sub-option: a label holding ";" or "||" stopped dash build with r(132), or r(111) for label("Origin; or not"); label(A;B) was refused as naming a variable "B)"; label("exclude(nosuch) me") was refused as an exclude().  The help said a label may be anything but a semicolon, and that repeated filter() options are joined with a semicolon.
  - Suggested fix: Split only outside quotes and parentheses, read exclude() only as a group's own sub-option, and do both in Mata, by value.
  - Resolution: Fixed; the help now says a label may hold any character and that the groups are joined with ||.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_3.cscript HC3-03 to HC3-09, HC3-11, HC3-19 and HC3-20 (HC3-03 to HC3-09 failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: The renderer split filter text the same way and read an apostrophe as a quote, so a label was lost or an exclude() silently dropped, in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_parse_filter_raw` (14487-14560 at 4ede074); the new `_dash_filter_split` and `_dash_filter_label`, copied into the served app
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23.  `label(Bob's cars) exclude(g2)` built with rc 0 in both modes, titled the widget "Bob's cars) exclude(g2" and dropped the exclude() without a word; a compound-quoted label kept its quote characters; and `type(range),exclude(g1)` built no widget.
  - Suggested fix: A Stata-quote- and paren-aware splitter matching the Mata one, and compound quotes stripped from the label.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_3.py `TestAFilterGroupEndsOnlyOutsideItsText`, `TestBothModesShowTheLabelAsWritten` and `TestAnApostropheInALabelLeavesExcludeAlone` (all failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: filter() with an abbreviated variable built no widget
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_prepare_spec` (the filter group's variable)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: `filter(fore)` built with rc 0 and no widget ("the variable is not in the data"), because `confirm variable` accepted the abbreviation and handed it to the renderer, which looks up the full name jsonio writes.
  - Suggested fix: unab the variable and rewrite the group with the full name.
  - Resolution: Fixed through `_dash_filter_setvars`.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_3.cscript HC3-16 (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: layout(auto) read the figures' proportions on the saved page and not in the served app
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_default_grid_cols` (17256-17280 at 4ede074) and the served app's `_dash_auto_cols` (20970-20975); the new shared `_dash_auto_grid_cols`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: four graphs drawn xsize(12) ysize(3) were one column on the saved page and a 2 x 2 grid served; at a ratio of 2 the saved page drew one column fewer and the served app did not.
  - Suggested fix: One shared rule in both modes.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `_dash_auto_grid_cols`, used by both, and the help states the rule.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_3.py `TestTheAutomaticGridIsOneRuleInBothModes` (10 of 15 grid cases failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: A qualifier written after the parenthesised plot groups was dropped in silence
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata `_dash_split_segs` (1528-1549 at 4ede074); dash.ado `_dash_plot` (3021 and 3204-3216) and the new `_dash_graph_qualifier`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23.  `dash (scatter mpg weight) (line mpg weight) if foreign==1` drew all 74 cars with rc 0, where twoway draws the 22 foreign ones; a parenthesis inside the qualifier (`if inlist(rep78,3,4)`) was read as a plot group and refused with r(198); and `IF ...` and a plot group after the qualifier were accepted.
  - Suggested fix: Collect every top-level qualifier piece and read it with `syntax [if] [in]` as the graph's own qualifier.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_1.cscript HC1-01 to HC1-05 and HC1-21 to HC1-23 (failing at 4ede074) and test/test_help_claims_1.py `TestTheGraphQualifierReachesEveryLayer`.

- [x] Title: twoway's own spellings mbands and fn were refused as plot types
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata `_expand_plottype_str` (621-694 at 4ede074)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: `twoway mbands mpg weight` and `twoway fn y=x` run with rc 0 (twowaygraph_g.class `_unab_cmd`); dash refused both with r(198).
  - Suggested fix: Map mbands to mband and fn to function.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_1.cscript HC1-07, which holds dash to 79 twoway spellings measured live, and test/test_help_claims_1.py `TestThePlotTypeSpellings`.

- [x] Title: tsline and tsrline drew against a variable where twoway draws against the tsset time
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_process_segment` (2456-2466 and 2563-2625 at 4ede074)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23 against twoway's saved .gph: `tsline mpg turn` draws two series against the time variable in twoway, and dash drew mpg against turn; `tsrline lo hi turn` is refused by twoway with r(103), and dash drew it against turn.
  - Suggested fix: Follow twoway: every tsline variable is a series against the tsset time, tsrline takes two, and untsset data is refused.
  - Resolution: Fixed: dash now reads both as twoway does (untsset data r(111)); a dashboard that relied on an explicit x must use line or rline instead, as in twoway.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_1.cscript HC1-09 (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: Seventeen plot types drawn alone had no hover tooltips, in either mode
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_build_hover`'s fallback (15005-15043 at 4ede074)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: the fallback tooltip read only columns named x and y from a figure's first renderer, so fpfit, fpfitci, function, lfitci, qfitci, lowess, lpoly, lpolyci, mband, mspline, pcarrow, pcarrowi, pcbarrow, pccapsym, pci, pcspike and rcapsym, each drawn alone, had no tooltip in either mode, while the help says every figure has hover tooltips.
  - Suggested fix: Build the tooltip from each glyph's own drawn columns, by glyph shape, across every renderer, keeping only safe field names.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_1.py `TestEveryFigureHasTooltips` (40 types in both modes; 34 cases failing at 4ede074), `TestTooltipsBesideADataLayer` and `TestTheFallbackTooltipIsSafe`.

- [x] Title: The served app counted a layer's in over the rows a filter kept, not over observations
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py, the served template's layer qualifier (20078-20088 and 20278-20286 at 4ede074); the new `_dash_layer_frame`, copied into the served app
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: a layer's own in at the first draw, and the graph's and the layer's in after a filter move, were applied by position to rows already narrowed; with `in 2/8` and the dropdown on Foreign the redrawn line took observations 4, 6, 8 and 10 instead of 2, 4, 6 and 8.  The saved page was right.
  - Suggested fix: Count in over the dataset, once, in a shared helper.
  - Resolution: Fixed for the layer and graph qualifiers the served recompute applies: `_dash_layer_frame`.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_1.py `TestAFilterMoveCountsObservations` and `TestTheGraphQualifierReachesEveryLayer` (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: xtick() and ytick() replaced the axis labels when given alone, and were dropped beside xlabel()
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_dash_apply_axis_options` (12941-12961 at 4ede074, `raw = xlabel or xtick`); test/test_axis_options.py `test_xtick_is_honoured` (213-216 at 4ede074), which asserted the defect
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23, from twoway's SVG and in both dash modes.  twoway draws xtick() positions as unlabelled marks at full tick length and keeps xlabel()'s labels, or the default ones.  dash drew labelled ticks at the xtick() positions in place of the default labels when xtick() was given alone, dropped the marks beside xlabel() -- the ordinary way to write it -- and drew nothing beside xlabel(none).  GAPS F4 and ROADMAP TR-23.1 had recorded it as fixed by WP-14/15; the help said xtick() was honoured.
  - Suggested fix: Carry the marks as minor ticks styled like the major ticks: FixedTicker(minor_ticks=...) for a fixed label set, otherwise a CustomJSTicker wrapping the axis's own ticker, with constant code and the positions passed as data; name every xtick() sub-option in the note.
  - Resolution: Fixed; two parts of WP-23 found it and fixed it independently, and one implementation was kept: `_dash_apply_tick_option`, which makes the axis's ticker a CustomJSTicker whose major, labelled ticks are the old ticker's and whose minor ticks are the xtick() positions (a numlist or a(b)c, #N, or minmax), styled as major ticks, with constant code and the positions passed as data; a categorical axis is left alone; `grid` draws grid lines at the marks, as twoway draws them (measured: 56 more line segments with `xtick(2500 3500, grid)`); another sub-option is named; and it applies to the figure's own axes, never a yaxis(2) axis.  Registered in the served app's copied helpers.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_8.py `TestXtickDrawsMarksWithoutLabels`, `TestTheTickMarkCodeCarriesNoUserText` and `TestXtickSubOptionsAreNamed` and test/test_help_claims_7.py `TestTicksWithoutLabels` (failing at 4ede074), with test_axis_options.py's `test_xtick_is_honoured` rewritten to twoway's rule.

- [x] Title: A function layer was always drawn in the first series' colour
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_function` (9188-9359 at 4ede074, `default_color(0, ...)` in four places) and its callers at 16691 and 20136
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: twoway gives a function the colour of its place among the layers (a second layer p2, #90353B under stcolor; a third p3, #55752F); every other mapper received its layer position and map_function received none, so a function was drawn in p1 wherever it stood, in both modes.
  - Suggested fix: Give map_function its layer position at both call sites.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_8.py `TestAFunctionTakesItsPlaceInTheCycle` (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: rline, tsrline and rconnected drew their upper boundary in the next series' colour
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_rline` (7783 at 4ede074, `color_idx + 1`) and `map_rconnected` (7829-7830)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: twoway draws both ends of a range line, and rconnected's markers, in the layer's own colour; dash drew the upper end in the next series' colour, and under s2mono in the next symbol too, in both modes.
  - Suggested fix: Use the layer's own colour index for both ends.

- [x] Title: range() was ignored on lfit, qfit, lfitci and qfitci, and kdensity refused a range written high to low

  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: twoway draws a fit's line over range() (x from 1000 to 6000, measured), while dash drew it over the observed range with no note, in both modes; and kdensity refused `range(50 0)`, which twoway draws as `range(0 50)`.
  - Suggested fix: Read range() for the fits and kdensity through one reader, in either order.

- [x] Title: kdensity's width() was ignored, and width() beside bwidth() was accepted
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_fold_bw` (1201 at 4ede074); dash_render.py `GlyphMapper.map_kdensity`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: twoway kdensity's width() is its bandwidth (identical sersets to bwidth()); dash drew the default bandwidth, and accepted `width(2) bwidth(2)`, which twoway refuses with r(198).
  - Suggested fix: Fold width() into bwidth() for kdensity and refuse the pair.

- [x] Title: fpfitci refused stdp, which twoway accepts
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado (the std-flag rule, 1439 at 4ede074, and `_dash_validate_opts`)

  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_fp_report_unsupported` (9620 at 4ede074)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: bwidth() and degree() on an fpfit are named as ignored, as they should be (twoway refuses them there); kernel() was dropped in silence.

  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_6.py `TestFpfitReportsTheSmoothingOptions` (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: Marker labels were anchored at their bottom-left corner at every clock position, and mlabgap() moved them diagonally
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `GlyphMapper._MLABEL_CLOCK` (3884 at 4ede074), `_apply_mlabel_opts` (5006) and the new `_mlabel_gap_px`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23 against twoway's SVG: every label was anchored at its bottom-left corner, so mlabposition(9) ran the label across its point and mlabposition(6) sat it on the point; and mlabgap() was added to both offsets whatever the position, floored at one pixel.

  - Resolution: Fixed, in both modes.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_5.py `TestTheClockPositionPlacesTheLabelAsTwowayDoes`, `TestTheClockHelpers` and the `test_mlabgap_*` tests, and test/test_help_claims_5.cscript HC5-04, HC5-05 and HC5-21 (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: An mlabposition() twoway does not recognise was drawn at 3 o'clock, and "03" was taken as 3 in silence

- [x] Title: The served app wrote date marker labels and date by() titles as day numbers, and applied mlabformat() to a date in silence

- [x] Title: dash kpi refused fractional percentiles such as p97.5, which the help promised
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado `_dash_kpi` (3812, 3828-3858 and 3998 at 4ede074); dash_parse.mata (the statistic reader, 434) and the new `_dash_kpi_pctile`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 at 4ede074: `dash kpi p97.5(price), name(k1) title("P")` stopped with "dash kpi: syntax is stat(varname), e.g. mean(mpg).", r(198), and p100 with "percentile must be between 1 and 99", while the help said p10, p90 and p97.5 are all accepted and both renderers computed fractional percentiles.  Stata's `_pctile` accepts any percentile strictly between 0 and 100.
  - Suggested fix: Read p# as a decimal with 0 < p < 100, as `_pctile` accepts.
  - Resolution: Fixed: a percentile of at most 15 digits strictly between 0 and 100, stored under one spelling (p097.50 is p97.5), its value `_pctile`'s in both modes, unfiltered, under every filter type and with a KPI if/in.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_4.cscript HC4-01 to HC4-07 (HC4-01 failing at 4ede074) and test/test_help_claims_4.py `TestEveryStatisticIsStatas`.

- [x] Title: Both renderers took the wrong order statistic for many fractional percentiles
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py `_stata_percentile` (9961 at 4ede074) and the saved page's `dash_pctl` (11630)
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: Stata treats n*p/100 as an integer when it is within a relative 1e-15 of one; both renderers used an absolute 1e-12, so they disagreed with Stata at large n -- p81.93 of 1 to 10,000 read 8194 in both modes where Stata reads 8193.5, one of 1,947 disagreements on a grid of Stata-measured cases.
  - Suggested fix: Stata's rule, in Python and in the page's JavaScript.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_4.py `TestStataPercentileRule` (Python, the page's JavaScript and the served app's copy, over 4,858 Stata-measured cases in test/fixtures/kpi_pctile_truth.json) and `TestAFractionalPercentileOfTenThousandRows`.

- [x] Title: A second axis's range was 0 to 1 for eighteen plot types, and the first axis ranged over the second axis's layer
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py (the second axis's range, 3813 and 12985 at 4ede074; the saved figure's primary ranges, 16614; the served app, unrestricted); the new `_dash_secondary_values`, `_dash_finish_secondary_axes` and `_dash_keep_primary_ranges`
  - Description: Measured 23sep2026 by WP-23: the second axis's range was read from the yvar and xvar keys alone, so range, paired-coordinate, histogram, kdensity, function and scatteri layers drew off their axis on a 0-to-1 range and bars were cut off; and the first axis ranged over every renderer, the right-hand layer's included (an empty DataRange1d renderer list means all of them).

  - Resolution: Fixed, in both modes.  Certified by test/test_help_claims_7.py `TestTheSecondAxisFitsTheLayer` (40 types) and `test_the_first_axis_is_scaled_to_its_own_layers` (failing at 4ede074).

- [x] Title: The primary axis options reached the second axis, and a second-axis layer's labels and arrowheads stayed on the first

- [x] Title: legend(top), legend(bottom), legend(left) and legend(right) were drawn inside the plot, where twoway refuses them

- [x] Title: A string colorvar() gave one legend key per observation
