- [x] Title: A command giving name() twice was accepted where twoway refuses it, so a second name() could replace a registered graph or register a name twoway never would
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 68327a8: `_dash_process_segment` (the plot parser's call at 2965, whose catch-all kept a second name() of the same list, and the c_local of the graph options at 3016, by which a later segment's name() overwrote an earlier one), `_dash_plot` (the after-comma parse at 3280 and its restore at 3366, by which the after-comma name() overwrote a group's), `_dash_report_ignored` (which then named the second as "name()"); the new `_dash_refuse_second_name` and dash_parse.mata `_dash_has_name_opt`
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at 68327a8 with the console binary, sysuse auto.  twoway refuses any command that gives name() twice -- in another plot group, after a paren command's comma, in another || segment or twice in one option list, written name() or nam(), naming the same graph or two -- with "option name() not allowed", r(198); only when its first name() names a graph it already holds without replace does it say "graph g1 already exists", r(110), first, and when the first says replace it drops the old graph before refusing.  dash took one of the two -- the last group's, the after-comma one over a group's, the first of one list -- named the other "ignoring unsupported option(s): name()", and went on.  With g1 registered, `dash (scatter price weight, name(g1)) (line price weight, name(g1, replace))`, the after-comma form, `name(g1, replace) name(g1)` in one list and the || form each replaced g1 with rc 0; with nothing registered, every such command registered a graph, and `dash (scatter price weight, name(g1)) (line price weight, name(g2))` registered g2 alone.  dash kpi already refused a second name(): its syntax declares no catch-all.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse a second name() in twoway's words before anything is registered: in one option list, by the name() -syntax- leaves to the catch-all; across the plot groups, || segments and the after-comma options, by counting the lists that gave one.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_has_name_opt` (dash_parse.mata) finds a name() spelled name or nam among an option list's top-level option names, quotes and parentheses honoured, so text such as title("name(x)") and an option spelled NAME() do not count; `_dash_process_segment` and `_dash_plot` refuse one left in a list's catch-all, `_dash_plot` counts the lists that gave name() and refuses at the second, and `_dash_refuse_second_name` prints "option name() not allowed" and exits 198 before anything is registered or replaced -- unlike twoway, which drops a graph its first name() replaces before it refuses.  Four hundred options in one list are past what Stata's own -syntax- reads, and it refuses them first with r(1003), "too many options", in twoway and dash alike (a hundred are refused r(198) by both).  dash.sthlp's Differences item 2 says so.  Certified by test/test_registry_collision.cscript REG-37 (every form with g1 registered), REG-38 (two names, nothing registered), REG-39 (one name() wherever written, and text or NAME() not a second), REG-40 (a KPI card) and REG-41 (a hundred in one list, sixty groups, and four hundred); REG-37, REG-38 and REG-41 failed at 68327a8, and REG-33, whose three commands were refused only because the name() dash read held no replace, is restated as the second-name() refusal it now is and failed at 68327a8 too.  Registered under name_option.

- [x] Title: `dash build` refused a dashboard of immediate graphs alone, and the one-line form refused one with no data in memory, where twoway draws both
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a17c552: `_dash_prepare_spec` (the refusal at 4758, before the jsonio export at 4788), `_dash_render_immediate` (the check at 3900-3903 that admitted a spec when any one plot was scatteri, pci, pcarrowi or function, the one-observation placeholder at 3904-3916, the refusal at 3918); dash_render.py at a17c552: the per-plot qualifier in the saved render loop (17561) and in the served app (21296, 21492); the new dash.ado `_dash_empty_data` and dash_parse.mata `_dash_spec_types_in`
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a17c552 with the console binary.  After `dash scatteri 1 2 3 4, name(a1)`, `dash build, saving(f.html) replace` said "dash build: no variables identified across registered graphs.", r(198), and after `dash pci 0 0 1 1, name(a2)` so did `dash build, saving(f.py) server replace`.  With no data in memory the one-line `dash scatteri 1 2 3 4, saving(f.html)` said "dash: no variables identified in plot specification.", r(198).  twoway draws scatteri, pci, pcarrowi and function alone, with data in memory or none (rc 0).  The one-line route drew them only by exporting one observation of the first variable in memory, a placeholder no layer read; and it admitted a spec when any one plot was of that family, so a spec holding a scatteri beside a line with no variables was rendered and died in Python, "failed to build figure", r(7102).
  - Suggested fix: When no variable is needed and every graph is scatteri, pci, pcarrowi and function alone, write the data file directly -- jsonio's layout with no variable and no observation -- on both routes; keep the refusal for any other graph that names no variable.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_prepare_spec` and `_dash_render_immediate` export no variable when every graph's plots are of the literal family (`_dash_spec_types_in`, which matches each "type" of a spec whole, in linear time) and write the data file with `_dash_empty_data`; jsonio is required only when there is a variable to export; the placeholder is gone; a graph of any other type that names no variable -- one dash no longer registers, but the registry is ordinary globals -- is refused on both routes as before.  twoway ignores if and in on scatteri, pci and function (their sersets hold every point whatever the qualifier), so both render modes now skip a literal layer's own qualifier: with no data the saved page said it "could not be applied" and the served app that its if_expr filter was skipped.  dash.sthlp's Plot types section says a graph of these types needs no data.  Certified by test/test_parser.cscript P1A-72 (built saved and served, with data in memory and with none; the one-line form with none; the exported file's text) and P1A-74 (the refusals, the r(7102) spec among them), both failing at a17c552, and P1A-75 (the helpers), and by test/test_immediate_dashboards.py `TestADashboardOfImmediateGraphsAloneIsDrawn` and `TestTheDataFileIsTheOneDashAdoWrites`, both modes.  Registered under plot_varlist_and_type.

- [x] Title: An immediate plot whose every point is missing was reported as drawing nothing from the observations, in a graph named '?'
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a17c552: `_dash_report_empty_layer` (16219), its call in the saved render loop (17678, which named a graph with no name '?') and in the served app (21553, which named it ''); the new `_dash_note_empty_layer`
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a17c552, sysuse auto.  `dash scatteri . . . ., saving(f.html)` drew nothing, as twoway does, and said "dash: graph '?' plot 1 (scatteri) drew nothing from 1 observations." -- the observation the one-line route's placeholder, '?' the saved page's name for a graph with none; `dash pci . . . .` and `dash pcarrowi . . . .` said the same.  Registered beside `dash scatter mpg weight, name(b5)` and built, it said "graph 'b4' plot 1 (scatteri) drew nothing from 74 observations.".  twoway draws nothing and says nothing (rc 0): an immediate plot draws from its own values, not from the observations.  The served app named a graph with no name ''.
  - Suggested fix: One rule, run by both modes: never report a scatteri, pci or pcarrowi layer; report a function layer drawn empty -- its expression was refused -- whatever the data, with no count of observations it never read; report any other layer when the graph's data has rows; name a graph with no name by the layer's place alone.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_note_empty_layer` is that rule, and both the saved render loop and the served app call it; `_dash_report_empty_layer` words it, "dash: plot 1 (scatter) drew nothing from 74 observations." for a graph with no name, and "dash: graph 'g1' plot 2 (function) drew nothing." for a function.  A function expression dash refuses is still reported, now with no data in memory as well.  Certified by test/test_parser.cscript P1A-73 and test/test_immediate_dashboards.py `TestAnImmediateLayerMissingEverywhereSaysNothing`, `TestALayerThatDrewNothingIsNamedByWhatItHas`, `TestARejectedExpressionIsStillReported` and `TestTheReportHelpers`, both modes, each failing at a17c552.

- [x] Title: A plot type with its comma glued on was an "unrecognised plot type"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a17c552: `_dash_process_segment` (2518, where gettoken read the plot type up to a blank alone)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a17c552.  `dash scatteri, name(x)` said "dash: unrecognised plot type 'scatteri,'." and the Supported: list, r(198); so did `dash scatter, name(x)`, `dash pci, name(x)`, `dash (scatteri, name(x))`, `dash scatteri 1 2 || scatteri, name(x)` and every other type written with its comma glued on.  twoway reads a plot type up to a blank or a comma: `twoway scatteri 1 2,name(x)` draws, and `twoway scatteri, name(x)` is refused for the values it lacks, "'0' invalid observation number", r(198).
  - Suggested fix: Read the plot type up to a blank or a comma.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_process_segment` reads it with gettoken's parse(" ,") and leaves the comma where it ends the varlist, so each such command is read as its type and refused for what it lacks (see the next entry).  Certified by test/test_parser.cscript P1A-68, failing at a17c552.

- [x] Title: A plot given nothing to draw was registered with no variables, or refused in words twoway never uses
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a17c552: `_dash_process_segment` (2767, where the varlist parse of scatter, line and every other type that takes a y and an x was skipped when there was no varlist), `_dash_parse_varpart_any` (1173, whose anything() was required), `_dash_render_immediate` (3918)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a17c552, sysuse auto, for twenty plot types.  `dash scatter , name(z1)` registered a graph with no variables, rc 0, and so did line, connected, area, bar, spike, lfit, qfit, lowess and tsline, by the path every type that takes a y and an x shares; the bare `dash scatter` said "dash: no variables identified in plot specification.", r(198); scatteri, pci, pcarrowi and function given nothing said "something required", r(100); `dash function y =, name(f1)` was registered with rc 0 and drawn as an empty layer, "drew nothing from 74 observations".  twoway refuses all of them, whether the comma is glued, spaced or absent, in parentheses, after || or with only an if, an in or a weight: every type that takes variables with "varlist required", r(100); scatteri, pci and pcarrowi with "'0' invalid observation number", r(198); function with "expression required", r(100), and `function y =` with "nothing found where expression expected", r(111).
  - Suggested fix: Always parse the varlist, so that -syntax- refuses nothing with twoway's "varlist required"; let the literal parser take nothing, and refuse it by type.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_process_segment` always parses the varlist of a type that takes one, so -syntax- says "varlist required", r(100), and nothing is registered; `_dash_parse_varpart_any` takes an empty list, which `_dash_immediate` refuses as a list with no whole point, segment or arrow ("dash: scatteri needs at least one point, two numbers #y #x."), r(198), as twoway refuses it, and `_dash_process_segment` refuses a function with no expression in twoway's words, r(100) or, after `y =`, r(111).  dash.sthlp's Plot types section says so.  Certified by test/test_parser.cscript P1A-69, P1A-70 and P1A-71, each failing at a17c552.  Registered under plot_varlist_and_type and immediate_scatteri_pci_values.

- [x] Title: pci was drawn in line's colour and width and with no legend key, where twoway draws it as the immediate pcspike it is
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a17c552: `GlyphMapper.map_pci_literal` (8437-8487: its colour from `default_color(color_idx, "line")`, its width `scheme.default_line_width`, no legend label)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 from twoway's SVG export of `twoway (scatteri 5 5) (pci 0 0 1 1)`, `twoway (scatteri 5 5) (pcspike y1 x1 y2 x2)` and `twoway (scatteri 5 5) (line y x)` under s2color, stcolor, s1mono, s2mono, s2gmanual and economist: pci is stroked exactly as pcspike -- under s2mono #A0A0A0 at 8.64 where a line is #000000, under economist #7B92A8 at 12.67 where a line is 19.01 -- and keyed in the legend beside scatteri.  dash drew pci in the p#lineplot colour (s2mono's second plot #000000, where its pcspike is #a0a0a0) at the scheme's `linewidth p` (economist's 6.0px, where its pcspike is 1.5px), and gave it no legend key, so `dash (scatteri 5 5) (pci 0 0 1 1)` drew no legend.
  - Suggested fix: Draw pci through map_pcspike, as pcarrowi is drawn through map_pcarrow.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `map_pci_literal` builds a source from the records -- its columns named as the Segment's fields, x0 to y1 -- and draws it with `map_pcspike`, so pci takes pcspike's colour, width, legend key and line options in both modes.  dash.sthlp's Plot types section says so.  Certified by test/test_immediate_dashboards.py `TestPciIsDrawnAsPcspike`, both modes, failing at a17c552 under s2mono and economist and for the legend key.  Found on the way and left as it is: under economist twoway draws pcspike and pci at the scheme's `linewidth pother`, thick (.8, two thirds of the line's 1.2), and dash draws pcspike, and now pci, at a hard-coded 1.5px -- pcspike's own width, a defect of the paired-coordinate family's widths rather than of pci.

- [x] Title: A function missing anywhere in its range drew nothing at all, and was reported as empty
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a17c552: `GlyphMapper.map_function` (the eval at 9543 and the except around it)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026.  `twoway function y = sqrt(x), range(-1 1) n(5)` draws the curve where it is defined -- its serset holds . . 0 .70710678 1 -- and `log(x)` over the same range its right half; `function y = 1/0`, `x/0`, `10^400` and `exp(1000*x), range(1 2)` are missing everywhere, and twoway draws nothing, in silence, rc 0.  dash drew nothing for sqrt(x) and log(x) over range(-1 1), and reported each of these layers as having drawn nothing: numpy warns on a value outside a function's domain, and the warning, raised inside an eval() whose builtins are emptied, looked up __import__ there and raised KeyError, which the evaluator took for a failed expression; 1/0 and 10^400 raised ZeroDivisionError and OverflowError in Python's float arithmetic.
  - Suggested fix: Evaluate with numpy's floating-point warnings off, and read Python's ZeroDivisionError and OverflowError and numpy's inf and nan as Stata's missing value.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `map_function` evaluates under `np.errstate(all="ignore")`, reads ZeroDivisionError and OverflowError as a layer missing everywhere, and draws every non-finite value as missing, so a function is drawn where it is defined and a function missing everywhere draws nothing without a report; an expression dash refuses to evaluate is still drawn empty and reported.  dash.sthlp's Plot types section describes it.  Certified by test/test_immediate_dashboards.py `TestAFunctionIsDrawnWhereItIsDefined` and `TestAnImmediateLayerMissingEverywhereSaysNothing::test_a_function_missing_everywhere_is_not_reported`, both modes, and test/test_parser.cscript P1A-73, each failing at a17c552.  test/test_security_surface.py and test/test_resource_limits.py pinned an empty layer for 9^9^9^9 and the other power towers; they now pin a layer whose every value is missing -- each still overflows as a float, never as an exact integer, in bounded time.

- [x] Title: A date variable whose display format held a double quote, a backtick or a dollar sign lost its format on every render, or every render of it was refused with "too few quotes"
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 68327a8: `_dash_formats_json` (3117; the format read with `: format' at 3128 and tested inside quotes), `_dash_date_fmts_swap` (3160; the saved formats joined as macro text at 3166) and `_dash_date_fmts_restore` (3173)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at 68327a8 by WP-24's program coverage.  A display format is user text: Stata accepts %td!" and %td!` (a ! puts any character into a date picture) and %tdDD!$DD.  With a date variable formatted %td!" or %tdDD!$DD, the one-line render and `dash build' both returned rc 0 and left the variable formatted %12.0g in the user's data -- the saved list the swap handed the restore was cut at the quote, or had $DD expanded as a global, so the restore read the wrong format; with %td!` every render of the variable was refused, r(132) "too few quotes", because the backtick opened a compound quote the test never closed.  A variable named twice in the swap list also lost its format: the second swap saved the %12.0g the first had set as the original.  dash's own callers never pass a variable twice.
  - Suggested fix: Read, escape and classify the format in Mata, by value, and carry the saved formats in a form no quoting can cut.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_formats_json` reads each format with `st_varformat()' and escapes it with `_json_escape' (which writes the backtick, the dollar and the apostrophe as \u00XX) and classifies it with a regular expression in Mata; `_dash_date_fmts_swap` saves each format as its bytes' decimal codes joined by dots, which survive any quoting, and `_dash_date_fmts_restore` rebuilds it in Mata; both take their list through `list uniq'.  Certified by test/test_program_coverage.cscript PC-01 to PC-10 (ordinary, edge and hostile formats, a Unicode variable name, and both render routes drawing a date axis and leaving the format and the data as they were); PC-03, PC-06 and PC-07 each fail with the fix reverted.  `st_varformat()' takes no abbreviation where `confirm variable' and `: format' take one, so the first version of this fix, integrated with the rest of WP-24, stopped every render of `dash scatter mpg weight if dis > 100' with r(3500) "invalid Stata variable name" -- a qualifier may abbreviate a variable, and the words of its string literals are among the names read, so `if make != "d"' did the same -- and left an abbreviated date variable %12.0g, because the restore runs under capture.  All three programs now take each variable as Stata stores it, once (`unab'), and the formats map is keyed by that name, the column the renderer reads.  Certified by PC-31, which fails with either half of that reverted.

- [x] Title: A jsonio that failed left every date variable formatted %12.0g
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 68327a8: `_dash_render_immediate` (the export at 3916) and `_dash_prepare_spec` (the export at 4742), each between `_dash_date_fmts_swap` and `_dash_date_fmts_restore`
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at 68327a8 with a stand-in jsonio that exits 459, on both routes: dash swaps each date variable's format to %12.0g so that jsonio exports the number, and restores it after the export -- but the export was not captured, so a jsonio that failed ended the program before the restore, and the user's date variables were left formatted %12.0g.
  - Suggested fix: Capture the export, restore the formats, then exit with jsonio's own code.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Both routes run `capture noisily quietly jsonio out ...', keep its return code, restore the formats whatever it was, and then exit with it; jsonio's own message is still shown.  Certified by test/test_program_coverage.cscript PC-11 (both routes, the stand-in jsonio), which fails with either route's capture reverted.

- [x] Title: dash's Mata library check did not name _dash_path_badchar, so a library without it passed and the first saving() failed with r(3499)
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 68327a8: `_dash_require_mata` (203; the `_needed' list at 207-213)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at 68327a8.  `_dash_require_mata` checks that every Mata function dash.ado calls is loaded, so a library that compiled half way is refused at once with the functions it lacks.  Its list did not name `_dash_path_badchar`, which `_dash_validate_path` calls, so a library without it passed the check and the first command given a saving() stopped with r(3499), "_dash_path_badchar() not found".
  - Suggested fix: Name it in the list, and make the list's completeness a test.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The list names `_dash_path_badchar` and `_json_escape` (which `_dash_formats_json` now calls); the same library now fails the check with "Missing: _dash_path_badchar()", r(198).  test/test_program_coverage_map.py fails when dash.ado calls a Mata function the list does not name.  Certified by test/test_program_coverage.cscript PC-30 and test/test_program_coverage_map.py.

- [ ] Title: An lfit of a variable with no spread draws a sloped segment, and numpy's "poorly conditioned" warning reaches the user
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 68327a8: `_fit_grid` (9799; a zero-width range widened to one unit at 9830-9831) and `GlyphMapper.map_lfit` (6068; `np.polyfit' at 6108)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test of the pipeline's edge cases: for an lfit of y on an x that takes one value, 2500, twoway draws every point of its line at (2500, 24.5), the mean of y.  dash widens the grid to 2500..2501 and fits a line through points that all share one x, so it draws a short sloped segment from (2500, 24.5) to (2501, about 24.505), and numpy's "Polyfit may be poorly conditioned" warning reaches the user's log.
  - Suggested fix: Stop widening a zero-width grid, and when x has no spread use the coefficients (0, the weighted mean of y).
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only; the defect is pinned by a strict xfail, test/test_pipeline_integration.py::TestEdgeCaseData::test_ec04_constant_x_lfit_draws_what_twoway_draws, which the suggested fix turns into a failure to be removed with it (checked on that test and its neighbour only).

- [ ] Title: A dataset with no observations is reported as missing the variables it has
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 68327a8: `DashRenderer._load_data` (`pd.DataFrame(records)' at 16581) and the served app's loader (`df = pd.DataFrame()' at 20949)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 end to end with the console binary: after `sysuse auto', `keep if 0', `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)' and `dash build', dash returns rc 0 but prints "variable(s) ['mpg', 'weight'] not found in data source", and a Bokeh MISSING_RENDERERS line reaches the console.  jsonio exports the variable names with no records, and a frame built from no records has no columns.  twoway draws the scatter's empty region in silence.
  - Suggested fix: Build the frame with the variable names as its columns, in both loaders.  With the empty-layer rule of a2c7502, the layer is then silent; whether an empty dataset should be named is a choice to make.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only and the note it should give is a choice; test/test_pipeline_integration.py::TestEdgeCaseData EC-01 asserts that the graph is built and nothing drawn, and does not pin the note, so it holds either fix.

- [x] Title: dash's Mata library check took a library in memory only in part for whole, and said a copy it had just loaded was not on the ado-path
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a2c7502: `_dash_require_mata` (203; the one-function check at 217-219, the repair at 221-241, the report at 243-259)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's mutation campaign over WP-07 and its program coverage.  The check asked for one function, `_dash_check_utf8`, and took the library for whole when it was there: with a library in memory only in part -- a copy of dash_parse.mata whose compile stopped half way, or a function defined by hand -- it passed, and the first command died in Mata: after `mata clear' and a lone `_dash_check_utf8', `dash scatter mpg weight, name(x)' said "<istmt>: 3499 _dash_split_segs() not found", naming an internal function (ROADMAP AC-07.3 held only in a fresh session).  And when the repair found dash_parse.mata and loaded it but the copy lacked a function, the report said the library "is not on the ado-path and not beside dash.ado", where it had just been loaded from there.
  - Suggested fix: Check every function at entry, repair a part library by dropping what is there before reloading the file (a function already defined stops `run' at its definition, r(3000)), and say where the file was loaded from when it is incomplete.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  `_dash_require_mata` asks for every function in its list at entry (0.13 ms a command, measured over 1,000 runs); where one is missing and dash_parse.mata can be found, it drops the library's own functions -- `_dash_*()` and the five others, which test/test_program_coverage_map.py holds to dash_parse.mata's definitions -- and runs the file; where the file was found and still lacks a function, the report names the file, as text, and calls it an older or damaged copy.  Certified by test/test_env_guards.cscript ENV-23a (a part library is repaired and the command runs; r(3499) before), ENV-23b (with nothing to repair from, it is refused by name, r(198); rc 0 before) and ENV-22 (the report of a copy found but incomplete), each failing at a2c7502, and by test/test_program_coverage_map.py::TestTheMataLibraryCheckNamesEveryFunctionDashCalls::test_the_repair_drops_every_function_the_library_defines.

- [x] Title: dash build, server refused a saving() file name holding '..', which the HTML route builds
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a2c7502: `_dash_build` (5022; the server branch's `regexm("`saving'", "\.\.")' at 5071, in front of `_dash_validate_path')
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a2c7502 by WP-24's mutation campaign over WP-08.  `_dash_validate_path` refuses a '..' path SEGMENT and takes an ordinary file name that holds two dots, so `dash build, saving("my..report.html")' builds (BUGS.md, "The path validator refused my..report.html and accepted -e.html").  The server branch of `_dash_build` kept the old rule in front of the validator, a '..' anywhere, so `dash build, saving("my..report.py") server' was refused, r(198), "saving() path may not contain '..'" -- ROADMAP WP-08's BR-08.3 and AC-08.4 held on the HTML route only.
  - Suggested fix: Drop the old check: the validator, asked for .py, applies the segment rule.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The server branch goes straight to `_dash_validate_path ... "py"', whose segment rule still refuses `../x.py'.  Certified by test/test_path_validation.cscript PV-20 (my..report.py is written; a '..' segment is refused), which failed at a2c7502.

- [x] Title: A double quote in dash build's saving() stopped the command with "too few quotes", r(132), before the path validator could refuse it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at a2c7502: `_dash_build` (5022; `if "`saving'" == ""' at 5066)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at a2c7502 by WP-24's mutation campaign over WP-08.  `_dash_build` tested saving() for emptiness inside plain double quotes before handing it to `_dash_validate_path`, so a path holding a double quote ended the quotes there: "too few quotes", r(132), on the HTML and the server route alike.  The command failed closed, but with a quoting error in place of the validator's "disallowed characters", r(198) -- the trap test_path_validation.cscript PV-02 records for the validator itself.
  - Suggested fix: Measure saving() by its length, and let the validator be the first to read it.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  The emptiness check reads `: length local saving'.  Certified by test/test_path_validation.cscript PV-21 (both routes: r(198) and "disallowed characters"), which failed at a2c7502 with r(132).

- [ ] Title: xline() and yline() are drawn black and dashed under every scheme, where s2color draws them cranberry and solid
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_dash_reference_lines` (15254; the style at 15285)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening, from twoway's SVG export of `twoway scatter mpg weight, xline(3000) yline(20)' (sysuse auto): under s2color each line is one solid segment in #C10534, cranberry -- scheme-s2color.scheme sets `color xyline cranberry' and `linepattern xyline solid' -- and under stcolor black and dashed.  dash draws `{"line_color": "#000000", "line_dash": "dashed", "line_width": 1.0}' whatever the scheme.  test/test_axis_options.py now holds the reference line to stcolor, the scheme its fixture was measured in.
  - Suggested fix: Read `color xyline', `linepattern xyline' and `linewidth xyline' from the scheme, as the other scheme styles are read, in both modes.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only; the scheme's own xyline style is not read yet.

- [ ] Title: A qualifier holding a subscript or a function call is skipped by the renderer, and every row drawn, where twoway draws the rows it selects
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_validate_if_expr` (10842) and the qualified-frame filter's warning (16152-16164)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening: `dash scatter mpg weight if mpg > mpg[1], saving(p.html)' returns rc 0, warns "if_expr filter skipped -- invalid syntax", and draws 74 rows; Stata's `count if mpg > mpg[1]' is 26.  `if abs(mpg) > 20' is skipped the same way (Stata: 36 rows).  The renderer's whitelist refuses subscripts and function calls, so dash says so rather than failing in silence, but it draws rows twoway does not.  test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-17 and JSE-23 use such a qualifier as the control for a skipped filter.
  - Suggested fix: Evaluate the qualifier in Stata (a marker variable exported with the data) or teach the whitelist Stata's subscripts and its safe functions; the controls in JSE-17/23 then need another qualifier the renderer skips.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only, and the choice between evaluating qualifiers in Stata and extending the whitelist is a design decision.

- [ ] Title: filterposition(left) under layout(auto) makes Bokeh print a W-1005 validation warning on every build
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: the filter sidebar, `column(*filter_row_widgets, sizing_mode="fixed", width=_SIDEBAR_WIDTH_PX)' (19422), which sets no height
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening: `sysuse auto', `dash scatter mpg weight, name(g1)', `dash build, saving(f.html) filter(foreign) filterposition(left) replace' prints "WARNING:bokeh.core.validation.check:W-1005 (FIXED_SIZING_MODE): 'fixed' sizing mode requires width and height to be set" and builds; with the filters at the top it prints nothing.  test/test_help_claims_1.cscript HC1-11 prints it on every run.  test/help_examples.py now flags a logged Bokeh warning in an example's output.
  - Suggested fix: Give the sidebar a height, or a sizing mode that stretches it with the page.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only; the page is drawn correctly, and the warning is Bokeh's.

- [ ] Title: The one-line form ignores by()'s cols() and rows(), laying the panels out as plain by() does
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_gopts_of` (19353), which finds a graph's options by its name, and `_dash_facet_cols` (14495)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening: `dash scatter mpg weight, by(foreign, cols(1)) saving(f.html)' puts the panels at (0,0) and (0,1), as `by(foreign)' does; the same graph registered with name(g1) and built puts them at (0,0) and (1,0).  The one-line graph has no name, so `_gopts_of' finds no options and `_dash_facet_cols' falls back to ceil(sqrt(n)) columns.
  - Suggested fix: Look the one-line graph's options up by its place, not its name.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only.

- [ ] Title: ylabel(#N) and xlabel(#N) ticks fall where Bokeh puts them, not where twoway does
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: the count form of the axis-label parser, `BasicTicker(desired_num_ticks=spec["count"])' (13420, 13531)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's regression test for "ylabel(#5, grid) was silently ignored": dash hands #N to Bokeh as desired_num_ticks, and Bokeh chooses the positions in the browser.  For `ylabel(#5)' on auto's mpg the saved page draws 15(5)40 over a y range of 10.55 to 42.45; twoway draws 10(10)40.  dash.sthlp calls #N "about N ticks, chosen over the data range", which is what dash does, but not where twoway puts them.
  - Suggested fix: Decide whether #N should reproduce twoway's nice-number ticks (computed in Python, drawn as fixed ticks) or stay Bokeh's, and document the choice.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it needs a decision between twoway's tick rule and Bokeh's, which WP-24 does not make; test/test_bug_regressions.py pins the count, not the positions.

- [ ] Title: The scheme's gsize text is parsed and read by nothing
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `SchemeParser` (its `text_font_size', from `gsize text')
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening with an AST scan of dash_render.py: `SchemeParser.text_font_size' has no load site.  The legend moved to `key_label', and no figure or CSS path reads `gsize text', so a scheme's text size reaches nothing it is meant to size.
  - Suggested fix: Give it its consumers (the texts `gsize text' sizes in twoway), or retire the key.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only; test/test_scheme_consumption.py::TestNoParsedKeyIsDiscarded pins it with a strict xfail, which a consumer or the key's retirement turns into a failure to be removed with it.

- [x] Title: inrange() with a missing bound selected no rows, in both modes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_stata_if_to_pandas` (10754; its `_inrange' at 10804-10810)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 with StataNow 19.5 by WP-24's function audit.  Stata reads a missing bound of inrange() -- `.' or `.a' to `.z' -- as no bound: inrange(x, ., 3) is x <= 3, inrange(x, 1, .) is x >= 1 and inrange(x, ., .) every non-missing x, and a missing x is never in range.  dash wrote ((x >= a) & (x <= b)) whatever the bounds, and the rewrite of Stata's missing idioms that follows it read `x >= .' as "x is missing": inrange(x, ., 3) and inrange(x, ., .) selected no row, and a bound of .a to .z was left as invalid syntax, so the qualifier was skipped and every row drawn.  Every graph, layer and KPI `if' was affected, saved and served.
  - Suggested fix: Leave out the comparison with a missing bound, and write (x == x) when both are missing.
  - Resolution: Fixed as suggested.  A bound that is a variable holding a missing value in some rows is still compared as NaN, which excludes those rows where Stata would not bound them; dash.ado exports what the qualifier names, so that case is reachable, and is not covered here.  Certified by test/test_function_audit.py::TestQualifierFunctionsSelectStatasRows (KPI cards in both modes held to Stata's own rows), which fails 8 of its cases with the old translation.

- [ ] Title: A linked by() graph with a line or connected layer logs Bokeh's E-1024 error on every saved build
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: the saved renderer's facet views (`DashRenderer._make_figure', a BooleanFilter CDSView on each by() panel's Line)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening, with Bokeh 3.9.0 and with the console binary: a by() graph under link() with a line or connected layer gives each panel's Line a CDSView with a BooleanFilter, and the build (rc 0) prints "ERROR:bokeh.core.validation.check:E-1024 (CDSVIEW_FILTERS_WITH_CONNECTED)" twice in the user's log.  Whether the browser draws those lines correctly was not checked.
  - Suggested fix: Give a connected glyph in a panel its own filtered source rather than a filtered view, as the computed layers have, or measure the drawing and document it.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only, and whether the lines are drawn wrong has not been measured.

- [ ] Title: A column name holding markup gets no tooltip and no note
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_dash_build_hover` (the name guard that drops a hostile column from the tooltips)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening: a pcspike whose column name carries markup gets no HoverTool in either mode, and nothing says so.  It is safe -- the name is never drawn as markup -- but dash.sthlp says every figure has hover tooltips.
  - Suggested fix: Keep the tooltip with the hostile field left out, or name the dropped field in a note.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only; test/test_help_claims_1.py now asserts that no tooltip field names the hostile column, with a plain-name control that gets its four ends.

- [ ] Title: A graph whose every layer is on yaxis(2) draws two y axes, where twoway draws one, on the left
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_dash_add_secondary_axes` and `_dash_finish_secondary_axes`
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening from twoway's export of `twoway scatter mpg weight, yaxis(2)': twoway draws a single y axis, on the left.  dash draws two, one on each side, as dash.sthlp describes a second axis, but not as twoway draws a lone one.
  - Suggested fix: When no layer is on the first y axis, draw the second one alone, on the left, as twoway does.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because WP-24 changed tests only, and dash.sthlp documents today's drawing.

- [ ] Title: A qualifier that abbreviates a variable is skipped by the renderer, and every row is drawn
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at a2c7502: `_stata_if_to_pandas` and `_dash_qualified_frame` (the qualifier is evaluated against the exported columns, which carry each variable's full name)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24 on a2c7502 and on the WP-24 tree alike: `sysuse auto; dash scatter mpg weight if dis > 100, saving(...)' returns rc 0 with "if_expr filter skipped -- invalid syntax", and the page draws all 74 cars where Stata's `count if displacement > 100' selects 60.  Stata reads `dis' as displacement; dash.ado exports displacement under its full name and hands the renderer the qualifier as written, so the name it asks for is not a column.
  - Suggested fix: Expand each abbreviated variable of a qualifier to its full name in dash.ado, outside the qualifier's string literals, before the qualifier is written to the spec.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it predates WP-24 and is a change to how dash.ado writes a qualifier, outside test integrity; test/test_program_coverage.cscript PC-31 holds only that such a command draws, with rc 0, on both routes.

- [ ] Title: A function layer naming a function Stata does not have draws nothing, where twoway refuses the command
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 7c28802: `GlyphMapper.map_function` (9378; its name check at 9452 raises "Unsafe name in function expression") and `_dash_report_empty_layer` (16290)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24's test hardening with the console binary: `dash function y = nosuchfunction(x), range(0 10), name(c)' returns rc 0, and the build draws an empty layer with the note "dash: graph 'c' plot 1 (function) drew nothing."; `twoway function y = nosuchfunction(x), range(0 10) nodraw' refuses it, "error in expression: nosuchfunction(x)", r(133) (measured again 24sep2026 with `dash function y = nosuchfunction(x), range(0 10) name(c)' and `dash build': rc 0 both).
  - Suggested fix: Refuse at registration, as twoway does, when the expression names a function outside the whitelist -- r(133), naming the function -- keeping the draw-time check for the expression text that reaches the renderer.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it is a change to what dash accepts, outside test integrity.  test/test_conformance.cscript C-03 uses this layer as the control whose empty-layer note it counts, and must be given another control when this is fixed.

- [ ] Title: A compound quote nested inside another is refused, where twoway accepts it
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata at 7c28802: `_dash_quote_step` (1414), which tracks a compound quote with an on/off flag, so the first closing `"' ends the outer quote
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24 with the console binary: `dash scatter mpg weight if make == `"say `"hi"' now"'' and `dash scatter mpg weight, title(`"a `"b"' c"')' are refused, "dash: unterminated quotation in the plot specification.", r(198); twoway accepts both, rc 0.
  - Suggested fix: Track compound-quote depth in `_dash_quote_step` instead of a flag, so each `" opens a level and each "' closes one.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it changes the parser every scanner shares, outside test integrity; test/test_qualifier_literals.cscript holds the literals dash reads today.

- [x] Title: cert_file_has missed any needle that straddled a 32,768-character piece of a line
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/cert_helpers.do at a2c7502: `_cert_file_has` (the fget() loop at 58)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24: Mata's fget() hands back a long line in pieces of 32,768 characters, and the reader searched each piece alone, so a needle across a piece boundary was never found.  A saved page is a few lines of a megabyte and more, so every one of the 423 cert_file_has calls in 37 certification scripts could miss what it looked for -- or, for an absence check, pass on what was there.
  - Suggested fix: Read the whole file, then search it.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `_cert_file_has` reads the file whole (fread in 1 MiB pieces, joined) and cert_file_count counts in the whole text.  Certified by test/test_conformance.cscript C-00, which tests the readers themselves and fails with fget() put back.

- [x] Title: Certification checks that searched a Stata log line by line depended on the length of the checkout's path
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/test_resource_limits.cscript at a2c7502 (RL-01 to RL-13), test/test_help_claims_1.cscript (HC1-15), test/test_help_claims_3.cscript (HC3-13), test/test_help_claims_5.cscript (HC5-03)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24: Stata wraps a log line at the line size, anywhere, and a Python warning starts with the path to dash_render.py, so where a message broke depended on where the checkout lives.  From a 212-character path test_resource_limits failed at RL-01, and HC1-15, HC3-13 and HC5-03 failed from a 210-character path; from this checkout they passed.  The maintainer's report of 23sep2026, RL-06 failing from a long worktree path, is this defect -- which case fails first depends on the length: reproduced 24sep2026 from a 258-character path, a2c7502 failed at RL-01's wrapped needle "set global dash_max_filter_options", and 2df1900 passed RL-01 to RL-14 from the same path.
  - Suggested fix: Search a log's logical lines, with Stata's wrapped continuation lines re-joined.
  - Resolution: Fixed: cert_log_has (test/cert_helpers.do) re-joins wrapped lines and every log search in those scripts uses it.  Certified by test/test_conformance.cscript C-00 (its log reader, which fails with the re-join disabled); each script now passes from the path it failed from, and fails there again with the re-join disabled.

- [x] Title: test_security_surface.py failed when run alone, and passed only when an earlier test had put test/ on the import path
  - File Names and Line Numbers: test/test_security_surface.py at a2c7502 (`from conftest import make_data_json' inside three functions, at 54-55, 256-258 and 774-776, before test/ was on sys.path)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24: run alone, or in a parallel worker where no earlier test had imported from test/, 28 served-app tests failed with ModuleNotFoundError (26 at 68327a8); run serially after other files it passed, the order masking it.  The maintainer's report of 23sep2026 (26 failures, run alone under the default xdist settings) is this defect: reproduced 24sep2026, `python3 -m pytest test/test_security_surface.py' alone gave 25 ModuleNotFoundError failures at a2c7502 and 166 passed at 2df1900, from the checkout and from another directory, and with --import-mode=importlib.
  - Suggested fix: Put test/ on the path and import at the top of the module.
  - Resolution: Fixed: the path insert and the import are at the top of the file.  Certified by test/test_security_surface_alone.py::test_its_served_app_helper_needs_no_earlier_test, which builds the served module in a fresh interpreter and fails with the old import order.

- [ ] Title: The one-line form overwrites an existing file named by saving() without replace, and ignores replace
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 7c28802: `_dash_render_immediate` (writes the page saving() names with no check for an existing file; `replace' is not among its options, so it is named in the "ignoring unsupported option(s)" note)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24 with the console binary: with f.html holding a file of the user's, `dash scatter mpg weight, saving(f.html)' returns rc 0 and replaces it with the dashboard; `twoway scatter mpg weight, saving(f.gph)' on an existing file refuses, "already exists", r(602), and `dash build, saving(b.html)' refuses too, "dash build: file '...' already exists. Use replace option to overwrite.", r(198).  Adding replace to the one-line form changes nothing: it is reported as unsupported and ignored.
  - Suggested fix: Accept replace on the one-line form and refuse an existing file without it, with dash build's message and r(198); document it in dash.sthlp beside saving().
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it changes what the one-line form accepts and refuses, outside test integrity; it is recorded here to be fixed first, since it can destroy a user's file.

- [ ] Title: A filter on a saved page leaves line, tsline, area, bar, spike and dropline layers, and a connected plot's line, as they were
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 7c28802: `map_line` (5432-5505; its `sorted_source` at 5468), `map_area` (5539-5622), `map_bar` (5624-5761), `map_spike` (5763-5821), `map_dropline` (8051-8147), `map_connected` (through `map_line`); `_build_filter_widgets` (`all_graph_sources` at 19236 holds only `self.sources`); dash.sthlp "What a filter move redraws" (1175)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24 at 7c28802, running the saved page's own filter callback under node: with 40 rows and a move that keeps 10, the page's scatter and dot layers go to 10, while line, tsline, area (VArea), bar (VBar), spike and dropline (Segment, and dropline's Scatter) and a connected plot's Line stay at 40, with link() and without; the served app redraws every one of them at 10.  Each of these layers draws from a copy of the columns its mapper builds, which the callback neither writes (`all_graph_sources`) nor recomputes (`agg_sources`).  dash.sthlp says the saved page "does not redraw a fitted line", implying every other layer is redrawn.
  - Suggested fix: Register each mapper-built copy with the filter callback, as computed layers are in `agg_sources`, so the script rebuilds it from the kept rows in the mapper's order (sorted by x for lines and areas), as the served app does.  A BooleanFilter view on a Line is not a fix: Bokeh rejects it (E-1024, open above).  Then parametrize test_filters.py's strict xfail over the seven types.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it is a change to the saved page's filter script, outside test integrity.  test/test_filters.py::TestFilterReachesTheRenderedSource::test_line_filter_updates_the_rendered_source pins the line case as a strict xfail that raises only the defect's own error, so a fix turns it into a failure to be removed with it.

- [ ] Title: A range layer with no second y variable is reported as a plot type with no glyph mapper
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 7c28802: `_dash_add_glyph` (16472-16488)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24: a spec whose rscatter, rarea or rcap layer gives `yvar1' and no `yvar2' draws nothing and warns "dash: no glyph mapper for plot type 'rscatter'; nothing was drawn for that layer." -- false: the mapper exists, and the missing second y variable is the cause; the empty-layer report follows it.  Only a spec written by hand reaches this: dash.ado always writes `yvar2'.
  - Suggested fix: When the type is a range type and its second y variable is empty, say that the layer names no second y variable.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it is a change to what the renderer reports, outside test integrity, and dash.ado never writes such a spec.

- [x] Title: BOKEH_ALLOW_WS_ORIGIN replaced the served app's websocket origin list without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 7c28802: the generated launcher's `__main__` block (22507-22545) and `_dash_server_app` (22455-22505, `allow_websocket_origin=_origins` at 22500); dash.sthlp's Security section (1222-1234); bokeh 3.9 server/views/ws.py `WSHandler.check_origin` (113)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 by WP-24 with a running app and the handshake a browser makes: Bokeh's websocket handler uses the variable, when it is set, instead of the list the app passes it.  Set to another host, the app refused localhost and 127.0.0.1 -- the page at the address the launcher printed stayed blank -- and --allow-websocket-origin did nothing; set to `*', every origin opened a session, http://attacker.example included, so any web site the user visited while the dashboard ran could read what it draws.  Nothing was printed, and dash.sthlp's Security section never named the variable.  CLAUDE.md rule 15 asks for environment variables to be verified at start-up.
  - Suggested fix: Read the variable when the launcher starts and say what it does; refuse `*', or add the variable's origins to the app's own list instead -- a choice of policy.
  - Resolution: Fixed as far as the silence: the launcher reads the variable at start, prints that Bokeh admits its origins instead of the app's list, naming them, and warns when it holds `*' that any web site can open a session; dash.sthlp's Security section says the same.  The replacement itself is Bokeh's rule and stays the operator's choice, as WP-20 pinned it ("An operator who sets it owns the origin policy"); whether dash should refuse `*', or treat the variable's origins as additions, is left to the maintainer.  Certified by test/test_adversarial_matrix.py::TestSessionOrigin::test_bokeh_lets_the_environment_replace_it (the note names the variable and what it admits; the app without it says nothing) and ::test_a_star_in_the_environment_is_warned_of, both of which fail without the note.

- [x] Title: Any dash command holding a double quote followed by an apostrophe stopped at the entry, before anything was stored
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: the `dash` entry (95 `regexm(`"`0'"', ...)', 100 `_args = substr(`"`0'"', ...)'), and the UTF-8 gates `mata: _dash_check_utf8(strtrim(`"`0'"'))' in `_dash_plot' (3398, with `_dash_split_segs' at 3401), `_dash_kpi' (4335), `_dash_prepare_spec' (4644), `_dash_build' (5204), `_dash_serve_spec' (5381) and `_dash_serve' (5473)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 at 2df1900 with the console binary: each site put the whole command inside compound quotes in a Stata expression or a Mata line, and a text holding "' -- one that opens with an apostrophe inside double quotes, title("'90s cars"), or a qualifier's literal "' or 1 or '" -- ended those quotes early.  A graph title or qualifier stopped with r(3000), `dash kpi mean(price), title("'90s price") name(k)' and `dash build, title("'90s cars")' with r(100) ") required", their compound-quoted forms with r(133), a KPI qualifier with r(198); nothing was registered.  twoway itself refuses the plain-quoted forms on this machine, r(109) "type mismatch", under version 16 to 19, and draws the compound-quoted ones; dash now stores the text in both forms, as the maintainer asked, and each test's comment records twoway's r(109).
  - Suggested fix: Read the command by value in Mata, st_local("0"), at the entry and at every gate.
  - Resolution: Fixed: the entry takes the subcommand and the rest of the command by value, and every gate reads `st_local("0")'.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-50 (a KPI title, plain and compound, stored and drawn as typed), JSE-53 (a dashboard title) and JSE-54 (dash serve), each failing at 2df1900; the graph cases, which also needed the plot chain's own sites (the entry below), are JSE-42..49.

- [x] Title: A plot command stopped, or stored its text cut, when a title, text option, value or qualifier held a double quote followed by an apostrophe
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_process_segment' (2557 `args', 2617, 2620, 2624, 2687, 2736, 2922), `_dash_graph_qualifier' (1258-1259), `_dash_plot' (3419, 3438, 3451, 3491-3496, 3655), `_dash_register_graph' (3915, 3936), `_dash_validate_value' (1672), `_dash_check_legend' (1494, 1498), `_dash_report_ignored' (3349), `_dash_validate_opts' (2112-2113, 2132-2133), the option parsers' emptiness tests (from 666 and from 903), the varlist parsers (1133, 1150, 1167, 1184, 1224)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 with the entry already read by value (and at 2df1900 in brackets): `title("'90s cars")' gave r(100) ") required" [r(3000)] and its compound form r(133); after a paren command's comma, r(109); subtitle, note, caption, a legend label and by()'s title the same.  `if make == "' or 1 or '"' gave r(198) [r(3000)].  After the plot groups, `if make != "'x"' returned rc 0 with the qualifier stored as `make !=' and the named graph's JSON stored as the word x.  `legend(label(1 "'90s") top)' let `top' through, a weight refusal echoed `[aweight=cond(make==]', and `foo("'x") bar(1)' left bar() unnamed.  Each time the text was handed on inside `"..."', where its "' ended the quote.  twoway refuses most of the plain forms (r(109), r(198), r(100)) and draws the compound ones.
  - Suggested fix: Read user text by value everywhere it is handed on: `: length local', `: copy local', Mata st_local()/st_global(), string scalars inside expressions, and one reader of a program's own arguments.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `_dash_text_args' (dash_parse.mata) reads a program's leading and trailing arguments and the user's text between them by value; `_dash_register_graph' takes the names of three string scalars; `_dash_render_immediate' takes the JSON alone.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-42 to JSE-49, each failing with the entry read by value and at 2df1900; each fix site reverted alone fails a named case, except the block around `capture unab' in `_dash_process_segment', which runs only after `confirm name' has accepted the text.

- [x] Title: xtitle(), ytitle(), their 2 forms and t1title() .. r2title() lost the quotes the user typed
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_parse_graph_opts' (599, 605, 612-614, 618-619) and `_dash_parse_plot_opts' (820, 826, 833-835, 840-841), declared `string', not `string asis'
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `ytitle("line 1" "line 2")' was stored and drawn as one line, where twoway draws two; `xtitle(`"a "b" c"')' lost its quotes and was drawn as a / b / c, where twoway draws a "b" c.  title() has always been `string asis'.
  - Suggested fix: Declare them `string asis', as title() is.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-46 (its axis block, which fails with the declarations reverted: stored 00000000, drawn 011).

- [x] Title: A KPI qualifier holding "' was stored cut, and the card showed the value over every row
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_kpi' (the qualifier and the text fields stored at 4570-4588)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `dash kpi mean(price) if make == "' or 1 or '", title("K") name(k)' was refused at 2df1900 (r(198)); with the entry read by value it registered but stored the qualifier cut to `make ==', and the saved card showed 6165.26 (the mean over all 74 cars) where Stata's `summarize price if ...' gives `.', and 6384.68 for `if make != "'x" & foreign == 1'.
  - Suggested fix: Store the KPI's if/in and every text field by value.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-51 (both forms, both modes, against Stata's summarize), which fails with the store reverted.

- [x] Title: update(), filterposition(), link() and a KPI name holding double quotes stopped with raw Stata errors instead of dash's own
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_kpi' (the update() check at 4450-4452, the name at 4429), `_dash_prepare_spec' (the filterposition() check at 4776-4780, the link() check at 4808-4821)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: each value was compared inside plain quotes -- `if "`update'" == ""', `inlist("`update'", ...)', `if "`filterposition'" != "top"', `if "`link'" != "" & "`link'" != "_all"' -- so a value holding balanced double quotes broke the expression: update(`"a"b"'), filterposition(`"le"ft"') and link(`"g1"x"') gave r(132) "too few quotes", update(`"filter" name(x)"') and link(`"g1" "g2"') r(109) "type mismatch", and a KPI name(`"q"7"') r(303).  An odd number of quotes -- update(br"ush), filter(fore"ign) -- is Stata's own "unmatched quote" from -syntax-, r(198), before dash runs, as twoway answers title(a"b); filter("foreign") and filter(`"fore"ign"') already had dash's own "not a valid Stata variable name".
  - Suggested fix: Validate each by value in Mata.
  - Resolution: Fixed: each is read and checked by value, and the result is dash's own r(198) with its usual message, or the value accepted where it is valid.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-55 and JSE-56.

- [x] Title: exclude("g1") passed dash's check and the renderer then dropped the widget
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_parse.mata at 2df1900: `_dash_filter_refusal' (its exclude() names, 2369)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: dash checked exclude()'s names with their quotes removed, and the renderer splits them at blanks with their quotes kept, so `exclude("g1")' passed dash's check as g1 and the renderer, finding no graph named "g1" with its quotes, dropped the widget; `link(`""g1""')' was likewise accepted as g1.
  - Suggested fix: Split the names as the renderer splits them.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `_dash_blank_words' and `_dash_link_refusal' split at blanks with the quotes kept, and dash refuses a name the registry lacks by name.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-56.

- [x] Title: A filter label holding "' was lost, or became dashboard options
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_build' (5308) and `_dash_serve_spec' (5458), which handed the filter text on as macro text
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `dash build, filter(foreign, label("') csv title(pwned) subtitle("))' titled the page "pwned" and turned on the CSV download of every row, through dash build and dash serve -- the label closed its quote and the rest became options; other labels holding "' were stored cut.
  - Suggested fix: Hand the filter text on as written, and take it out again with the scanner that split it.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-57 (CLAUDE.md rule 18's bypass case for these surfaces), which fails with the hand-off reverted.

- [x] Title: saving() into a directory that does not exist ended in a Python traceback
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: `_dash_build' and the one-line form, which handed such a path to the renderer
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `dash build, saving('/private/tmp/x.html')' -- the apostrophe makes the path relative, to a directory that does not exist -- passed the path validator and ended in Python with r(7102), "FileNotFoundError", on every route; without `replace' it said the file "already exists".  twoway answers such a path with r(603).
  - Suggested fix: Refuse a saving() whose directory does not exist before rendering, naming the directory.
  - Resolution: Fixed: `_dash_saving_dir' refuses it with r(603), naming the directory, on every route, before anything is rendered.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-52.

- [x] Title: A compound-quoted text lost the quotes it holds, and bare words around a string were dropped
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_parse_textbox_opt' (1650-1654, which took every plain-quoted run as a line)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026 against twoway's graph export svg: `dash scatter mpg weight, title(`"a "b" c"') saving(...)' drew the two lines "a " and " c" in both modes, where twoway draws the one line a "b" c; title(`"a "b""' "second") drew "a ", a blank line and "second"; title(a "b" c) drew "b" alone.  The spec carries the text as typed, compound quotes included; the renderer did not read it as Stata does.
  - Suggested fix: Read a text option as Stata's textbox.class does: no double quote, one line; otherwise each token a line, a compound string one token holding its quotes.
  - Resolution: Fixed with `_textbox_lines' and `_textbox_string_end'.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-60..62 and test/test_textbox_multiline.py `TestTheTextIsReadAsStataReadsIt', `TestBothModesDrawTheTextAsTyped' and `TestTextboxLines'.

- [x] Title: A comma inside a compound string or a parenthesis started a text's sub-options
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_textbox_split_point' (1537-1559)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: title(`"a "b, color(red) size(huge)"') drew "a " in red at 24px, and title(x (a, color(red) size(huge)) y) drew "x (a" in red; twoway draws both whole, in the scheme's colour and size.
  - Suggested fix: Find the comma outside every string, compound ones included, and every parenthesis, as -_parse comma- does.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-63 and test/test_textbox_multiline.py `TestTextboxSplitPoint'.

- [x] Title: An empty string did not end a text's lines
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_parse_textbox_opt'
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: dash drew title("a" "" "b") as "a", a blank line and "b"; twoway draws "a" only, and no title at all for title("" "a").
  - Suggested fix: End the lines at the first empty string, as textbox.class does.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_textbox_multiline.py::TestTheTextIsReadAsStataReadsIt::test_an_empty_string_ends_the_lines.

- [x] Title: The text-box sub-option reader took quadratic time
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_parse_textbox_opt' (1657)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: 32.5 s on a sub-option text of 50,000 "a(" and 71.6 s on 40,000 `"a( "'; from the command, `title(`"x"', size(large) "<25,000 a(>")' took 21.0 s for the whole build.
  - Suggested fix: Cut the text after the comma at its last ")" before the pattern runs.
  - Resolution: Fixed: the same build takes 1.9 s.  Certified by test/test_textbox_multiline.py's exhaustion case (CLAUDE.md rule 18).

- [x] Title: dash expanded macros in user text a second time
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado at 2df1900: the `dash' entry (103 `_dash_kpi `_args'', 107 `_dash_build `_args'', 115 `_dash_serve `_args'', 122 `_dash_plot `0''); `_dash_plot' (3451-3458, 3472, 3491-3496, 3551-3556, 3559, 3574, 3587, 3601-3603, 3655, 3658); `_dash_process_segment' (2687, 2933, 3013-3014, 3042, 3064-3065, 3085, 3100); the option parsers' folds and sreturn copies (638-644, from 698, from 874); `_dash_graph_qualifier' (1258-1261); `_dash_fold_bw' (1296, 1323); `_dash_fold_mlab' (1396); `_dash_validate_value' (1916); `_dash_validate_opts' (2023, 2112, 2132); `_dash_plot_opts_to_json' (2447-2449); `_dash_register_graph' (3925, 4020-4021); `_dash_render_immediate' (4068); and in `_dash_kpi', `_dash_prepare_spec', `_dash_build', `_dash_serve_spec' and `_dash_serve' every copy of the user's text through compound quotes and the one-line `if' that validated fmt()
  - Description: Reported 23sep2026 at c0200c0 and measured 24sep2026 at 2df1900 with the console binary: text a user typed escaped, so that it reaches dash as written -- a title typed Cost in \$US, a \`=exp' -- was expanded again inside dash: with global J6_G "Audi 5000", a title typed with \$J6_G and \`=1+1' was stored and drawn with the global's value and the =exp's result; \`=filewrite(...)' in a title, a KPI title, a dashboard title or a graph name wrote its file; `name(\$J6_N)' registered the graph as the global's value, `saving("\$J6_D/...")' wrote the page there, `legend(\$J6_T)' was refused as the word the global held, `[aw=\$J6_W]' weighted by price, and a qualifier typed with a global drew 21 rows where Stata's count of the typed text gives 22.  Two mechanisms: a command line built from a macro (`_dash_plot `0'', `_dash_kpi `_args'') and a copy through compound quotes each expand a macro inside the substituted text once more; and a one-line `if exp command' expands its command again, even through macval() -- fmt(\$m) reached the validator as %9.2f that way.  twoway expands the typed text again too, so the rule held is "never a second time", not twoway parity.  Where dash commands are built from data -- a do-file writing titles or qualifiers from a dataset's strings -- this ran the data's text as Stata code.
  - Suggested fix: Hand user text on by value everywhere it passes from program to program: macval() on each forwarding line, `: copy local' or Mata for copies, block-form `if's, and a name guard before `_dash_name_split'.
  - Resolution: Fixed.  Certified by test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-65..69 (the plot route: titles, text options, a legend label, axis titles, qualifiers, names, saving(), and the values dash checks), JSE-58 (the KPI, build and serve programs, driven directly) and JSE-70 (the entry's hand-off to dash kpi, dash build and dash serve), each with a global, an =exp whose file write is checked absent, and a macro reference, CLAUDE.md rule 18's injection cases; each fails at 2df1900.  JSE-70 fails with the entry's three macval()s reverted, the KPI title then stored as "AAudi 5000 B 2 C 1 D  E" and the file written.

- [ ] Title: The renderer does not draw a legend label given in compound quotes
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_dash_apply_legend', the label override at 13135-13148 (its pattern at 13143)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `legend(label(1 `"'90s lc"'))' is stored as typed (test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-46), but the page draws the default key text: the pattern accepts only a plain-quoted label.  twoway draws the label.
  - Suggested fix: Read the label with the renderer's text reader, `_textbox_lines'.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it is a change to the renderer's legend reader, beyond the quoting this work fixed.

- [ ] Title: A by() whose text holds a backtick, a dollar sign or a backslash draws no panels, without a word
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash_render.py at 2df1900: `_DASH_UNSAFE_BY' (12632-12635), `_dash_parse_by' (12733-12734)
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: `by(foreign, title(`"'90s bc"'))', or a by() title holding $, is stored as typed, but the graph is drawn without facets, and nothing says why.  twoway draws two panels.
  - Suggested fix: Hold the variable list alone to the unsafe-text pattern, or warn when it refuses.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because it is a change to the renderer's by() reader; test/test_json_escaping.cscript JSE-46 checks its rows before it builds the compound form, so it does not depend on the panels.

- [ ] Title: An option whose value Stata's syntax cannot bind is named as unsupported, where twoway refuses the command
  - File Names and Line Numbers: dash.ado: the option parsers' catch-all `*' and `_dash_report_ignored'
  - Description: Measured 24sep2026: -syntax- leaves `jitter(x"' _dup(3) "INJ" `")' whole to the catch-all (syntax [, JITter(string) *] binds nothing to jitter), so `dash scatter mpg weight, jitter(x"' _dup(3) "INJ" `")' registers with "ignoring unsupported option(s): jitter()" and jitter() not applied; msize() the same.  twoway refuses both, "type mismatch", r(109).  At 2df1900 dash stopped at its first line that put the command inside quotes (r(3000); r(100) with the entry read by value) -- a refusal by accident, with a raw error; test/test_adversarial.cscript P20-18 now holds that no directive runs and that jitter() is named and not applied.
  - Suggested fix: Refuse a catch-all option whose name is one the parser declares, r(198), naming it.
  - Resolution: Not fixed: it stays open because whether dash should refuse here, as twoway does, is a choice of policy left to the maintainer.
